# goopg Design Documents

Design documents are **part of the deliverable** for this project, not scratch
notes. Every non-trivial subsystem must land alongside (or just before) its
design doc. See `.ralph/specs/GOAL_AND_REQUIREMENTS.md` §9 for the rules.

## Conventions

- Filenames use the form `<milestone-or-spec-id>-NNNN-short-slug.md`.
  `<milestone-or-spec-id>` is `root` for the foundational requirements spec
  and a milestone identifier such as `0002` for milestone-scoped docs.
  `NNNN` is a zero-padded sequence number within that identifier.
- Reserve a concrete filename before implementation work starts; do not
  leave bare `NNNN-*` placeholders in active plans.
- Each doc opens with a short metadata block: status, date, supersedes.
- Status values: `draft`, `accepted`, `superseded`, `historical`.
- When a new doc supersedes an older one, mark the older doc
  `superseded` and add a `superseded by:` link forward. Do not delete it.
- Cite upstream PostgreSQL with repository-relative paths
  (e.g. `postgres/src/backend/storage/buffer/bufmgr.c`).

## Design Bundles

- [fix-for-q5/README.md](fix-for-q5/README.md) - multi-file planner design set for making TPC-H Q5 choose the expected binary hash-join shape while keeping the rest of the TPC-H plan set stable.
- [bootstrap-procedure/README.md](bootstrap-procedure/README.md) - 12-file specification of every PG18 artefact goopg's `initdb` must produce **and** continuously maintain so a vanilla PG18 standby can attach via `pg_basebackup` at any time. Supersedes the reactive M0106-0010 step-3* loop (116+ docs). Covers ControlFile, WAL bootstrap, shared/local catalogs, BKI-derived catalogs, system views, relcache init files, streaming-replication readiness, a per-operation continuous-maintenance matrix, and a 35-task batched implementation roadmap.
- [perf-optimize/](perf-optimize/) - 10-chapter performance refactor design (00-overview, 01-memory-context `mctx`, 02-datum-pointer-free, 03-executor-concrete, 04-mvcc-procarray, 05-activity-perbackend, 06-bufpool-lockfree, 07-wal-fsm-insert, 08-runtime-internals, 09-migration-and-rollout). Targets c=10 SO 2 307→8 000 TPS, c=50 SU 347→2 000 TPS, c=100 SU SKIP→500 TPS, `gcBgMarkWorker` 63 %→<15 %, `runtime.futex` <8 %. All on-disk formats stay PG-compatible. Implemented under [M0107](../milestones/0107-performance-optimization-refactor.md); PG-compat invariants enumerated in [0107-0001-m0106-pg-compat-invariants.md](0107-0001-m0106-pg-compat-invariants.md).

| 0002-0001 | [Checkpointing (M0002)](0002-0001-checkpointing.md) | accepted | Production-grade checkpointer: GUCs (`checkpoint_timeout`, `checkpoint_completion_target`, `max_wal_size`, `min_wal_size`, `full_page_writes`), max_wal_size volume trigger, spread/paced writeback, full-page-image WAL records on first dirty per epoch, SQL `CHECKPOINT` verb. |
| 0002-0002 | [Concurrent B-tree (M0002)](0002-0002-btree-concurrency.md) | accepted (L1+L2+L3a) | Plan to remove the global B-tree mutex: L1 RWMutex (read parallelism), L2 per-page latches + Lehman-Yao right-link descent (high keys in BTPageOpaque, format v2), L3a atomic split WAL records (RecordKindBtreeSplit), L3b writer-vs-writer concurrency + page deletion (planned). |
| 0002-0003 | [Logical Redo Records (M0002)](0002-0003-redo-records.md) | draft | Recover from the FPI-everything regression. Adds Pool.MarkDirtyChangeRecord (once-per-epoch FPI baseline + logical records for subsequent dirties), RecordKindHeapInsert + RecordKindBtreeInsert + RecordKindHeapDelete + RecordKindHeapVacuum. Migrated paths: writeHeapRow, btree non-split insert, UPDATE/DELETE xmax stamps, VACUUM page prune. Btree metapage updates and btree internal-node insert remain on FPI-every-dirty until subsequent loops migrate them. pgbench -i WAL ~1.6 GB → ~21 MB; pgbench -t 30 default mixed stays flat. |
| 0003-0001 | [Planner Overview (M0003)](0003-0001-planner-overview.md) | draft | Milestone-level planner overview covering SELECT planning pipeline, join algorithm selection rules (nested-loop/hash/merge), EXPLAIN/cardinality seams, and deferred cost-based optimizer work. |
| 0003-0002 | [Join Executors (M0003)](0003-0002-join-executors.md) | draft | Join executor algorithms and planner promotion rules for disjoint-equality joins. INNER/LEFT use hash join; RIGHT/FULL use merge join; nested-loop remains the universal fallback for non-equality/cross shapes. |
| 0003-0003 | [Statistics & Cardinality (M0003)](0003-0003-statistics-and-cardinality.md) | draft | `planner.EstimateRows(n)` flows row-count estimates bottom-up: SeqScan reads `Stats.RowCount`; Filter/Limit/Sort/Project scale; Aggregate uses NDistinct on single-column GROUP BY; Join uses upstream's `\|L\|*\|R\|/max(NDistinct)` for disjoint-equality hash/merge joins. Surfaced via EXPLAIN's `(rows=N)` suffix; suppressed for unanalysed tables. Cost-model proper, predicate selectivity, join reordering deferred. |
| 0003-0004 | [HammerDB TPC-H Integration (M0003)](0003-0004-hammerdb-tpch-integration.md) | draft | TPC-H DDL runs end-to-end. Adds NUMERIC/DECIMAL codec (varlen text round-trip; integer→string formatting for INSERTs), decimal / scientific literal lexer (TokenNumericLit + parser.NumericConst), text→numeric assignability for HammerDB-shape multi-row INSERTs, `to_timestamp(text, fmt)` builtin, and `ALTER TABLE … ADD FOREIGN KEY` syntax (enforcement no-op). All eight HammerDB CREATE TABLEs and FK ALTERs verified via psql 18.3. |
| 0003-0005 | [CASE Expressions (M0003)](0003-0005-case-expressions.md) | draft | Both searched (`CASE WHEN cond THEN result …`) and simple (`CASE expr WHEN val THEN result …`) forms. parser.CaseExpr + planner.CaseExpr + executor evalCaseExpr; first-match-wins, NULL-is-not-matched. analyzer's analyzeCaseExpr unifies branch types same-or-compatible-else-unknown. Required for TPC-H Q1, Q12, Q14. |
| 0003-0006 | [Date / Interval Arithmetic (M0003)](0003-0006-date-interval-arithmetic.md) | draft | `date 'YYYY-MM-DD'` / `timestamp 'YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM:SS'` typed-string-literal sugar (parser.TypedStringLit) and `interval 'N' unit` (parser.IntervalLit, units day/month/year). New Datum KindInterval with months+days fields. evalBinary handles `time ± interval` via Go's time.AddDate (upstream-aligned month overflow). EXTRACT(field FROM ts) added with year/month/day/hour/minute/second/dow/doy/quarter/epoch fields. Required for TPC-H Q1/Q4/Q5/Q6/Q7/Q8/Q9/Q12/Q14/Q15/Q20. |
| 0003-0007 | [EXPLAIN (M0003)](0003-0007-explain.md) | draft | Parser KwExplain + parser.ExplainStmt wrap an inner stmt; planner.Explain wraps the planned inner Node; executor's explainOp pre-order walks the tree and emits one row per node as a single-column QUERY PLAN text result. Hash-join vs nested-loop visible in the label. Wire layer reports CommandComplete tag "EXPLAIN". Options/ANALYZE/JSON deferred. |
| 0003-0008 | [Subqueries (M0003)](0003-0008-subqueries.md) | draft | Scalar uncorrelated subqueries — `(SELECT …)` in expression position. Plus `[NOT] IN (subquery \| val_list)` and `[NOT] EXISTS (subquery)`. parser.SubqueryExpr / InExpr / ExistsExpr → planner mirrors → executor evalSubquery / evalInExpr / evalExistsExpr. Three-valued NULL semantics for IN; multi-column / multi-row violations report 42601 / 21000. Correlated subqueries (parameter pull-up) deferred. |
| 0003-0009 | [Views (M0003)](0003-0009-views.md) | draft | `CREATE [OR REPLACE] VIEW name [(col_list)] AS SELECT …` and `DROP VIEW [IF EXISTS]`. Catalog stores the view's parser AST; planScanRangeVar plans the inner SELECT recursively at every reference. Column types flow from the inner plan; column names from explicit aliases or target-list inference. Required for HammerDB TPC-H Q15. DML on views, catalog persistence, recursive views deferred. |
| 0003-0010 | [ANALYZE Statistics (M0003)](0003-0010-analyze-statistics.md) | draft | catalog.TableStats (RowCount, Pages, AvgWidth) + ColumnStats (NDistinct, NullFrac) populated by a new executor analyzeOp. Full-table scan under a fresh ReadCommitted snapshot via the existing buffer pool; per-column distinct sets tracked through the same datumKey canonicalisation aggregateOp uses. SQL ANALYZE dispatches via Build's type-check on the AnalyzeStmt. Sampling, MCV lists, histograms, and stats persistence deferred. |
| 0003-0011 | [LIKE Pattern Matching (M0003)](0003-0011-like-pattern-matching.md) | draft | `expr [NOT] LIKE pattern` parses at comparison precedence (postfix-style alongside [NOT] IN), analyzer requires text-on-text → bool, executor's `matchSQLLike` is the byte-level recursive matcher with `%` (any run), `_` (single byte), and `\` escape. Required for TPC-H Q2/Q9/Q13/Q14/Q16/Q20. ILIKE, ESCAPE clause, and prefix-anchor index extraction deferred. |
| 0003-0012 | [NUMERIC Arithmetic (M0003)](0003-0012-numeric-arithmetic.md) | draft | KindNumeric carrier (`int64 mantissa, int8 scale`) replaces the prior KindString-only path. NumericConst literals, codec decode, +/-/*/divide arithmetic with scale alignment, NUMERIC↔INT comparison, and sum/avg accumulators all wired through. Bounded by int64 (sufficient for TPC-H SF1 magnitudes); arbitrary-precision, typmod enforcement, %/^, and binary wire format deferred. |
| 0003-0013 | [BETWEEN Operator (M0003)](0003-0013-between-operator.md) | draft | `expr [NOT] BETWEEN low AND high` desugars at parse time to `(expr >= low) AND (expr <= high)` (or NOT-wrapped for the negated form), so analyzer/planner/executor reuse existing comparison + boolean paths. Precedence handling pins `BETWEEN ... AND high AND y` to parse as `(BETWEEN-tree) AND y`. Date codec also extended in this loop so date-typed columns share the 8-byte nanos-since-epoch on-disk frame with timestamps. |
| 0003-0014 | [Derived Tables (M0003)](0003-0014-derived-tables.md) | draft | `(SELECT …) AS alias` in a FROM clause: parser.RangeVar grows a Subquery *SelectStmt; analyzer's synthesizeSubqueryTable analyzes the inner and synthesizes a *catalog.Table (alias-named, columns from target list); planner's planSubqueryRangeVar plans the inner recursively and exposes its Output() through the rangeBinding. Required for TPC-H Q13. LATERAL, CTEs, and parenthesised plain relations deferred. |
| 0003-0015 | [pg_catalog Views (M0003)](0003-0015-pg-catalog-views.md) | draft | `pg_catalog.pg_indexes` virtual view (one row per index on each user table) joins the existing pg_class view; required for HammerDB's checkschema probe `SELECT tablename, indexname FROM pg_indexes WHERE tablename = '<t>'`. LookupTable falls back to pg_catalog when an unqualified lookup misses, so users can write `pg_indexes` without a schema prefix (mirrors upstream's implicit search_path entry). Configurable search_path, indexdef/tablespace reconstruction, and the rest of pg_catalog deferred. |
| 0003-0016 | [Join-Order Reordering (M0003)](0003-0016-join-order-reordering.md) | accepted | Cost-based pre-pass that permutes the comma-FROM list so small-cardinality tables join first when ANALYZE has supplied row counts. Greedy nearest-neighbour by cardinality with equality-edge preference, executed at parser-AST level so no resolved ColumnRef.Index needs remapping. Skips when stats missing, when an explicit JOIN clause is present, or when the result is the source order. TPC-H Q5's `customer, orders, lineitem, supplier, nation, region` reorders to `region, nation, supplier, lineitem, orders, customer`. |
| 0003-0017 | [Result-Parity Testing (M0003)](0003-0017-result-parity-testing.md) | accepted | In-tree parity tester that runs each Q1..Q22 against goopg AND upstream PostgreSQL 18.3 on the same synthetic dataset and diffs the rows. Adds a minimal upstream-PG lifecycle wrapper (initdb/pg_ctl). Fail-closed only on goopg-errors-while-upstream-OK regressions; row-content divergences logged as a triage list. Current matrix (after two NUMERIC-handling fixes): 19/22 identical, 3 divergent — all pure NUMERIC-precision deltas in Q1/Q8/Q14 (gated on arbitrary-precision NUMERIC). No structural gaps remain on the synthetic dataset. |
| 0004-0001 | [Go Test Utility Library (M0004)](0004-0001-go-test-utility-library.md) | draft | Documents `internal/testutil/util` and `internal/testutil/cluster` harness APIs for TAP-port tests: command runner, temp/file helpers, cluster lifecycle control, SQL client helpers, config/log assertions. |
| 0004-0002 | [TAP Test Port Strategy (M0004)](0004-0002-tap-test-port-strategy.md) | draft | Strategy and rules for porting upstream TAP rows into Go tests with per-test upstream path headers, adaptation policy for v0 feature gaps, and reproducible matrix generation via `cmd/gen-tap-coverage`. |
| 0005-0001 | [Streaming Replication Architecture (M0005)](0005-0001-streaming-replication-architecture.md) | accepted | Process model (primary walsender(s) + standby walreceiver), wire-protocol surface (replication-mode startup, IDENTIFY_SYSTEM / CREATE_REPLICATION_SLOT / START_REPLICATION SQL commands, MsgCopyBoth + WAL-data/keepalive/standby-status inner framing wire-compatible with upstream PG 18.3), state-transition diagram, replication-slot retention semantics, GUC surface, promotion path via control-socket PROMOTE, and the explicit hook list for each follow-up loop's implementation seam (protocol/server/control/wal/catalog touchpoints with file:line refs). |
| 0005-0002 | [Standby Recovery and Replay (M0005)](0005-0002-standby-recovery-and-replay.md) | accepted | Continuous standby-side WAL replay. Refactors `wal.ReplayRecords` into a per-record `wal.ApplyRecord` kernel and adds a streaming `wal.StreamReplayer` driven by a `wal.RecordIterator`, applying each record as it lands via the writer's flush-event subscription. Restart-resume relies on per-record `pd_lsn` idempotency rather than a separate apply cursor. Wired into `cmd/goopg start` standby boot alongside the existing walreceiver goroutine. |
| 0005-0003 | [Replication Observability (M0005)](0005-0003-replication-observability.md) | accepted | `pg_stat_replication` (one row per active walsender) and `pg_stat_wal_receiver` (zero/one row, the standby's walreceiver). Backed by new in-process `wal.Senders` and `wal.Receivers` registries with monotonic LSN advance via atomic CAS and `(slot, pid)`-sorted snapshots for stable row order. Walsender registers on entry to the streaming loop and publishes sent_lsn after each WAL frame + standby-reported LSNs after each status reply; walreceiver registers on Dial and publishes received_lsn + last-message timestamp on each frame. Column shape mirrors PG 18.x; lag intervals, hot-standby feedback, and per-record `client_addr`/`reply_time` deferred. |
| 0005-0004 | [Slot-aware WAL Retention (M0005)](0005-0004-slot-aware-wal-retention.md) | accepted | Post-checkpoint pruning of `pg_wal/`. Adds `Writer.RemoveOldSegments(keepLSN)` (serialised on the writer loop), `Slot.Invalidated` + `Slots.InvalidateLagging`, and a `SlotAwareRetainer` wired into the checkpointer. Keep horizon = `min(checkpointLSN, min(RestartLSN ∀ live slots))`; `max_slot_wal_keep_size` evicts laggy slots so a single standby can't pin WAL forever. `cmd/goopg start` builds the retainer from the GUC and threads `rt.Slots`/`rt.WAL` into `server.Config` so replication slots actually work in the production binary. |
| 0005-0005 | [Standby Promotion (M0005)](0005-0005-promotion.md) | accepted | Failover for streaming replication. Adds `OnPromote` callback to `control.Listener` + PROMOTE command handler, `Promote` seam on `server.Config`, and a `standbyController` in `cmd/goopg` that owns the receiver+replayer goroutines and exposes a drain-and-promote method (cancel receiver → wait → snapshot WrittenLSN → poll ApplyLSN to target → cancel replayer → remove `standby.signal`). New `goopg promote -D <dir>` CLI sends PROMOTE over the control socket. Single-timeline only; trigger-file polling and demotion deferred. |
| 0005-0006 | [replcluster Harness + E2E Acceptance Test (M0005)](0005-0006-replcluster-harness.md) | accepted | `internal/testutil/replcluster` orchestrates a primary+standby pair: pre-creates the slot offline via `wal.OpenSlots(...).Create(...)`, clones the primary's data dir to the standby (substitute for missing `pg_basebackup`), stamps `standby.signal` + `primary_conninfo`/`primary_slot_name` into the standby's postgresql.conf, then starts both. `TestReplicationEndToEnd` is the M0005 acceptance test: verifies streaming via `pg_stat_wal_receiver`, cross-checks `pg_stat_replication`, drives a CHECKPOINT to confirm WAL flow, then promotes. Also fixes a quiet bug: `goopg start` now auto-discovers `<datadir>/postgresql.conf` so a standby's primary_conninfo line takes effect without an explicit `-config` flag. |
| 0005-0007 | [Replication Event Logging (M0005)](0005-0007-replication-event-logging.md) | accepted | Canonical `event=<name>` vocabulary for replication lifecycle moments (`walreceiver_dial_failed`, `walreceiver_connected`, `walreceiver_disconnect`, `standby_replay_error`, `standby_replay_stopped`, `walsender_disconnect`, `slot_lag_warning`, `slot_invalidated`, `wal_segments_recycled`). Constants in `internal/wal/repllog.go`; producers in walreceiver/replayer/walsender/retainer. New `warnLaggingSlots` sweep on `SlotAwareRetainer.Retain` emits a `slot_lag_warning` INFO when a slot's lag passes `LagWarnFraction` (50%) of `max_slot_wal_keep_size`, giving the operator time to act before invalidation. Per-slot WARN log on actual invalidation. |
| 0006-0001 | [Sampling, MCV Lists, and Equi-depth Histograms (M0006)](0006-0001-sampling-and-mcv-histograms.md) | accepted | Reservoir-sampling collector replaces M0003's full distinct-set walk in ANALYZE. Sample size = `default_statistics_target * 300` (upstream's `targrows`); the GUC is threaded through `executor.Context.StatsTarget` from the session registry on both simple and extended query paths. Per-column MCV list (upstream `STATISTIC_KIND_MCV` admission rule with margin 1.25) plus equi-depth histogram boundaries over the non-MCV portion (`STATISTIC_KIND_HISTOGRAM`); orderable kinds only (int / bool / string / time / numeric). MCV / histogram values stored as `Datum.Format()` strings on `catalog.ColumnStats`. Persistence (`0006-0002`) and planner consumption (`0006-0003` / `0006-0004`) deferred to subsequent loops. |
| 0006-0002 | [Stats Persistence Through the Catalog Snapshot (M0006)](0006-0002-stats-persistence.md) | accepted | `catalog.TableEntry` grows `Stats *TableStats` (JSON-tagged with `omitempty`); `Snapshot` deep-copies and `Restore` re-installs `Table.Stats` so MCV lists, equi-depth histograms, RowCount, Pages, AvgWidth, NDistinct, and NullFrac all survive a clean stop / start. JSON tags freeze the wire format (`row_count`, `pages`, `avg_width`, `columns`, `ndistinct`, `null_frac`, `mcv`, `histogram`, `value`, `frequency`). Snapshots saved before this commit (no `stats` key) round-trip cleanly into `Stats==nil` and restore as unanalysed relations. New `cloneTableStats` helper guards against aliasing between source and restored slices. |
| 0006-0003 | [Clauselist Selectivity (M0006)](0006-0003-clauselist-selectivity.md) | accepted | `internal/planner/selectivity.go` replaces the M0003 flat `1/3` Filter multiplier with `clauseSelectivity` — an upstream-shape `clauselist_selectivity` analogue. Equality probes the column's MCV list and falls back to non-MCV mass / non-MCV NDistinct then `1/200`; range predicates interpolate the equi-depth histogram with mass-scoped MCV-hit accounting; AND multiplies under independence; OR uses inclusion-exclusion; `IN (vals)` sums per-value selectivities capped at 1.0; NOT inverts. Planner-side constant rendering mirrors `Datum.Format()` byte-for-byte so MCV lookup is a string equality. Unanalysed tables and unrecognised shapes still fall back to the M0003 `defaultGenericSelectivity` constant — the documented contract. New `columnStatsForChild` walks SeqScan / Filter / Sort / Project chains to find the per-column stats. |
| 0006-0004 | [Cost-Driven INNER-Join Algorithm Selection (M0006)](0006-0004-join-algorithm-selection.md) | accepted | `internal/planner/joincost.go` adds `chooseInnerJoinAlgo(L, R)` scoring hash (`build + probe`), merge (`L·log2(L) + R·log2(R) + L+R`), and nested-loop (`L*R`) under unit-row costs. Wired into both INNER call sites — `planner.go` (JOIN ... ON) and `pushdown.go` (comma-FROM CROSS-promotion). RIGHT/FULL stay merge (semantics-driven), LEFT stays hash (executor outer-row preservation), CROSS / non-equality stay nested-loop. When either side returns `EstimateRows == 0` the cost selector declines and the M0003 rule-based hash default holds — that's the documented fallback. Stats-aware EXPLAIN: `Seq Scan on <t>` now appends `(stats)` when `Table.Stats != nil` so an operator can verify which scans feed the cost model. `seq_page_cost` / `random_page_cost`, indexed-rescan nestloop pricing, and `enable_*` GUC consultation deferred. |
| 0007-0001 | [WAL Segment Preallocation (M0007)](0007-0001-wal-segment-preallocation.md) | accepted | `wal.Config.Preallocate` (driven by the new `wal_init_zero` GUC, default `on`) zero-fills new segments to `SegmentSize` and fsyncs them at creation time, plus the parent directory, so subsequent commit-path syncs don't pay for inode metadata or filesystem allocations on the hot path. The encoded zero header (`len=0 && crc=0`) is reserved as the EOS sentinel: `Writer.Append` rejects empty payloads (`ErrEmptyPayload`); `decodeRecord` callers stop on `isZeroHeader`. `detectWritePos` now scans the last segment record-by-record (via `scanLastSegmentEnd`) so it works for both legacy short-tail segments and full-size preallocated tails. `cmd/goopg start` threads the GUC through `initdb.Open(OpenOptions{WALInitZero})`. `fdatasync` switch (0007-0002), `wal_recycle`, eager next-segment lookahead, and counters deferred. |
| 0007-0002 | [`fdatasync` on the WAL Commit Path (M0007)](0007-0002-fdatasync-commit-path.md) | accepted | Replaces the per-segment `f.Sync()` in `flushUpTo` with a build-tagged `dataSync` helper: `unix.Fdatasync(int(f.Fd()))` on Linux (matches upstream's `pg_fdatasync`), `f.Sync()` on every other platform. The Linux path skips inode metadata flushes that no longer change between commits thanks to 0007-0001's preallocation. Full `fsync` is preserved at segment creation and the post-creation directory flush — those moments still need durable metadata. New files: `internal/wal/sync_linux.go`, `internal/wal/sync_other.go`. The misleading `wal: fdatasync %s` error prefix in `flushUpTo` is now accurate. `wal_sync_method` GUC selector and segment-removal directory fsync deferred. |
| 0008-0001 | [Logical Decoding Pipeline (M0008)](0008-0001-logical-decoding-pipeline.md) | accepted | Milestone-level design plus six implementation slices. **Loop 1:** logical slots + `pg_replication_slots` view. **Loop 2:** reorder buffer + decoder + `OutputPlugin` interface. **Loop 3:** `RecordKindXactCommit`/`XactAbort` + `Classify(d, r)`. **Loop 4:** `mvcc.Manager.SetXactMarkerLogger(fn)` + `initdb.Open` hook so every txn boundary lands a marker in the WAL. **Loop 5:** `SlotDecoder.Run(ctx)` consumes a `RecordIterator` from the slot's `RestartLSN`, dispatches via `Classify` into a `*Decoder` driving an `OutputPlugin`, and advances `ConfirmedFlushLSN` on every commit. **Loop 6 (snapshot builder skeleton):** `internal/wal/snapshot.go` introduces `CatalogSnapshot` (immutable per-RelOid map, built via `BuildCatalogSnapshot(c)` over the new `catalog.InMemory.AllTables()` accessor) and `SlotSnapshot{Catalog, MVCC}`. `NewSlotDecoderWithSnapshot(...)` attaches the bundle so a future pgoutput implementation can interpret tuple bytes against schema as it stood at slot creation. v0 cut: snapshot is fixed for the slot's lifetime; full upstream `SnapBuild` state machine and schema-change replay deferred. pgoutput, publication / subscription DDL, apply worker, and per-slot catalog-xmin retention hook in vacuum / pruning remain deferred. |
| 0008-0002 | [pgoutput Output Plugin (M0008)](0008-0002-pgoutput-plugin.md) | accepted | `internal/wal/pgoutput.go::PgOutput` implements the `OutputPlugin` interface with wire-shapes matching upstream pgoutput v1: `B` (begin), `C` (commit), `R` (relation, lazy-emitted once per session via an `emittedRel` set), `I` (insert), `D` (delete with 0-attr `K` body since v0 HeapDelete carries no pre-image). All multi-byte ints big-endian. Tuple body decoder mirrors `executor/codec.go::DecodeRow` byte-for-byte (duplicated rather than imported because `executor` depends on `wal`); supports `int4`/`int8`/`bool`/`text`/`varchar`/`numeric`/`timestamp`/`date`. Replica identity reports DEFAULT for every relation (catalog tracking lands with 0008-0003). Per-column `pg_type` OID mapping via `pgoTypeOIDFor`. `'U'` UPDATE deferred (executor emits paired HeapDelete + HeapInsert; reorder-buffer fold is its own loop). `T`/`Y`/`M`/2PC/streaming-mode out of scope. New tests pin the byte shapes for B/C/R/I/D plus the relation-once-per-session contract and unknown-rel skip behaviour. |
| 0008-0003 | [Publication / Subscription Catalog (M0008)](0008-0003-publication-subscription-ddl.md) | accepted | First slice of the publication / subscription DDL surface — the catalog substrate. New `internal/catalog/pubsub.go::PubSub` registry with `Publication` (name, OID, AllTables, publish flags, table list) and `Subscription` (name, OID, conninfo, publication list, slot name, enabled). `Create*` / `Drop*` / `Lookup*` / `*s()` accessors return deep copies. Five virtual catalog views in `replication_views.go`: `pg_publication`, `pg_publication_rel`, `pg_publication_tables`, `pg_subscription`, `pg_subscription_rel` — all backed by the registry, registered in `initdb.Open` next to `pg_replication_slots`. Column shapes mirror upstream PG 18.x; columns goopg doesn't track yet (`pubowner`, `pubviaroot`, `subbinary`, `substream`, `subtwophasestate`, `prqual`, `prattrs`) emit empty/`f`. `pg_subscription_rel` emits zero rows in this loop — tablesync state lands with the apply worker. Parser surface (CREATE/DROP/ALTER PUBLICATION + SUBSCRIPTION), executor wiring, slot provisioning at CREATE SUBSCRIPTION time, and on-disk persistence remain deferred to subsequent loops. |
| 0008-0004 | [Apply Worker and Table Sync (M0008)](0008-0004-apply-worker-and-tablesync.md) | accepted | Subscriber-side milestone design plus the first decoder slice. `internal/wal/pgoutput_decoder.go::DecodeMessage` is the byte-for-byte inverse of 0008-0002's encoder: parses one pgoutput v1 message (`B`/`C`/`R`/`I`/`D`) into a typed `DecodedMessage{Kind, XID, CommitLSN, EndLSN, Relation, RelOID, NewTuple, OldTuple}` plus `DecodedRelation` / `DecodedAttr` / `DecodedColumn`. Reader cursor enforces big-endian framing and surfaces `ErrTruncatedMessage` on short payloads. Tests pin the encoder/decoder symmetry for B / C / R-then-I (int4 + text body round-trips byte-for-byte to status='t' columns "42" and "alpha") / D (empty K body) plus truncated-payload guards. ApplyWorker scaffolding, TCP transport, the `i`/`d`/`s`/`r` tablesync state machine, slot-start handshake, and DELETE / UPDATE row resolution remain deferred to subsequent loops. |
| 0008-0005 | [Logical-replication observability (M0008)](0008-0005-logical-replication-observability.md) | accepted | Subscriber-side observability — first slice. `internal/wal/subscriber_mon.go::Subscribers` is the in-process registry of active subscriber workers (one `leader` per subscription + zero-or-more `tablesync` workers per relation; `parallel` reserved for a future loop). Each `Subscriber` carries identity (subID/subname/workerType/pid/leaderPID/relOID) plus atomic-backed `receivedLSN` / `latestEndLSN` counters and timestamp pairs (`lastMsgSendTime` / `lastMsgReceiptTime` / `latestEndTime`) so `Snapshot` is lock-free per entry. Snapshot ordering: `(subname, worker_type, relid, pid)` — stable across repeated SELECTs. `internal/initdb/replication_views.go::registerStatSubscriptionView` installs `pg_catalog.pg_stat_subscription` with the upstream PG 18.x columns (`subid`, `subname`, `worker_type`, `pid`, `leader_pid`, `relid`, `received_lsn`, `last_msg_send_time`, `last_msg_receipt_time`, `latest_end_lsn`, `latest_end_time`); `leader_pid` and `relid` render blank for leader rows, `latest_end_time` stays blank until the first `MarkMessage` with a non-zero endLSN. Wired into `initdb.Open` next to the existing replication views; `Runtime.WalSubscribers` exposes the registry to apply / tablesync workers. Tests: registry round-trip, sort stability, LSN monotonicity, MarkMessage timestamp/LSN updates, view rendering for a leader + tablesync pair. Apply-worker / tablesync-manager hookup that actually `Register`s into the registry, plus structured replication-event logging, remain follow-up slices. |
| 0008-0006 | [Structured replication-event logging (M0008)](0008-0006-structured-replication-event-logging.md) | accepted | Logical-replication structured logging built on the existing `slog` + `internal/wal/repllog.go` event-constant pattern from M0005. Adds five new event constants: `EventApplyCommit`, `EventApplyError`, `EventTablesyncStarted`, `EventTablesyncCompleted`, `EventTablesyncStateChange`. `executor.ApplyWorker.SetLogger(*slog.Logger)` plumbs a logger; `applyCommit` emits `event=apply_commit` with sub/xid/lsn, the per-message error path emits `event=apply_error` with sub/kind/rel_oid/lsn/err, and `promoteSyncedRels` emits `event=tablesync_state_change` with from=s/to=r/lsn. `server.TableSyncConfig.Logger` and `server.TableSyncManagerConfig.Logger` plumb the same logger through the tablesync transport; `RunTableSync` emits `tablesync_started` at entry, `tablesync_state_change` lines at every actual `i→d` and `d→s` transition (the i→d line is suppressed when the previous state was already `d` — re-entry on a partial sync), and `tablesync_completed` at exit with the row count. nil logger falls back to `slog.Default()` everywhere. The first draft introduced a `wal.ReplicationLogger` interface; rejected in favor of extending the established slog vocabulary so dashboards built against the M0005 event constants extend naturally. Tests: TestApplyWorkerLogsCommitAndPromotion (B/R/I/C through an apply worker with a tablesync rel at `s` produces both `apply_commit` with the right LSN and `tablesync_state_change` from=s to=r); TestRunTableSyncLogsLifecycle (happy-path sync of two rows produces `tablesync_started`, `tablesync_state_change i→d`, `tablesync_state_change d→s`, and `tablesync_completed` with `rows:2`). Production composition (server / cmd-level wiring that attaches a real logger to every live apply / tablesync worker) and walsender / walreceiver lifecycle events for logical-only flows remain follow-ups. |
| 0009-0001 | [AIO Core (M0009)](0009-0001-aio-core.md) | accepted | Foundational submit / wait / complete substrate under `internal/aio/`. Public types: `Direction` (read/write), `File` (`io.ReaderAt + io.WriterAt`), `Op { File, Buffer, Offset, Direction, Callback }`, `Result { N, Err }`, `Handle` (Wait blocks until completion, idempotent), `Method` interface, `Engine` factory. Two methods delivered: `MethodSync` (every Submit performs the syscall on the calling goroutine; useful as the safe default and as a fast-path for tests), `MethodWorker` (fixed-size goroutine pool drains a bounded channel that doubles as work queue + concurrency bound; default depth `4×workers` when `MaxConcurrency==0`). `MethodIOUring` reserved — `NewEngine` returns `ErrUnsupportedMethod` until the Linux-only follow-up lands. Engine carries process-wide atomic counters (`Submitted`, `Completed`, `Errored`, `InFlight`); `Stats()` returns a coherent snapshot. `io.EOF` is bubbled as-is and does not count as Errored — matches upstream's "EOF is an expected outcome" semantics. GUCs registered (all `ContextPostmaster`): `io_method` (enum sync/worker/io_uring, default `worker`, mirrors upstream), `io_workers` (default 3, range 1..1024), `io_max_concurrency` (default 0 lets the method pick — 4×workers for worker, uncapped for sync). GUCs are registered but not yet consumed — no caller plumbs through the engine in this slice; that wiring is the follow-up. Tests: TestEngineSyncReadWriteRoundTrip (write+read at the same offset round-trips through sync; counters reflect both Ops), TestEngineSyncCallback (optional Callback sees same Result as Wait), TestEngineSyncWaitIdempotent (double-Wait same Result no block), TestEngineWorkerParallelExecution (100 concurrent writes through 4 workers / cap=8 commit cleanly; in-flight counter never exceeds cap), TestEngineWorkerSubmitAfterCloseSurfacesError (close-vs-submit race surfaces engine-closed error rather than deadlock), TestNewEngineRejectsIOUring + TestNewEngineRejectsUnknown (ErrUnsupportedMethod surfaces with the offending value), TestEngineSyncReadEOF (EOF in Result.Err but Errored counter stays 0). Read-stream API, caller integrations (heap scan, checkpointer, WAL writer), pg_aios view, and the io_uring method remain follow-up slices. |
| 0009-0002 | [Read-Stream API (M0009)](0009-0002-read-stream.md) | accepted | Predictive-prefetch surface layered on the AIO core. `internal/aio/read_stream.go` ships `NextBlockFunc` (returns next byte offset or `EndOfStream`), `ReadStreamConfig` (Engine/File/BlockSize/NextBlock/Lookahead), `ReadStream` with `Next()` and `Close()`. `NewReadStream` validates the config (non-nil Engine/File/NextBlock + positive BlockSize), clamps Lookahead to `[1, MaxReadStreamLookahead=256]` (zero falls back to `DefaultReadStreamLookahead=4`), and primes the prefetch window with up to Lookahead `Engine.Submit` calls. Every `Next` blocks on the head prefetch's Wait, returns its bytes (truncated to the underlying ReadAt's reported byte count), and refills the window. Returned slices alias internal buffers and are valid until the next `Next` / `Close` call. `io.EOF` is the trailing sentinel after the callback returns `EndOfStream` AND the queue has drained. `Close` waits for in-flight prefetches to land rather than abandoning them so the engine's `InFlight` counter stays honest. Operates on `File`+offsets rather than buffer-manager `Buffer` handles to keep the abstraction reusable for non-heap-scan prefetchers (ANALYZE / vacuum). Deferred: contiguous-merge ("io_combine_limit"), sequential ramp-up, `Reset()` for restartable scans. Tests: TestReadStreamSequentialRoundTrip (4-block stream at Lookahead=2 round-trips bytes + trailing EOF + Submitted=4), TestReadStreamLookaheadCapsConcurrentSubmits (gateFile observes the engine's InFlight never exceeds Lookahead mid-stream), TestReadStreamHonoursDefaultLookahead, TestReadStreamClampsHugeLookahead, TestReadStreamRejectsInvalidConfig, TestReadStreamSurfacesPerBlockError (empty file + non-zero offset → io.EOF), TestReadStreamCloseDrainsInFlight (post-Close InFlight=0). Heap-scan / bitmap-heap-scan / ANALYZE caller integration is the next slice. |
| 0009-0003 | [AIO engine lifecycle + storage integration substrate (M0009)](0009-0003-aio-storage-integration.md) | accepted | Bridge slice that makes the AIO engine an actual lifecycle component of the running server, and gives the storage Manager a narrow prefetch-shaped seam so future heap-scan / ANALYZE / checkpointer / WAL callers can issue reads through it without each rewiring the engine themselves. (The M0009 milestone definition originally reserved `0009-0003` for "AIO checkpointer + WAL"; this substrate claims the slot and the checkpointer/WAL + observability docs become `0009-0004` / `0009-0005`.) `storage.AIOEngine` interface (`Submit(AIOSubmitOp) AIOHandle` over `AIOFile`/`AIOHandle` types) keeps `internal/storage` free of an `internal/aio` import. `Manager.SetAIO(eng)` attaches an engine; `Manager.PrefetchBlock(rel, blk, buf) (AIOHandle, error)` submits via the engine when set and falls back to a pre-completed `preCompletedHandle{n, err}` when not — so the caller's Submit/Wait code path is identical regardless. `relFile.ReadAt` makes the relfile satisfy `AIOFile`; the existing per-relfile mutex is shared between `Pin` and `PrefetchBlock` so parallel callers don't race on the `*os.File` cursor. `OpenOptions` gained `AIOMethod` / `AIOWorkers` / `AIOMaxConcurrency`; non-empty `AIOMethod` constructs an `aio.Engine`, surfaces it on `Runtime.AIO`, and calls `mgr.SetAIO(adapter)`. The adapter (`aioEngineAdapter` + `aioFileAdapter` + `aioHandleAdapter`) lives in `internal/initdb/open.go` to keep storage free of the aio import; `WriteAt` on the file adapter panics because PrefetchBlock is read-only. `Runtime.Close()` closes the engine AFTER the storage manager so in-flight prefetches drain cleanly. `cmd/goopg start` reads the `io_method` / `io_workers` / `io_max_concurrency` GUCs via new `stringGUC` / `intGUC` helpers; `io_uring` is silently downgraded to `worker` with a warn-level log line (`event=aio_method_fallback`) until the io_uring runtime probe lands. Heap-scan caller integration also lands in this same doc: `Pool.SetPrefetchEnabled(bool)` + `Pool.Prefetch(tag)` (warms the kernel page cache via Manager.PrefetchBlock, drops the AIO handle, atomic-load fast-path when disabled), and `seqScanOp.refillPrefetchWindow` keeps `seqScanLookahead=4` blocks ahead of curBlock prefetched. Tests: TestPrefetchBlockSyncFallback (no engine — pre-completed handle yields read bytes), TestPrefetchBlockUsesAttachedEngine (recording engine sees the right offset; bytes round-trip), TestPrefetchBlockOutOfRange (past-EOF surfaces ErrShortRead via Wait, matches ReadBlock), TestOpenAttachesAIOEngineWhenMethodSet (Runtime.AIO non-nil after AIOMethod="sync"), TestOpenLeavesAIONilWithoutMethod (no AIOMethod → no engine, existing synchronous paths unchanged), TestPoolPrefetchDisabledIsNoOp, TestPoolPrefetchEnabledFiresThroughEngine, TestSeqScanFiresPrefetchesAcrossBlocks (600-row scan; recording engine sees `[1, nBlocks]` submits). Bitmap-heap-scan / direct read-stream-driven seqScan + checkpointer + WAL writer caller integrations remain follow-up slices. |
| 0009-0004 | [AIO observability (M0009)](0009-0004-aio-observability.md) | accepted | Aggregate `pg_stat_aio` view + per-I/O `pg_aios` view + startup log line. **Aggregate (`pg_stat_aio`):** backed by `*aio.Engine.Stats()`; columns are `method` (sync/worker) + monotonic counters (`submitted`, `completed`, `errored`, `in_flight`). Single-row when an engine is attached, zero-row when none is. EOF is excluded from `errored` per the engine's "EOF is expected" semantics. **Per-I/O (`pg_aios`):** layered on per-handle tracking added to `aio.Engine` in the same slice. Engine gained `nextID` (atomic.Uint64), `inflight map[uint64]inFlightEntry` guarded by `inflightMu sync.RWMutex` (acquired only on Submit/completion — never on the hot path), and Handle gained `id` + `submittedAt`. New `Engine.registerInFlight(h, op)` runs after building the Handle and before kicking off the actual I/O so concurrent Snapshots always see the entry. New `Engine.finishHandle(h, r)` replaces the previous `completionBookkeeping(r)` — combines counter bumps, inflight-map removal, and `Handle.finish(r)` in one call. `Engine.InFlight() []InFlightInfo` returns a sorted copy (sort key: ID, monotonic by submission order). View columns: `io_id`, `operation` (read/write via Direction.String), `off`, `length`, `submitted_at` (RFC3339Nano UTC), `elapsed_us` (now − submittedAt). **Startup log:** `cmd/goopg start` emits `event=aio_engine_attached method=worker workers=N max_concurrency=N` right after Open returns when `rt.AIO != nil`, mirroring upstream's "AIO method = ..." shape; existing `event=aio_method_fallback` warn-level line covers the io_uring-requested-but-unavailable case. Both views wired into `initdb.Open`. Tests: TestStatAIOViewEmptyWithoutEngine + TestStatAIOViewReflectsEngineCounters (aggregate); TestEngineInFlightSnapshot (gateFile blocks ReadAt so test observes two outstanding Ops, asserts ID monotonicity / direction / offset / length, post-Wait inflight is empty); TestPgAiosViewEmptyWithoutEngine + TestPgAiosViewReflectsInFlightHandles (gated worker method blocks two reads, view returns two rows with right columns, post-Wait 0 rows). AIO wait events ("waiting on AIO completion" via pg_stat_activity-shaped surface), per-direction / per-relation counter breakdown, latency histograms, and `pg_aios.target_desc` (needs target identity on aio.Op) remain follow-up slices — all unblocked now that per-handle tracking is in place. |
| 0009-0005 | [AIO checkpointer + WAL writer (M0009)](0009-0005-aio-checkpointer-and-wal.md) | accepted | Write-side substrate + Pool.FlushAllPaced caller integration (landed); WAL writer integration deferred. **Substrate:** `storage.AIOSubmitOp` gained a `Direction` field (`AIODirRead` / `AIODirWrite`); `AIOFile` requires both `ReadAt` and `WriteAt` (relFile forwards both); `Manager.WriteBlockAIO(rel, blk, buf) (AIOHandle, error)` submits via the engine with `Direction=AIODirWrite` or falls back to a synchronous `writeBlock + preCompletedHandle` when no engine is attached; out-of-range surfaces a descriptive Wait error. The `aioEngineAdapter.Submit` honours `op.Direction`; `aioFileAdapter.WriteAt` forwards to the storage file. **Pool.FlushAllPaced caller wiring:** reshaped from a per-slot serial loop into a batched WAL-flush + parallel AIO-submit + Wait loop. New `Pool.SetAsyncFlushBatchSize(n)` + `MaxFlushBatchSize=256` cap (clamped negative→1, ×Max→Max). Per batch: phase 1 RLocks all `slot.contentMu` (writers can't tear pwrite-d bytes); phase 2 runs ONE `wal.FlushUpTo(maxLSN)` for the batch (every page has `pd_lsn ≤ maxLSN` so a single durability barrier covers them all — WAL-before-data preserved); phase 3 submits every data write through `Manager.WriteBlockAIO`; phase 4 Waits on every handle (drains all even on first error so engine InFlight stays coherent); phase 5 clears dirty bits where the slot's tag still matches. With no engine attached, `WriteBlockAIO` returns a pre-completed handle synchronously and the loop runs writes serially — bit-equivalent to legacy at batch=1. Pacing widens from per-slot to per-batch; M0002's smoothed-checkpoint contract is preserved at batch=1 = serial. `initdb.Open` calls `pool.SetAsyncFlushBatchSize(8)` after attaching the engine. `flushSlot` is unchanged for eviction-path callers (Pin / PinNew evict-and-flush). Tests (substrate): TestWriteBlockAIOSyncFallback / TestWriteBlockAIOUsesAttachedEngine / TestWriteBlockAIORejectsOutOfRange. Tests (caller wiring): TestFlushAllPacedBatchedSubmitsThroughEngine (3 dirty slots + recording engine + batch=4 → 3 submits all DirWrite; bytes round-trip after Invalidate+Pin), TestFlushAllPacedBatchSizeOneEquivalentToLegacy (default batch=0 → serial path still flushes a dirty slot correctly), TestSetAsyncFlushBatchSizeClamps. The exec-package recording-engine fake was extended to dispatch on Direction so TestSeqScanFiresPrefetchesAcrossBlocks's flush step still round-trips. WAL writer per-segment writeback through the engine remains a follow-up slice — substrate already in place. |
| 0009-0006 | [AIO io_uring method (M0009)](0009-0006-aio-io-uring.md) | accepted | Linux-only `io_uring` AIO method matching upstream `pgaio_uring.c`. `internal/aio/method_iouring_linux.go` talks to the kernel directly via syscall 425 (`io_uring_setup`) + 426 (`io_uring_enter`) — no third-party wrapper. `newMethodIOUring` runs the probe inline: any errno returns the sentinel `errProbeFailed`, which `NewEngine` matches via `errors.Is` and uses to silently fall back to `MethodWorker` (matches upstream's behaviour when the kernel can't honour `io_uring`: ENOSYS, sysctl `io_uring_disabled=1` EPERM, EINVAL on too-old kernels, ENOMEM under locked-memory limits). The non-Linux stub (`method_iouring_other.go`) returns the same sentinel on every other GOOS. Submission model: SQ/CQ rings + SQE array are mmap'd from the byte offsets the kernel returned (3× `mmap MAP_SHARED\|MAP_POPULATE`); SQE/CQE Go structs (`ioSqe` 64 B, `ioCqe` 16 B) mirror the kernel UAPI byte-for-byte. `Submit` serialises SQE writes under `submitMu` (io_uring's SQ is single-producer), release-stores the new tail, calls `io_uring_enter(submit=1)`. A dedicated reaper goroutine blocks in `io_uring_enter(0, min_complete=1, GETEVENTS)` and drains CQEs into the per-handle map (`pending[userData]`); idle ring costs one parked goroutine, no busy spin. `Close` wakes the reaper out of GETEVENTS by submitting an `IORING_OP_NOP` SQE with sentinel user_data `0xFFFF...FFFF` — closing the ring fd alone doesn't reliably unblock the syscall on every kernel. Slot semaphore caps in-flight at `min(MaxConcurrency, sqEntries)`; `MaxConcurrency=0` defaults to `sqEntries=32`. `Engine.FallbackFrom() (requested, reason)` reports any io_uring → worker fallback; `cmd/goopg start` logs `event=aio_method_fallback requested=io_uring actual=worker reason=...` post-Open so an operator triaging "but I asked for io_uring" can grep for it. Fd exposure: optional unexported interface `fdHaver { Fd() uintptr }`; `*os.File` satisfies it, and `storage.relFile` / `initdb.aioFileAdapter` / `initdb.walAIOFileAdapter` add forwarding methods so storage and WAL caller chains preserve fd visibility. In-memory test files don't satisfy `fdHaver` and Submit runs the I/O inline via `runOp` (correct, just no kernel acceleration). Tests: `TestNewEngineIOUringConstructs` (cross-platform — io_uring always constructs, FallbackFrom reports correctly), `TestEngineIOUringReadWriteRoundTrip` (Linux-only — pwrite-then-pread round-trips bytes through the kernel ring; per-direction counters bump; in-flight gauge returns to zero), `TestEngineIOUringParallel` (Linux-only — 64 concurrent writes + 64 reads verify no slot collisions in the userData→Handle map). Both Linux-only tests `t.Skip` when the engine fell back so the suite passes on any Linux host regardless of `io_uring_disabled` / seccomp. Registered files, SQPOLL, linked ops, fixed buffers, and io_uring-specific latency histograms remain follow-up slices. |
| 0010-0001 | [WAL direct-I/O write path (M0010)](0010-0001-wal-direct-io-write-path.md) | accepted | Linux O_DIRECT WAL writes for the primary writer + walreceiver, gated by a new `wal_direct_io` GUC (`TypeBool`, default `off`, `ContextPostmaster`). **Phase 1 (probe + plumbing)**: `wal.Config.DirectIO` plumbing, `probeDirectIO(walDir)` runs once at construction when DirectIO=true (opens `<walDir>/.wal_direct_io_probe` with `O_RDWR\|O_CREAT\|O_DIRECT`, observes EINVAL / EOPNOTSUPP, removes the sentinel; success returns empty reason, EINVAL/EOPNOTSUPP returns a human-readable fallback reason). `Writer.DirectIORequested()` / `Writer.DirectIOFallbackReason()` are stable for the writer's lifetime. `cmd/goopg start` logs `event=wal_direct_io_active` (probe ok) or `event=wal_direct_io_fallback reason=...` (probe rejected) — mirrors M0009 `event=aio_*` shape. **Phase 2 (alignment-safe writes)**: `state.directIOActive = directIORequested && fallback==""`. `enableDirectIO(f)` uses `fcntl(F_SETFL, flags\|unix.O_DIRECT)` to flip the flag on the live fd AFTER preallocation finishes (preallocation's 64-KiB heap-buffer zero-fill can't satisfy O_DIRECT alignment, so the flip is post-preallocation). `state.writeAt` dispatches to `state.writeAtDirectIO`: per-write RMW (pread aligned region into per-state mmap'd scratch → overlay user bytes → pwrite full aligned region back). `directIOScratchCap=1 MiB`; outsized writes loop through the scratch. Aligned scratch lazy-allocated via `unix.Mmap(MAP_PRIVATE\|MAP_ANONYMOUS)` (mirrors `internal/storage/arena.go`); freed in `state.close`. Block size hard-coded at 4 KiB (every modern Linux DIO-capable FS satisfies this; STATX_DIOALIGN-driven detection deferred). AIO+DirectIO bypasses the engine and uses synchronous RMW — engine-side aligned-copy is a perf-only follow-up (`Phase 2.b` in the design doc). Walreceiver's WAL-persist path inherits via the shared writer fd (`wal.Writer.Append` → `state.writeAt` → the same O_DIRECT-aware code path; no separate code path). On non-Linux GOOS the probe always falls back so directIOActive=false; the buffered legacy path runs unchanged. Tests: TestDirectIODisabledByDefault, TestDirectIOEnabledProbesFilesystem, TestDirectIOFallbackReasonStable (Phase 1); TestDirectIORoundTripWithPreallocation (three appends + flush + ReadAll round-trip via RMW; SegmentSize=4 KiB), TestDirectIORecordSpanningBlocks (12 KiB payload across ~3 block boundaries — every byte round-trips). Phase 2 tests `t.Skip` on probe fallback. Crash-restart correctness rides on the existing `TestPreallocatedSegmentRecoversCleanly` (the byte stream is identical under O_DIRECT vs buffered, so the M0007-0001 EOS-sentinel decoder sees no difference). The walsender in-memory WAL handoff (M0010-0002) and `pg_stat_wal_io` observability surface (M0010-0003) remain follow-up slices. |
| 0010-0002 | [Walsender in-memory WAL handoff (M0010)](0010-0002-walsender-in-memory-wal-handoff.md) | accepted | Bounded in-memory WAL byte ring backing walsender's `RecordIterator` so streaming replication doesn't depend on OS page-cache residency — pairs with M0010-0001's `wal_direct_io=on` (writes bypass the cache, so the ring is the cache replacement for sender reads). New `wal_sender_memory_buffer` GUC (`TypeInt`, default 16 MiB / 16777216, range `[0, 1 GiB]`, `ContextPostmaster`); 0 disables the ring. New `wal.MemRing`: fixed-size `[]byte` ring keyed by 0-based LSN-byte position. `Append(pos, data)` mirrors writer bytes (called from `state.append` AFTER `state.writeAt` succeeds — a failed pwrite must not appear in the ring). `ReadAt(pos, out)` returns `(n, true)` on full hit, `(0, false)` on miss (no partial-overlap stitching — caller fetches the whole range from disk in one go). Single `sync.RWMutex`: writers Lock during memcpy + head/tail advance, readers RLock during their own memcpy so a concurrent eviction can't free bytes mid-read. `hits` / `misses` are atomic counters bumped per `ReadAt`; observable via `MemRing.Hits()` / `Misses()` (M0010-0003's `pg_stat_replication.send_buffer_*` surface reads these). Eviction is FIFO: when `tail - head > cap`, head advances to `tail - cap`. Reset path handles post-recovery first-Append at non-zero `writePos`; oversized Appends keep only the trailing `cap` bytes. Iterator integration: `RecordIterator.readBytesAt` consults `it.writer.MemRing().ReadAt(pos, out)` first; on hit returns from RAM (zero syscalls), on miss falls through to the legacy per-segment pread loop. `cmd/goopg start` reads the GUC into `OpenOptions.WALSenderMemoryBuffer` → `wal.Config.SenderMemoryBuffer`; logs `event=wal_sender_memory_buffer_attached capacity_bytes=N` at startup when configured. Lagging senders that fall outside the retention window cleanly miss and resume from disk without dropping the connection — protocol semantics unchanged. Tests: TestMemRingNilSafe (NewMemRing(0)=nil; every method nil-safe), TestMemRingRoundTripWithinCap, TestMemRingEvictsOldBytesOnOverflow, TestMemRingPartialOverlapMisses, TestMemRingWraps (cross buffer-boundary write), TestMemRingWriteLargerThanCap, TestMemRingConcurrentReads (4 readers vs 100 writes — pins the lock-during-memcpy invariant); TestIteratorReadsFromMemRing (ring on + segment file deleted post-Append → iterator still streams, proving RAM source; Hits()>0), TestIteratorFallsBackToDiskWithoutRing (ring off + segment intact → pread succeeds, legacy path unchanged). Walsender's `pg_stat_replication.send_buffer_*` counters surface (M0010-0003) and the AIO+ring composition remain follow-up slices. |
| 0010-0003 | [WAL direct-I/O & walsender ring observability (M0010)](0010-0003-wal-direct-io-observability-and-operations.md) | accepted | SQL-queryable observability surface for M0010-0001 (`wal_direct_io`) and M0010-0002 (in-memory WAL ring). New `pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_io` virtual view: one row when a WAL writer is attached, eight columns: `direct_io_active` (`t`/`f` — true when DirectIO requested AND probe succeeded), `direct_io_fallback_reason`, `direct_writes` (lifetime count of `writeAtDirectIO` regions written via O_DIRECT pwrite), `tail_rmw_writes` (subset where the user range wasn't already block-aligned and the writer paid for a real read-modify-write — `tail_rmw_writes / direct_writes` is the alignment-induced overhead ratio), `send_buffer_capacity_bytes`, `send_buffer_bytes_resident` (current `tail - head` from the ring under RLock), `send_buffer_hits`, `send_buffer_misses`. Counter wiring: new `directIOCounters` struct (atomic `directWrites` / `tailRMWWrites`) shared between `wal.Writer` and `state` via pointer; `state.writeAtDirectIO` bumps both per-region; `Writer.DirectIOWrites()` / `Writer.TailRMWWrites()` accessors expose the counts. New `MemRing.BytesResident()` for the resident column. `pg_stat_replication` extended with the same `send_buffer_hits` / `send_buffer_misses` / `send_buffer_bytes_resident` trio (cluster-wide values appearing on every per-sender row) so `\watch pg_stat_replication` shows ring health alongside per-sender lag in one query. Tests: TestStatWALIOEmptyWithoutWriter (view registered with nil writer → 0 rows; pins the "view exists, just empty" contract), TestStatWALIORendersWriterCounters (capacity matches GUC; bytes_resident moves with appends; column shape stable), TestStatWALIOActiveWhenProbeOk (probe success → active=t; small Append bumps both direct_writes and tail_rmw_writes; `t.Skip` on probe fallback). Built and full `go test ./...` green. Operator playbook in the design doc covers when to enable / when not to / how to size the ring. |
| 0011-0001 | [B-tree NUMERIC key ordering (M0011)](0011-0001-btree-numeric-key-ordering.md) | accepted | Sortable byte encoding for NUMERIC B-tree keys: `EncodeNumericKey(mantissa, scale) []byte` produces a scale-invariant byte string such that bytewise compare matches numeric order. Layout: `sign(1) \|\| biased exp(4 BE) \|\| normalized digits \|\| terminator(1)`. Sign byte (`0x00` neg, `0x01` zero, `0x02` pos) settles cross-sign cases; exponent is biased-uint big-endian (inverted for negatives so larger E sorts smaller); digits are stripped of trailing zeros and emitted as ASCII (inverted `9-d` for negatives); terminator is `0x00` (positives) / `0xFF` (negatives) so prefix-equal-but-shorter sorts on the correct side of the digit run. Zero is the single-byte sentinel `[0x01]`. Numerically-equal inputs (e.g. `(10,1)` and `(100,2)`, both `1.0`) produce identical bytes — required for UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY equality. `CompareKeys` switched to straight `bytes.Compare` (length-first compare was wrong for variable-length keys; int4 keys are all 4 bytes so the change is a no-op for the existing path). Decoding is intentionally not provided — index probes always re-encode from the live datum. Bound: encoding length ≤ 25 bytes (1 sign + 4 exp + ≤19 digits + 1 term). Tests: TestEncodeNumericKeyZeroIsSingleByte, TestEncodeNumericKeyScaleInvariance (1 == 1.0 == 1.00 == 1.000 etc.), TestEncodeNumericKeySignOrder (every neg < zero < every pos), TestEncodeNumericKeyMonotone (sort 23-element representative slice numerically, assert encoded slice sorts identically under CompareKeys), TestEncodeNumericKeySamePrefixDifferentLengths (terminator behaviour for 1.9 vs 1.99 and the negative mirror), TestEncodeNumericKeyMinInt64 (int64-min edge case via uint64 absolute-value path). DDL acceptance, backfill, and HammerDB TPC-H validation are M0011-0002 / M0011-0003 follow-ups. |
| 0011-0002 | [B-tree NUMERIC build & UNIQUE/PRIMARY KEY (M0011)](0011-0002-btree-numeric-build-and-uniqueness.md) | accepted | Wires `EncodeNumericKey` (M0011-0001) into the live DDL, backfill, and index-scan paths so HammerDB TPC-H's `CREATE INDEX ... ON ... (col numeric)` no longer aborts with `btree v0 only supports int4 keys`. Four changes: (1) `BTPageOpaque.HighKey` becomes `[]byte` in memory and a 32-byte variable-length slot (`MaxHighKeyLen=32`, with a 2-byte length prefix at offset 14 replacing `_padding`) on disk; opaque area grows from 24 to 48 bytes; `btreeVersion` bumps from 2 to 3 (older btrees error with `ErrNotABTree` — pre-GA, no migration tooling). (2) Split path's `len(rightItems[0].key) != len(op.HighKey)` length-equality guard is replaced by a `MaxHighKeyLen` upper bound; the right page's first item just becomes the new HighKey verbatim. (3) `createSingleColumnBTreeIndex` accepts `numeric`/`decimal` via a new `isSupportedBTreeKeyType` predicate (still rejects everything else with `0A000`). `backfillSingleColumnBTree` switches the dedup map from `map[int32]struct{}` to `map[string]struct{}` keyed on the encoded byte form so `(10,1)` and `(100,2)` collapse into one UNIQUE entry. New shared helper `encodeBTreeKeyForColumn(v, col, pos)` branches on column type — int4 path is unchanged (range-check + `EncodeInt4`); numeric path runs `EncodeNumericKey(m, s)` on `KindNumeric`, with `KindInt` promoted to `(n, 0)`. (4) `indexScanOp.lookupKey` calls the same helper, so probe keys land on the same bytes backfill produced. Tests: TestDDLCreateNumericBTreeIndexAcceptsType (smoke: 3 rows, search round-trip), TestDDLNumericUniqueIndexCollapsesScales (1.0 / 1.00 → 23505 unique_violation), TestDDLNumericIndexSplitWithVariableLengthHighKey (600 rows with mantissa/scale variation force at least one leaf split with non-uniform key lengths — pre-M0011-0002 split-path errored), TestDDLInt4IndexUnchangedRegressionGuard (int4 path byte-for-byte unchanged). Full `go test ./...` green. NumericConst-driven IndexScan probes (e.g. `WHERE col = 1.5`) and HammerDB end-to-end TPC-H validation are M0011-0003. |
| 0011-0003 | [HammerDB TPC-H NUMERIC index validation (M0011)](0011-0003-hammerdb-tpch-numeric-index-validation.md) | accepted | End-to-end validation of the NUMERIC B-tree CREATE INDEX path against the eight-table HammerDB TPC-H schema. Two pieces: (1) Planner — `planIndexScanFromWhere` accepts `*NumericConst` (in addition to `*IntegerConst` / `*ParamRef`) on the rhs of `col = const`, so a query like `SELECT … FROM orders WHERE o_orderkey = 12345` (where `o_orderkey` is `NUMERIC` and the literal parses as `NumericConst`) actually emits an `IndexScan` rather than falling through to `SeqScan + Filter`. The executor side's `encodeBTreeKeyForColumn` already encodes via `EncodeNumericKey` for the numeric column path. (2) Validation test — `internal/executor/tpch_numeric_index_test.go` brings up the TPC-H schema via `tpch.DDL()` + `tpch.SampleInserts()` and runs the 13 single-column NUMERIC `CREATE INDEX` statements that mirror HammerDB's `pgolap.tcl` lines 511-544 (region_pk … idx_lineitem_orderkey_fkidx). All 13 land cleanly. A direct `btree.Search(EncodeNumericKey(k, 0))` round-trips on `orders_pk`, and a planner+executor pass on `SELECT o_orderkey FROM orders WHERE o_orderkey = 4` walks the plan tree to confirm an `IndexScan` was emitted (via the new `planContainsIndexScan` helper that drills past Project/Filter wrappers) and returns the expected single row. (3) Negative test — `TestTPCHCompositeIndexStillRejected` pins that the four HammerDB composite indexes (PARTSUPP_PK, LINEITEM_PK, LINEITEM_PART_SUPP_FKIDX) continue to surface `0A000 only single-column btree indexes are supported in v0` since multi-column composite is explicitly out of B-tree v0 scope per the milestone. When composite-index support lands, the assertion inverts. The slice closes M0011: `bench/tpch/run_all.sh` reaches the "CREATING TPCH INDEXES" stage without hitting the int4-only restriction; the run still stops at the first composite index, but that's separate scope. Full `go test ./...` green. |
| 0012-0001 | [Lock manager architecture (M0012)](0012-0001-lock-manager-architecture.md) | accepted | PostgreSQL-style SQL lock manager core surface — relation-level lock tags, mode-compatibility matrix, holder/waiter tracking, Acquire/Release/ReleaseAll API. New `internal/lockmgr` package. Eight lock modes (`AccessShareLock` … `AccessExclusiveLock`) with numeric values matching upstream's `lockdefs.h` so log lines and future SQL views share the vocabulary. `conflictTab[1..8]` is a verbatim port of `LockConflicts[]` from `postgres/src/backend/storage/lmgr/lock.c` (lines 65-104), encoded as a `[9]Mask` with slot 0 unused so 1-based indexing matches. `LockTag{DB, Rel}` is the v0 tag granularity (forks intentionally collapsed — relation lock covers heap and indexes, matching upstream). Per-tag `lockState` carries `holders map[BackendID]Mask`, `waiters []*Waiter`, and a cached `granted` mask; lazy-allocated on first Acquire and GC'd to nil when both holders and waiters drain. `Acquire(ctx, b, t, m)` grants immediately when no conflict against `grantedExcept(b)` AND no waiters queued (the second check enforces strict head-of-line FIFO so a strong waiter doesn't starve behind a stream of compatible-with-current-holders newcomers); otherwise enqueues a `Waiter{b, m, signal}` with a buffered chan and parks on `<-signal`. Cancellation splices the waiter out under `lm.mu` and handles the rare race where Release promoted us between context cancel and lock acquisition (releases the just-granted bit, runs another wake-pass). `Release` runs the wake-pass head-first and stops at the first non-promotable waiter (FIFO). `ReleaseAll(b)` drops every mode held by backend `b` across every tag — the executor's commit/rollback hook. `ConflictsWith(m, held)` is exported so the M0012-0002 deadlock detector can ask the same question against the lock table. Single coarse `lm.mu sync.Mutex` — per-tag striping deferred. Self-conflict is impossible: `grantedExcept(b)` excludes the requester's own holdings so a backend can stack modes (e.g. `RowExclusive` + `AccessShare` for `INSERT … SELECT`). Tests: TestLockConflictMatrixMatchesUpstream (exhaustive 8-mode cross-check vs upstream's bitmasks), TestLockManagerNoConflictGrantsImmediately, TestLockManagerCompatibleModesCoexist (two backends both AccessShare), TestLockManagerConflictBlocksAndWakesOnRelease (headline path), TestLockManagerSelfDoesNotConflict (RowExclusive + AccessExclusive same backend), TestLockManagerIdempotentAcquire (re-Acquire of held mode is a no-op), TestLockManagerWaiterCancellationCleansUp (no leaked queue entry, no leaked holder), TestLockManagerReleaseAllWakesEveryone (txn-end hook), TestLockManagerFIFOFairness (head waiter wakes before later waiters; later AccessShare correctly queues behind a waiting AccessExclusive), TestLockManagerGCEmptiesState. Race-clean (`go test -race`). Wait-for-graph deadlock detection (M0012-0002) and executor integration (M0012-0003) build on top of this surface without changes here. |
| 0012-0002 | [Deadlock detection algorithm (M0012)](0012-0002-deadlock-detection-algorithm.md) | accepted | Wait-for graph + cycle detection + victim selection on top of M0012-0001's lock manager. New `internal/lockmgr/deadlock.go` (`runDeadlockCheck`, `CheckDeadlocksNow`, `findCycle`, `cancelVictimLocked`). Wait-for graph derivation: for every parked Waiter `w` on tag `t`, emit edge `w.Backend → h` for every holder `h` of any conflicting mode (`ConflictsWith(w.Mode, holders[h])`); self-edges excluded. Cycle detection: iterative three-colour DFS (white/grey/black); a grey-on-grey edge is a back-edge → cycle reconstructed via parent pointers. Victim selection: youngest backend in the cycle (max `BackendID`) — matches upstream's "cancel youngest xact" heuristic. Cancellation contract: `Waiter` grew a second `victim chan struct{}`; the detector splices the victim out of its parked queue under `lm.mu`, sends on `victim`, and runs the wake-pass so backends queued behind the victim get a chance to advance. The victim's `Acquire` selects on `signal` / `victim` / `ctx.Done()` and on `victim` returns `ErrDeadlockDetected` (sentinel exported for higher layers to translate to SQLSTATE `40P01`). The Acquire goroutine then calls `lm.ReleaseAll(b)` outside the mutex so survivor backends' wake-passes fire. Scheduling: every parked Acquire arms a `time.AfterFunc(deadlockTimeout, lm.runDeadlockCheck)` (default 1s, matches upstream's `deadlock_timeout` GUC; tunable per-manager via `SetDeadlockTimeout`). Concurrent timer fires serialise on `lm.mu` and the detector is idempotent. Tests: TestDeadlockDetectsTwoSessionCycle (classic A↔B), TestDeadlockYoungestBackendIsVictim (BackendID 5 vs 9 → 9 cancelled), TestDeadlockDetectsThreeSessionCycle (A→B→C→A multi-edge cycle, exactly 1 cancellation), TestDeadlockNoCycleNoCancel (linear wait chain → false-positive guard), TestDeadlockTimerSchedulesCheck (20ms timeout, no synchronous trigger — pins the AfterFunc wiring), TestDeadlockSurvivorMakesProgress (post-cancellation survivor's blocked Acquire returns nil). All race-clean. Out of scope: `lock_timeout` (separate from deadlock_timeout), predicate-deadlock / SSI, soft-cancel retry, cross-database locks. Executor integration (M0012-0003) maps `ErrDeadlockDetected` to a `*ExecError{Code:"40P01"}` and routes it back to the client. |
| 0012-0003 | [Lock-wait integration & test matrix (M0012)](0012-0003-lock-wait-integration-and-test-matrix.md) | accepted | Closes M0012 by wiring the lockmgr core (M0012-0001) and detector (M0012-0002) into the executor. `executor.Context` grew `LockMgr *lockmgr.LockManager` and `BackendID lockmgr.BackendID` fields (both optional; nil-LockMgr makes the helper a no-op so existing tests keep working). New `Context.acquireRelLock(rel, mode)` funnel in `internal/executor/context.go` translates `lockmgr.ErrDeadlockDetected` → `*ExecError{Code: "40P01", Message: "deadlock detected"}` and `context.Canceled` / `DeadlineExceeded` → `*ExecError{Code: "57014"}` (matches upstream's query-cancel SQLSTATE). Wired into 5 operators: `seqScanOp.Open` and `indexScanOp.Open` take `AccessShareLock`; `insertOp.Open`, `updateOp.Open`, `deleteOp.Open` take `RowExclusiveLock`. `server.Config` grew `LockMgr` field; `server.Server` grew `nextBackendID atomic.Uint32` so each Query message gets a fresh monotonically-increasing backend ID (the M0012-0002 youngest-backend victim policy depends on this monotonic shape). `dispatch.go` plumbs both fields into the executor Context and calls `LockMgr.ReleaseAll(backendID)` from the deferred txn-end block (after Commit/Rollback) so a leftover holder can never outlive the connection. v0 locks are statement-scoped (txn-scoped only because each Query message currently maps to one txn); transaction-scoped semantics across multi-statement txns lands when the wire layer plumbs explicit BEGIN/COMMIT through. Tests in `internal/executor/lock_integration_test.go`: TestExecutorAcquireHelperNilLockMgr (regression guard for nil-LockMgr no-op contract that every existing storage_test fixture relies on), TestExecutorAcquireHelperGrantsLock, TestExecutorDeadlockTwoSession (A↔B cycle exercising the helper directly — pins the ErrDeadlockDetected → 40P01 mapping), TestExecutorDeadlockThreeSession (A→B→C→A multi-edge cycle, exactly one 40P01, victim is BackendID 3), TestExecutorNonDeadlockContention (linear waiter chain — no false-positive 40P01, both waiters resolve to nil after Release). All race-clean. Out of scope (deferred): DDL paths (DROP TABLE, ALTER TABLE) need AccessExclusiveLock but operator structure for DDL is different — separate slice; catalog-level locks (pg_class etc.); `lock_timeout` GUC; `pg_locks` SQL view. |
| 0013-0001 | [WAL buffers architecture (M0013)](0013-0001-wal-buffers-architecture.md) | accepted | Bounded in-memory WAL buffer between `state.append` and segment files. New `wal_buffers` GUC (TypeInt, BootVal 16 MiB, range `[0, 1 GiB]`, ContextPostmaster); 0 disables (legacy behaviour — every Append still flows through `writeAt` directly so existing tests/fixtures keep passing byte-for-byte). New `internal/wal/wal_buffer.go` defines a LSN-addressed ring (`base`/`head`/`tail` are absolute byte LSNs, not slice offsets) with byte-wise wraparound and a two-slice `readForDrain` so a wrapped drain produces no allocation. `state` grew `walBuf *walBuffer` plus `drainedLSN uint64` watermark (last byte already written to a segment file). Invariant: `flushedLSN ≤ drainedLSN ≤ writeLSN`; walBuf covers `(drainedLSN, writeLSN]`. `state.append` branches: (a) walBuf nil OR `len(record) > cap` → bypass to `writeAt` directly (advances drainedLSN to writeLSN — fragmenting an oversized record across drains complicates contiguous-stream semantics for no benefit); (b) buffered append where `need = len(record) - free()` triggers `drainBufferBytes(need)` if positive, then copies the record into walBuf. `flushUpTo` adds Stage 1 `drainBufferUpTo(targetLSN)` ahead of the existing Stage 2 dataSync — every byte ≤ targetLSN lands in segment files (and on `s.dirty`) before the durability barrier runs. `state.writeAt`'s existing dirty-flag bookkeeping covers drained segments automatically — the "sync debt" the milestone calls out is just the natural state.dirty entries. Walsender's M0010-0002 `MemRing` keeps mirroring every Append regardless of buffered/bypass path so streaming-from-RAM is unchanged. Plumbed cmd/goopg start → `OpenOptions.WALBuffers` → `wal.Config.WALBuffers` → `state.walBuf`. Tests: TestWALBufferDisabledByDefault (legacy 0-cap regression guard), TestWALBufferRetainsRecordsInRAM (5 small Appends with 64 KiB cap → segment file size unchanged), TestWALBufferOverflowDrainsToSegments (256-byte cap + 8×50-byte payloads → segment file grows), TestFlushUpToDrainsBufferThenSyncs (Stage 1 + Stage 2 + ReadAll round-trip), TestWALBufferRecordLargerThanBufferBypasses (256-byte record vs 64-byte cap), TestWALBufferReadAllRoundTrip table-test across three caps (0, 64 KiB, 256 B forced drains) — every configuration round-trips byte-identical. Full `go test ./...` green. Eviction-driven drain verification (M0013-0002) and counters/observability (M0013-0003) are follow-up slices. |
| 0013-0002 | [Overflow + eviction durability ordering (M0013)](0013-0002-overflow-and-eviction-durability-ordering.md) | accepted | Pins the WAL-before-data invariant under buffered WAL for both eviction-driven and checkpoint-driven flush paths. The M0013-0001 commit gave `wal.Writer.FlushUpTo` two-stage semantics (Stage 1 drainBufferUpTo + Stage 2 dataSync); the existing `Pool.flushSlot` (single-page eviction, line 886) and `Pool.flushDirtyBatch` (batched flush, line 836) both already call `wal.FlushUpTo(pageLSN)` before `mgr.WriteBlock`, so the invariant is automatically preserved by construction. This slice is **pure additive testing** — no production code changes — that pins the invariant against future regressions. New `internal/storage/wal_buffer_eviction_test.go` (external `package storage_test` so it can import `wal` without cycle). Two tests: TestEvictionDrainsBufferedWALBeforeWritingPage wires a real `*wal.Writer` with `WALBuffers=64 KiB` into a one-slot Pool, Appends a small record (segment file 0 bytes — record in walBuf), mutates a heap page with `MarkDirtyWithLSN(endLSN)`, forces eviction via a second `PinNew`, then asserts both (a) the heap byte persisted on disk AND (b) the WAL segment file is now non-zero AND (c) `wal.ReadAll` returns the appended payload. The dual assertion is the regression hook: if anyone disables Stage 1 (drops `drainBufferUpTo` from `flushUpTo`), the heap byte still persists but segment bytes stay 0 — recovery would lose the record while the data mutation survived. TestFlushAllPacedDrainsBufferedWAL exercises the same invariant for the checkpoint path (`Pool.FlushAllPaced` → `flushDirtyBatch` → `FlushUpTo(maxLSN)`): three pages with three buffered LSNs, after FlushAllPaced every payload is durable in the WAL and every heap byte is on disk. Failure-mode catalogue in the design doc maps each potential bug (Stage 1 skipped / Stage 2 skipped / wrong call ordering / stale maxLSN) to the test that catches it. Counters and observability surface for forced-drain events land in M0013-0003. Full `go test ./...` green. |
| 0013-0003 | [WAL buffers observability + rollout (M0013)](0013-0003-wal-buffers-observability-and-rollout.md) | accepted | Closes M0013. Adds the SQL-queryable surface and startup log line operators need to verify the M0013-0001 buffer is doing what it should and to triage anomalies. New `walBufferCounters` struct (mirrors the directIOCounters pattern — single allocation owned by Writer, shared with state via pointer) holds two `atomic.Uint64` counters: `overflowDrainBytes` (lifetime bytes drained because Append would overflow the buffer — high values mean undersized `wal_buffers`) and `flushDrainBytes` (lifetime bytes drained by FlushUpTo's Stage 1 — typical and healthy, the buffer's natural durability cost). New `drainReason` enum classifies each `drainBufferBytes` call at the call site so attribution is explicit. Four new Writer accessors: `WALBuffersCapacity()` (static GUC value), `WALBuffersBytesResident()` (live byte count via the new `opWALBufStat` writer-loop op so reads serialise against append/drain), `WALBuffersOverflowDrainBytes()`, `WALBuffersFlushDrainBytes()`. The `pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_io` view in `internal/initdb/wal_io_views.go` extends with four columns at the end (`wal_buffers_capacity_bytes`, `wal_buffers_bytes_resident`, `wal_buffers_overflow_drain_bytes`, `wal_buffers_flush_drain_bytes`) — keeps the operator's "one place to look" experience rather than spawning a sibling view. Startup log line in `cmd/goopg start`: `event=wal_buffers_attached capacity_bytes=N` when `wal_buffers > 0`, suppressed otherwise (mirrors the existing `event=wal_sender_memory_buffer_attached` shape). Test: TestWALBufferCountersTrackDrains in `internal/wal/wal_buffer_test.go` — drives 8×50-byte payloads against a 256-byte cap to bump OverflowDrainBytes (no FlushDrainBytes movement), then a small append + FlushUpTo to bump FlushDrainBytes; pins each counter only moves on its trigger. The existing `pg_stat_wal_io` view tests (TestStatWALIOEmptyWithoutWriter / TestStatWALIORendersWriterCounters / TestStatWALIOActiveWhenProbeOk) all keep passing — they assert by column name not position so the four-column extension is transparent. Full `go test ./...` green. M0013 closes — every DoD item maps to a landed slice (DoD #1/2/3/5 → M0013-0001; #4 → existing state.dirty bookkeeping; #6/7/8 → M0013-0002 tests; M0013-0003 covers the observability piece of #8). |
| 0014-0001 | [XLOG page and segment layout compat (M0014, steps 1–2)](0014-0001-xlog-page-and-segment-layout-compat.md) | accepted | Step 2 (landed 2026-04-29) wires the page-header helpers from step 1 into the writer's append path and the readers (RecordIterator + ReadAll), gated by the new `Config.PageHeaders` flag (default `false`). When the flag is on: `state.append` calls `emitWithPageHeaders(record, writePos, segSize, sysID, tli)` which interleaves a 40-byte `XLogLongPageHeader` at every segment boundary (stamps `xlp_sysid`/`xlp_seg_size`/`xlp_xlog_blcksz` from the new `Config.SystemID`/`SegmentSize`/`XLOGBlockSize`) and a 24-byte short header at every other 8 KiB page boundary. Records crossing a page boundary stamp `XLP_FIRST_IS_CONTRECORD` and `xlp_rem_len = bytes_remaining` on the next page; multi-page records re-decrement `rem_len` page-by-page. `state.writePos` and `Writer.WrittenLSN()` advance over the combined stream length so the upstream invariant **LSN = byte offset in the on-disk WAL stream** is preserved; `Append` returns `startLSN = writePos + leading_header_bytes + 1` so the LSN lands on the first record byte. Reader side: `RecordIterator.Next` skips any header at the cursor before checking write-tail; `readRecordBytesAt` is the new helper that mirrors the writer's interleave and returns both the record bytes and the physical advance count (record bytes + skipped header bytes). `ReadAll` auto-detects format via `DetectWALFormat(walDir)` (silent fallback to legacy on classification errors so tiny test segments still work) and dispatches to `readAllPageAware` for `WALFormatPGCompat`. `scanLastSegmentEnd` (the writer's startup detect-write-position helper) consults `cfg.PageHeaders` directly; the EOS sentinel becomes two-flavoured — all-zero page header at a page boundary OR all-zero record header mid-page (preserves the M0007 / 0007-0001 preallocated-tail contract). MemRing capture, walBuf path, and writeAt layering all consume `stream` instead of the bare record so direct-I/O alignment, AIO submission, and walsender RAM streaming see the same physical bytes the segment carries. New tests in `xlog_emit_test.go`: TestPageEmissionLongHeaderAtSegmentStart (TLI/SysID round-trip + XLPLongHeader bit), TestPageEmissionShortHeaderAtPageBoundary (XLPFirstIsContRecord + exact `xlp_rem_len = record_size - (XLOGBlockSize - SizeOfXLogLongPHD)` arithmetic), TestPageEmissionRecordCrossesPage (multi-record stream, ReadAll round-trip + Append/ReadAll LSN cross-check), TestPageEmissionRecordCrossesSegment (segment-spanning record → long header on next segment with both XLPLongHeader and XLPFirstIsContRecord set), TestPageEmissionIteratorRoundTrip (streaming RecordIterator), TestPageEmissionRecoversCleanly (close + reopen with Preallocate=true → WrittenLSN continues + ReadAll sees every record), TestPageEmissionLegacyDefaultUnchanged (rollout invariant: `PageHeaders=false` produces byte-identical output to omitting the field). The XLogRecord-header switchover (M0014-0002 step 2) and pg_waldump validation (M0014-0003) remain deferred — `Config.PageHeaders=true` today produces upstream-shaped pages with goopg's legacy 8-byte length+CRC32-IEEE record frames inside; pg_waldump won't accept those until the record-frame switchover lands. Step 1 history (the helper foundation): New `internal/wal/xlog_page.go` defines upstream-compatible types targeting PG18: `XLogPageHeader` (24 bytes, mirrors upstream's `XLogPageHeaderData` field-by-field — Magic/Info/TLI/PageAddr/RemLen with 4-byte MAXALIGN tail), `XLogLongPageHeader` (40 bytes, embeds the short header plus sysid/seg_size/xlog_blcksz cross-check). Constants `XLOGBlockSize=8192`, `XLOGPageMagic=0xD119` (PG18), `SizeOfXLogShortPHD=24`, `SizeOfXLogLongPHD=40`, and the five `XLP_*` flag bits including `XLPAllFlags` for validity checking. `EncodeXLogPageHeader` writes little-endian (host byte order on x86_64/aarch64 — matches upstream's de-facto LE assumption; cross-arch transfer is out of M0014 scope) and rejects undefined `xlp_info` bits so a future flag addition that forgets to update `XLPAllFlags` surfaces immediately. `DecodeXLogPageHeader` returns the typed sentinel `ErrInvalidPageHeader` (wrapped via `%w`) on magic mismatch so the M0014-0004 legacy-format detector has a clean branch. `EncodeXLogLongPageHeader` auto-sets `XLPLongHeader` so callers don't have to remember; `DecodeXLogLongPageHeader` enforces it. `XLogFileName(tli, segno, segSize)` produces upstream's `<TLI:%08X><Log:%08X><Seg:%08X>` form (24 hex chars total — same width as the legacy format but with a different semantic split). `ParseXLogFileName(name, segSize)` is the strict inverse using `strconv.ParseUint(base=16)` per 8-char field so partial-parse garbage like `0000000100000000000000FG` returns ok=false (Go's `fmt.Sscanf %X` is permissive — strict parse is required for the writer's segment-listing loop to skip unrecognised names cleanly). Coexists with the legacy `formatSegmentName` / `parseSegmentName` so the writer/reader switchover in M0014-0001 step 2 lands atomically. Tests: TestXLogFileNameRoundTrip (5 representative TLI/segno cases including log-boundary 255→256), TestParseXLogFileNameRejectsGarbage, TestXLogPageHeaderRoundTrip, TestXLogLongPageHeaderRoundTrip, TestDecodeXLogPageHeaderRejectsBadMagic (typed sentinel via `errors.Is`), TestEncodeXLogPageHeaderRejectsUndefinedFlags (XLPAllFlags contract), TestDecodeXLogLongPageHeaderRequiresLongBit. Full `go test ./...` green. The buffer (M0013-0001) is byte-addressed and unaffected by per-page header insertion. |
