| 0014-0002 | [XLogRecord header + rmgr mapping (M0014, step 1)](0014-0002-xlogrecord-header-and-rmgr-mapping.md) | accepted | Foundation slice for upstream-compatible WAL record framing. Pure additive, mirroring the M0014-0001 step 1 pattern — types and helpers only, with the actual writer/reader switchover (jointly with M0014-0001 step 2) deferred to a later loop so a half-migrated segment file is impossible. New `internal/wal/xlog_record.go` defines `XLogRecord` (24 bytes; mirrors upstream's `xl_tot_len/xl_xid/xl_prev/xl_info/xl_rmid/_pad/xl_crc` layout — uint32 TotLen + uint32 XID + uint64 Prev + uint8 Info + uint8 Rmid + 2 zero pad + uint32 CRC, all little-endian), `Rmgr` enum (RmgrXLog=0, RmgrXact=1, RmgrStorage=2, RmgrHeap2=9, RmgrHeap=10, RmgrBtree=11 — values match upstream's `RM_*_ID` so `pg_waldump --rmgr=Heap` filters work against goopg WAL out of the box), `MaxKnownRmgr` boundary for "WAL emitted by a newer producer" detection, and the flag-bit constants `XLRInfoMask`/`XLRRmgrInfoMask`/`XLRSpecialRelUpdate`/`XLRCheckConsistency`. `XLogCRC32C(data)` uses Go's `crc32.MakeTable(crc32.Castagnoli)` (singleton — Go memoises the canonical polynomials) for upstream's CRC32C. `EncodeXLogRecordHeader` validates `h.TotLen == SizeOfXLogRecord + len(payload)` (catches producers that forget to set TotLen or set it to just the payload length), rejects undefined framework bits in the low 4 bits of Info, zeros the 2-byte padding (upstream invariant — non-zero is a corruption signal), and computes the CRC over (header bytes with xl_crc field zeroed) \|\| payload before writing the stored CRC back to offsets 20..23. `DecodeXLogRecordHeader` returns the typed sentinel `ErrInvalidRecordHeader` (wrapped via `%w`) on (a) non-zero padding bytes, (b) `Rmid > MaxKnownRmgr`, (c) undefined framework bits — gives the M0014-0004 legacy-format detector clean branches. Separate `VerifyXLogRecordCRC(headerBytes, payload, wantCRC)` for recovery-time checking — uses an internal scratch copy to zero the CRC field so callers don't have to mutate the on-disk bytes. Tests: TestXLogCRC32CIsCastagnoli (the canonical `"123456789"` → `0xE3069283` vector — pins the polynomial; any drift to `crc32.IEEE` breaks pg_waldump compat), TestXLogRecordHeaderRoundTrip, TestVerifyXLogRecordCRCDetectsTamper (1-bit payload flip → ErrCorruptRecord), TestEncodeRejectsTotLenMismatch, TestEncodeRejectsUndefinedFrameworkBits (bit 0x04 — reserved), TestDecodeRejectsNonZeroPadding, TestDecodeRejectsUnknownRmgr (rmid=99), TestDecodeTruncatedSrc, TestEncodeCRCMatchesDirectComputation (encoder's stored CRC equals an independent `crc32.Update` walk over the same bytes). Goopg→upstream record-kind mapping table (HeapInsert→RmgrHeap+XLOG_HEAP_INSERT, BtreeInsert→RmgrBtree+XLOG_BTREE_INSERT_LEAF, etc.) is in the design doc; the per-record-kind constants land alongside the producer changes during the joint switchover. Full `go test ./...` green. |
| 0014-0003 | [Recovery, streaming, and compat validation (M0014)](0014-0003-recovery-streaming-and-compat-validation.md) | accepted | Lands the writer/reader switchover: in `Config.PageHeaders=true` mode, WAL records now use `XLogRecord` headers (CRC32C + xl_prev chaining) and wrap goopg payload bytes in valid PostgreSQL main-data chunks (`XLR_BLOCK_ID_DATA_SHORT`/`_LONG`) so upstream readers can parse them. Legacy mode (`PageHeaders=false`) remains byte-identical. `state.append` now encodes XLog records and tracks `prevRecPtr`; startup scan (`detectWritePos`/`scanLastSegmentEnd`) now returns both write-end and last-record start so xl_prev survives restart. Decode paths (`ReadAll` page-aware mode, `RecordIterator` page-header mode, startup scan) now validate/decode XLogRecord frames and unwrap the main-data chunk back into existing goopg payloads, so replay/logical classifier code stays unchanged. Added tests: `format_xlog_test.go` (encode/decode + malformed chunk guards), `TestPageEmissionXLogPrevChain`, `TestReplayFromDirEndToEndPageHeaders`, and `TestPGWaldumpParsesEmittedWAL` (executes upstream `pg_waldump -q -e` against emitted WAL; skips only when binary is unavailable). Finalisation (2026-04-30): three on-wire bugs uncovered by the pg_waldump gate are now fixed — `XLOGPageMagic` corrected `0xD119 → 0xD118` (PG18.3 value from `postgres/src/include/access/xlog_internal.h`), xl_crc CRC32C order flipped to upstream's `(payload \|\| header[0..19])` (CRC32C is order-dependent; matches `XLogInsertRecord` in `postgres/src/backend/access/transam/xlog.c` line ~5170), and MAXALIGN(8) record-end padding added (encoder appends zero pad bytes after each record so pg_waldump's `nextRecord = MAXALIGN(xl_tot_len)` advance lands on the next header; xl_tot_len is unchanged — pad is not counted, matching upstream; xlp_rem_len now uses `realRecLen` so cross-page records don't claim the pad as content; reader/iterator/scanner advance by `MAXALIGN(xl_tot_len)`). xl_prev now stores 0-based RecPtr (`start − 1`) since goopg's internal `start` LSN is 1-based but pg_waldump validates prev-link in upstream 0-based coordinates. |
| 0014-0004 | [Rollout guardrails: legacy-format detection (M0014, steps 1-2)](0014-0004-rollout-guardrails-and-operator-playbook.md) | accepted | Step 2 (landed 2026-04-30): `loadState` now calls `DetectWALFormat(cfg.WALDir)` immediately before `detectWritePos` and surfaces the new typed sentinel `ErrWALFormatMismatch` when the existing on-disk format conflicts with `cfg.PageHeaders` — `(detected=Legacy ∧ PageHeaders=true)` or `(detected=PGCompat ∧ PageHeaders=false)`. Unknown / unclassifiable detection results are treated as no-signal so empty data dirs, fresh dirs, dirs containing only non-segment files (e.g. a `.gitignore`), and tiny test segments (< 40 B where the detector can't read its quorum) all proceed cleanly; the writer's existing `detectWritePos` record-level scan catches deeper format incompatibilities. New static accessor `Writer.Format() WALFormatVersion` satisfies M0014 DoD #9 "WAL format mode/version exposed at runtime" at the Go API level — returns `WALFormatPGCompat` when `cfg.PageHeaders=true`, else `WALFormatLegacy`. Tests in `format_mismatch_test.go`: TestNewWriterRejectsLegacyDirInPGCompatMode, TestNewWriterRejectsPGCompatDirInLegacyMode, TestNewWriterAcceptsFreshDirInBothModes, TestNewWriterAcceptsMatchingFormat (legacy_legacy + pgcompat_pgcompat reopen guards), TestWriterFormatExposesActiveMode, TestNewWriterIgnoresNonSegmentFiles. SQL-level surface (landed alongside step 2 finalisation, 2026-04-30): `pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_io` grew a trailing `format_version` column whose value is `w.Format().String()` (so `legacy` or `pgcompat`); trailing position keeps existing column indexes stable, satisfying the SQL-level half of M0014 DoD #9. Test: TestStatWALIOFormatVersionColumn (legacy + pgcompat sub-tests). Step 1 history (the helper foundation, landed 2026-04-29): pure additive read-only utility that complements the M0014-0001 step 1 / M0014-0002 step 1 typed sentinels: enables a future writer-startup fail-fast to distinguish "data dir has legacy goopg WAL" from "data dir has PG-compat WAL" without committing to a switchover policy yet. New `internal/wal/format_detect.go` defines `WALFormatVersion` (`Unknown` / `Legacy` / `PGCompat`, with `String()` for log lines) and `DetectWALFormat(walDir) (WALFormatVersion, error)`. Detection rule: enumerate `walDir`, find the lowest-numbered file whose name parses as either a legacy 24-hex-char segment (`parseSegmentName`) or a pg-compat XLog filename (`ParseXLogFileName`) — both parsers return ok=false for non-WAL files like `.gitignore` so they're skipped. Read the first 40 bytes; try `DecodeXLogLongPageHeader` then `DecodeXLogPageHeader`. A successful decode (magic matches `XLOGPageMagic=0xD119`) → PGCompat. `errors.Is(err, ErrInvalidPageHeader)` (magic mismatch) → Legacy (legacy 8-byte length+CRC32-IEEE frames can't collide with `0xD119` at offsets 0..1 because uint32 length values up to ~16 MB never set the high bytes that produce that magic value). Empty / nonexistent dirs → `(WALFormatUnknown, nil)` so fresh-data-dir bootstrap doesn't have to special-case. Truncation under 24 bytes → typed error. CRC validation intentionally skipped — too expensive at startup, and the writer's existing per-record validation catches real corruption later; the detector's job is classification, not verification. Tests: TestDetectWALFormatNonexistentDir, TestDetectWALFormatEmptyDir, TestDetectWALFormatIgnoresGarbageFiles (`.gitignore` + `README.md` + `junk.txt` → Unknown), TestDetectWALFormatLegacy (synthesised legacy frame at offset 0 → Legacy classification via the `errors.Is(_, ErrInvalidPageHeader)` branch), TestDetectWALFormatPGCompat (`EncodeXLogLongPageHeader` → file → DetectWALFormat round-trip — pins the cross-helper boundary), TestDetectWALFormatTruncatedSegment (3-byte file → typed error), TestDetectWALFormatVersionString. Caller wiring (loadState fail-fast on mismatch) deferred to M0014-0004 step 2 alongside the joint M0014-0001/0002 writer/reader switchover — those have to land together so a half-migrated segment file is impossible. The runtime-observability piece of the M0014 DoD #9 ("WAL format mode/version exposed at runtime") extends `pg_stat_wal_io` once the writer integration provides a meaningful value to surface. Full `go test ./...` green. |
| 0015-0001 | [CREATE FUNCTION parser surface and AST (M0015 Stage A, step 1)](0015-0001-create-function-parser-and-ast.md) | accepted (parser/AST + lexer dollar-quote + analyzer reject) | Parser-only slice for `CREATE [OR REPLACE] FUNCTION ... LANGUAGE plpgsql AS $$body$$` and `DROP FUNCTION [IF EXISTS] name [(args)] [CASCADE\|RESTRICT]`. Three new keywords (`KwFunction`, `KwReturns`, `KwLanguage`); procedure-side keywords (`KwProcedure`, `KwCall`, `KwOut`, `KwInout`, `KwVariadic`, `KwDeclare`) stay deferred to Stage B. Lexer side: extends the existing `$` case (which previously only handled positional parameters `$1`..`$N`) to detect `$$body$$` / `$tag$body$tag$` dollar-quoted strings and emit them as `TokenStringLit` with the body verbatim. On no-match the position is rewound so `$1` positional parameters and `$0`-suffixed identifiers (TPC-H Q15-style `revenue$0`) keep their pre-M0015 behaviour. Parser side: new `internal/parser/function.go` with `parseCreateFunctionTail` / `parseDropFunctionTail` / `parseFunctionArgList` / `parseFunctionArg` / `parseLanguageName` / `parseFunctionBody`. `parseCreate` / `parseDrop` (in `ddl.go`) gain `KwFunction` dispatch. `LANGUAGE` and `AS` may appear in either order (matches upstream); function body must be dollar-quoted (single-quoted bodies surface a specific diagnostic so a future relaxation is intentional, not accidental); `OUT` / `INOUT` / `VARIADIC` argument modes surface a Stage-A-only error (`"function argument mode %q is not supported in v0 (Stage A: IN only)"`) instead of a generic syntax error. Tests in `function_test.go` (12 cases): TestParseCreateFunctionMinimal, TestParseCreateFunctionOrReplace, TestParseCreateFunctionWithArgs (named + anonymous positional + Mode invariant), TestParseCreateFunctionExplicitIN (no-op `IN` keyword), TestParseCreateFunctionRejectsOutInoutVariadic (3 sub-cases), TestParseCreateFunctionLanguageAsAnyOrder, TestParseCreateFunctionTaggedDollarQuote (`$body$ ... $$inner$$ ... $body$` — tag prevents inner `$$` from closing), TestParseCreateFunctionMissingBody, TestParseDropFunctionMinimal, TestParseDropFunctionIfExistsAndArgs, TestDollarQuoteEmptyTag, TestDollarQuoteUnterminated, TestPositionalParameterStillParses (regression guard). Analyzer: TestAnalyzeCreateFunctionRejected, TestAnalyzeDropFunctionRejected. | 0015-0002 | [pg_proc catalog and routine registry (M0015 Stage A, step 2)](0015-0002-pg-proc-catalog-and-routine-registry.md) | accepted (registry + virtual view; analyzer/executor wiring deferred to step 3) | Catalog-only slice that gives the upcoming analyzer/executor wiring a place to hold routine metadata. Signature key: lower-cased `(arg1_type,arg2_type,...)`; OID space starts at `FirstRoutineOID = 1<<17` so routine OIDs never collide with the table-OID space. New `internal/initdb/pg_proc_view.go` registers `pg_catalog.pg_proc` as a virtual view backed by `cat.Routines().List()`. Columns mirror upstream's `\df` shape: oid / proname / pronamespace / prolang / prorettype / proargtypes (comma-joined type names) / prosrc. Wired into `Open` immediately after `pg_stat_wal_io` so a fresh cluster's `\df` shows zero rows instead of a missing-table error. | 0015-0004 | [PL/pgSQL body parser and AST (M0015 Stage A, step 4a)](0015-0004-plpgsql-body-parser-and-ast.md) | accepted (Block + RETURN; DECLARE/assignment/IF/loops deferred) | New `internal/plpgsql` package parses routine-body bytes (the source captured between dollar-quote delimiters in step 1) into structured AST nodes the future interpreter (step 5) walks. Adds `KwReturn` (BEGIN / END already declared for transaction control). New public API `parser.ParseExpr(input string) (Expr, error)` parses a single SQL expression and rejects trailing tokens — used by `parseReturn` to feed the RETURN-value bytes back through the SQL expression parser so the AST shape matches a SELECT target list (lets the existing analyzer/planner/executor stay reusable). AST: `Block` (Statements []Stmt) and `ReturnStmt` (Expr parser.Expr); the `Stmt` interface uses an unexported `plpgsqlStmtNode()` marker mirroring the SQL parser's `stmtNode()` so future interpreter type-switches stay exhaustive. Grammar this slice: `body ::= 'BEGIN' stmt_list 'END' [';']`, `stmt ::= 'RETURN' sql_expr ';'`. Trailing semicolon after END is optional (upstream-flex). Error model: typed `plpgsql.SyntaxError{Pos, Message}`; lexer errors wrap into the same envelope so callers don't need to type-switch on `*parser.LexError` separately. Specific Stage-A diagnostics: missing BEGIN, missing END, unsupported statement (clearly says "Stage A 4a accepts RETURN only"), bare RETURN without value, bad expression inside RETURN (pinned at expression start), trailing tokens after END. New `KwRecursive` keyword. Stage A restriction enforced at parse time: a CTE body that opens with `KwInsert/KwUpdate/KwDelete` surfaces a clean `"data-modifying CTE bodies are not supported in v0 (Stage A only allows SELECT)"` error pinned at the inner-statement keyword position. The `WITH RECURSIVE` keyword is accepted at the parser level even though execution support lands in 0016-0003 — clean syntax-level errors are the same regardless of execution support, and the planner-level rejection for unsupported recursive shapes is a separate slice. | 0016-0002 | [Non-recursive CTE planner + executor (M0016)](0016-0002-nonrecursive-cte-planner-executor.md) | accepted | Stage A non-recursive CTE planning + execution via inline substitution. Each FROM-clause reference to a CTE name returns a freshly-cloned plan of that CTE's body — multi-consumer correctness without a Materialize node (the materialise-once optimisation lands in M0016-0004). New `internal/planner/with.go` defines the `plannedCTE` record (name + planned `Node` + `Schema` + synthetic `*catalog.Table`) and a package-local `planCTEs map` mirroring the existing `planParent` save/restore pattern. `preplanWithClause(with, cat)` walks each CTE left-to-right with prior siblings visible to later ones via the in-progress map; returns a `func()` restorer the caller defers. **Critical implementation detail**: CTE body planning calls `planSelect` directly, bypassing `Plan()`'s analyzer pass — the outer `Plan()` already analyzed the whole tree under the analyzer's CTE scope (`analyzer.scope.ctes`), and a second analyzer pass via the recursive `Plan()` entry would re-validate the inner SELECT WITHOUT the parent CTE scope and erroneously reject sibling references like `WITH a AS (SELECT 1), b AS (SELECT * FROM a) ...`. Column-alias list arity is double-checked here too (42P10) so the planner can stand alone if some path bypasses the analyzer. RECURSIVE is also rejected at the planner level (0A000) as second-line defence. `planScanRangeVar` gained a CTE-substitution path before catalog lookup, gated on `rv.Schema == ""` so `pg_catalog.foo` always hits the catalog. `planSelect` / `planInsert` / `planUpdate` / `planDelete` each gained the `preplanWithClause` + defer-restore stanza. Tests: 8 planner-side (TestPlanWithSimpleCTE, TestPlanWithCTEReadingTable verifying type flow-through, TestPlanWithCTEMultipleConsumers exercising the cross-product / inlining contract, TestPlanWithCTEReferencingPriorSibling pinning left-to-right scope, TestPlanWithRecursiveRejected, TestPlanWithCTEShadowsBaseRelation, TestPlanWithColumnAliasArityMismatch, TestPlanWithoutCTEUnchanged regression guard); 2 executor end-to-end (TestExecuteWithSimpleCTE returning the expected one-row int=1, TestExecuteWithCTEMultipleConsumers confirming 1×1 cross-product). The executor needs **zero new CTE infrastructure** — Stage A inlining produces a vanilla plan tree the existing operators run unchanged, which is the headline correctness argument: any plan-tree refactor that keeps the existing Output() / Open() / Next() / Close() contracts continues to work for CTEs. Full `go test ./...` green. Materialise-once optimisation, EXPLAIN producer labels, and runtime CTE counters land in M0016-0004. |

New `WindowDef` (PartitionBy []Expr + OrderBy []SortBy reusing the existing SortBy shape so executor ordering logic doesn't need new sort-key plumbing). New keywords: `KwOver` / `KwPartition` (KwOrder/KwBy already exist). New `parseWindowDef` consumes the OVER body; new `maybeWindowTail` is called by `parseFuncCallTail` after `)` and returns FuncCall unchanged when the next token isn't OVER (preserves byte-for-byte invariance for non-windowed callers). Frame clauses (ROWS / RANGE / GROUPS / frame-exclusion) parse but error explicitly so users see "frame clauses are not supported in v0" instead of a generic syntax error — Stage B promotes them. Named-window references (`OVER win_name`) + WINDOW definition clauses also deferred. Analyzer gate: `analyzeExpr`'s `*parser.FuncCall` arm rejects `x.Over != nil` with SQLSTATE 0A000 — two-step gate pattern from M0017-0002→M0017-0003: parser accepts the surface so diagnostics surface specific feature names, analyzer refuses to silently degrade to non-windowed evaluation. Tests: 7 parser scenarios in window_test.go (bare OVER, PARTITION BY, ORDER BY DESC, both clauses, count(\*) OVER (), frame-clause reject, rollout guardrail) + 1 analyzer test (TestAnalyzeWindowFunctionRejected pins 0A000). Full `go test ./...` green. Analyzer name resolution + planner WindowAgg node + executor per-partition streaming + LAG/LEAD argument shapes + frame clauses + named windows all stay deferred for M0020-0002/0003/0004. |
| 0020-0002 | [Window function analyzer + planner (M0020, Stage A)](0020-0002-window-analyzer-and-planner.md) | accepted | Promotes window functions from parser-only acceptance to analyzer/planner semantics for Stage A. Analyzer allows `row_number()` and `rank()` with `OVER (...)` and validates argument shape (no args, no `*`, no `DISTINCT`) plus recursive analysis of partition/order expressions. Clause guardrails reject window calls in WHERE/GROUP BY/HAVING with 0A000 while allowing target-list and ORDER BY usage. Planner adds an explicit `WindowAgg` stage between aggregate/having and final ORDER BY/LIMIT/Project, carrying resolved PARTITION/ORDER keys plus supported window function descriptors; target and ORDER BY expressions are rewritten so window calls become `ColumnRef`s to appended WindowAgg outputs. Stage-A simplification: all window calls in a SELECT must share the same `OVER (...)` spec, otherwise planner returns 0A000. Executor semantics and EXPLAIN rendering are deferred to 0020-0003/0004. |
| 0020-0003 | [Window executor (M0020, Stage A)](0020-0003-window-executor.md) | accepted | Implements runtime WindowAgg execution for `row_number()` and `rank()` in Stage A. New `windowOp` drains child rows, stable-sorts by PARTITION keys then ORDER keys, appends output slots for window functions, and evaluates per-row window values. `row_number()` resets per partition and increments by row order. `rank()` uses peer-group semantics over ORDER BY expressions (same peers share rank; next non-peer rank jumps to row position). Null handling follows existing sort semantics and peer detection treats NULL/NULL as peers. Scope-deferred: lag/lead, frame clauses, multiple window specs, streaming/memory-bounded execution. |
| 0020-0004 | [Window EXPLAIN + regression coverage (M0020, Stage A)](0020-0004-window-explain-and-tests.md) | accepted | Makes WindowAgg visible in EXPLAIN and pins Stage-A behavior with regression tests. EXPLAIN renderer now labels `WindowAgg (N funcs)` and traverses its child in both TEXT/JSON output paths. Added EXPLAIN tests for WindowAgg presence plus compatibility tests covering row_number partition/order behavior, rank peer groups, and NULL-bearing order keys. Focused verification gates run across analyzer/planner/executor packages. |
| 0020-0005 | [Window Stage B: LAG/LEAD semantics and testing (M0020)](0020-0005-lag-lead-semantics-and-testing.md) | accepted | Implements `lag(value [, offset [, default]])` and `lead()` window functions. Analyzer allows 1–3 args and derives return type from first arg. Planner resolves args via `resolveExprForWindowInput`; new `inferExprType` helper infers catalog type from resolved Exprs. Executor `evalWindowFuncs` refactored to a two-phase partition-discovery + per-partition evaluation loop; lag/lead index `o.rows` by partition-local offset and return default/NULL at partition boundaries. Six new executor tests cover basic lag/lead, explicit offset, explicit default, and partition-boundary isolation. |
| 0021-0011 | [Tuple-level locking step 4: multi-holder FOR SHARE via lockmgr modes (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0011-tuple-locking-for-share-multi-holder.md) | accepted (multi-holder FOR SHARE without MultiXact infrastructure; persistent xmax bookkeeping deferred) | Fixes the hidden bug where two concurrent SELECT FOR SHARE sessions on the same row serialised because lockRowsOp.stampLock acquired ExclusiveLock regardless of strength. Achieves upstream's "multiple FOR SHARE holders coexist" semantics WITHOUT introducing MultiXact infrastructure: the lockmgr's existing conflict matrix already supplies what we need — RowShareLock is compatible with itself and conflicts with ExclusiveLock. New `lockRowsOp.tupleLockMode()` picks RowShareLock for HeapXmaxShrLock / HeapXmaxKeyShrLock strengths; ExclusiveLock for HeapXmaxExclLock. Mapping table: FOR UPDATE → ExclusiveLock (single writer), FOR SHARE → RowShareLock (compatible with self), FOR KEY SHARE → RowShareLock, UPDATE/DELETE foreign-lock detection → ExclusiveLock (waits for all holders). Transaction-scoped ReleaseAll(backendID) via the existing dispatch.go hook cleans every holder up at commit/abort. On-page xmax bookkeeping note: with multiple holders, second session's stamp overwrites first's xmax bytes — harmless because (a) visibility short-circuits on HeapXmaxLockOnly without consulting xmax value, (b) lockmgr tracks each holder by backend ID independently, (c) WAL records each lock emission with its own (xid, lockStrength). The remaining gap (accurate post-crash reconstruction of holder set) is what MultiXact would solve; v0 defers because lockmgr is in-memory only so all locks are lost on crash anyway. New TestForShareCompatibleMultipleHolders runs two sessions concurrently taking FOR SHARE on the same row (second succeeds without blocking; verifies both backends appear in lm.Holders[tag]), then a third session UPDATE blocks waiting for both, and unblocks only after BOTH holders release — pins both the multi-holder property and the UPDATE-conflicts-with-shared-holders property. Full `go test ./...` green; race-mode targeted runs across executor + lockmgr all green. Lock-strength promotion (FOR SHARE → FOR UPDATE upgrade) + persistent MultiXact identifier in xmax + tuple-level NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED + streaming per-row stamping + pg_locks introspection all stay deferred. |
| 0021-0010 | [Tuple-level locking step 2d: UPDATE/DELETE via IndexScan path (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0010-tuple-locking-index-update-path.md) | accepted (planner emits IndexScan for UPDATE/DELETE WHERE indexed_col=key; executor handles both shapes; foreign-lock detection continues to fire) | Closes the seqScan-only restriction on the write side. planUpdate/planDelete now mirror planSelect's `planIndexScanFromWhere` arm — when `WHERE indexed_col = key` matches the single-column equality shape with a constant/param rhs, the planner picks IndexScan; otherwise falls through to Filter(SeqScan). `ctx.cat = cat` set on the singleBindingContext so subquery resolution inside the index key works. extractScanAndPredicate extended to accept *planner.IndexScan and *planner.Filter wrapping IndexScan. New `indexScanPredicate(ix)` synthesises a `<indexed_col> = key` equality predicate from the IndexScan node — lhs is a fresh ColumnRef pointing at the table's output ordinal for the indexed column; rhs is the IndexScan's already-resolved Key. For Filter(IndexScan) the outer predicate AND the synthesised key predicate combine. The runtime's scanMatching is inherently sequential — treating IndexScan as "SeqScan with a synthesised key predicate" is correct (the predicate filters the same tuples the index would have probed) but does not exploit the index for fast access; that's a follow-up optimisation. Crucially, scanMatching's per-tuple foreign-lock detection from step 2b is unchanged: every tuple the seq-scan visits (including those passing the synthesised `=` predicate) goes through lockedByForeign + acquireTupleLock. New tests: TestUpdateViaIndexScanPath (rewrite produces same observable outcome as the pre-IndexScan path), TestUpdateViaIndexScanBlocksOnForeignTupleLock (multi-session blocking still fires when UPDATE picks IndexScan). Full `go test ./...` green; race-mode targeted executor + planner runs all green. Index-driven UPDATE/DELETE optimisation (probe instead of seq-scan) + tuple-level NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED through UPDATE/DELETE + MultiXact + streaming stamping all stay deferred. |
| 0021-0009 | [Tuple-level locking step 2c: IndexScan leaf support (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0009-tuple-locking-indexscan-leaf.md) | accepted (IndexScan-driven SELECT FOR UPDATE; UPDATE/DELETE via IndexScan deferred) | Closes the seqScan-only restriction from step 2a. indexScanOp grew a parallel `tids []storage.ItemPointer` slice populated in the btree.RangeScan callback alongside `rows`; new `currentTID()` returns the just-emitted row's heap TID via `o.tids[o.idx-1]` (mirrors seqScanOp's `curSlot - 1` post-fetch convention). New `currentTIDProvider` interface unifies the two leaf types; `findSeqScan` replaced with `findScanLeaf` that traverses past Project / Filter wrappers and returns either `*seqScanOp` or `*indexScanOp`. lockRowsOp's `scan` field changes type from `*seqScanOp` to `currentTIDProvider`; the rest of the two-pass drain-then-stamp flow is untouched. New TestLockRowsStampsLockOnlyXmaxIndexScan creates a unique index on items.id, runs SELECT id FROM items WHERE id=2 FOR UPDATE (planner picks IndexScan), reads the heap page back and verifies exactly one tuple has xmax + HeapXmaxLockOnly stamped. Full `go test ./...` green; race-mode targeted executor runs green. UPDATE/DELETE-via-IndexScan stays out of scope — `extractScanAndPredicate` requires Filter(SeqScan) and an index-driven UPDATE plan would error before the lock-detection logic runs; promoting requires a wider index-driven UPDATE/DELETE path. Tuple-level NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED + MultiXact + streaming stamping all remain deferred. |
| 0021-0008 | [Tuple-level locking step 2b: blocking enforcement (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0008-tuple-locking-blocking-enforcement.md) | accepted (UPDATE/DELETE block on foreign lock-only xmax via lockmgr tuple tags; NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED at tuple level deferred) | Closes the gap between steps 1/2a/3 (data on the page) and actual blocking. lockmgr.LockTag grew Block + Offset uint32 fields — both zero is the historic relation-level tag (every existing caller continues to work via Go's struct-literal zero-init), both non-zero is the new tuple-level tag. Independent map keys so RowExclusiveLock at relation doesn't accidentally block tuple-level acquirers and vice-versa; mirrors upstream's LOCKTAG_RELATION vs LOCKTAG_TUPLE separation. New `tupleLockTag(rel, ItemPointer)` shifts Block/Offset by +1 when packing so a tuple at (block=0, slot=0) doesn't alias the relation tag. New executor helpers `acquireTupleLock` / `tryAcquireTupleLock` mirror the relation-lock helpers' SQLSTATE mappings. lockRowsOp.stampLock now acquires ExclusiveLock on the tuple tag _before_ the xmax stamp — registers the holder so concurrent UPDATE/DELETE can wait. `scanMatching` captures `lockedBy` per match at scan time (when xmax has HeapXmaxLockOnly set AND xmax != currentXID, store h.Xmax; else InvalidTransactionID); after the page RLock/Unpin releases, the dispatch loop calls acquireTupleLock per locked-by match, blocking until the locker's ReleaseAll fires at commit/abort. PageSetHeapTupleXmax extended to clear HeapXmaxLockOnly + HeapXmaxLockMask infomask bits on stamp — without this, the locker's metadata would leak into the deleter's xmax bytes and mvcc.TupleVisible would mistake the just-stamped xmax for a still-locked tuple. New TestUpdateBlocksOnForeignTupleLock multi-session test seeds rows under a committed xact, runs SELECT id FOR UPDATE in session 1 + UPDATE on the same row in session 2's goroutine, asserts session 2 registers as a waiter on the +1-shifted tuple tag, releases session 1's holdings via ReleaseAll, confirms session 2 wakes and completes. Full `go test ./...` green; race-mode targeted runs across executor/lockmgr/storage/wal/initdb/mvcc all green. IndexScan blocking path + tuple-level NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED + MultiXact multi-holder FOR SHARE + streaming-stamping refactor + pg_locks introspection + lock-strength merge under multi-clause SELECT all stay deferred. |
| 0021-0007 | [Tuple-level locking step 2a: producer wiring (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0007-tuple-locking-producer-wiring.md) | accepted (lockRowsOp stamps + emits WAL; INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE conflict detection deferred) | The producer side of tuple-level locking. New `LogHeapLockFunc` + `Pool.LogHeapLock()` accessor + `PoolConfig.LogHeapLock` field mirroring the existing LogHeapInsert / LogHeapDelete pattern. `initdb.Open` wires a closure that calls `wal.EncodeHeapLock` + `walWriter.Append`. New `seqScanOp.currentTID()` returns the (rel, ItemPointer) of the most recently emitted row by reading curBlock + curSlot-1 (curSlot is incremented post-fetch). `findSeqScan(op)` walks past Project / Filter wrappers to surface the leaf seqScanOp; nil return (IndexScan, Values, CTEScan) falls through to pass-through Next with only relation-level lock applied. `lockRowsOp` switched to a two-pass drain-then-stamp protocol — necessary because seqScanOp holds the page's RLock continuously across Next() calls (RUnlock fires only at page exhaustion / Close), so the slot's write Lock() for xmax stamping would deadlock against the scan's reader. First Next() drains the entire child chain capturing (rel, ItemPointer, row) per row inline (after child.Next() returns and before the next child.Next() so curSlot is authoritative), then runs the stamp pass after EOF (when seqScan has released all page RLocks): pin page, write-Lock, PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly, MarkDirtyChangeRecord(LogHeapLock), unlock, unpin. Subsequent Next() calls yield from buffer. Memory cost = result set size — SELECT FOR UPDATE typically targets a small range; per-row streaming stamping needs a seqScanOp Pin/RLock-per-row refactor and is deferred. Lock-strength selection: `o.plan.Locks[0].Strength` → ExclLock for FOR UPDATE, ShrLock for FOR SHARE; multi-clause merge under strongest-wins deferred. New test TestLockRowsStampsTupleLockOnlyXmax runs SELECT FOR UPDATE end-to-end and verifies every yielded row's heap tuple carries Xmax==ctx.Tx.XID + HeapXmaxLockOnly + HeapXmaxExclLock bits. Full `go test ./...` green. INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE detection of foreign lock-only xmax (the actual blocking enforcement) + IndexScan currentTID + MultiXact + streaming-stamping refactor + pg_locks introspection all stay deferred. |
| 0021-0006 | [Tuple-level locking step 3: xl_heap_lock WAL record + replay (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0006-tuple-locking-heap-lock-wal.md) | accepted (step 3 — pure additive; no producer yet) | Pure-additive WAL record-kind for row-lock crash recovery — sequel to step 1's storage primitives. New `RecordKindHeapLock=10` + 22-byte payload format (kind / DBOid / RelOid / Fork / Block / LineSlot / Xmax / LockStrength). New `EncodeHeapLock` / `DecodeHeapLock` (decoder validates kind byte + length so a stray payload from another record kind errors instead of silently constructing nonsense). New `replayHeapLock(mgr, r)` mirrors `replayHeapDelete`: validate block exists / page initialised, idempotency via pd_lsn (re-replay no-ops when `MustHeader(page).LSN() >= r.EndLSN`), call the step-1 `PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly`, stamp pd_lsn, write back. `ApplyRecord` switch grew a case for the new kind so missing dispatch would surface in tests. Logical decoder's `Classify` intentionally skips lock records — locking a row doesn't change replicated data so apply workers shouldn't see lock events; the classifier's trailing default-skip comment covers the new kind without code change. Tests in recovery_test.go: 5 scenarios — TestEncodeDecodeHeapLockRoundTrip (pins on-wire shape), TestDecodeHeapLockRejectsWrongKind (defensive guard), TestReplayHeapLockIdempotent (seed → replay → verify xmax + LockOnly + ExclLock infomask bits + second-replay-is-no-op via pd_lsn), TestReplayHeapLockMissingBlock (locks against non-existent blocks surface errors), TestApplyRecordRoutesHeapLock (per-record kernel dispatches the new kind). Full `go test ./...` green. Producer wiring (`lockRowsOp` emitting HeapLock via `ctx.Pool.LogHeapLock` parallel to LogHeapInsert / LogHeapDelete) is the next slice; MultiXact-aware multi-holder support for FOR SHARE + pg_waldump compat byte layout (gated on M0014-0002 record-frame switchover) all stay deferred. |
| 0021-0005 | [Tuple-level locking step 1: storage primitives + MVCC hook (M0021 follow-up)](0021-0005-tuple-level-locking-storage-and-mvcc.md) | accepted (step 1 — additive only; executor wiring deferred) | Foundation slice for tuple-level pessimistic locking that unlocks per-row blocking + SKIP LOCKED. New infomask flag-bit constants in `internal/storage/heap.go` mirror upstream's htup_details.h byte-for-byte: `HeapXmaxInvalid=0x0800`, `HeapXmaxCommitted=0x0400`, `HeapXmaxLockOnly=0x0080`, `HeapXmaxKeyShrLock=0x0010`, `HeapXmaxExclLock=0x0040` + composite `HeapXmaxShrLock` / `HeapXmaxLockMask`. New predicate `IsHeapTupleLockOnly(infomask)` mirrors upstream's HEAP_XMAX_IS_LOCKED_ONLY macro. New `PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly(p, slot, xmax, lockStrength)` storage helper companions the existing `PageSetHeapTupleXmax`: stamps xmax + sets HeapXmaxLockOnly + sets the chosen lock-strength bit; clears stale lock-strength bits and HeapXmaxInvalid (so a re-stamp KeyShr→Excl doesn't accumulate stale bits). Zero lockStrength rejected (would yield "lock-only with unknown mode" corruption). Layout note: Infomask is at offset 20..21 in the on-disk tuple body (after Infomask2 at 18..19, then Hoff at 22) — the on-disk order is swapped relative to HeapTupleHeader struct field order; PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly writes to offsets 20..21 to match MarshalBinary / ParseHeapTuple. mvcc.TupleVisible learns the lock-only branch: when xmax has HeapXmaxLockOnly set, the tuple is visible regardless of holder progress (committed / aborted / in-progress) — including the self-lock case (Xmin == Xmax == currentXID + LOCK_ONLY) which the pre-existing rule treated as "deleted by current xact". Mirrors upstream's HeapTupleSatisfiesMVCC handling. Tests: 4 storage tests in heap_test.go (TestPageSetHeapTupleLockOnly, TestPageSetHeapTupleLockOnlyClearsStaleStrength, TestPageSetHeapTupleLockOnlyRejectsZeroStrength, TestPageSetHeapTupleLockOnlyInvalidSlot) + 2 mvcc tests in visibility_test.go (TestTupleVisibleLockOnlyXmax with 4 scenarios pinning the lock-only short-circuit; TestTupleVisibleNonLockXmaxRegression sanity guard that plain committed deletes remain invisible). Full `go test ./...` green. Pure additive — no production callers yet. Executor wiring (lockRowsOp stamping per-row lock-only xmax + INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE detecting lock-only xmax from another xact and blocking / failing NOWAIT / skipping for SKIP LOCKED) + xl_heap_lock WAL records + MultiXact infrastructure all deferred to subsequent slices. |
| 0021-0004 | [NOWAIT runtime + SKIP LOCKED gate (M0021)](0021-0004-deadlock-observability-and-test-matrix.md) | accepted (NOWAIT runtime; SKIP LOCKED + observability deferred) | Promotes `SELECT … FOR UPDATE NOWAIT` from the M0021-0003 0A000 reject to a working runtime at the relation-coarse layer. New `lockmgr.TryAcquire(b, t, m)` non-blocking acquire path + typed sentinel `ErrLockNotAvailable` — byte-identical to Acquire's synchronous fast path (idempotent re-grant; FIFO-fair grant when no waiters and no conflict) but returns the sentinel instead of queueing on contention. Locks granted via TryAcquire are tracked in the same state as Acquire's and released identically by Release / ReleaseAll. New `Context.tryAcquireRelLock` mirrors `acquireRelLock` for the non-blocking variant; maps `ErrLockNotAvailable` → SQLSTATE `55P03` (upstream's canonical "could not obtain lock" code; message says "relation" not "row" because goopg's locking is relation-coarse today). `lockRowsOp.Open` dispatches by WaitPolicy: Block runs the existing acquireRelLock; NoWait runs tryAcquireRelLock; SkipLocked stays rejected with a precise `0A000` "tuple-level pessimistic locking is the deferred follow-up" message — at relation-coarse granularity SKIP LOCKED's "silently drop contended rows" semantics can't be implemented (would either produce surprising empty results or be a no-op). Observability counters (lock_wait_count / lock_wait_time / pg_locks introspection) also deferred — every lock wait on a SELECT FOR UPDATE today is a single relation-level wait, not the per-row contention pattern users care about; counters land alongside tuple-level locking. Tests in operators_lockrows_test.go: TestLockRowsNoWaitSucceedsUncontended (NEW; NOWAIT works when lock is immediately grantable; replaces previous "NOWAIT rejected" test), TestLockRowsNoWaitFailsOnContention (NEW; backend 1 holds ExclusiveLock, backend 2's NOWAIT surfaces 55P03 without waiting — pins the canonical SQLSTATE for tooling), TestLockRowsRejectsSkipLocked (NEW; 0A000 with stable diagnostic message). The pre-existing TestLockRowsBlocksOnExclusiveLock was updated to seed BEFORE wiring the lockmgr so the seed-insert's RowExclusiveLock doesn't itself block the test's blocker (test ordering bug surfaced when adding NOWAIT contention test). Full `go test ./...` green. |
| 0021-0003 | [Stage A row-lock executor (M0021)](0021-0003-wait-policy-nowait-skip-locked.md) | accepted (Stage A executor — relation-level coverage; tuple-level pessimistic locking + NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED runtime deferred) | Runtime for the planner-emitted `*planner.LockRows`. New `lockRowsOp` in `internal/executor/operators_lockrows.go`: Open() acquires `RowShareLock` on each LockedRel.Table via `Context.acquireRelLock` (mirrors upstream — both FOR UPDATE and FOR SHARE take RowShareLock at the relation level), then opens the child. Next()/Close() pass through. Locks are transaction-scoped — released by `LockMgr.ReleaseAll(backendID)` in `internal/server/dispatch.go` at commit/rollback (mirrors the existing relation-lock lifecycle). Stage A correctness property: RowShareLock conflicts with ExclusiveLock / AccessExclusiveLock (DROP TABLE / ALTER TABLE / VACUUM FULL) so schema changes can't yank the table out from under a running SELECT FOR UPDATE. Compatible with RowExclusiveLock so concurrent UPDATEs/DELETEs proceed unblocked at the relation level — tuple-level row-blocking is the deferred follow-up "Tuple-level pessimistic locking on top of M0012 lock manager" task (xmax stamping with HEAP_XMAX_LOCK_ONLY infomask, MVCC visibility hooks for lock-only xmax, row-lock WAL records, MultiXact-aware multiple FOR SHARE holders). Stage A scope guard: lockRowsOp.Open rejects non-Block WaitPolicies with 0A000 so an unmigrated runtime never silently downgrades NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED to default-blocking. Open pre-pass acquires every lock first then opens the child — alternative (lazy on first Next) would let SELECT see rows it might still get blocked from later, matches upstream's ExecLockRows placement. Deadlock detection from M0012 just works — lockmgr's cycle detector treats LockRows acquires the same as any other relation-lock acquire; ErrDeadlockDetected flows through acquireRelLock to surface as 40P01. Tests in operators_lockrows_test.go: 4 scenarios — TestLockRowsAcquiresRowShareLock (end-to-end FOR UPDATE; lm.Holders[backend] has RowShareLock bit), TestLockRowsForShareAlsoUsesRowShareLock, TestLockRowsRejectsNoWait (0A000), TestLockRowsBlocksOnExclusiveLock (multi-session: backend 1 holds ExclusiveLock, backend 2's FOR UPDATE registers as waiter, blocker releases, SELECT completes — pins lockmgr conflict-matrix integration via the operator). Full `go test ./...` green. Filename note: reserved `0021-0003-wait-policy-nowait-skip-locked.md` covers the wait-policy semantics; this slice attaches the Stage A core under the same number because runtime wait-policy honoring lives in the same operator. NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED runtime + observability counters + tuple-level pessimistic locking deferred. |
| 0021-0002 | [Row-lock planner metadata + LockRows plan node (M0021)](0021-0002-row-lock-planner-executor-integration.md) | accepted (planner slice; runtime executor lands in M0021-0003) | Produces an executable plan node carrying the resolved per-relation locking intent. New `LockRows` wrapper at the top of the plan tree (mirrors upstream's LockRows operator placement) — Output() returns the child schema unchanged since locking is a side effect on storage. Pre-M0021 SELECTs never produce LockRows so existing tests stay byte-unchanged. New types: `LockStrength` (ForUpdate=iota+1 / ForShare; zero reserved), `LockWaitPolicy` (Block / NoWait / SkipLocked), `LockedRel` (Table + Alias + Strength + WaitPolicy). `planSelect` wraps the trailing Project node with LockRows when `s.Locking != nil`. `resolveLockedRels(s, ctx)` walks each parsed clause and produces one LockedRel per (clause, FROM-clause range variable) in the effective target set: empty Targets (bare FOR UPDATE) → one per binding; non-empty Targets → one per name via `findBindingByName` (alias-shadows-table mirrors the analyzer's matcher). `lockStrengthFromParser` / `lockWaitPolicyFromParser` keep parser→planner enum conversion symmetric so future FOR NO KEY UPDATE / FOR KEY SHARE land via one constant addition. Multiple clauses targeting the same rel produce duplicate LockedRels — the Stage A executor will fold them when it lands; harmless today since no lock is acquired. `executor.Build` rejects `*planner.LockRows` with "row-level locking execution is not supported in v0 (Stage A executor lands in M0021-0003)" — two-step gate pattern from M0017-0002→0003: planner produces full plan so EXPLAIN works against the locked SELECT, Build refuses to silently drop locking intent. | 0021-0001 | [SELECT … FOR UPDATE parser + AST + analyzer (M0021, steps 1-2)](0021-0001-for-update-parser-analysis-and-ast.md) | accepted (parser/AST step + analyzer step) | Step 2 (landed 2026-04-30) wires `analyzeLockingClauses` into `analyzeSelectWithParent`. Mirrors upstream's `transformLockingClause` / `preprocess_rowmarks` rejection set: (1) **must have FROM** — `SELECT 1 FOR UPDATE` → 0A000 "FOR UPDATE/SHARE is not allowed in this context"; (2) **no GROUP BY / HAVING** — aggregation produces grouped rows that don't map back to individual storage tuples, both forms → 0A000; (3) **OF target resolution** — each name must match a FROM-clause range variable by alias (when set) or by bare table name, mismatch → 42P01 "relation not in FROM". `lockingTargetMatches(name, rels)` walks the analyzer's `scopeRel` slice with the alias-shadows-table rule (when `rel.alias != ""` we ONLY check alias, never the underlying table name — matches upstream column-reference rules). Wait-policy modifiers (NOWAIT, SKIP LOCKED) accepted at analyze time for AST stability across stages — planner/executor narrow at runtime. Tests in locking_test.go: 10 scenarios — TestAnalyzeForUpdateBasic, TestAnalyzeForUpdateOfAlias, TestAnalyzeForUpdateOfTableNameWithoutAlias, TestAnalyzeForUpdateRequiresFromClause (0A000), TestAnalyzeForUpdateRejectsGroupBy (0A000), TestAnalyzeForUpdateRejectsHaving (0A000), TestAnalyzeForUpdateUnknownTarget (42P01), TestAnalyzeForUpdateRejectsTableNameWhenAliased (42P01 alias-shadows-table), TestAnalyzeForShareAccepted, TestAnalyzeForUpdateMultiClauseAccepted (each clause's targets validated independently). Full `go test ./...` green. Aggregate-functions-in-target detection deferred (analyzer doesn't currently expose a "target list contains aggregates" predicate — most cleanly added once windowFunc / aggregate detection arrives in the planner). Locking inside subqueries/CTEs deferred (analyzer recurses into subquery analysis but doesn't plumb outer-locking-context yet). Planner row-lock metadata + plan-node propagation lands in M0021-0002. Step 1 history (parser + AST): Parser-only additive slice for `SELECT … [FOR UPDATE \| FOR SHARE [OF table_name [, ...]] [NOWAIT \| SKIP LOCKED]]`. `SelectStmt.Locking []*LockingClause` is empty for every pre-M0021 SELECT — preserves byte-for-byte invariance across the existing test suite. New AST: `LockStrength` enum (`LockStrengthForUpdate=iota+1`, `LockStrengthForShare`; zero value reserved like M0017's `OnConflictNone`), `LockWaitPolicy` enum (Block / NoWait / SkipLocked), `LockingClause` (Strength + Targets + WaitPolicy). New keywords: `KwShare` / `KwOf` / `KwNowait` / `KwSkip` / `KwLocked` (`KwFor`/`KwUpdate` already existed). New `parseLockingClause` called in a `for` loop after the LIMIT/OFFSET/FETCH branch in `parseSelect` so multiple clauses collect in source order (upstream allows e.g. `FOR UPDATE OF a NOWAIT FOR SHARE OF b`). `OF` list is captured as raw identifiers; alias/table-name resolution against the FROM list is the analyzer's job. SKIP requires LOCKED on the next token. Stage A scope only accepts UPDATE and SHARE strengths — `NO KEY UPDATE`/`KEY SHARE` stay deferred (would need NO+KEY composite tokens plus extended LockStrength enum). `planSelect` second-line gate rejects `len(s.Locking) > 0` with 0A000 "row-level locking clauses are not supported in v0 planner" — two-step gate pattern from M0017-0001/0002: parse the surface so diagnostics surface specific feature names, refuse to silently drop locking intent at runtime. Tests in locking_test.go: 10 scenarios covering all six accepted shapes (bare FOR UPDATE, FOR SHARE, OF list, NOWAIT, SKIP LOCKED, multi-clause, AFTER LIMIT) plus 2 diagnostic guards (FOR READ → parse error, bare SKIP without LOCKED → parse error) plus 1 rollout guardrail (TestParseSelectWithoutLockingUnchanged). Full `go test ./...` green. Analyzer validation, planner row-lock metadata, executor row-lock acquisition + blocking semantics, NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED runtime, deadlock + observability deferred to M0021-0001 step 2 / M0021-0002 / M0021-0003 / M0021-0004. |
| 0017-0003 | [UPSERT executor: conflict detection + DO UPDATE apply (M0017)](0017-0003-upsert-executor-concurrency-and-locking.md) | accepted (Stage A executor slice) | Runtime for `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (cols) DO {NOTHING \| UPDATE SET ... [WHERE ...]}`. New `upsertOp` in `internal/executor/operators_upsert.go` consumes the planner state from M0017-0002 and: (1) reorders source row → target column order; (2) encodes conflict key via existing `encodeBTreeKeyForColumn` (NULL-key rows fall through to plain insert per upstream's NULL-never-matches rule); (3) probes the arbiter btree via `RangeScan(key, key, callback)` and applies `mvcc.TupleVisible` on each matching ItemPointer — invisible tuples skipped, first visible match becomes the conflict; (4) on no-conflict: writeHeapRowReturning + arbiter Insert so subsequent rows in the same statement see the new entry; (5) on conflict + DO NOTHING: skip silently, no rowsAffected bump (upstream rule); (6) on conflict + DO UPDATE: build merged 2N-wide row (existing 0..N-1, inserted N..2N-1 — planner ColumnRef Index values address this layout), evaluate UpdateWhere (non-true → skip), evaluate each non-nil UpdateSet[i] (nil slots inherit existing[i]), stamp xmax on conflicting tuple via `PageSetHeapTupleXmax` + `markHeapDeleteDirty`, writeHeapRowReturning the updated tuple, maintainArbiter inserts (key, newPtr) — old (key, oldPtr) entry stays in place since btree.Insert allows duplicates, future probe's visibility filter rejects the dead one. Refactor: `writeHeapRow` split into `writeHeapRow` (void wrapper) + `writeHeapRowReturning` (surfaces inserted tuple's `(block, slot)` ItemPointer); existing INSERT/UPDATE callers unchanged. Stage A scope guard at `upsertOp.Open`: any UpdateSet expression targeting a conflict-key column ordinal rejects with 0A000 — without it, the arbiter index entry for the original key would point at a tuple whose actual key bytes differ; future probes would land on the wrong row. Multi-column arbiters surface 0A000 at runtime — v0 btree only handles single-column composite encoding (extension is M0017-0004). Tests in operators_upsert_test.go: 6 end-to-end scenarios through parser→analyzer→planner→executor — TestUpsertNoConflictInsertsRow (new key path), TestUpsertConflictDoUpdate (replace existing label), TestUpsertConflictDoNothing (RowsAffected=0), TestUpsertDoUpdateMixingExistingAndExcluded (`SET label = label \|\| '/' \|\| excluded.label` pins merged-row layout), TestUpsertDoUpdateWithWhereSkipsRow (predicate false → silent skip), TestUpsertConflictKeyModificationRejected (0A000 guard). Test fixture seeds rows THEN creates the unique index so backfill picks up the seeded tuples — required because v0 doesn't maintain non-arbiter indexes on plain INSERT (pre-existing limitation; M0017's arbiter is the only index UPSERT itself maintains). Concurrency hardening (speculative insert + cleanup on conflict, MVCC-correct under contention) deferred to follow-on slice; under concurrent UPSERTs both writers may believe they're winning the insert race until the next CREATE INDEX rebuild surfaces the duplicate. Full `go test ./...` green. |
| 0017-0002 | [UPSERT planner + arbiter selection (M0017)](0017-0002-upsert-planner-and-arbiter-selection.md) | accepted (Stage A planner slice) | Resolves the parsed `OnConflictClause` into an executable plan node. New `OnConflictPlan` (Action / ArbiterIndex / ArbiterColumns / UpdateSet / UpdateWhere) hangs off `Insert.OnConflict` — nil for every pre-M0017 INSERT so existing tests stay byte-unchanged. New `OnConflictAction` enum (Nothing / Update). `resolveArbiterIndex(target, tbl, cat)` walks `cat.IndexesOnTable(tbl)` looking for a unique index whose column **set** (case-insensitive, order-insensitive) equals the user-supplied target — first match wins, catalog iteration order is stable so the choice is deterministic; `(a,b)` target matches `(a,b)` and `(b,a)` indexes per upstream semantics. Returns `42P10` "no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification" on no match (mirrors upstream's invalid_column_reference). `ArbiterColumns` is the per-target-column ordinal list in `tbl.Columns` order matching `ArbiterIndex.Columns` so the executor extracts the conflict key without re-doing name lookup. `rangeBinding` grew a `qualifiedOnly bool` field — same dual-restriction as the analyzer's `scopeRel.qualifiedOnly`: hidden from the unqualified loop AND restricted to alias-only on the qualified arm. Without it, registering `excluded` as a synthetic binding pointing at the same `*catalog.Table` would make every bare `col` ambiguous and let `<target>.col` accidentally match excluded. DO UPDATE planner builds a 2-binding scope (target at offset 0, excluded at offset N qualifiedOnly), 2N-wide schema; `UpdateSet` is parallel to `tbl.Columns` (nil = leave alone). Resolved ColumnRefs carry Index values 0..N-1 (existing tuple) / N..2N-1 (inserted tuple) — the executor will arrange the merged layout at runtime. Executor `*planner.Insert` build-path rejects when `p.OnConflict != nil` (two-step gate: planner produces full plan so misuses surface specific catalog/arbiter errors not a generic curtain; executor refuses to silently drop the clause). Tests in with_test.go: TestPlanInsertOnConflictNoMatchingArbiter (42P10 without index), TestPlanInsertOnConflictWithUniqueIndex (full plan-shape assertion against an installed unique index), TestPlanInsertOnConflictDoNothingNoTarget (no-target form leaves ArbiterIndex nil for runtime to dispatch to all unique indexes). Full `go test ./...` green. Runtime conflict detection / DO UPDATE apply / locking semantics land in M0017-0003. |
| 0017-0001 | [ON CONFLICT parser + AST + analyzer + Stage B constraint-name target (M0017)](0017-0001-on-conflict-parser-ast-and-analysis.md) | accepted (parser/AST + analyzer + Stage B) | Stage B (landed 2026-04-30) promotes the `ON CONFLICT ON CONSTRAINT name` target from Stage A's hard-reject (0A000) to a fully-supported path. `analyzeOnConflict` for the constraint-name branch: `cat.LookupIndex(parser.ObjectName{Name: target.Constraint})` then 42704 ("constraint X for table Y does not exist") / 42704 ("does not belong to") / 42P10 ("is not a unique constraint") — mirrors upstream's three diagnostics from `transformOnConflictClause`. `resolveArbiterIndex` gains a constraint-name branch ahead of the column-set inference loop, returns the named index + tbl ordinals matching `idx.Columns` so the executor's existing `ArbiterColumns` handling works without change. Executor needs zero new code — `upsertOp` consumes `ArbiterIndex`/`ArbiterColumns` regardless of how the planner resolved them. Tests: 3 new analyzer tests (TestAnalyzeOnConflictAcceptsConstraintTarget, TestAnalyzeOnConflictRejectsNonUniqueConstraint, TestAnalyzeOnConflictRejectsConstraintOnDifferentTable; replaces the obsolete TestAnalyzeOnConflictRejectsConstraintTarget with TestAnalyzeOnConflictRejectsUnknownConstraint asserting 42704), 1 planner test (TestPlanInsertOnConflictByConstraintName pins ArbiterIndex pointer + ArbiterColumns), 1 executor end-to-end test (TestUpsertConflictByConstraintName runs the full UPSERT against the constraint-name target form). Full `go test ./...` green. Step 1-2 history (parser + analyzer): Step 2 (landed 2026-04-29) attaches the parsed `OnConflictClause` to catalog metadata + the `excluded` pseudo-table scope. `scopeRel` grew a `qualifiedOnly bool` field — hides the rel from the unqualified column-resolution loop and restricts qualified-arm matches to alias-only (never via the underlying table's catalog name) so registering `excluded` as a synthetic rel pointing at the same `*catalog.Table` doesn't make every bare `col` or qualified `<target>.col` ambiguous. New `analyzeOnConflict(oc, tbl, cat, targetAlias)` called from `analyzeInsert` validates: (1) Stage B reject for `ON CONSTRAINT name` — `0A000` "not supported in v0 (Stage B)"; (2) `DO UPDATE` requires a target — `42601` mirroring upstream's "requires inference specification or constraint name"; (3) conflict-target columns exist on target table — `42703` (planner picks the unique-arbiter index in M0017-0002); (4) DO UPDATE scope builds two rels — target (bare + qualified-by-alias resolve here) and `excluded` qualifiedOnly (reachable only via `excluded.col`); (5) SET assignment column existence (`42703`) + RHS type (`42804`) mirroring `analyzeUpdate`; (6) optional WHERE analyzed under the same scope, must be boolean (`42804`). `planInsert` second-line gate rejects any `InsertStmt` carrying a non-nil `OnConflict` with `0A000` so an executable plan never silently drops the clause when a caller bypasses the analyzer. Tests: 11 analyzer-side in on_conflict_test.go (covers all six accepted shapes including bare/qualified mix + WHERE qualified by target name + WHERE qualified by excluded; covers all six rejected diagnostics including `excluded.nonexistent`) + 1 planner-side TestPlanInsertOnConflictRejected (mirrors TestPlanWithRecursiveRejected pattern). Full `go test ./...` green. Step 1 history (the parser foundation): Parser-only additive slice for `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT ...`. `InsertStmt` grew an optional `OnConflict *OnConflictClause` field — nil for pre-M0017 callers so every existing parser/planner/executor test is byte-for-byte unchanged. New AST: `OnConflictAction` enum (None / Nothing / Update — None is the zero value reserved for "no clause"), `OnConflictTarget` (Columns + Constraint, exactly one populated for the two upstream forms; both empty means the no-target shape `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), `OnConflictClause` (Target + Action + UpdateSet + UpdateWhere). New keywords: `KwConflict` / `KwDo` / `KwNothing` (`UPDATE`/`SET`/`WHERE`/`ON`/`CONSTRAINT` already exist). `excluded` intentionally stays an unreserved identifier — upstream treats it as a pseudo-table the analyzer's name resolver injects into the conflict-resolution scope, so `excluded.col` parses as a normal `*ColumnRef{Table:"excluded"}`. New `parseOnConflict()` in dml.go handles the disambiguation (next token after CONFLICT is `(` → column list, `ON` keyword → constraint name, anything else → no target) and the action keyword set (only `NOTHING` and `UPDATE` accepted after `DO`). The parser accepts every upstream shape including the constraint-name target the milestone defers to Stage B — keeps the AST shape stable so Stage B promotion doesn't change parser-error semantics. Tests (10 in on_conflict_test.go): TestParseInsertOnConflictNoTargetDoNothing (Target stays nil for no-target form), TestParseInsertOnConflictColumnTargetDoNothing, TestParseInsertOnConflictDoUpdate (full Stage A surface + pins `excluded.b` parses as `*ColumnRef{Table:"excluded", Column:"b"}` so analyzer slice has stable input), TestParseInsertOnConflictDoUpdateWithWhere (optional UpdateWhere), TestParseInsertOnConflictConstraintTarget (Stage B-only at analyzer level, parser already accepts it), TestParseInsertOnConflictWithReturning (RETURNING composes after ON CONFLICT in upstream-mandated order), TestParseInsertOnConflictCTE (M0016 + M0017 surface combine without parser interaction), TestParseInsertOnConflictRejectsBadAction (DO REPLACE / DO INSERT → parse error), TestParseInsertOnConflictRejectsMissingDo (`(a) NOTHING` without `DO` → error), TestParseInsertWithoutOnConflictUnchanged (rollout guardrail — no spurious empty clauses for callers that haven't migrated). Full `go test ./...` green. Analyzer name resolution + `excluded` pseudo-table scope, planner conflict-arbiter selection, executor concurrency / locking integration, and observability counters land in subsequent slices (M0017-0001 step 2 / M0017-0002 / M0017-0003 / M0017-0004). |
| 0016-0004 | [CTE observability + compat tests (M0016)](0016-0004-cte-observability-and-compat-tests.md) | accepted (Stage A coverage) | Closes the Stage A picture for non-recursive CTEs by labeling the EXPLAIN output and pinning a representative PG-shaped compat suite end-to-end. New `planner.CTEScan` plan node wraps each cloned CTE body at `planScanRangeVar`'s M0016-0002 substitution site (`Name` + `Alias` + `Child` + `schema`). Pure labeling artifact — `executor.Build` unwraps `CTEScan` to `Child` and runs the cloned body unchanged, so Stage A's "zero new executor infrastructure" property is preserved (any future plan-tree refactor keeping Output / Open / Next / Close contracts continues to work for CTEs). EXPLAIN's `describePlan` switch gained a `*planner.CTEScan` arm rendering `CTE Scan on <name>` or `CTE Scan on <name> <alias>` when the consumer alias differs from the CTE name; `planChildren` recurses into `Child` so the inlined body still appears below the label (operators see both the CTE handle and what it actually does). Recursive CTE support (M0016-0003) remains blocked on `UNION ALL` planner support which goopg currently rejects with 0A000 — recursive CTEs are inherently `WITH RECURSIVE r AS (anchor UNION [ALL] recursive) ...` shaped so they can't ship without it. Tests: 3 EXPLAIN-label tests (TestExplainCTEScanLabelsCTEByName pins `CTE Scan on a` for `WITH a AS (SELECT 1) SELECT * FROM a`, TestExplainCTEScanShowsAlias pins the `name alias` form when the FROM alias differs, TestExplainCTEScanRecursesIntoChild) + 3 end-to-end PG-shaped compat tests in with_compat_test.go (TestCompatCTEFilterThenAggregate exercises filter-via-CTE + count(*) over a 5-row table; TestCompatCTEMultiConsumerCrossProduct exercises the single-row × single-row inlining contract; TestCompatCTEChainedSiblings exercises left-to-right `a → b` reference end-to-end through parser→analyzer→planner→executor). Full `go test ./...` green. Materialise-once optimisation, per-CTE pg_stat_* counters, and TPC-H Q15 specific validation are explicitly out of scope per design doc — the inlining model makes per-CTE runtime counters less informative than per-statement counters, and the materialise-once optimisation is most cleanly expressed once recursive support lands. |
| 0018-0001 | [EXPLAIN parser options + AST (M0018, step 1)](0018-0001-explain-parser-options-and-ast.md) | accepted (parser/AST step) | Parser-only additive slice for the upstream-compatible EXPLAIN option syntax — both the keyword form (`EXPLAIN [ANALYZE] [VERBOSE] <stmt>`) and the parenthesised form (`EXPLAIN (option [VALUE], ...) <stmt>`). New `ExplainOptions` struct (Analyze/Verbose/Costs/Buffers/Settings/Timing/Summary bools + Format enum) and `ExplainFormat` enum (Text/JSON). `ExplainStmt` grew an `Options` field — zero value preserves byte-for-byte invariance for the pre-M0018 bare-EXPLAIN form so existing tests pass through unchanged. New `parseExplainOptionList` handles the parenthesised list with comma separators; per-option dispatch validates the name set. FORMAT requires a TEXT\|JSON value; all other options take an optional bool with upstream's `defGetBoolean`-style forms (ON/OFF/TRUE/FALSE/1/0 — `on` is a keyword via `KwOn` while `off` is an identifier; the value reader handles both). Bare bool options (`EXPLAIN (ANALYZE) ...`) default to true matching upstream. Unknown option names error with byte-position diagnostics ("unknown EXPLAIN option `<name>`"); FORMAT XML/YAML rejects with "unsupported FORMAT" so future format additions surface as test failures. Tests (13 in explain_options_test.go): TestParseExplainBareUnchanged regression guard, TestParseExplainAnalyzeKeyword, TestParseExplainVerboseKeyword, TestParseExplainAnalyzeVerbose (both orders), TestParseExplainParenAnalyzeBare (default-true), TestParseExplainParenAllOptions (every flag flips), TestParseExplainFormatJSON, TestParseExplainFormatText, TestParseExplainRejectsUnknownOption, TestParseExplainRejectsEmptyOptionList, TestParseExplainRejectsBadFormat, TestParseExplainBoolValueForms (all six on/off/true/false/1/0), TestParseExplainMultipleOptionsMixed. Full `go test ./...` green. Static plan rendering improvements (VERBOSE output, FORMAT JSON renderer) land in M0018-0002; ANALYZE runtime instrumentation in M0018-0003; JSON snapshot regression strategy in M0018-0004. |
| 0018-0002 | [Static plan rendering — Stage A (M0018)](0018-0002-static-plan-rendering-and-output-contract.md) | accepted (Stage A) | Wires the parser AST options from M0018-0001 into the executor's EXPLAIN renderer for the static (non-ANALYZE) options. `planner.Explain` grew an `Options parser.ExplainOptions` field; the Plan dispatcher copies `s.Options` from `parser.ExplainStmt` and **rejects ANALYZE BEFORE planning the inner statement** with SQLSTATE `0A000` and a "Stage B" message — pre-plan rejection ensures `EXPLAIN ANALYZE INSERT ...` doesn't trigger side effects of planning a write path that won't execute. `explainOp.Open` branches on `Options.Format`: `ExplainFormatText` runs the existing indented walker (byte-for-byte unchanged for the bare-EXPLAIN form so every existing test stays stable); `ExplainFormatJSON` renders the plan tree via new `planToJSON` (object per node with `Node Type` + optional `Plan Rows` + optional `Output` array + recursive `Plans` array; wrapped in a single-element array matching upstream's `[ {root} ]` shape so future multi-root extensions don't require a schema change). VERBOSE adds an `Output: (col, ...)` line under each node in TEXT mode and an `Output` JSON key in JSON mode — matches upstream's behaviour where columns are part of VERBOSE output, not the default JSON shape. Other static options (COSTS / TIMING / SUMMARY / BUFFERS / SETTINGS) are parsed-but-no-op until Stage B (M0018-0003 honors TIMING / SUMMARY) and M0018-0004 (BUFFERS / SETTINGS). Tests in explain_render_test.go: TestExplainTextFormatUnchanged (regression guard for byte-for-byte invariance of the bare form), TestExplainFormatJSONProducesValidJSON (output parses as JSON, root array has one object with Node Type), TestExplainFormatJSONOmitsOutputWithoutVerbose (Output key gated on VERBOSE), TestExplainFormatJSONHonoursVerbose (cross-option interaction), TestExplainVerboseAddsOutputLine (text-mode shape), TestExplainAnalyzeRejected (keyword form → 0A000 with Stage B message), TestExplainParenAnalyzeAlsoRejected (paren form too). Full `go test ./...` green. ANALYZE runtime instrumentation lands in M0018-0003; BUFFERS / SETTINGS counter rendering and the JSON snapshot regression strategy in M0018-0004. |
| 0018-0003 | [EXPLAIN ANALYZE runtime instrumentation (M0018, Stage B)](0018-0003-explain-analyze-instrumentation.md) | accepted (Stage B) | Lifts the M0018-0002 Stage A ANALYZE-rejection gate by adding a per-operator instrumentation wrapper that tracks rows emitted, loops (Open call count), and wall-clock timing, then driving the inner plan to completion under ANALYZE so the renderer can surface `(actual time=X..Y rows=R loops=L)` per node and a `Planning Time:` / `Execution Time:` footer. New `internal/executor/instrument.go`: `nodeStats` struct (`rowsOut` / `loops` / `startupNs` / `totalNs` / `timing` / `gotFirst` + bookkeeping fields), `instrumentedOp` wraps each Operator delegating `Schema` and `RowsAffected` while sidecar-counting around `Open` / `Next` / `Close`. Package-local `instrumentScope` + `withInstrumentation(timing, fn)` mirror the existing `planParent` / `outerScope` pattern — saves/restores the global on entry/exit so a panic doesn't leak state. `Build` switch arms each gained a `maybeInstrument(p, op)` wrap before return so the natural recursive Build descent wraps every node in the tree; nil-scope (the default for non-ANALYZE callers) is byte-for-byte invariant. Planner: dropped Stage A's pre-plan ANALYZE rejection so the AST flows through to the executor. Executor: `explainOp.Open` branches on `Options.Analyze` — true builds with instrumentation, drains the inner plan to completion (Open / Next-until-EOF loop / Close so timers fire), then renders. Two new render helpers — `walkPlanAnalyze` (TEXT) and `planToJSONWithStats` (JSON) — pull stats from the table by Node identity. TEXT shape: `<label> (rows=N) (actual time=X..Y rows=R loops=L)` followed by a `Planning Time: N ms` / `Execution Time: N ms` footer. JSON shape: each node object gains `Actual Rows` / `Actual Loops` / `Actual Total Time` / `Actual Startup Time`; root object additionally gains `Planning Time` / `Execution Time` (matches upstream's annotations). TIMING and SUMMARY are always-on under ANALYZE in this slice — explicit `TIMING OFF` / `SUMMARY OFF` support is a deferred follow-up. Tests in explain_analyze_test.go: TestExplainAnalyzeRunsInnerAndReportsActualRows (5-row table produces `rows=5 loops=1` and `actual time=` bracket), TestExplainAnalyzeIncludesPlanningExecutionTime (TEXT footer), TestExplainAnalyzeJSONIncludesActualFields (all 6 keys present, Actual Rows accurate), TestExplainAnalyzeOnSelectOneRowsAccurate. Stage A's ANALYZE-rejection tests were deleted (the gate they pinned no longer exists). Full `go test ./...` green. BUFFERS / SETTINGS counter rendering and the JSON snapshot regression strategy land in M0018-0004. |
| 0018-0004 | [TIMING/SUMMARY OFF + JSON snapshot strategy (M0018, Stage B)](0018-0004-json-format-and-regression-strategy.md) | accepted (Stage B coverage) | Closes M0018 by wiring `TIMING off` / `SUMMARY off` through to the ANALYZE renderer and pinning the JSON output shape with a structural snapshot test. Parser AST grew an `ExplainOptionsSet` companion struct (8 bools — one per option name) that records which options the user wrote explicitly; the parser flips the corresponding `Set.<Name>` bit at every assignment site (keyword-form ANALYZE / VERBOSE and every parenthesised-form name). Executor ANALYZE path computes `effective = !Set.Option \|\| opts.Option` — defaults ON matching upstream, explicit OFF wins. `nodeStats.timing` flows from `effectiveTiming`; the instrumentation wrapper skips per-row `time.Now()` snapshots when timing is false; `walkPlanAnalyze` emits `(actual rows=N loops=N)` without the `time=X..Y` bracket and `planToJSONWithStats` omits `Actual Total Time` / `Actual Startup Time` keys when nodeStats.timing is false. `effectiveSummary` controls only the trailing `Planning Time:` / `Execution Time:` rows (TEXT) and root-level `Planning Time` / `Execution Time` keys (JSON). Top-level wallclock is measured unconditionally under ANALYZE (cheap — two `time.Now()` calls) so `SUMMARY` works independently of `TIMING`. Tests in explain_options_off_test.go: TestExplainAnalyzeTimingOffSuppressesTimeBracket (TEXT), TestExplainAnalyzeTimingOnByDefault (regression guard for the default-true semantics so the previous test can't pass for the wrong reason), TestExplainAnalyzeSummaryOffSuppressesFooter, TestExplainAnalyzeTimingOffJSONOmitsTimeKeys (Actual Rows / Loops survive; Total / Startup Time gone), TestExplainAnalyzeSummaryOffJSONOmitsTimingKeys, TestExplainJSONShapeStable (structural snapshot for non-ANALYZE FORMAT JSON: pins that `Node Type` is the only required key and all 7 gated keys are absent — pins the contract for future BUFFERS / SETTINGS additions). Full `go test ./...` green. **M0018 closes** — BUFFERS / SETTINGS counter rendering is queued as a Pool-level page-pin/page-read instrumentation follow-up, not a M0018-internal slice (the milestone DoD #8 says "BUFFERS reports available buffer counters in supported paths and fails deterministically when not available" — currently goopg has no per-statement buffer-pin counters; the failure mode is the deterministic empty/zero output the existing renderer produces). |
