| 0040-0001 | [Subquery Caching and IN‑Subquery Unnest](0040-0001-subquery-caching-and-unnest.md) | accepted | Two-part optimisation: (1) cache correlated subquery results per outer-key to eliminate per-row re-execution; (2) extend the unnest pass to rewrite `IN(subquery)` as hash semi-join. Targets TPC‑H Q20 ≤ 120 s. See `docs/milestones/0040-correlated-subquery-optimization.md`. |
| 0041-0001 | [Close Remaining TPC-H Parity Gaps](0041-0001-close-parity-gaps.md) | landed | Analysis and first fix batch — bindings‑based posMap with `(table*, alias)` self‑join key + split agg‑expr / HAVING remap (parity 14→15, closed Q3/Q11). Subsequent batches in `0041-0002` and `0041-0003` finished the job. See `docs/milestones/0041-close-parity-gaps.md`. |
| 0041-0002 | [Fix Remaining Divergent TPC-H Queries](0041-0002-fix-remaining-6-queries.md) | landed | Second batch: MHJ executor (chain `visited` + branched build + per‑step `buildKeyCol`), bushy‑DP specific‑edge marking, inline‑view Project remap, and outer‑Join key re‑resolution by name. Closed Q5 and Q10 (parity 15→17). |
| 0041-0003 | [Q7 and Q9 Final Parity Fixes](0041-0003-q7-q9-final-fixes.md) | landed | Final batch closing the milestone. Disabled the broken OID‑based MHJ posMap, captured pushOneConjunct's residuals into `mh.Filters`, switched MHJ executor to multi‑row hash with `expandChain` Cartesian DFS, scope‑guarded pushdown via `allColumnRefNamesInScope`, applied `joinPredicateMatch` in lazy hash join, and made `predRebind` try both sides by Name. Parity 17→**19** — `TestTPCHResultParity` PASSES (only Q1/Q8/Q14 precision‑allowlisted divergences remain, errored=0). |
| 0041-0004 | [NUMERIC Precision Fix (Q1, Q8, Q14)](0041-0004-numeric-precision-fix.md) | landed | Closes the last three TPC‑H precision divergences fundamentally. New `numericDiv` matches upstream's `select_div_scale` rule (NBASE=10000 weights + half‑away rounding); Datum gains a `*big.Int` overflow lane so Q8's `mantissa = 10^20` round‑trips. Allowlist emptied — `TestTPCHResultParity` runs at **identical=22, divergent=0, errored=0**. |
| 0042-0001 | [PostgreSQL I/O Subsystem Survey](0042-0001-pg-io-survey.md) | accepted | English reference doc covering upstream PG's WAL writes/durability, page reads/writes/eviction, background writer, checkpointer, WAL buffer ring, dedicated WAL writer process, and per-backend process. Cites `postgres/src/backend/...` exhaustively. Anchor for the M0042 refactor docs. See `docs/milestones/0042-pg-io-alignment.md`. |
| 0042-0002 | [Buffered-I/O Migration](0042-0002-buffered-io-migration.md) | accepted | Removes `O_DIRECT` / `O_DSYNC` / page-aligned-RMW paths from `internal/wal/writer.go` and `internal/storage/smgr.go`, retires `wal_direct_io` GUC and `Manager.AlignedIO` toggle. Deleted 4 files, removed 5 methods, simplified writeAt dispatch. All tests pass; `git grep O_DIRECT internal/` returns only comments. |
| 0042-0003 | [WAL Buffer + WAL Writer Alignment](0042-0003-wal-buffer-and-writer-alignment.md) | accepted (Phase 1) | Synchronous commit: xactMarkerLogger now calls FlushUpTo(endLSN) on XactCommit. Background walwriterLoop goroutine (WalWriterDelay option, default 200ms in production). synchronous_commit/wal_writer_delay/wal_writer_flush_after GUCs. Three durability tests pass with -race. |
| 0042-0004 | [Client Backend Goroutine Alignment](0042-0004-client-backend-goroutine-alignment.md) | accepted | Documents per-connection goroutine model in server.go. Pool.OnFlushAll hook + activity.GetBackendType assertion (panics if non-checkpointer calls FlushAll). TestBackendGoroutineDoesNotFsync + TestCheckpointerFlushAllIsAllowed. All -race clean. |
| 0044-0001 | [VARCHAR B-tree Key Encoding (M0044)](0044-0001-varchar-key-encoding.md) | accepted | Self-terminating, sortable `EncodeVarchar` encoding for `varchar(N)` B-tree keys. Uses `0x01` as escape introducer: `0x00`→`[0x01,0x01]`, `0x01`→`[0x01,0x02]`, terminator `0x00`. Bytewise order matches C-locale string order. Self-terminating design makes it safe for composite keys. Extends `encodeBTreeKeyForColumn` + `isSupportedBTreeKeyType` in DDL executor. Five btree unit tests + three executor integration tests (CREATE INDEX, UNIQUE rejection, RangeScan parity). Unblocks 8 failing TPC-H varchar/char/timestamp supplementary indexes. |
| 0044-0002 | [CHAR B-tree Key Encoding (M0044)](0044-0002-char-key-encoding.md) | accepted | `EncodeChar` = trim trailing spaces + `EncodeVarchar`. Implements PostgreSQL blank-padded comparison semantics: 'A' and 'A         ' produce identical key bytes. Adds `isCharType` (char/character/bpchar) + char case in `encodeBTreeKeyForColumn`. Five unit tests (trim equality, monotone order, all-spaces edge, leading-space preservation, TPC-H shapes) + two integration tests (CREATE INDEX, UNIQUE duplicate rejection). |
| 0044-0003 | [TIMESTAMP B-tree Key Encoding (M0044)](0044-0003-timestamp-key-encoding.md) | accepted | `EncodeTimestamp(microsSince2000)` = `EncodeInt8` (identical 8-byte sign-flipped big-endian encoding). Adds `pgEpoch` + `isTimestampType` + timestamp case in `encodeBTreeKeyForColumn` (converts `KindTime` `time.Time` to microseconds via `t.Sub(pgEpoch).Microseconds()`). Five unit tests (chronological order, epoch boundary crossing, fixed-length, equality, TPC-H date shapes) + two integration tests (CREATE INDEX, RangeScan `[1995-01-01, 1995-12-31]` parity). Enables l_shipdate/o_orderdate indexes for TPC-H date-range queries. |
| 0044-0004 | [Compound Mixed-Type B-tree Indexes (M0044)](0044-0004-compound-mixed-types.md) | accepted | Verifies `encodeCompositeBTreeKey`'s no-separator concatenation produces correctly-ordered keys for all `{int4, int8, numeric, varchar, char, timestamp}` combinations. Documents the self-termination proof for each encoding. Seven btree property tests: (varchar,int4), (timestamp,numeric), (char,int8), (varchar,varchar), (timestamp,varchar,int4), varchar prefix-safety, all-types. Four executor integration tests for canonical TPC-H compound index shapes: (timestamp,numeric), (char,numeric), (varchar,numeric), 3-column (timestamp,numeric,numeric). |
| 0044-0005 | [Index-Scan Planner Integration for New Key Types (M0044)](0044-0005-index-scan-planner-integration.md) | accepted | Extends `planIndexScanFromWhere` to accept `*StringConst` (varchar/char probes) and `*TypedStringLit` (timestamp probes) as valid index probe key expressions. The storage-layer encoding functions already handle these types; only the planner eligibility switch needed extending. Four planner unit tests (varchar/char/timestamp IndexScan selection, no-index SeqScan fallback) + three executor end-to-end tests (varchar/char/timestamp index probe returns correct row). |
| 0045-0001 | [detectWritePos from Non-Zero Starting WAL Segment (M0045)](0045-0001-detect-write-pos-from-non-zero-segment.md) | accepted | Fixes crash-restart bug identified in run-007: `detectWritePos` rejected any WAL directory whose first retained segment wasn't 0, crashing clusters that had run retention. Fix: (1) drop the `if segNos[0] != 0` rejection, (2) initialise `writePos = firstSegNo*segSize` so the result is an absolute LSN, (3) change gap-detection loop to compare against `firstSegNo+i`. Four unit tests: non-zero first seg (run-007 reproducer with 0x23F), zero-first-seg regression, gap detection after non-zero start, single-segment retention case. |
| 0045-0002 | [Restart Replay of Post-Checkpoint WAL Records (M0045)](0045-0002-restart-replay-of-post-checkpoint-records.md) | accepted | Changes `ReplayRecords` to replay from the last checkpoint (inclusive) rather than up to it. Previously `replayLimit` stopped at the checkpoint, skipping post-checkpoint records whose pages might not be on disk. New `replayStart` returns the START index of the last checkpoint so `ReplayRecords` applies `records[startIdx:]`. Pre-checkpoint records are safely skipped (checkpoint flushed them). Updated two existing end-to-end tests (now expect post-checkpoint page image) + added `TestReplayRecordsPostCheckpointAfterRetention`. |
| 0045-0003 | [Checkpoint Marker Discovery without pg_control (M0045)](0045-0003-checkpoint-marker-discovery.md) | accepted | Adds `DiscoverLastCheckpointLSN(walDir, segSize)` to `recovery.go`. Reads all retained WAL segments (starting from first available, not hardcoded segment 0) and scans records for the last `RecordKindCheckpoint` entry; returns its absolute EndLSN. Also fixes `ReadAll` / `readStream` to start from the first retained segment and apply a `baseOffset = firstSegNo*segSize` to all returned LSNs so they remain absolute even after WAL retention removes segment 0. Five unit tests: checkpoint in newest seg, checkpoint in older seg with post-checkpoint data, no checkpoint error, fresh cluster, post-retention scenario. |
| 0045-0004 | [Integration Test: Kill-and-Restart Regression (M0045)](0045-0004-integration-test-kill-and-restart.md) | accepted | `TestRestartAfterRetention` in `internal/server/restart_after_retention_test.go`: Phase 1 seeds 10 000 rows with 1 MiB WAL segments + SlotAwareRetainer, forces two checkpoints so segment 0 is pruned; Phase 2 hard-kills (context cancel + runtime close, no graceful shutdown); Phase 3 restarts against the same data dir; Phase 4 asserts all 10 000 rows survive. Confirms M0045-0001/0002/0003 end-to-end. Runs in < 1 s. |
| 0039-0002 | [Range Index Scan (B-tree range predicates)](0039-0002-range-index-scan.md) | accepted | Extends the planner to emit `Filter(IndexScan{LowKey,HighKey})` for range predicates (`<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=`, `BETWEEN`) on single-column B-tree indexes. `BTree.RangeScan` updated to support nil lo/hi (unbounded). New helpers: `collectAndConjuncts`, `isConstantExpr`, `flipRangeOp`, `tryRangeIndexScan`. Key expressions may be any constant expression (including date arithmetic). Filter layer handles strict-inequality boundary rows. 4 planner tests + 3 executor integration tests. TPC-H parity identical=22. |
| 0040-0002 | [Recursive Subquery Unnest Inside IN/SubqueryExpr Inner Plans (M0040-0004)](0040-0002-recursive-subquery-unnest.md) | accepted | Adds `walkSubqueryPlansInExpr` called at end of Filter processing in `unnestSubqueriesInPlan`. Recursively applies `unnestSubqueriesInPlan` to inner plans of SubqueryExpr/InExpr nodes that could not be pulled up to the current level. Fixes Q20's lineitem scalar subquery (correlated with partsupp) which was never processed because the outer non-correlated IN blocked entry into the inner plan. Result: lineitem aggregate computed once (not per-partsupp-row). 1 new planner test. TPC-H parity identical=22. |
| 0043-0003 | [MHJ Int64 Fast-Path Hash Tables (M0043-0003)](0043-0003-mhj-int64-fastpath.md) | accepted | Adds `datumToInt64Key` + dual-mode `intHashTbls`/`hashTbls` in `multiHashJoinOp`. When all build rows for a table have int64-representable keys (KindInt or KindNumeric scale=0), uses `map[int64][]Row` — zero allocation on probe. Eliminates ~22M string allocations per Q9 SF=1 query. Also fixes double `datumKey()` in build loop and replaces `fmt.Sprintf` with `strconv.AppendInt` in `canonicalNumericKey`/`datumKey`. 10-case unit test + fast-path correctness test. TPC-H parity identical=22. |
| 0030-0007 | [pg_xact Commit Log (M0030)](0030-0007-pg-xact-commit-log.md) | accepted | Flat-byte clog at `$PGDATA/global/pg_xact` mapping each XID to Unknown/Committed/Aborted. Written at commit/abort via xactMarker hook; consulted by `loadUserTablesFromHeap` to skip rows from crashed transactions. `InitializeAsCommitted` handles upgrade from pre-clog clusters. Bootstrap XIDs 1+2 marked committed at initdb. |
| 0046-0001 | [Heap-Only Tuples (M0046)](0046-0001-hot-updates.md) | accepted | HOT-update path: when no indexed column changes, insert new tuple version on the same heap page, mark `HEAP_HOT_UPDATED` / `HEAP_ONLY_TUPLE`, skip index inserts. Index-scan `followHOTChain` traverses CTID links. WAL `RecordKindHeapHotUpdate = 13`. 5 tests. |
| 0046-0002 | [Opportunistic page pruning (M0046)](0046-0002-page-pruning.md) | accepted | `PagePruneOpt` reclaims universally-dead tuples inline when a HOT-update finds a full page. HOT chain roots become `ItemIDRedirect` (index stays valid); HOT-only and standalone dead tuples become `ItemIDUnused`. `followHOTChain` handles redirects. WAL `RecordKindHeapPruneOpt=14`. GUC `enable_opportunistic_prune`. 8 tests. |
| 0046-0003 | [Free Space Map (M0046)](0046-0003-free-space-map.md) | accepted | In-memory FSM (`storage.FSM`): `GetPageWithFreeSpace` / `RecordFreeSpace` / `RecordFreeSpaceForPage`. `writeHeapRowReturning` consults FSM before `PinNew`; VACUUM (`VacuumWithFSM`) updates FSM after each page. `vacuumOp` wires VACUUM SQL. 9 tests. |
| 0046-0004 | [Visibility Map (M0046)](0046-0004-visibility-map.md) | accepted | In-memory VM (`storage.VisibilityMap`): ALL_VISIBLE bit per page. `VacuumWithFSMAndVM` sets bits via `PageAllVisible`. `IndexOnlyScan` plan node + `indexOnlyScanOp`: key-decode fast-path (zero heap reads) when ALL_VISIBLE. `markHeapDeleteDirtyAndClearVM` clears on writes. 10 tests. |
| 0046-0005 | [Tuple freezing & anti-wraparound (M0046)](0046-0005-tuple-freezing-and-wraparound.md) | accepted | `PageFreezeOldTuples` rewrites xmin→2 for tuples < freezeBelow. `VacuumOptions.FreezeBelow` triggers per-page freeze pass. `Table.RelFrozenXID` tracks minimum unfrozen xmin. | 0047-0001 | [B-tree bulk load (M0047)](0047-0001-btree-bulk-load.md) | accepted | `BulkCreate`: sort + sequential leaf fill + bottom-up internal levels. `CREATE INDEX` calls `bulkBuildBTree` (collect→sort→`BulkCreate`) instead of looped Insert. | 0098-0002 | [WAL Group Commit (M0098)](0098-0002-wal-group-commit.md) | accepted | Replaces per-transaction fdatasync with a batched group-commit queue: FlushUpTo appends groupFlushReq to flushGroup.queue and signals via 1-buffered channel; writer goroutine drains entire queue in one flushUpTo call; all waiters notified via close(req.done). Added runtime.LockOSThread() on writer goroutine. Fixed RecordIterator.closed from bool to atomic.Bool (race exposed by LockOSThread scheduling). Expected 8–15× TPS gain for write workloads. |
| 0098-0003 | [Buffer Pool 128-Partition Locking (M0098)](0098-0003-buffer-pool-128-partition-locking.md) | accepted | Replaces single poolMu + byTag with 128 bufferPartition structs (each: mu + byTag + ioByTag + ioCond); tagPartition(BufferTag) FNV-1a hash selects partition; evictMu guards clockHand/bgwriterHand/pinCount/usageCount/dirty/valid. Pin fast path locks only one partition; eviction locks evictMu then old-partition briefly. Expected 3–6× TPS gain for Select Only at -c 100. |
| 0098-0004 | [EvalPlanQual — Row Recheck on Concurrent UPDATE (M0098)](0098-0004-eval-plan-qual.md) | accepted | Replaces immediate SQLSTATE 40001 on concurrent xmax conflict with EvalPlanQual wait+retry: epqWait() calls TxnMgr.WaitForXID + refreshes snapshot; epqRecheckVisible() re-reads tuple for visibility; up to maxEPQRetries=3 retries before 40001. tryApplyHOTUpdate conflict → (false, nil) fallback; updateOp and deleteOp conflict → retry loop with skip on invisible row. Updated TestConcurrentHOTUpdateDetectsRace for new EPQ semantics. |
| 0098-0005 | [Cross-Session Plan Cache (M0098-0005)](0098-0005-plan-cache.md) | accepted | 16-shard FNV-1a sharded plan cache (512 total entries, FIFO eviction). Key: normalizeCompatSQL(sql). Wired into simple-query (single-stmt) and extended-protocol paths; DDL invalidates all shards. planCacheIsCacheable: excludes DDL/Transaction/Copy nodes. Server.pc initialized when storage handles present. |
| 0098-0006 | [Parser Lexer Pool (M0098-0006a)](0098-0006-parser-lexer-pool.md) | accepted | Add tokenSlicePool + parserPool (sync.Pool); lexInto() variant appends into pre-allocated slice; Parse + ParseExpr reuse pooled backing arrays; token slice returned to pool after AST is built. Eliminates ~700 bytes + 2 allocs per Parse call. BenchmarkParseUpdate: 536 B/op (was ~1.7 KB). |
| 0098-0007 | [PGO + GOAMD64=v3 + Runtime Knobs (M0098)](0098-0007-pgo-goamd64-runtime-knobs.md) | accepted | Makefile build target: GOAMD64=v3 always + -pgo=./default.pgo when present. cmd/goopg/main.go: debug.SetGCPercent(200) when GOGC not set; GOMEMLIMIT logging when env var present. Expected: 3–8% TPS from ISA/inlining improvements; GC-pause reduction from GOGC=200. |
| 0099-0001 | [evictMu Pin Fast-Path Deserialization (M0099)](0099-0001-evictmu-pin-fastpath-deserialization.md) | draft | Removes global evictMu from cache-hit Pin path by making Slot.pinCount an atomic.Int32 + CAS-based victim claim in evictLocked. Cache-hit Pin requires only partition lock + atomic Add; evictMu retained only for clock-sweep cursor. Expected: 30–60% TPS gain across all workloads at -c 100. |
| 0099-0002 | [WAL Group Commit Batching Policy (M0099)](0099-0002-wal-group-commit-batching-policy.md) | draft | Adds commit_delay_us (default 1000 µs) + commit_siblings (default 5) to handleGroupFlush: sleeps briefly after the first waiter arrives to accumulate more concurrent requestors before fdatasync. Expected: 2–4× additional write TPS gain on top of M0098-0002 group-commit baseline. |
| 0099-0003 | [Deadlock-Safe Conflict Waiting (M0099)](0099-0003-deadlock-safe-conflict-waiting.md) | draft | Adds wait-for-graph (WFG) with cycle detection to epqWait: registers waitingXID→blockingXID, walks graph for cycles (64-hop bound), aborts only on confirmed deadlock; non-cycle conflicts call WaitForXID with 5s timeout. maxEPQRetries raised 3→10. Expected: abort rate 2.2%→~0.3%; 5–15% TPS gain for Standard. |
| 0099-0004 | [pgbench Client/Thread Matrix Validation (M0099)](0099-0004-pgbench-client-thread-matrix-validation.md) | draft | Defines 8-config × 3-workload measurement matrix (clients: 10/25/50/100/150/200/100/50; threads matching or swapped); pass/fail criteria for M0099-0005 (survey) and M0099-0006 (canonical validation at -c100 -j100); result archival schema for bench/pgbench-compare/results/. |
| 0100-0001 | [Isolation-Level Snapshot Semantics (M0100)](0100-0001-isolation-level-snapshot-semantics.md) | draft | Gate the per-statement `SnapshotFor` refresh in `internal/server/dispatch.go:295-300` on `tx.Isolation == ReadCommitted`. RR/Serializable hold the BEGIN-time snapshot via the already-correct `state.firstSnapshot` cache in `internal/mvcc/manager.go:197-224`. Closes the M0096-0013 "RR refreshes snapshot per statement" residual blocker. |
| 0100-0002 | [Eager XID Materialisation for ON CONFLICT Wait Propagation (M0100)](0100-0002-eager-xid-materialization-at-begin.md) | draft | INSERT/upsert paths materialise XID before the heap write so concurrent `findInProgressConflict` sees the in-progress xmin; `probeArbiterWaiting` becomes a probe-wait-rescan loop atop `WaitForXID`. Preserves M0093's read-only TPS win (no XID at BEGIN, only at first write). Closes M0096-0005. |
| 0100-0003 | [Row-Level Wait on In-Progress xmax for UPDATE/DELETE (M0100)](0100-0003-row-level-wait-on-in-progress-xmax.md) | draft | Re-enable blocking `WaitForXID` in `epqWait` (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go:78-95`) after the page-pin-release audit at every `epqWait` call site; supersedes M0098-0004/M0099-0004's snapshot-refresh-only retry which cannot emit `<waiting …>`. Fallback: session-scoped `goopg.wait_on_xmax` GUC if global re-enable regresses pgbench. |
| 0100-0004 | [EvalPlanQual Concurrent UPDATE Recheck (M0100)](0100-0004-evalplanqual-recheck.md) | draft | After M0100-0003's wait, follow the t_ctid chain to the latest tuple version, re-evaluate the UPDATE qual, re-bind SET expressions to the new row. Completes the chain-following branch that M0098-0004 explicitly deferred. ReadCommitted only; RR raises 40001 at the same site. |
| 0100-0005a | [IsolationRunner BOOL Wire-Text Reversal (M0100)](0100-0005a-isolation-runner-bool-wire-text-reversal.md) | accepted | `runResultRow` reverses lib/pq's `bool` → `"true"/"false"` decode for BOOL columns identified via `DatabaseTypeName() == "BOOL"`, restoring PostgreSQL PQprint-equivalent `t`/`f` wire-text in IsolationRunner output. Unblocks BOOL-column drift on insert-conflict-do-update-3 and any other spec selecting `boolean` columns. Server-side wire format already correct — fix is harness-only. |
| 0100-0005b | [Isolation Spec Parser: Preserve Continuation-Line Indentation (M0100)](0100-0005b-isolation-spec-continuation-indent.md) | accepted | `readBlock` no longer TrimSpaces the line that bears the closing `}` — leading whitespace on inline-brace continuation lines (e.g. `INSERT ...\n                  ON CONFLICT (i) DO UPDATE ... }` in insert-conflict-do-update-4) now survives the parser, matching upstream isolationtester's verbatim echo of step SQL into `expected/<spec>.out`. Brace-on-own-line shape unchanged. Pure parser-side slice; output-side `<waiting …>`/header-inlining/column-width parity tracked separately. |
| 0100-0005c | [IsolationRunner Step Header & `<waiting ...>` Format Parity (M0100)](0100-0005c-isolation-runner-header-and-waiting-format.md) | accepted | Push the layout decision into the parser so the runner has one verbatim format. `readBlock` prepends a leading `\n` to step SQL when the opening `{` sits at end-of-line; the runner emits `step %s: %s` and `step %s: %s <waiting ...>` (single format) — first-line inlining for inline-brace specs (insert-conflict-do-update-4), leading-newline for brace-at-EOL specs (insert-conflict-do-update-3), and the `<waiting ...>` suffix appended to the SQL's final physical line (not on its own continuation line). `flattenSQL` removed. Closes the output-side header/waiting-format residual from 0100-0005b. |
| 0100-0005d | [Isolation Spec Parser: Preserve Trailing `\n` When `}` On Own Line (M0100)](0100-0005d-isolation-spec-trailing-newline.md) | accepted | `readBlock` now appends a trailing `\n` to the body when the closing `}` sits on its own line, mirroring upstream `specscanner.l` (whose `{space}` class is `[ \t\r\f]`, so embedded newlines are kept). Step assignment no longer `TrimRight`s the body. With the trailing `\n`, the runner's `step %s: %s <waiting ...>\n` format renders `<waiting ...>` on a fresh line with a single leading space — the `merge-match-recheck.out` shape (`\tUPDATE SET ...;\n <waiting ...>\n`). Inline closing-brace specs (e.g. insert-conflict-do-update-4) are unchanged. |
| 0100-0005e | [seqScanOp page RLock per-tuple scoping (M0100)](0100-0005e-seqscan-page-rlock-per-tuple.md) | accepted | `seqScanOp.Next` scopes the page RLock to a single tuple's decode + materialise instead of whole-page iteration. Holding the RLock across yields blocked concurrent UPDATEs whenever a parent WHERE clause called a blocking UDF (e.g. `pg_advisory_lock`) — the `lock-committed-update` deadlock cycle. Row is materialised via `cloneRowOwned` before RUnlock so the yielded slot is safe to read after the page becomes writable. `releasePinned` drops the RLock acquisition. All 24 permutations of `TestPort_IsolationLockCommittedUpdate` now execute end-to-end (was: 5-minute timeout). |
| 0100-0005f | [`stampLock` preserves real updater's xmax (no MultiXact) (M0100)](0100-0005f-lockrows-preserve-real-xmax.md) | accepted | `lockRowsOp.stampLock` reads the tuple header before calling `PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly`; if `t_xmax` is non-zero, non-lock-only, and belongs to a different transaction, the page-level lock-only stamp + WAL emit are skipped (lockmgr tuple-tag lock retained). Without the guard, a session's `FOR KEY SHARE` over a row whose UPDATE was in flight would erase the updater's deletion stamp — after the updater committed, the lock-only short-circuit in `TupleVisible` reported the dead old version as visible. Closes the residual `lock-committed-update` `s1hint` 2-row diff after M0100-0005e. |
| 0100-0005g | [decodeValueSize handles serial / bigserial (M0100)](0100-0005g-decode-value-size-serial-types.md) | accepted | `decodeValueSize` in `internal/executor/codec.go` adds `serial` to the int4 arm and `bigserial` to the int8 arm so the projection-skip path matches the encoder. Without it, the size-scan path fell through to the varlen default, read the int4-encoded SERIAL bytes as a length prefix, and overshot the next column's offset — every index-maintenance scan that skipped a leading SERIAL column then decoded the next column as NULL (or as garbage), making `CREATE INDEX` on a non-leading int column reject the row. Unblocks `TestPort_IsolationDropIndexConcurrently1` global setup. |
| 0100-0005h | [EXPLAIN EXECUTE renders prepared plan (M0100)](0100-0005h-explain-execute-prepared-rewrite.md) | accepted | `internal/server/dispatch.go` substitutes the prepared statement's parsed `Query` for `ExplainStmt.Inner` when the inner is an `ExecuteStmt`, before the planner runs.  Previously the planner wrapped `ExecuteStmt` in a `Utility` node and EXPLAIN rendered `Utility *parser.ExecuteStmt` instead of the actual plan tree.  Skips the plan cache for the rewritten statement so a re-`PREPARE` of the same name cannot serve a stale plan.  26000 surfaced for unknown / unprepared names.  Unblocks every isolation spec that uses `EXPLAIN … EXECUTE` (currently `drop-index-concurrently-1`). |
| 0100-0005i | [EXPLAIN (COSTS OFF) formatter parity (M0100)](0100-0005i-explain-costs-off-formatter-parity.md) | accepted | `walkPlan` / `walkPlanAnalyze` in `internal/executor/operators_explain.go` split into `*Filtered` drivers that skip `Project` (always) and `Filter` (predicate folds to the next scan as `Filter:` detail) wrapper nodes. Adds PG-style detail lines (`Sort Key:`, `Index Cond:`, `Filter:`) via a new `emitNodeDetailLines` helper and a PG-style expression renderer `formatExprPG`. Gates `(rows=N)` on `opts.Costs` so `EXPLAIN (COSTS OFF)` renders bare labels matching upstream. Closes the bulk of the `drop-index-concurrently-1` EXPLAIN-output diff; residual diffs are now real planner gaps (constant-sort-key elimination, cost-model SeqScan-vs-IndexScan choice) tracked separately. |
| 0100-0005j | [RangeVar bare-alias column list (M0100)](0100-0005j-rangevar-bare-alias-column-list.md) | accepted | `parser.parseRangeVar` (`internal/parser/select.go`) bare-alias fall-through branch now consumes the optional `(c1, c2, ...)` column-alias list, mirroring the AS-branch. Unblocks the `merge-join` isolation spec global setup (`generate_series(1,3) g(x)`). Pinned by `TestParseRangeVarBareAliasWithColumnList` and `TestParseRangeVarBareAliasMultiColumnList`. |
| 0100-0005k | [FK violation error shape (M0100)](0100-0005k-fk-violation-error-shape.md) | accepted | `assertParentExists` (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go`) threads the referencing table through, synthesises the upstream `<table>_<col>_fkey` constraint name, and splits MESSAGE / DETAIL the way libpq exposes `PQresultErrorField`. Closes the `on table ""` empty-string bug that masked downstream `fk-snapshot.spec` diffs. Pinned by `TestFKConstraintName{AutoGenerated,NilTable}`, `TestFKValsForDetail{FormatsInts,FormatsMixedTypes}`, and `TestFKViolationMessageMatchesPGShape`. |
| 0100-0005l | [IsolationRunner: strip lib/pq `(SQLSTATE)` suffix (M0100)](0100-0005l-formatpqerror-strip-sqlstate-suffix.md) | accepted | `formatPQError` (`internal/testport/framework/isolation_runner.go`) detects `*pq.Error` and emits `pqErr.Message` directly, dropping the trailing ` (<code>)` that lib/pq v1.12.3's `(*Error).Error()` appends. Upstream PostgreSQL isolationtester prints only `PG_DIAG_MESSAGE_PRIMARY`. Affects every FK / unique-violation / partition-routing error line in the 21-spec target. Pinned by `TestFormatPQErrorStripsSQLStateSuffix` and `TestFormatPQErrorFallsBackOnNonPQ`. |
| 0100-0005m | [FK violation MESSAGE names routed leaf partition (M0100)](0100-0005m-fk-violation-partition-routed-name.md) | accepted | `insertOp.Next` (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go`) hoists `routeToPartition` above the FK check and threads the routed leaf as a separate `reportTbl` parameter into `checkFKInsert` / `assertParentExists` (`internal/executor/operators_fk.go`). MESSAGE now names the leaf (`fk_parted_pk_2`); constraint name inherits from the partitioned parent (`fk_parted_pk_a_fkey`). Resolves the L21 partition-routed name diff in `fk-snapshot.spec`. Pinned by `TestFKViolationPartitionRoutedShape`. |
| 0100-0005n | [Cross-partition UPDATE: moved-tuple EPQ error (M0100)](0100-0005n-cross-partition-update-moved-tuple-error.md) | accepted | New storage primitive `PageSetHeapTupleMovedPartition` writes the upstream sentinel `(InvalidBlockNumber, MovedPartitionsOffsetNumber=0xFFFD)` into `t_ctid` alongside the xmax stamp on the old slot of a cross-partition UPDATE. EPQ retries in `updateOp` (SeqScan + idxScan) and `deleteOp` (SeqScan) now call `epqSlotMovedToAnotherPartition` before the chain-follow; on hit they raise the PG-canonical `0A000 / tuple to be locked was already moved to another partition due to concurrent update` (`errMovedToAnotherPartition`). Pinned by `TestPageSetHeapTupleMovedPartition`, `TestIsMovedToAnotherPartitionNegatives`, `TestEPQSlotMovedToAnotherPartitionDetectsSentinel`, `TestEPQSlotMovedToAnotherPartitionRejectsPlainXmax`, `TestErrMovedToAnotherPartitionShape`. Narrows the 21-spec diff for `partition-key-update-1.spec` non-trigger permutations on `foo`. |
| 0100-0005o | [Partition-child trigger firing for UPDATE / DELETE (M0100)](0100-0005o-partition-child-trigger-firing.md) | accepted | `updateOp.Next` SeqScan and `deleteOp.Next` paths now track the source `*catalog.Table` for each pending mutation (`pendingUpdate.scanTbl` / `victim.scanTbl`) and fire BEFORE UPDATE / BEFORE DELETE triggers from that table instead of the parent. Fixes `partition-key-update-1.spec` `footrg` permutations where the trigger lives on the leaf partition (`footrg1`) and rewrites the partition key on UPDATE. Pinned by `TestPartitionChildTriggerFiresOnParentUpdate` and `TestPartitionChildTriggerFiresOnParentDelete` in `internal/server/notice_test.go`. |
| 0100-0005p | [PL/pgSQL `NEW.<col>` assignment actually mutates the trigger row (M0100)](0100-0005p-plpgsql-new-field-assignment.md) | accepted | `internal/plpgsql/parser.go::parseDottedExprStmt` now emits a real `AssignStmt{Target: "_new_<col>"}` for `NEW.<col> [:= \| =] <expr>` instead of falling through to `_plpgsql_noop`; `OLD.*` keeps its no-op semantics. `internal/executor/plpgsql_runtime.go::executePLpgSQLTriggerBody` reconstructs the returned row from the frame's `_new_<col>` slots via `rebuildNewRowFromFrame` so partition routing observes the mutation. Unblocks all `footrg` permutations of `partition-key-update-1.spec` — `func_footrg_mod_a` now actually sets `NEW.a := 2`, the cross-partition stamp fires, and the EPQ moved-partition error raises on concurrent updaters. Diff floor drops from L55 to L72 (next gap is FK-side moved-partition wiring on `foo_range_parted`). Pinned by `TestParseTriggerNewFieldAssignColonEquals`, `TestParseTriggerNewFieldAssignBareEquals`, `TestParseTriggerOldFieldAssignStaysNoop` (parser), and `TestTriggerDrivenPartitionKeyRewriteMovesRowAcrossPartitions` (end-to-end). |
| 0100-0005q | [FK INSERT check waits for in-flight xmax and surfaces cross-partition moves (M0100)](0100-0005q-fk-check-wait-moved-partition.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_fk.go` adds `scanRelForFKMatch` + `scanTableForMatchFKWait`: when the FK INSERT scan finds a matching parent row whose xmax is from an in-flight non-self transaction, it `WaitForXID`s the updater, refreshes the snapshot, and either re-scans (aborted / same-partition UPDATE) or raises `0A000 tuple to be locked was already moved to another partition due to concurrent update` when the updater committed a cross-partition move. `internal/executor/operators_storage.go::epqChainCheckMovedPartition` detects the sentinel via a two-strategy walk: (1) follow the `t_ctid` chain for up to 64 hops; (2) fall back to scanning the relation for any tuple stamped by the same xmax whose `t_ctid` carries `MovedPartitionsOffsetNumber` (handles goopg's non-HOT UPDATE path, which doesn't update the old tuple's `t_ctid`). Snapshot refresh happens BEFORE the sentinel check so `HasAborted(xid)` correctly suppresses the spurious-0A000 path on ROLLBACK. Closes the range-parted leg of `partition-key-update-1.spec` (6 permutations); `TestPort_IsolationPartitionKeyUpdate1` flips from SKIP to PASS. Pinned by `TestEpqChainCheckMovedPartitionDirectSentinel`, `TestEpqChainCheckMovedPartitionViaFallbackScan`, `TestEpqChainCheckMovedPartitionNoSentinel`, `TestEpqChainCheckMovedPartitionFallbackIgnoresUnrelatedSentinel` in `internal/executor/operators_fk_wait_test.go`. |
| 0100-0005r | [Runtime unique-constraint violation on INSERT (M0100)](0100-0005r-insert-runtime-unique-constraint.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_storage.go` adds `checkUniqueIndexesForInsert` (probes every unique/primary btree on `tbl` for matching live tuples via `RangeScan` + heap-tuple visibility classification) and `isLiveForUniqueCheck` (xmin/xmax → live? per a conservative subset of upstream's `HeapTupleSatisfiesDirty`). Wired into `insertOp.Next` BEFORE `writeHeapRowReturning` in both partition-routed and non-partitioned branches. `maintainUniqueIndexesForInsert` is unchanged so apply-worker re-applications stay skip-on-duplicate (M0103-0007 rung-1 invariant). `upsertOp` path is also unaffected because ON CONFLICT routes through `probeArbiterWaiting` first. Closes the L34/L36 `ERROR:  duplicate key value violates unique constraint "test_pkey"` line of `read-write-unique.spec` for M0100-0005's 21-test pass goal; remaining diffs in that spec are SERIALIZABLE first-read snapshot timing and SSI predicate-lock waits, both separate scope. Pinned by `TestInsertRuntimeUniqueViolationRaises23505` and `TestInsertRuntimeUniqueViolationAllowsAfterRolledBackInsert` in `internal/executor/insert_unique_constraint_test.go`. |
| 0100-0005s | [INSERT … ON CONFLICT waits for in-flight xmax on visible match (M0100)](0100-0005s-upsert-waits-inflight-xmax.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_upsert.go`: `findInProgressConflict` now also surfaces visible-being-deleted tuples (xmin settled, xmax non-lock-only in-flight non-self) — the case `_bt_check_unique` waits on in upstream. `probeArbiterWaiting` reordered to check in-progress conflicts before the visible probe so a visible-but-being-deleted match no longer short-circuits as a settled conflict. Closes the missing wait on `partition-key-update-2.spec` (full close also needs upsert partition routing, separate scope). Pinned by `TestUpsertDoNothing_WaitsForInFlightDelete` in `internal/server/upsert_waits_inflight_xmax_test.go` (asserts s2 INSERT blocks on s1's in-flight DELETE then completes correctly post-commit). |
| 0100-0005u | [`isLiveForUniqueCheck` honours self-xact deletion (M0100)](0100-0005u-isLiveForUniqueCheck-self-xact-delete.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_storage.go::isLiveForUniqueCheck` now short-circuits `xmax == ctx.Tx.XID` to "dead" before falling through to `IsXIDActive(xmax)`, mirroring `HeapTupleSatisfiesDirty`'s self-xact xmax handling. xmin arm gets a parallel self-xid → live guard. Without this, M0100-0005r's runtime unique check classified a row deleted earlier in the same transaction as a "still-live duplicate" because `IsXIDActive(self) == true`, raising 23505 on `DELETE FROM t WHERE k=K; INSERT INTO t VALUES(K);` shapes (`fk-snapshot.spec` permutations `s1brr s1dfp s1ifp1 s1c s1sfn` and the RC analogue). Pinned by `TestIsLiveForUniqueCheck_SelfXactDeleteIsDead` (`internal/executor/insert_unique_constraint_test.go`); concurrent-delete arm asserts `(xmin=committed, xmax=other-active)` still classifies as live so M0100-0005r semantics do not regress. |
| 0100-0005t | [Partition-aware INSERT … ON CONFLICT + per-leaf arbiter inheritance (M0100)](0100-0005t-upsert-partition-routing-and-leaf-arbiter.md) | accepted | Three coupled edits close `partition-key-update-2.spec`: (a) `execCreatePartitionChild` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) now copies parent PRIMARY KEY / UNIQUE B-tree indexes onto each child via `createBTreeIndex` (`<child>_pkey`, `<child>_<col>_key`); (b) `updateOp.Next` cross-partition write switches to `writeHeapRowReturning` and calls `maintainUniqueIndexesForInsert` on the destination leaf so moved tuples are findable via the leaf's PK after commit; (c) `upsertOp` (`internal/executor/operators_upsert.go`) gains `leafTrees` cache + `routeAndOpenLeaf`/`resolveLeafArbiter` so each row routes via `routeToPartition`, the leaf's matching unique/primary index is opened and swapped into `arbiterTree`, and probe/insert/maintenance run against the leaf's rel/cols/tree. Non-partitioned targets untouched. Pinned by `TestPort_IsolationPartitionKeyUpdate2` PASS and `TestUpsertPartitioned_RoutesToLeafAndProbesLeafArbiter` (three-step scenario asserting leaf-side conflict detection on both partitions and final row count of 2). |
| 0100-0005v | [Multi-line `permutation` keyword in isolation spec parser (M0100)](0100-0005v-isolation-spec-multi-line-permutation.md) | accepted | `internal/testport/framework/isolation.go` accepts the bare-keyword form `permutation\n   <tokens>` used by `insert-conflict-specconflict.spec`. Regex relaxed to `^permutation(?:\s+(.+))?$`; continuation reader splits the break condition so indented `#`-only lines are skipped instead of terminating the block. Blank-line terminator semantics preserved. Pinned by `TestParseIsolationSpecMultiLinePermutation`. Closes the parser-side block on `TestPort_IsolationInsertConflictSpecconflict` (every step previously surfaced as `unused step name`); spec now advances to the next real engine gap (CREATE FUNCTION attribute-after-body grammar — separate scope). Adjacent already-passing isolation tests unchanged. |
| 0100-0005w | [Parent DELETE waits for in-flight child INSERT; RR/Ser raises 40001 (M0100)](0100-0005w-fk-on-delete-waits-inflight-child-insert.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_fk.go` adds `detectInFlightChildInsert` (scans child + partition/inheritance children for matching rows whose xmin is in-flight, non-self, and still active per `Manager.IsXIDActive`) and `fkChildWaitForInFlightInsert` (bounded wait+retry loop: post-`WaitForXID`, RR/Serializable returns `40001 could not serialize access due to concurrent update` when the inserter committed; RC refreshes the snapshot and loops). `enforceFKOnDelete` invokes the wait at the top of each per-FK iteration, gated to skip deferred FK checks (NO ACTION INITIALLY DEFERRED) which run at COMMIT time. `internal/mvcc/manager.go` adds `HasAbortedXID(xid)` — a locked search over `m.abortedXIDs` — because RR snapshots are frozen at BEGIN and cannot reflect post-BEGIN aborts. Closes the two remaining `fk-snapshot.spec` permutations (`s2dp2` CASCADE leg + SET NULL leg) — `TestPort_IsolationFkSnapshot` flips from `defer` to PASS. Pinned by `TestFKDelete_RR_RaisesSerializationOnConcurrentChildInsert`, `TestFKDelete_RC_CompletesAfterConcurrentChildInsertCommit` (`internal/server/fk_delete_waits_inflight_child_insert_test.go`), and `TestManagerHasAbortedXID` (`internal/mvcc/has_aborted_xid_test.go`). |
| 0100-0005y | [`tableoid` system column for partition + non-partitioned scans (M0100)](0100-0005y-tableoid-system-column.md) | accepted | `rangeBinding.tableOidColIdx` plus a new `TableOidExpr` plan expression let `resolveColumnRefAt` (planner) and `resolveColumnRefTypeAt` (analyzer) recognise the `tableoid` system column. Non-partitioned base relations resolve to a constant `TableOidExpr` typed `oid`; partitioned (and inheritance) unions wrap each leaf SeqScan in a Project that adds a trailing `tableoid` column populated with the leaf's OID, so `SELECT tableoid::regclass, * FROM <partitioned> ORDER BY a` reports the actual leaf relname (e.g. `foo2`) rather than the parent. The executor's `*planner.CastExpr` arm gains an `oid::regclass` short-circuit that calls the new `(*catalog.InMemory).LookupTableByOID` accessor and returns the relname as a string. Drive-by fix: `seqScanOp.Close` no longer calls `o.pinned.RUnlock()` (the M0100-0005e per-tuple RLock scoping moved that inside `Next`), removing a `sync: RUnlock of unlocked RWMutex` fatal-panic that fired on any `LIMIT`-bounded scan. Pinned by `TestTableoidRegclass_NonPartitioned` and `TestTableoidRegclass_Partitioned` (`internal/server/tableoid_test.go`); adjacent isolation tests `LockCommittedUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoNothing`, `FkSnapshot`, `PartitionKeyUpdate{1,2,3}` unchanged (all still PASS). |
| 0100-0005-lockrows-partition-tid-stamp | [lockRowsOp per-row xmax stamp on partitioned tables (M0100 follow-up)](0100-0005-lockrows-partition-tid-stamp.md) | accepted | `lockRowsOp.drainAndStamp` skipped the per-row lock-only xmax stamp for partitioned tables because `findScanLeaf` returned nil on the `setOp` (UNION ALL of leaf SeqScans) that the planner produces for partitioned scans. Fix: (a) add `case *setOp: return v` in `findScanLeaf` — `setOp` now implements `currentTIDProvider`; (b) `setOp.currentTID()` delegates to `findScanLeaf(active_child)` (left while `!leftDone`, right once `leftDone`), reaching the correct leaf `seqScanOp`. Without the fix, `upsertOp.findInProgressConflict` Case 3 (lock-only xmax) never fired for `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on a partitioned table, causing `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` to proceed without waiting. Flips `TestPort_IsolationInsertConflictDoUpdate4` from SKIP to PASS. Pinned by `TestLockRowsStampsXmaxOnPartitionedTableLeaf` in `internal/executor/operators_lockrows_test.go`. |
| 0100-0005-heap-xmax-invalid | [Clear HeapXmaxInvalid on xmax stamp (M0100 regression fix)](0100-0005-heap-xmax-invalid-clear-on-stamp.md) | accepted | `PageSetHeapTupleXmax` and `PageSetHeapTupleMovedPartition` now also clear `HeapXmaxInvalid (0x0800)` when stamping a real xmax. Canonical-WAL inserts (`writeHeapRowReturning` with `ctx.LogCanonical != nil`) set this flag to mark "xmax is not a deleter" on fresh rows; without clearing it on the stamp path, `isConcurrentlyUpdated` short-circuits to false (skips EPQ wait) so concurrent DELETE/UPDATE on a canonical-WAL tuple completes immediately instead of blocking. Only visible on the external-cluster path; in-process server tests use `LogCanonical = nil` so `HeapXmaxInvalid` is never set. `PageSetHeapTupleLockOnly` already cleared this flag — fix makes the two write-stamp functions consistent. Restores `TestPort_IsolationPartitionKeyUpdate1` (was SKIP, now PASS) and `TestPort_IsolationPartitionKeyUpdate4` (was SKIP, now PASS) after M0106-0010 canonical-WAL insert landing. Pinned by `TestPageSetHeapTupleXmaxClearsHeapXmaxInvalid` and `TestPageSetHeapTupleMovedPartitionClearsHeapXmaxInvalid` in `internal/storage/heap_test.go`. |
| 0100-0005aa | [Cross-partition UPDATE fires BEFORE DELETE on source partition (M0100)](0100-0005aa-cross-partition-update-before-delete-trigger.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_storage.go::updateOp.Next` SeqScan branch, right after `routeToPartition` computes `isCrossPartitionMove` and before the moved-partition xmax stamp, fires the source leaf's BEFORE DELETE row triggers via `fireTriggers(ctx, pu.scanTbl, "before", "delete", pu.oldRow, nil)` — mirrors upstream `ExecCrossPartitionUpdate -> ExecDelete`. RETURN NULL suppresses the row (skip flag honored). The EPQ retry path additionally refreshes `pu.oldRow = cloneRow(baseRow)` after the SET-rebind so OLD reflects the EvalPlanQual-refetched row (partition-key-update-4.spec's "trigger is not run *before* the row is refetched" comment). `internal/plpgsql/parser.go::parseDottedExprStmt` now accepts `OLD.<field> := expr` (target `_old_<field>`), reversing the M0100-0005p no-op semantics for OLD writes — the BEFORE DELETE trigger body in partition-key-update-4 legitimately mutates OLD before reading it via embedded `INSERT INTO triglog select OLD.*`. `internal/executor/plpgsql_runtime.go::executePLpgSQLStmt` AssignStmt case now propagates `_old_<col>` / `_new_<col>` writes back to `frame.trig.OldRow` / `frame.trig.NewRow` so `substituteTriggerRefs` (called by `execPLpgSQLEmbeddedSQL`) sees the mutation. Closes `partition-key-update-4.spec` perm 2 (and perm 4 by symmetry); `TestPort_IsolationPartitionKeyUpdate4` flips from `defer` (perm-2 only diff: `(0 rows)` vs `1\|ABC update2 trigger`/`(1 row)`) to PASS. Pinned by `TestParseTriggerOldFieldAssign` (renamed from `TestParseTriggerOldFieldAssignStaysNoop`) and `TestCrossPartitionUpdateFiresBeforeDeleteOnSourcePartition` (`internal/server/notice_test.go`). Adjacent isolation tests `LockCommittedUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoNothing`, `FkSnapshot`, `PartitionKeyUpdate{1,2,3}` unchanged. |
| 0100-0005z | [Non-HOT UPDATE t_ctid link + cross-page EPQ chain follower (M0100)](0100-0005z-non-hot-update-ctid-link-and-epq-chain.md) | accepted | New `internal/storage/heap.go::PageSetHeapTupleCtid` overwrites only the `t_ctid` bytes on an existing tuple — visibility (`xmin`/`xmax`/`infomask`) is untouched. New executor helpers `epqFollowChain` (raw cross-page t_ctid walk; tail-only predicate eval like upstream `heap_get_latest_tid`) and `stampOldCtid`. SeqScan + IndexScan UPDATE paths in `internal/executor/operators_storage.go` now: (a) call `stampOldCtid(puRel, oldBlk, oldSlot, newPtr)` after every non-cross-partition `writeHeapRowReturning` so the old tuple's CTID points at the successor, and (b) fall back to `epqFollowChain` when `epqFollowHOT` returns not-found — followHOTChain requires the `HeapHotUpdated` infomask bit, which goopg's non-HOT UPDATE path never sets. The DELETE EPQ branch gets the same fallback so cross-page chain follow benefits both writers. Both UPDATE EPQ branches now thread `(blk, slot)` (previously only slot was carried, breaking as soon as the chain crossed pages). Closes `partition-key-update-4.spec` permutation 1 (was: silently skipped UPDATE → final row `foo1\|1\|ABC update2`; now: `foo2\|2\|ABC update2 update1` matching upstream). Permutations 2 and 4 (footrg trigger variants) remain `defer` — cross-partition UPDATE in goopg fires only `before update` triggers; upstream additionally fires `before delete` on the source partition (cross-partition UPDATE = DELETE+INSERT internally). That follow-up is separate scope. Pinned by `TestPageSetHeapTupleCtid`, `TestPageSetHeapTupleCtidInvalidSlot` (`internal/storage/heap_test.go`) and `TestCrossPartitionUpdate_EPQReevaluatesSetAfterConcurrentInPlace` (`internal/server/cross_partition_update_epq_test.go`); pre-fix the executor test fails with the wrong final-row diagnostic, post-fix passes. Adjacent isolation tests `LockCommittedUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoNothing`, `FkSnapshot`, `PartitionKeyUpdate{1,2,3}` unchanged (all still PASS). |
| 0100-0005x | [Upsert dirty-snapshot probe + RR/SER 40001 raise on in-flight insert commit (M0100)](0100-0005x-upsert-dirty-probe-and-rr-serialization-raise.md) | accepted | `internal/executor/operators_upsert.go`: (a) `findInProgressConflict` signature widens to also return `isInFlightInsert` so the caller can distinguish Case 1 (xmin in-flight) from Case 2 (visible-being-deleted, xmax in-flight); (b) `probeArbiterWaiting` raises `40001 could not serialize access due to concurrent update` when the waited-on xact was Case 1, our isolation is RR/SER, and the xact committed — mirrors upstream `_bt_check_unique`'s serialization break for snapshot-invisible unique conflicts; (c) `probeArbiter` switches from `mvcc.TupleVisible` to `isLiveForUniqueCheck` (the DirtySnapshot subset from M0100-0005r) so a Case 2 post-wait re-probe correctly classifies the just-deleted row as dead under RR's frozen snapshot. Closes `TestPort_IsolationPartitionKeyUpdate3` (all 8 permutations: 4 RR + 4 SER, both `s2donothing`-first and `s3donothing`-first paths). Pinned by `TestUpsertDoNothing_RR_RaisesSerializationOnInFlightInsertCommit` and `TestUpsertDoNothing_RC_DoesNotRaiseSerializationOnInFlightInsertCommit` in `internal/server/upsert_rr_inflight_insert_test.go`; adjacent `WaitsForInFlightDelete` (M0100-0005s), `LockCommittedUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoUpdate`, `InsertConflictDoNothing`, `FkSnapshot`, `PartitionKeyUpdate1`, `PartitionKeyUpdate2` unchanged (all still PASS). |

Verified: targeted tests PASS; full `./internal/initdb/` — same 15 pre-existing baseline failures as Step 3da (no new regressions); cross-package smoke `./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS. | 0106-0010 step 3dm phase A | [pg_rewrite TupleDesc fixed to PG18 canonical 8-column form (M0106-0010 step 3dm phase A)](0106-0010-step3dm-pg-rewrite-schema-fix.md) | accepted | Rewrites `internal/initdb/relcache_init.go::pgRewriteAttrs` from a 7-column drifted layout `(oid, ev_class, ev_type, ev_action, ev_owner, ev_enabled, rulename)` to the canonical PG18 8-column layout `(oid, rulename, ev_class, ev_type, ev_enabled, is_instead, ev_qual, ev_action)` from `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_rewrite.h:32-44`. Three substantive shape changes: (a) the spurious `ev_owner` slot is removed (PG18 tracks rule ownership via the owning relation's `pg_class.relowner`, not a column on pg_rewrite); (b) `is_instead bool` and `ev_qual pg_node_tree` are added (both `BKI_FORCE_NOT_NULL`); (c) `ev_action`'s storage type changes from `text` (OID 25) to `pg_node_tree` (OID 194, varlena, alignment `'i'`). The `pg_rewrite_rel_rulename_index` entry in `initdb.go:2403` already assumed the canonical column positions `indkey=[3, 2]` (ev_class at col 3, rulename at col 2) — under the prior 7-column layout that referenced slots `ev_type` and `ev_class`; without this fix any future seeded pg_rewrite tuple would have produced an index that pointed at the wrong columns. `nailedLocalRels` entry at OID 2618 bumps `relnatts` 7 → 8 in lockstep so the init-file TupleDesc agrees with the heap layout. Step 3dm phase A scopes out the actual heap-tuple seed for the view's `_RETURN` rule (extracted via psql against an upstream PG and saved to `.ralph/tmp_pg_stat_wal_receiver_ev_action.txt`, 5928 bytes) because rewriting the ev_action's RTE relid from PG-shipped 12240 to goopg's 12100 and seeding into `base/{1,5}/2618` + `pg_rewrite_rel_rulename_index` + `pg_rewrite_oid_index` is a multi-loop scope; phase A's job is purely to make the destination TupleDesc safe to write into. Pinned by 2 new tests in `pg_rewrite_schema_test.go` — `TestPgRewriteAttrsMatchesPg18FormPgRewrite` (all 8 `(name, TypeOID, Num, Len, NotNull)` tuples) and `TestNailedLocalRelsPgRewriteRelnatts8` (`RelNatts == 8` + `len(Attrs) == 8`). Verified: targeted `TestPgRewrite|TestNailedLocalRels|TestPgClassRowForView|TestPgStatWalReceiver|TestPgIndex|TestPgClassOidIndex|TestNailedIndexRelnatts ./internal/initdb/` PASS; cross-package smoke `./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS; pre-existing `TestSynchronousCommitFlushesByDefault` baseline failure (tracked as M0106-0012) reproduced under the unmodified baseline via `git stash` round-trip — no new regressions. Next blocker (Step 3dm phase B): seed the heap tuple for OID `pg_stat_wal_receiver._RETURN`. |

Two layers: (a) `pgClassRow` learns a `RelKind == 'v'` branch — `relam=0`, `relfilenode=0`, `relhasrules=true` (views have no storage per `RELKIND_HAS_STORAGE` macro in pg_class.h:200; relhasrules=true makes PG's relcache fetch the rewrite rule on view open); existing relkind='r' / 'i' byte layouts unchanged. (b) New `pgStatWalReceiverAttrs()` returns the 15 columns verbatim from `system_views.sql:945-963` with type OIDs matching `pg_proc.dat:5671` (int4=23, text=25, pg_lsn=3220, timestamptz=1184); `attnotnull=false` on every column because view columns inherit nullability from the underlying expression. One new entry `{12100, "pg_stat_wal_receiver", 2249, 'v', 15, false, pgStatWalReceiverAttrs()}` appended to `nailedLocalRels`; OID 12100 is a goopg-private stable assignment in PG18's `FirstUnpinnedObjectId..FirstNormalObjectId` range (12000..16383) — PG assigns system_views.sql view OIDs dynamically so there is no canonical OID to mirror. Cross-package smoke `./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS. Pre-existing 15 baseline failures unchanged via `git stash` round-trip — no new regressions. Step 3dl deliberately scopes out the `pg_rewrite` row carrying the `ev_action` `pg_node_tree` because canonical `nodeToString` serialization of the rewrite Query tree is itself a multi-step problem; without the rule, PG's rewriter will produce a follow-on error (`rule "_RETURN" for view ... does not exist`) or silently return zero rows, which becomes the Step 3dm blocker. |
| 0106-0010 step 3dk | [pg_proc OID 3317 OUT-args arrays (M0106-0010 step 3dk)](0106-0010-step3dk-pg-proc-3317-out-args-arrays.md) | accepted | Foundation step toward physically materialising the `pg_stat_wal_receiver` view. Three layers cooperate: (a) `internal/executor/codec.go::encodeValuePG` learns a `KindBytes` passthrough for the five array types it already handled (`aclitem[]`, `text[]`, `oid[]`, `int2[]`, `char[]`) so a caller-supplied binary `ArrayType` blob is emitted verbatim; (b) `internal/initdb/initdb.go` gains three encoders — `oidArrayBytes`, `charArrayBytes`, `textArrayBytes` — each producing a PG-canonical 1-D ArrayType with the 24-byte header (`vl_len_`, `ndim=1`, `dataoffset=0`, `elemtype`, `dim[0]=N`, `lbound[0]=1`) followed by the per-type payload (4-byte LE OIDs / packed 1-byte chars / 4-byte-aligned varlena text elements); (c) `pgProcEntry` gains optional `AllArgTypes` / `ArgModes` / `ArgNames` fields, `pgProcRow` chooses per-column between `NewStringDatum("")` (empty-array fallback, unchanged for every other entry) and `NewBytesDatum(<helper>(...))`. The OID 3317 row now carries the 15 OUT-arg columns verbatim from `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_proc.dat:5671-5673` (proallargtypes = `{int4,text,pg_lsn,int4,pg_lsn,pg_lsn,int4,timestamptz,timestamptz,pg_lsn,timestamptz,text,text,int4,text}` → `[]uint32{23,25,3220,23,3220,3220,23,1184,1184,3220,1184,25,25,23,25}`; proargmodes = `{'o' × 15}`; proargnames = `{pid,status,receive_start_lsn,…,conninfo}`). PG's `build_function_result_tupdesc_d()` will resolve `s.<col>` in the next-step (3dl) view rewrite rule via these arrays. Regression pins: 4 new tests in `internal/initdb/pg_proc_outargs_test.go` — `TestOidArrayBytesShapeMatchesPGConstructArray` (24-byte header + 3×4-byte payload, total=36), `TestCharArrayBytesShapeMatchesPGConstructArray` (header + 3 packed chars, total=27), `TestTextArrayBytesShapeMatchesPGConstructArray` (header + 3 4-byte-aligned varlenas, total=47), `TestPgProcRowStatGetWalReceiverOutArgsMatchPgProcDat` (pins the 15-element triple on `pgProcInitialEntries()[3317]`). All 4 + the prior Step 3dj pin (`TestPgProcRowStatGetWalReceiverIsSRF`) PASS. Cross-package smoke `go test -count=1 ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS. Full `./internal/initdb/` shows the same 15 pre-existing baseline failures as Step 3dj — verified identical via `git stash` round-trip; no new regressions. Next blocker (Step 3dl): seed the view itself — pg_class row (`relkind='v'`, goopg-stable OID), 15 pg_attribute rows matching `system_views.sql:945-963`, and a pg_rewrite row carrying the parser-output query tree for `SELECT … FROM pg_stat_get_wal_receiver() s WHERE s.pid IS NOT NULL`. |

| 0106-0010 step 3di | [SEGV chain eliminated; new blocker is pg_stat_wal_receiver view absence (M0106-0010 step 3di)](0106-0010-step3di-segv-chain-eliminated-pg-stat-wal-receiver-missing.md) | accepted | Diagnostic / scoping step (no production code change). Closes the SEGV attribution arc that began at Step 3da. Re-running `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` with Step 3dh's fix in place confirms the PG-against-goopg SIGSEGV chain is **gone**: the PG18 standby now executes its full startup sequence — `starting up replication slots`, `initializing for hot standby`, `completed backup recovery with redo LSN 0/4210`, `consistent recovery state reached at 0/4288`, `database system is ready to accept read-only connections`, `updating PMState from PM_RECOVERY to PM_HOT_STANDBY`, `started streaming WAL from primary at 0/0 on timeline 1`, `sending hot standby feedback xmin 0`. The new failure is at the SQL layer, not the kernel: the test's `waitForPhysicalStreamingGoopgToPG` poll calls `standby.QueryScalar(t, "SELECT status FROM pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_receiver")`, which `t.Fatalf`s on `ERROR: 42P01: relation "pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_receiver" does not exist`. Root cause: `pg_stat_wal_receiver` is created by PG's `system_views.sql` script (not a bootstrap catalog row), and goopg currently models it as a *virtual* view in `internal/initdb/replication_views.go` / `internal/wal/replmon.go`; no row exists in physical `pg_class` / `pg_attribute` / `pg_rewrite`. The underlying SRF `pg_stat_get_wal_receiver()` is also unmaterialized in `pg_proc`. No code change in 3di — the existing E2E test is the regression guard, and the Step 3dh pins (`TestNailedPgDatabaseDatnameIndexHasNameDescriptor`, `TestBootstrapPgDatabaseDatnameIndexWritesPopulatedBtree`) remain the lowest-level guard on the SEGV chain's final hop. Step 3dj scope: seed `pg_stat_get_wal_receiver` (OID 3317) as a physical pg_proc row (C-language SRF, proretset=true, with OUT-arg list matching the view columns), then seed `pg_stat_wal_receiver` as a physical pg_class row (relkind='v') with pg_attribute rows for its 16 columns and a pg_rewrite row carrying the rule action. Plausible later candidates surfaced by 3dj's re-run: `pg_stat_replication`, `pg_replication_slots`, `pg_stat_activity`. |

| 0106-0010 step 3df | [SIGSEGV backtrace: si_addr + saved x86_64 registers (M0106-0010 step 3df)](0106-0010-step3df-segv-backtrace-si-addr-and-registers.md) | accepted | Diagnostic-only step (no production code change). Closes the attribution gap exposed by Step 3de: with both candidate pointers byte-correct (leaf `IndexTuple` in `global/2676` and heap `Form_pg_authid::rolname` in `global/1260`), the surviving `btnamecmp+0x52 → namecmp → __strncmp_avx2` SIGSEGV cannot be attributed without seeing which pointer was dereferenced. Extends `tools/segv_backtrace/segv_backtrace.c` to emit `[GOOPG_SEGV_BACKTRACE] si_addr=0x<16 hex>` (the faulting address from `siginfo_t.si_addr`, always emitted — works on every architecture) plus `[GOOPG_SEGV_BACKTRACE] regs: RDI=0x… RSI=0x… RDX=0x… RAX=0x… RIP=0x… RSP=0x…` (gated by `#if defined(__x86_64__)`, pulled from `uc->uc_mcontext.gregs[REG_*]` — the SysV-AMD64 call-convention slots that identify args 1..3, return value, instruction pointer, stack pointer). New `static void hex16(uint64_t, char[16])` + `static void write_reg(const char *label, size_t, uint64_t)` keep the handler async-signal-safe — stack-resident 18-byte buffers, two `write(2)` calls per register, no `printf`/`strlen`/malloc/locale. Embedded copy `internal/testutil/pgcluster/segv_backtrace_src.txt` synced byte-for-byte; the `ensureSegvBacktraceSO` cache filename derives from `sha256(segvBacktraceSource)[:16]` so the new bytes auto-trigger a re-compile (no manual hash bump). `TestEnsureSegvBacktraceSOBuilds` extended (not replaced) with exact-match `si_addr=0x0000000000000000` (helper does `int *p=0;*p=1;` → NULL si_addr) plus label-presence asserts for `regs:` and every label in `{" RDI=0x", " RSI=0x", " RDX=0x", " RAX=0x", " RIP=0x", " RSP=0x"}`. Register values deliberately not pinned (RIP/RDI are call-site-specific across compiler/glibc builds). All 4 shim tests + `make ralph-state-guard` PASS. Next blocker (Step 3dg): re-run `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 GOOPG_TEST_SEGV_BACKTRACE=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async`, capture the new `si_addr=` and `RDI=`/`RSI=` lines from `tmp/m0106-step3df/e2e_run1.log`, and attribute the crash to either the leaf-side `NameData *` (arg1=RDI) or the scan-key side `Name *` from `MyProcPort->user_name` (arg2=RSI) — the fix at Step 3dg depends entirely on which pointer the kernel reports as bad. |

| 0106-0010 step 3dd | [SIGSEGV backtrace via LD_PRELOAD shim (M0106-0010 step 3dd)](0106-0010-step3dd-segv-backtrace-ld-preload.md) | accepted | Diagnostic-only step (no production code change). Closes the silent-SIGSEGV blind spot exposed by Step 3dc(1): after the `cache lookup failed for type 23` ERROR was eliminated, the E2E standby still crashed in a client backend with `client backend (PID N) was terminated by signal 11: Segmentation fault` and no preceding ERROR/FATAL line — PG installs no SIGSEGV handler of its own. New `tools/segv_backtrace/segv_backtrace.c` is an async-signal-safe `sigaction(SIGSEGV)` shim (constructor-installed; writes a `[GOOPG_SEGV_BACKTRACE]` header, calls `backtrace(3)` + `backtrace_symbols_fd(STDERR_FILENO)`, restores `SIG_DFL`, re-raises). `internal/testutil/pgcluster/segv_backtrace.go` embeds the same source (as `.txt` because Go rejects loose `.c` files in non-cgo packages — byte-equality pinned by `TestSegvBacktraceSourceMatchesToolsCopy`) and content-addressed-builds `libsegv_backtrace_<hash>.so` into `$TMPDIR/goopg-segv-backtrace/` on first `Cluster.Start()` when `GOOPG_TEST_SEGV_BACKTRACE=1`; build failures degrade gracefully (single-line WARNING in pg.log, no LD_PRELOAD). `cluster.go::Start` now `appendLDPreload`s the .so path into the postmaster's env only; backends are forked, not exec'd, so a single postmaster preload covers every backend. Other `exec.Command` sites (pg_ctl/psql/pgbench) are deliberately untouched. Regression pins: byte-equality between embedded and canonical copies, gate-off no-op (no LD_PRELOAD leak into production runs), full end-to-end shim verification (build the .so, exec a null-deref helper under LD_PRELOAD, assert marker AND footer on stderr), and `appendLDPreload` merge cases (absent / empty-existing / pre-populated). E2E re-run captured the SEGV backtrace at `tmp/m0106-step3dc/e2e_segv_run.log:1685-1720`: crash is `btnamecmp+0x52 → FunctionCall2Coll → _bt_compare → _bt_first → btgettuple → index_getnext_slot → systable_getnext → SearchCatCache → SearchSysCache → GetSysCacheOid → get_role_oid → hba_getauthmethod → ClientAuthentication`. Next blocker (Step 3de): seed `pg_authid_rolname_index` (OID 2676) as a populated 2-page btree over the existing bootstrap superuser (`postgres`) and the test role (`ryo`), and confirm the underlying `pg_authid` heap rows carry valid `Form_pg_authid::rolname` payloads. |

All `prorettype`/`proargtypes`/`provolatile` values sourced verbatim from `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_proc.dat` — text/name recv/send and the entire array I/O quad carry `provolatile = 's'`, everything else `'v'`; `array_in`/`array_recv` carry the canonical three-argument `(cstring|internal, oid, int4)` signature. `bootstrapPgProcOidIndex` and `bootstrapPgProcTuples` already handle arbitrary entry counts; the heap file is now multi-BlockSize on overflow and `TestBootstrapPgProcTuplesWritesRowsToBase1And5` relaxes its size check from `== BlockSize` to "non-zero multiple of BlockSize". Array types (`_char`, `_text`, `_float4`, `_oid`, `_aclitem`, `_timestamptz`, `_pg_statistic`) share the generic `array_in/out/recv/send` quad (750/751/2400/2401); `aclitem` and the three pseudo types (`table_am_handler`, `index_am_handler`, `internal`) carry 0 in typreceive/typsend — they have no binary I/O in upstream. `pgTypeRow` emits the four OIDs at columns 16–19 instead of zero; on-disk fixed-part layout unchanged. | 0106-0010 step 3cx | [pg_authid OS-user role + rolname/oid index population (M0106-0010 step 3cx)](0106-0010-step3cx-pg-authid-os-user-and-indexes.md) | accepted | Closes the FATAL `28000: role "ryo" does not exist` from `InitializeSessionUserId` (miscinit.c:802) surfaced by Step 3cw.
