Fix: `bootstrapPostgresRole` (`internal/initdb/initdb.go`) now returns `([]pgAuthidEntry, error)` where each entry carries `{OID, Rolname, TID}`; the OS user is seeded at `FirstNormalObjectId=16384` while `postgres` stays pinned at `BOOTSTRAP_SUPERUSERID=10`. New IndexTuple builder `pgBuildIndexTupleNameKey(heapBlk, heapOff, name)` in `internal/initdb/btree_index_bootstrap.go` emits a 72-byte single-NAME-column tuple (8-byte IndexTupleHeader + NAMEDATALEN=64 zero-padded NameData; already MAXALIGN'd). New `bootstrapPgAuthidIndexes(dataDir, entries)` builds both 2-page btrees (metapage + populated leaf-root) — oid-keyed via existing `pgBuildIndexTupleOidKey` for 2677, name-keyed via the new builder for 2676 — and writes to `global/<oid>` (both indexes are shared). `Init` captures `pgAuthidEntries` from `bootstrapPostgresRole` and calls `bootstrapPgAuthidIndexes` right after `bootstrapPgDatabaseOidIndex`. Regression pins (`internal/initdb/pg_authid_indexes_test.go`): `TestPgBuildIndexTupleNameKeyLayoutMatchesPG18` (byte-exact 72-byte layout with asymmetric block-number 0xDEADBEEF; t_info=0x0048; NameData zero-padded past name bytes); `TestPgBuildIndexTupleNameKeyTruncatesAtNamedataLen` (`namestrcpy` truncation semantics); `TestBootstrapPgAuthidIndexesWritesPopulatedBtrees` (both 2-page files at `global/2676` and `global/2677`; `btm_root == 1`; line-pointer counts == seeded entry count; mandatory presence of a `"ryo"` leaf in 2676 and an OID-16384 leaf in 2677 — the exact keys that triggered the FATAL). Verified: targeted tests PASS; `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3cw (no new regressions); cross-package smoke `go test -count=1 ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS. Next blocker (Step 3cy): TBD once `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` is re-run end-to-end past the `role "ryo" does not exist` line. |
| 0106-0010 step 3cw | [pg_amproc_fam_proc_index populated 2-page btree (M0106-0010 step 3cw)](0106-0010-step3cw-pg-amproc-fam-proc-index.md) | accepted | Closes the FATAL `XX000: missing support function 1 for attribute 1 of index "pg_authid_rolname_index"` surfaced by Step 3cv. Root cause: `pg_amproc_fam_proc_index` (PG18 OID 2655) was still a Step-3k empty btree placeholder (`btm_root = P_NONE`). PG's `IndexSupportInitialize` (relcache.c → indexam.c) fills `irel->rd_support` from a sysscan of this index; with zero rows it stores `InvalidOid` in every slot and FATALs the first time an index dispatch reaches its comparison function. `pg_authid_rolname_index` is the first dispatch target — opened early in `InitPostgres` for role lookups. Fix: new 4-column composite-key IndexTuple builder `pgBuildIndexTupleOidOidOidInt2Key` (24-byte tuple, layout matches PG `index_form_tuple` for no-nulls `oid_ops, oid_ops, oid_ops, int2_ops`) plus `bootstrapPgAmprocFamProcIndex(dataDir, tids)` in `internal/initdb/btree_index_bootstrap.go` that sorts `pgAmprocInitialEntries` lexicographically by (family, lefttype, righttype, num), builds the 2-page btree (metapage + populated leaf-root) via `pgBuildBtreeLeafRootPage` / `pgBuildBtreeMetapageWithRoot`, and writes to `base/{1,5}/2655 + global/2655`. `bootstrapPgAmprocTuples` widened to return `([]heapTID, error)`; `Init` captures the TIDs and calls the new bootstrap. The 36-row `pgAmprocInitialEntries` set fits in a single leaf page (~1 KiB) so no bulk-load generalisation needed; future work to generalise the 16-byte bulk-load builder to 24-byte composite keys is left for when an index exceeds ~292 entries. Regression pins (new in `internal/initdb/pg_amproc_fam_proc_index_test.go`): `TestPgBuildIndexTupleOidOidOidInt2KeyLayoutMatchesPG18` (byte-exact 24-byte layout with bi_hi/bi_lo split, t_info=0x0018, zero MAXALIGN pad — catches the Step-3s LE-uint32 trap) and `TestBootstrapPgAmprocFamProcIndexWritesPopulatedBtree` (end-to-end: 2 blocks at all three locations, `btm_root == 1`, leaf line-pointer count == 36, mandatory presence of `(1976,23,23,1)` btint4cmp + `(1994,19,19,1)` btnamecmp — the latter is the one whose absence triggered the FATAL). Verified: targeted tests PASS; `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3cv (no new regressions); cross-package smoke PASS; `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` confirms the `missing support function` FATAL is gone. Next blocker (Step 3cx): first standby FATAL is now `28000: role "ryo" does not exist` from `InitializeSessionUserId` (miscinit.c:802) — `pg_authid` is missing the OS-user role and/or `pg_authid_rolname_index` is unpopulated. |
| 0106-0010 step 3cv | [pg_shseclabel PG18 schema alignment (M0106-0010 step 3cv)](0106-0010-step3cv-pg-shseclabel-pg18-schema.md) | accepted | Closes the persistent `XX000: invalid attalign value:` (empty `%c`) PG-standby user-backend FATAL surfaced by Step 3cu. Root cause: `pgShseclabelAttrs()` declared a 6-column schema (`oid`, `classoid`, `objoid`, `objsubid`, `provider`, `label`) and `nailedSharedRels` entry for OID 3592 set `RelNatts: 6`, but PG18's `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_shseclabel.h` defines exactly 4 columns (`objoid`, `classoid`, `provider`, `label`) and `Schema_pg_shseclabel` baked into PG's `formrdesc("pg_shseclabel", Natts_pg_shseclabel=4, …)` at `RelationCacheInitializePhase2` allocates a 4-element `rd_att` array. The on-disk pg_class.relnatts=6 caused the first standby user-backend's `write_relcache_init_file(true)` to iterate 6 slots over the 4-element array, OOB-reading two garbage CompactAttribute slots into `global/pg_internal.init`; every subsequent backend's `load_relcache_init_file(true)` then parsed the garbage and FATALed at `populate_compact_attribute_internal, tupdesc.c:105` (attlen=488=sizeofRelationData, attalign=0x00, attstorage=0xa0 — the OOB read fingerprint). Diagnostic that nailed it: temporary `elog(LOG, ...)` with `backtrace_symbols` in tupdesc.c plus per-attr trace in `load_relcache_init_file` (both reverted after investigation per AGENT.md) showed `relno=19 rel_oid=3592 relnatts=6 attr[0..3] OK attr[4] attrelid=126 attlen=488 attalign=0x00`. Fix: rewrite `pgShseclabelAttrs()` to the PG18 4-column schema in exact order (objoid attnum 1, classoid 2, provider 3, label 4 — note `oid` and `objsubid` were never real columns of this catalog); change `nailedSharedRels[3592].RelNatts` 6 → 4. The companion index `pg_shseclabel_object_index` (OID 3593, attnums {1,2,3}) had a comment naming the keys as "objoid, classoid, provider" but in goopg's old attr order pointed to `oid, classoid, objoid` — silently wrong; the renumbering also fixes the index. Regression pins (new, in `internal/initdb/pg_shseclabel_pg18_schema_test.go`): `TestPgShseclabelAttrsMatchesPG18FormPgShseclabel` (strict 4-attr fixture by name/TypeOID/Num/Len/NotNull) and `TestNailedSharedRelsPgShseclabelRelnattsIsFour` (load-bearing relnatts==4 guard + SharedSecLabelRelationId/SharedSecLabelRelation_Rowtype_Id pins). Verified: `go build ./...` PASS; targeted `go test -run 'TestPgShseclabel\|TestNailedSharedRelsPgShseclabel' ./internal/initdb/` PASS (2/2 new tests); `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3cu (no new regressions); cross-package smoke `go test -count=1 ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS; `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` confirms `invalid attalign value:` FATAL is gone. Next blocker (Step 3cw): first standby FATAL is now `XX000: missing support function 1 for attribute 1 of index "pg_authid_rolname_index"` — a missing `pg_amproc` row for `name_ops` family's comparison support proc. |
| 0106-0010 step 3cu | [pg_db_role_setting nailed shared rel + index 2965 (M0106-0010 step 3cu)](0106-0010-step3cu-pg-db-role-setting-nailed-rel.md) | accepted | Closes the FATAL `XX000: could not open relation with OID 2964` that PG-standby user backends hit immediately after Step 3ct unblocked `pg_database` row decoding. OID 2964 is `pg_db_role_setting` per `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_db_role_setting.h:34` (BKI_SHARED_RELATION) and is opened by `process_settings(MyDatabaseId, GetSessionUserId())` at the tail of `InitPostgres` to apply per-database/per-role GUC defaults. Pure catalog-seed addition mirroring Step 3ch's pg_tablespace pattern; no encoder, builder, or `Init` flow change. New `pgDbRoleSettingAttrs()` returns the 3-column PG18 schema (setdatabase oid 26/4 NotNull, setrole oid 26/4 NotNull, setconfig text[] 1009/-1 NULLABLE CATALOG_VARLEN). `nailedSharedRels` gains heap entry `{2964, "pg_db_role_setting", 83, 'r', 3, true, pgDbRoleSettingAttrs()}` (RelType=83 is safe — pg_db_role_setting is not formrdesc'd) and idxSpec `{2965, "pg_db_role_setting_databaseid_rol_index"}`. `pgIndexInitialEntries` shared section gains `entry(2965, 2964, []int16{1,2}, []uint32{oidOps, oidOps}, []uint32{0,0}, true, true)` (UNIQUE PRIMARY composite per `DECLARE_UNIQUE_INDEX_PKEY` at pg_db_role_setting.h:51). `bootstrapSharedCatalogPlaceholders` heap list gains 2964 so the empty 8 KiB heap at `global/2964` exists; the shared-index placeholder loop in `bootstrapPostgresDatabase` gains 2965 (alongside 2671/2/6/7, 2694, 2695, 3593, 6246/7, 6001/2). The seed threads automatically through the existing flow: `bootstrapPgClassTuples → bootstrapPgAttributeTuples → bootstrapPgIndexTuples` (TID captured in `pgIndexTIDs[2965]`) → `bootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndex` (leaf at file 2679) → `bootstrapPgClassOidIndex` (leaf at 2662) → `bootstrapPgAttributeRelidAttnumIndex` (2 composite-key leaves at 2659). New pins `TestNailedSharedRelsContainsPgDbRoleSetting` / `TestNailedSharedRelsContainsPgDbRoleSettingDatabaseidRolIndex` / `TestPgDbRoleSettingDatabaseidRolIndexSeededFromInitialEntries` in `pg_db_role_setting_nailed_test.go`; existing `TestPgIndexInitialEntriesIndkeyMatchesPG18` map extended with `2965:{1,2}` (strict count guard); `TestBootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndexWritesPopulatedBtree::mustHave` extended with 2965. Verified: `go build ./...` PASS; targeted `go test -run 'TestPgDbRoleSetting\|TestNailedSharedRelsContainsPgDbRoleSetting\|TestPgIndexInitialEntriesIndkeyMatchesPG18\|TestBootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndex' ./internal/initdb/` PASS; `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3ct (no new regressions); cross-package smoke `go test -count=1 ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS. |
| 0106-0010 step 3ct | [pg_database heap row matches PG18 Form_pg_database (M0106-0010 step 3ct)](0106-0010-step3ct-pg-database-pg18-row-layout.md) | accepted | Closes the `TRAP: failed Assert("j > attnum"), File: "heaptuple.c", Line: 642` PG-standby user-backend abort surfaced by Step 3cs's note. Root cause: `bootstrapPostgresDatabase` wrote a 16-column pre-PG15 schema (missing `dathasloginevt` + `daticurules`; `daticulocale` not renamed to `datlocale`; `datcollate`/`datctype` typed as `name` not `text`) and never stamped `HEAP_HASVARWIDTH` in `t_infomask`. PG18 reads the row through `formrdesc`-baked `Desc_pg_database` (18 cols) — schema drift was fully invisible to local tests but produces immediate FATALs on every standby connect once the syscache lookup succeeds. The missing `HEAP_HASVARWIDTH` flag is the actual trigger: `nocachegetattr` (heaptuple.c:520) skips its var-width early-exit guard when the bit is unset, falls through to the fast path, breaks the fixed-prefix loop at the first attlen<=0 attribute, and asserts `j > attnum`. `CheckMyDatabase`'s `SysCacheGetAttr(DATABASEOID, tup, Anum_pg_database_datcollversion)` (attnum 17) is the first path that hits this. Fix: rewrite `bootstrapPostgresDatabase` to emit a PG18-canonical 18-column row sourced verbatim from `pg_database.h`, route through `executor.NullBitmapPG` + `storage.NewHeapTupleWithNulls` for the 4 trailing nullable cols (`datlocale`, `daticurules`, `datcollversion`, `datacl`), and explicitly OR `storage.HeapHasVarWidth` into `t_infomask`. `relcache_init.go::pgDatabaseAttrs` is updated in lockstep (16 → 18 cols, types corrected); `nailedSharedRels` pg_database `RelNatts` bumped 16 → 18 so goopg's internal pg_attribute heap + init-file blob agree. Regression pins (new in `internal/initdb/pg_database_pg18_schema_test.go`): `TestPgDatabaseAttrsMatchesPG18FormPgDatabase` (strict 18-attr fixture against pg_database.h) and `TestBootstrapPostgresDatabaseTupleHasVarWidthAndNullBitmap` (end-to-end pin asserting `HEAP_HASVARWIDTH | HEAP_HASNULL` and `t_natts == 18` on both template1 + postgres heap tuples). Verified: `go build ./...` PASS; targeted `go test -run 'TestPgDatabaseAttrs\|TestBootstrapPostgresDatabaseTuple' ./internal/initdb/` PASS; `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3cs (no new regressions, baseline-diff confirmed via `git stash`); cross-package smoke `go test -count=1 ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS; `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` re-run confirms the `Assert("j > attnum")` TRAP is gone. Next blocker (Step 3cu): first standby FATAL is now `FATAL: XX000: could not open relation with OID 2964` (pg_db_role_setting) raised by `process_settings(MyDatabaseId, GetSessionUserId())` immediately after `CheckMyDatabase` returns; cascading `invalid attalign value:` FATALs on follower backends are the Step 3cs catcache-stale pattern and disappear once the first FATAL is closed. |
| 0106-0010 step 3cs | [pg_database_oid_index populated btree at global/2672 (M0106-0010 step 3cs)](0106-0010-step3cs-pg-database-oid-index-populated.md) | accepted | Closes the FATAL `cache lookup failed for database 5` at `CheckMyDatabase, postinit.c:335` surfaced by `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` after Step 3cr routed `pg_database_oid_index` (OID 2672) to `global/2672` via `reltablespace=1664`. The empty btree placeholder satisfied `mdopen()` but PG's `CheckMyDatabase` probes syscache `DATABASEOID` to validate `MyDatabaseId`; with no index entries the lookup returns NULL and every backend FATALs. Fix: new `bootstrapPgDatabaseOidIndex` in `internal/initdb/btree_index_bootstrap.go` overwrites `global/2672` with a populated 2-page btree (metapage + leaf-root) carrying oid-keyed `IndexTuple`s for both `pg_database` heap rows written deterministically by `bootstrapPostgresDatabase` (template1 at TID (0,1)/oid 1; postgres at TID (0,2)/oid 5). Index lives only under `global/` (shared catalog routing). Wired into `Init` immediately after `bootstrapPostgresDatabase`. New pin `TestBootstrapPgDatabaseOidIndexWritesPopulatedBtree` in `pg_database_oid_index_test.go` asserts file size = 2 × BlockSize, leaf line-pointer count = 2, both `IndexTuple`s carry the expected ascending oid keys (1, 5) and embedded heap block 0. Verified: `go build ./...` PASS; targeted test PASS; `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3cr (no new regressions); cross-package smoke `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS; E2E re-run shows the database-5 FATAL gone (the previously-cascading `invalid attalign value` follower FATALs also disappear). Next blocker (Step 3ct): standby backends now Assert-PANIC at `TRAP: failed Assert("j > attnum"), File: "heaptuple.c", Line: 642` (`slot_deform_heap_tuple` null-bitmap loop) — likely a `t_natts` / null-bitmap mismatch in a row PG opens immediately after `CheckMyDatabase`. |
| 0106-0010 step 3cr | [pg_class.reltablespace for shared catalogs (M0106-0010 step 3cr)](0106-0010-step3cr-pg-class-reltablespace-shared.md) | accepted | Closes the FATAL `could not open file "base/5/2672"` that PG-standby user backends hit immediately after Step 3cq let them past InitPostgres. Root cause: `bootstrapPgClassTuples` wrote `reltablespace = 0` for every nailed rel — including the eight shared heaps and their indexes — but PG's `RelationInitPhysicalAddr` (relcache.c:1347-1354, with explicit comment "we do not look at relisshared here") routes file paths purely from `pg_class.reltablespace`: shared catalogs must store GLOBALTABLESPACE_OID = 1664 to resolve to `global/<relfilenode>`, otherwise `spcOid = MyDatabaseTableSpace` and the path becomes `base/<MyDatabaseId>/<relfilenode>`. `formrdesc` sets reltablespace=1664 in memory at Phase 2 (relcache.c:1948), but Phase 3 then overrides rd_rel with the on-disk pg_class row, so the on-disk value must match. Second, independent layer of the bug: `relcache_init.go::flattenRels` created each index from `idxSpec` with `IsShared = false` (struct zero value) regardless of the parent heap's flag, so shared-catalog indexes (e.g. pg_database_oid_index OID 2672) were encoded with relisshared=false too. Fix: (a) `flattenRels` now propagates `IsShared` from `heaps[0]` to each emitted `indexNailed` entry; (b) new helper `pgClassReltablespaceFor(isShared)` returns 1664 for shared and 0 for local, called from `pgClassRow` at the reltablespace Datum slot; (c) `buildPgClassBlob` writes 1664 at struct offset 92 for shared rels in the init-file encoder too, keeping the two paths in sync. New file `internal/initdb/pg_class_reltablespace_test.go` pins: `TestPgClassRowSharedReltablespaceIsGlobalTablespaceOID` (Datum value), `TestPgClassRowSharedReltablespaceInEncodedPayload` (bytes [92:96] LE uint32 == 1664 after `EncodeRowPG`), `TestFlattenRelsPropagatesIsSharedToIndexes` (every `RelKind='i'` in `nailedSharedRels` has `IsShared=true`; every such entry in `nailedLocalRels` has `IsShared=false`). Verified: `go build ./...` PASS; targeted `go test -run 'TestPgClassRowShared\|TestFlattenRelsPropagatesIsSharedToIndexes' ./internal/initdb/` PASS (3/3); `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — same 15 baseline failures as Step 3cq (no new regressions, baseline-diff confirmed via `git stash`); cross-package smoke PASS. Next blocker (Step 3cs): pursue whichever FATAL surfaces next now that `global/2672` is reachable. |
| 0106-0010 step 3cq | [pg_type heap canonical typalign — root-cause + implementation (M0106-0010 step 3cq)](0106-0010-step3cq-pg-type-heap-canonical-typalign.md) | accepted | Step 3cq proper landed 2026-05-18. Closes the FATAL `invalid attalign value:` at `populate_compact_attribute_internal, tupdesc.c:105` every PG-standby user backend hit immediately after `InitPostgres`. Root cause (diagnostic loop): PG18 `StartupXLOG` (xlog.c:5633) unconditionally calls `RelationCacheInitFileRemove()` at WAL recovery, wiping every `pg_internal.init`; backends rebuild tupledesc from heap and `TupleDescInitEntry` reads `typeForm->typalign` via SysCache TYPEOID lookup. goopg's pg_type heap was still goopg-v0 encoded via `catalog.EncodePGTypeRow`, so the `Form_pg_type *` cast yielded garbage typalign (`'\0'`). Fix: new `internal/initdb/pg_type_bootstrap.go` adds `pgTypeColDefs()` (32-col, fixed part 148 bytes mirroring PG18 `FormData_pg_type` so typalign lands at offset 128), `pgTypeCanonical(oid)` (authoritative typname/typlen/typbyval/typtype/typcategory/typalign/typstorage for 33 OIDs sourced verbatim from `pg_type.dat`), `pgTypeOIDsUsedByNailedAttrs()` (walks `nailedSharedRels + nailedLocalRels` via `pgAttrEntriesForRel`), `pgTypeInitialEntries()`, `pgTypeRow()` (NULL trailers via Step 3i null-bitmap path), and `bootstrapPgTypeTuples(dataDir)` which uses `writeMultiPageHeapRows(dataDir, "1247", cols, rows)` to overwrite `base/{1,5}/1247`. Wired into `Init` immediately after `bootstrapPgAttributeTuples` so it overwrites the v0 layout `bootstrapSystemCatalogs` wrote earlier. Regression pins (all new in `pg_type_bootstrap_test.go`): `TestPgTypeColDefsLayoutMatchesPG18` (probes int4 / OID 23 — byte 128 == 'i', byte 129 == 'p'), `TestPgTypeInitialEntriesCoverNailedAttrTypeOIDs` (strict guard — every TypeOID a nailedAttr references must have a `pgTypeCanonical` entry), `TestPgTypeRowCanonicalTypalignByte` (every entry encoded via `EncodeRowPG` has `payload[128] == e.Align`, `payload[129] == e.Storage`), `TestBootstrapPgTypeTuplesWritesCanonicalHeap` (end-to-end heap walk asserts byte 128 ∈ {c,s,i,d}, byte 129 ∈ {p,e,x,m} for every tuple at both base/1 and base/5). Verified: `go build ./...` PASS; targeted `go test -run TestPgType ./internal/initdb/` PASS (5/5 new tests); `go test -count=1 ./internal/initdb/` — 15 baseline failures (prior 14 + `TestBootstrappedPGTypeRowsReadable` which now joins `TestBootstrappedPGClassRowsReadable` / `TestBootstrappedPGAttributeRowsReadable` because goopg-v0 `catalog.DecodePGTypeRow` cannot parse the PG-canonical heap; same family as Steps 3i / 3w); cross-package smoke `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/server/ ./internal/storage/ ./internal/catalog/ ./internal/mvcc/` PASS. Next blocker (Step 3cr): one-shot `FATAL: could not open file "base/5/2672"` (pg_database_oid_index, shared) raised by the autovacuum-launcher-equivalent first backend. |

| 0106-0010 step 3ba | [multi-leaf btree leaf P_HIKEY uses firstright, not lastleft (M0106-0010 step 3ba)](0106-0010-step3ba-multi-leaf-btree-hikey-firstright.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: cache lookup failed for attribute 2 of relation 3593` PG-standby boot blocker that has been firing on every backend `InitPostgres` since Step 3az silenced the multi-leaf btree assertion. The error fires from `RelationGetIndexAttOptions → get_attoptions(3593, 2)` (relcache.c:6008 → lsyscache.c:1074) while opening `pg_shseclabel_object_index` (OID 3593). Direct page dumps confirm the underlying heap row exists at `base/{1,5}/1249` (block 0, offset 60) and `pg_attribute_relid_attnum_index` (OID 2659) has the corresponding entry at leaf 1 slot 407. But that entry sits at the **leaf boundary**: leaf 1 ends at (3593, 2) and leaf 2 starts at (3593, 3). PG's `_bt_compare` (nbtsearch.c:806–831) for a forward-direction scan key against a heapkeyspace pivot with `keysz == ntupatts && heapTid == NULL && scantid == NULL` returns 1 (strictly greater), and `_bt_moveright` then steps right to leaf 2 where the key doesn't exist. Step 3az set the P_HIKEY's source tuple to `group[len(group)-1]` (= lastleft); PG's `_bt_truncate` (nbtutils.c:3776) instead uses **firstright** — the first data tuple of the *next* leaf. Fix: `pgBuildBtreeBulkLoad` now sources the pivot from `leafGroups[li+1][0]`. With HIKEY = (3593, 3), the scankey (3593, 2) compares strictly less in the second key (2 < 3), `_bt_compare` returns -1, and the search stays on leaf 1. Pivot encoding helper (`pgBuildBtreeLeafHighKey`) is unchanged. `TestPgBuildBtreeBulkLoadTwoLeafLayoutMatchesPG18` updated to read firstright (`tuples[maxTuplesPerNonRightmostLeaf]`) instead of lastleft. Safe for every currently bulk-loaded index because they're all UNIQUE — consecutive keys are distinct, so PG's `_bt_keep_natts` returns `nkeyatts` (no heap-TID tiebreaker needed, no suffix truncation). `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances past the cache-lookup blocker; new blocker surfaces as `could not open relation with OID 2328` (= pg_db_role_setting) — out of scope, Step 3bb territory. |
| 0106-0010 step 3az | [multi-leaf btree leaf P_HIKEY pivot + P_NONE sentinel fix (M0106-0010 step 3az)](0106-0010-step3az-multi-leaf-btree-hikey-and-pnone.md) | accepted | Closes the `TRAP: failed Assert("_bt_check_natts(...)") nbtsearch.c:707` PG-standby boot blocker that has been firing on every backend startup since Step 3aw activated the multi-leaf bulk-load path for `pg_attribute_relid_attnum_index` (OID 2659). Two independent encoding bugs in `internal/initdb/btree_index_bootstrap.go` had to be fixed in lockstep: (1) `pgBuildBtreeBulkLoad` set every non-rightmost leaf's P_HIKEY to a verbatim copy of the leaf's last data tuple, but PG18 V4 heapkeyspace btrees require leaf high keys to satisfy `BTreeTupleIsPivot()` (INDEX_ALT_TID_MASK in t_info, BT_IS_POSTING clear). New helper `pgBuildBtreeLeafHighKey(dataTuple, nkeyatts)` allocates a fresh buffer, ORs INDEX_ALT_TID_MASK into t_info preserving size bits, and writes `nkeyatts` into ip_posid with zero status bits (no BT_PIVOT_HEAP_TID_ATTR). (2) The `pNone` package constant was declared `0xFFFFFFFF` with a comment conflating `P_NONE` with `InvalidBlockNumber`; PG's `#define P_NONE 0` (nbtree.h) and `#define InvalidBlockNumber 0xFFFFFFFF` (block.h) are distinct sentinels. Writing `0xFFFFFFFF` into `btpo_next` of the "rightmost" leaf made `P_RIGHTMOST(opaque)` false, so PG's `P_FIRSTDATAKEY` macro treated slot 1 as a high-key pivot — the actual non-pivot data tuple there failed the same `_bt_check_natts` heapkeyspace pivot branch. Fix: `pNone uint32 = 0`. The single-leaf fast path was unaffected because `pgBuildBtreeLeafRootPage` never wrote btpo_prev/btpo_next (zero-fill from `make([]byte, BlockSize)` was already correct P_NONE). New regression pin `TestPgBuildBtreeLeafHighKeyMatchesPGPivotEncoding` (pins helper's byte layout including no-mutation invariant); `TestPgBuildBtreeBulkLoadTwoLeafLayoutMatchesPG18` updated to demand pivot encoding instead of verbatim equality. `GOOPG_RUN_BLOCKED_M0102_E2E=1 TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances past 0 TRAPs to a new blocker: `cache lookup failed for attribute 2 of relation 3593` (`pg_shseclabel_object_index`) — out of scope, Step 3ba territory. |

| 0106-0010 step 3av | [Multi-leaf btree bulk-load builder (M0106-0010 step 3av)](0106-0010-step3av-multi-leaf-btree-bulk-load.md) | accepted | Lands the refactor scoped in step 3au so the pg_extension nailed-rel seed (415 pg_attribute_relid_attnum_index entries vs the 407 single-leaf cap) can ship in a follow-up loop with no further btree work. New `pgBuildBtreeBulkLoad(sortedTuples, nkeyatts) ([]byte, error)` returns a complete on-disk file. Fast path (≤ 407 fixed-size tuples) is byte-identical to the legacy `pgBuildBtreeMetapageWithRoot(1, 0) ‖ pgBuildBtreeLeafRootPage(tuples)` pair — pinned across 0/1/12/407 inputs by `TestPgBuildBtreeBulkLoadSingleLeafByteIdenticalToLegacy`, so existing callers (pg_opclass_oid_index, pg_class_oid_index, pg_index_indexrelid_index) are protected from regression while staying on the legacy pair. Slow path (> 407 tuples) emits PG18 nbtsort format: metapage at block 0, N BTP_LEAF pages with `btpo_prev`/`btpo_next` sibling links and P_HIKEY at slot 1 on every non-rightmost leaf (rightmost slid left per `_bt_slideleft`), root at block N+1 with `BTP_ROOT` + `btpo_level=1` + N downlinks. Leftmost downlink is the 8-byte zero-attribute "minus infinity" pivot (nbtsort.c:1001–1008); subsequent downlinks copy the corresponding leaf's first data tuple with `INDEX_ALT_TID_MASK = 0x2000` set in `t_info`, `ip_blkid` = child block (struct-order `bi_hi`/`bi_lo` halves, same trap as Step 3s heap TIDs), `ip_posid` = `nkeyatts & BT_OFFSET_MASK = 0x0FFF` per `BTreeTupleSetDownLink` / `BTreeTupleSetNAtts` (nbtree.h:563/603). Pinned by `TestPgBuildBtreeBulkLoadTwoLeafLayoutMatchesPG18` for 500 tuples (file size, every metapage field, both leaves' opaque + items + P_HIKEY copy, root opaque + both downlinks' offset/length/`ip_blkid`/`ip_posid`/`t_info`/key bytes). `bootstrapPgAttributeRelidAttnumIndex` (OID 2659) is the only caller migrated this step; the pg_extension seed (Step 3aw) can now ship as a pure catalog-seed change. |
| 0106-0010 step 3au | [pg_extension nailed-rel blocked by single-leaf btree — multi-leaf refactor scoped for Step 3av (M0106-0010 step 3au)](0106-0010-step3au-multi-leaf-btree-prereq.md) | investigation | E2E re-run after Step 3at surfaces FATAL `could not open relation with OID 3079` = `pg_extension` per `pg_extension_d.h:23`. The steady-state nailed-rel fix (8 pg_attribute rows + `nailedLocalRels` entry + `bootstrapMappedLocalCatalogHeaps` OID list + `localRelMap`) is *not* viable in isolation: adding pg_extension's 8 attribute rows pushes `bootstrapPgAttributeRelidAttnumIndex`'s populated leaf-root btree at file OID 2659 past its single-page capacity (407-tuple cap = 8KB − 24B page header − 16B btree opaque ÷ 20B per tuple+lp). Empirically confirmed: `git stash` of the pg_extension changes restores green tests; reapplying triggers `btree leaf overflow inserting tuple 407` in 20+ tests that init goopg. Step 3av must refactor `pgBuildBtreeLeafRootPage` into a multi-leaf bulk-load builder (`pgBuildBtreeBulkLoad`) producing the PG18-compatible 2-level format: metapage at block 0, leaves at blocks 1..N with sibling links (P_NONE 0xFFFFFFFF leftmost/rightmost) and P_HIKEY at slot 1 (for non-rightmost), root at block N+1 with BTP_ROOT + level=1 + N downlinks. Internal-node downlinks per nbtsort.c:563/nbtree.h:603 — `INDEX_ALT_TID_MASK` (bit 0x2000) set in t_info, `t_tid.ip_blkid = child block` (bi_hi:bi_lo encoding per Step 3s), `t_tid.ip_posid = nkeyatts & BT_OFFSET_MASK`. Leftmost downlink is "minus infinity" zero-attribute pivot (size 8 bytes). After Step 3av lands the multi-leaf builder, the pg_extension seed is a pure catalog-seed change (Step 3au-followup). |

The error is emitted from `postgres/src/backend/access/common/relation.c:61` because `RelationBuildDesc(2600) → ScanPgRelation(2600)` returned no row — goopg's `localRelMap` advertised the relfilenode mapping but no `pg_class` tuple existed for OID 2600. Fix adds pg_aggregate to `internal/initdb/relcache_init.go::nailedLocalRels` with `{2600, "pg_aggregate", 83, 'r', 22, false, pgAggregateAttrs()}`. New `pgAggregateAttrs()` returns the 22-column PG18 schema sourced verbatim from `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_aggregate_d.h` (`Anum_pg_aggregate_*` 1–22) and `pg_aggregate.h` (column type declarations); per-column `(TypeOID, Len, NotNull)` matches PG18 (regproc=24/4, oid=26/4, int2=21/2, int4=23/4, char=18/1, bool=16/1, text=25/-1; `agginitval`/`aggminitval` nullable). `RelType=83` is safe — pg_aggregate is not formrdesc'd (no `AggregateRelation_Rowtype_Id` constant exists in PG18 headers), so the Step 3v `relation->rd_att->tdtypeid == relp->reltype` assertion does not fire. The single nailedLocalRels entry threads automatically through the existing bootstrap flow: `bootstrapPgClassTuples` writes the `Form_pg_class` row, `bootstrapPgAttributeTuples` writes 22 `pg_attribute` rows, `bootstrapPgClassOidIndex` adds the leaf to `base/{1,5}/2662 + global/2662`, `bootstrapPgAttributeRelidAttnumIndex` adds 22 composite-key leaves to `2659`, and the init file gains a `Form_pg_class` blob. Complementary: new generic `bootstrapMappedLocalCatalogHeaps` in `internal/initdb/initdb.go` seeds `InitPage`-stamped empty 8-KiB heap pages at `base/{1,5}/<oid>` for every mapped local catalog that lacks a dedicated bootstrapper (~30 OIDs covering pg_aggregate through pg_db_role_setting; pg_type=1247 excluded because `bootstrapSystemCatalogs` already populates it in goopg's internal format; pg_authid=6239 excluded as shared). Only pg_aggregate=2600 is load-bearing for Step 3w; the others are forward-looking infrastructure that future steps don't have to re-add. Pinned by `TestNailedLocalRelsContainsPgAggregate` (asserts the nailedLocalRels entry's OID/Name/RelKind/RelNatts + spot-checks `Attrs[0]` matches `Anum_pg_aggregate_aggfnoid=1`+regproc) in `internal/initdb/pg_aggregate_nailed_test.go` and `TestBootstrapMappedLocalCatalogHeapsWritesEmptyHeapPages` (pins the canonical OID list and asserts each file is 8 KiB, InitPage-stamped, present under both base/1 and base/5) in `internal/initdb/pg_mapped_local_catalog_heap_test.go`. E2E `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances past the FATAL to next blocker `FATAL: could not open relation with OID 2650` = `pg_aggregate_fnoid_index` (PG18 `DECLARE_UNIQUE_INDEX_PKEY(pg_aggregate_fnoid_index, 2650, …)`), Step 3x territory. |
| 0106-0010 step 3v | [pg_shseclabel reltype must equal SharedSecLabelRelation_Rowtype_Id (M0106-0010 step 3v)](0106-0010-step3v-pg-shseclabel-reltype.md) | accepted | Closes the standby-boot PANIC loop that surfaced after Step 3u (manifested as `SELECT status FROM pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_receiver` hanging until the 300s test deadline). Walsender/walreceiver handshake actually succeeds (`started streaming WAL from primary at 0/0 on timeline 1`); the failure is every client backend tripping `TRAP: failed Assert("relation->rd_att->tdtypeid == relp->reltype"), File: "relcache.c", Line: 4293` during `RelationCacheInitializePhase3`, which terminates the whole postmaster and triggers a crash-restart loop where every new probe query simply blocks. Root cause: `nailedSharedRels` recorded `pg_shseclabel` `RelType = 4065`, but PG18's authoritative `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_shseclabel_d.h::SharedSecLabelRelation_Rowtype_Id = 4066`. When goopg's `pg_internal.init` is rejected (separate, deeper layout bug — `buildRelationDataBlob` writes `rd_id` at offset 0 vs PG18's offset 72, so PG reads `rd_isnailed` as false and the nailed-rel sanity check at relcache.c:6538 fails), Phase2 falls back to `formrdesc("pg_shseclabel", 4066, …)`. Phase3 then reads the heap row whose `reltype` column comes from `pgClassRow(rel).RelType = 4065` → Assert(4066 == 4065) PANICs. The bug was dormant through Steps 3a–3u because earlier blockers crashed each backend before Phase3's nailed-rel verification loop ran. Fix is a one-line change to `internal/initdb/relcache_init.go::nailedSharedRels` (4065 → 4066); the value flows through both `pgClassRow` and `buildPgClassBlob` automatically. Pinned by new `TestNailedRelTypesMatchPG18FormrdescConstants` (`internal/initdb/pg_nailed_reltype_test.go`) which audits every nailed shared + local catalog's `RelType` against the corresponding `*Relation_Rowtype_Id` constant in `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_*_d.h`. The init-file layout bug remains a separate carry-over: formrdesc fallback is functionally correct + PG rewrites `pg_internal.init` itself once Phase3 completes, but wastes I/O on every backend startup. |
| 0106-0010 step 3u | [pg_attribute trailing array columns must be NULL (M0106-0010 step 3u)](0106-0010-step3u-pg-attribute-null-attoptions.md) | accepted | Closes the `PANIC: ERRORDATA_STACK_SIZE exceeded` blocker that every PG-standby client backend hit immediately after `PM_HOT_STANDBY`. Root cause: `pgAttributeRow` wrote `executor.NewStringDatum("")` for `attoptions` (and three sibling array columns), which `encodeValuePG` serialised as a 1-byte non-NULL empty varlena (`0x03`). PG's `RelationGetIndexAttOptions` → `index_opclass_options` then saw "attoptions present", entered the `ereport(ERROR, errmsg("operator class %s has no options", generate_opclass_name(opclass)))` branch, and the errmsg's `generate_opclass_name` → `OpclassIsVisible` → `get_namespace_oid` → `index_open(pg_namespace_nspname_index = 2684)` → `RelationInitIndexAccessInfo` re-entered the same `index_opclass_options` path on the very index whose error message was being formatted. Five-deep recursion overflows `errordata_stack_depth` and PANICs at `elog.c:762`. Path was dormant before Step 3o populated the shared-critical-index pass that flips `criticalRelcachesBuilt`; the guard at `relcache.c:6006` had short-circuited the `get_attoptions` call. Fix: `pgAttributeRow` emits `executor.NullDatum` for `attacl`, `attoptions`, `attfdwoptions`, `attmissingval`. With `attoptions == NULL`, `index_opclass_options` returns early at indexam.c:1062 without ereport, and the recursion never starts. The Step 3i null-bitmap plumbing (`writeMultiPageHeapRows` → `NewHeapTupleWithNulls`) handles the layout shift transparently. Pinned by `TestPgAttributeRowEmitsNullForOptionalArrayColumns` (asserts cols 20–23 are NULL in every pg_attribute heap row). E2E `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances past the PANIC; PG standby reaches PM_HOT_STANDBY and stays alive. New blocker (Step 3v): the `SELECT status FROM pg_catalog.pg_stat_wal_receiver` probe hangs rather than crashing. |
| 0106-0010 step 3t | [pg_namespace nspname/oid index seeds (M0106-0010 step 3t)](0106-0010-step3t-pg-namespace-index-seeds.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: could not open relation with OID 2684` PG-standby boot blocker that surfaced after Step 3s let stored TIDs round-trip correctly. Root cause: `pg_namespace_nspname_index` (OID 2684) and `pg_namespace_oid_index` (OID 2685) had empty-placeholder relfiles seeded by Step 3k but neither was registered in `pgIndexInitialEntries()` (no `Form_pg_index` heap row, no leaf tuple in the populated `pg_index_indexrelid_index` btree at file 2679, no entry in the per-index TID map) nor in `nailedLocalRels` (no pg_class row written by `bootstrapPgClassTuples`). The first `RelationIdGetRelation(2684)` therefore ran a sysscan against `pg_class_oid_index` (OID 2662) — which after Step 3s correctly returns heap TIDs — and FATAL'd because no row exists for 2684. The fix-plan note hypothesised OID 2684 = `pg_amop_fam_strat_index`; authoritative `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_namespace.h` shows 2684 = `pg_namespace_nspname_index` (`btree(nspname name_ops)` UNIQUE) and 2685 = `pg_namespace_oid_index` (`btree(oid oid_ops)` UNIQUE PRIMARY KEY); `pg_amop_fam_strat_index` is actually OID 2653. No builder/encoder/Init flow change required — adding the two `entry(…)` calls flows TIDs through the existing plumbing. Pinned by `TestPgNamespaceIndexesSeededFromInitialEntries` (asserts `(IndRelid, IndKey, IsUnique, IsPrimary)` per OID), `TestNailedLocalRelsContainsPgNamespaceIndexes` (asserts `RelKind='i'`, `RelNatts=1`, PG18 label per OID), `TestPgIndexInitialEntriesIndkeyMatchesPG18` extended with both OIDs, and `TestBootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndexWritesPopulatedBtree::mustHave` extended. |
| 0106-0010 step 3s | [Index Tuple BlockIdData Encoding Fix (M0106-0010 step 3s)](0106-0010-step3s-index-tuple-block-id-encoding.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: could not open file "base/5/1249.1" (target block 196608): previous segment is only 6 blocks` PG-standby boot blocker that surfaced after Step 3r let `SearchSysCache1(INDEXRELID, …)` actually reach `pg_attribute_relid_attnum_index` (OID 2659) and dereference a stored TID. Root cause: `pgBuildIndexTupleOidKey` and `pgBuildIndexTupleOidInt2Key` were writing `heapBlk` as a single LE `uint32`, but PG's `ItemPointerData.ip_blkid` is the struct `(bi_hi uint16, bi_lo uint16)` where `BlockIdGetBlockNumber == (bi_hi<<16)|bi_lo`. For block 3, the bytes `[03,00,00,00]` decode as `bi_hi=3, bi_lo=0 → 196608`, exactly the FATAL block number. The bug was silent through Steps 3l/3m/3o/3p because the only TIDs ever dereferenced before Step 3r's OID correction pointed at heap block 0 (round-trips identically under either encoding). Fix replaces the `le.PutUint32(out[0:4], heapBlk)` write with two LE uint16 halves (`bi_hi at [0..2]`, `bi_lo at [2..4]`) in both encoders. Test pins `TestPgBuildIndexTupleOidKeyLayoutMatchesPG18` and `TestPgBuildIndexTupleOidInt2KeyLayoutMatchesPG18` rewritten to assert `(bi_hi<<16)|bi_lo == heapBlk` (previously pinned the bug as a single LE uint32). Four `WritesPopulatedBtree` pins (pg_opclass_oid_index, pg_class_oid_index, pg_index_indexrelid_index, pg_attribute_relid_attnum_index) decode the block via the same bi_hi/bi_lo halves. Carry-over: `internal/storage/heap.go` writes `t_ctid` with the same LE-uint32 pattern; not load-bearing for boot (only read during UPDATE chain following) but a follow-up step will harmonise it once the first physical-rep UPDATE surfaces it. E2E `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances past the 196608 FATAL to `FATAL: could not open relation with OID 2684` (`pg_amop_fam_strat_index`), Step 3t territory. |
| 0106-0010 step 3r | [Correct pg_index 2678/2679 PG18 OID assignment (M0106-0010 step 3r)](0106-0010-step3r-pg-index-2678-2679-pg18-oid-correction.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: column is not in index` PG-standby boot blocker that surfaced after Step 3q. Root cause: Step 3p (built populated 2-page btree at file OID 2678) and Step 3q (split the pg_index entry under the wrong OID labels) both got the PG18 OID assignment backwards. Authoritative `postgres/src/include/catalog/pg_index_d.h` says `IndexIndrelidIndexId=2678` (`pg_index_indrelid_index`, key indrelid, NON-UNIQUE via `DECLARE_INDEX`) and `IndexRelidIndexId=2679` (`pg_index_indexrelid_index`, key indexrelid, UNIQUE PRIMARY KEY). PG's `MAKE_SYSCACHE(INDEXRELID, pg_index_indexrelid_index, 64)` therefore traverses OID 2679. With goopg's 2679 row labelled `pg_index_indrelid_index` (`indkey={2}`) and the populated btree at file 2678, the first `SearchSysCache1(INDEXRELID, …)` falls back to a sysscan on the empty file 2679; `genam.c:446` walks `indkey={2}`, never finds the caller's `sk_attno=1` (indexrelid), and FATALs. Fix: swap the two `pgIndexInitialEntries` rows so 2678 = (`{2}`, false, false) and 2679 = (`{1}`, true, true); swap the `nailedLocalRels` labels; change `bootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndex` to write to file OID 2679 (file 2678 keeps its Step-3k empty placeholder, fine because `pg_index_indrelid_index` is never used by early syscache lookups). Tests updated in place: `TestPgIndex2678And2679AreDistinctWithCorrectFlags`, `TestNailedLocalRelsContainsPgIndexIndexrelidIndex` (now guards both OIDs and their PG18 labels), `TestPgIndexInitialEntriesIndkeyMatchesPG18`, `TestBootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndexWritesPopulatedBtree` (file-path strings 2678 → 2679). |
| 0106-0010 step 3q | [Split pg_index OIDs 2678 and 2679 (M0106-0010 step 3q)](0106-0010-step3q-pg-index-indexrelid-and-indrelid-split.md) | superseded-by-3r | Closes the `FATAL: cache lookup failed for index 2671` PG-standby boot blocker that survived Step 3p (PARTIAL). Root cause: `pgIndexInitialEntries()` (initdb.go) + `nailedLocalRels` (relcache_init.go) BOTH omitted OID 2678 (`pg_index_indexrelid_index`) itself — pg_class had no row for the index relation, pg_index had no row for `indexrelid=2678`, so Step 3p's populated 2678 btree was missing a 24th leaf entry. The single `2679` row in `pgIndexInitialEntries` carried the row content of OID 2678 (key={1}, unique+primary) under the wrong OID — PG18 ships 2678 = `pg_index_indexrelid_index` (key=indexrelid attnum 1, UNIQUE PRIMARY) and 2679 = `pg_index_indrelid_index` (key=indrelid attnum 2, UNIQUE only). Step 3q splits the row in two and adds 2678 to `nailedLocalRels` + the three empty-placeholder OID lists in `initdb.go`. No Step 3p code change required — once the 2678 heap row lands, its TID flows through the existing `pgIndexTIDs` map plumbing into the btree builder. Pinned by `TestPgIndex2678And2679AreDistinctWithCorrectFlags` (both entries present with correct IndKey/IsUnique/IsPrimary), `TestNailedLocalRelsContainsPgIndexIndexrelidIndex` (2678 present as `RelKind='i'` with `RelNatts=1`), and updates to `TestPgIndexInitialEntriesIndkeyMatchesPG18` (extended to 24 entries; 2679 corrected to {2}) + `TestBootstrapPgIndexIndexrelidIndexWritesPopulatedBtree::mustHave` slice (extended with 2678). E2E `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances past `cache lookup failed for index 2671` to the next blocker `column is not in index` (Step 3r territory). |

Bytes verified identical between the standalone `goopg init` output and the PG-standby data dir after pg_basebackup. **PARTIAL**: the FATAL persists after Step 3p lands because `pgIndexInitialEntries()` + `nailedLocalRels` BOTH omit OID 2678 itself — pg_class has no row for the index, pg_index has no row for `indexrelid=2678` — so the LOCAL critical-index pass at `relcache.c:4183` (`load_critical_index(IndexRelidIndexId=2678, IndexRelationId=2610)`) silently fails through some path that leaves the relcache partial and defeats my populated 2678 btree on the SHARED pass. Step 3q (next loop) adds OID 2678 to both lists; the Step 3p btree code requires no further change once Step 3q is in. |
| 0106-0010 step 3o | [Populate pg_attribute_relid_attnum_index btree leaf tuples (M0106-0010 step 3o)](0106-0010-step3o-pg-attribute-relid-attnum-index-tuples.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: pg_attribute catalog is missing N attribute(s) for relation OID …` PG standby-boot blocker that surfaced after Step 3n. Root cause: once the seven local critical indexes finish loading, `criticalRelcachesBuilt` flips to true and `RelationBuildTupleDesc` drives every subsequent column lookup via `systable_beginscan(AttributeRelidNumIndexId=2659, {attrelid=X, attnum>0})`. The empty btree placeholder produced by Step 3k returns zero rows, so the `if (need > 0) elog(ERROR, …)` check at the end of `RelationBuildTupleDesc` FATALs on the first shared critical index (`pg_database_datname_index` = OID 2671). Fix overwrites `base/{1,5}/2659` + `global/2659` with a 2-block btree (metapage + populated leaf-root) carrying one IndexTuple per pg_attribute heap row, keyed on the composite `(attrelid, attnum)`. Introduces goopg's first composite-key index tuple builder: `pgBuildIndexTupleOidInt2Key(heapBlk, heapOff, attrelid, attnum)` emits the 16-byte tuple PG's `index_form_tuple` produces for an `oid_ops, int2_ops` no-nulls 2-attribute key (8-byte header + 4-byte attrelid + 2-byte attnum + 2-byte MAXALIGN pad). Heap-row TIDs come from a widened `writeMultiPageHeapRows` (now returns `([]heapTID, error)`) routed through a refactored `bootstrapPgAttributeTuples` (now returns `map[pgAttrTIDKey]heapTID`); six other callers discard the TID slice with `_, err :=`. Pinned by `TestPgBuildIndexTupleOidInt2KeyLayoutMatchesPG18` (byte-exact 16-byte layout) and `TestBootstrapPgAttributeRelidAttnumIndexWritesPopulatedBtree` (file = 2 blocks at all three on-disk locations; metapage points to block 1; leaf-item count == sum of attnum>0 entries; each IndexTuple's (attrelid, attnum, TID) round-trips against the pg_attribute heap map). |
| 0106-0010 step 3n | [pg_index indkey PG18 attnum fixes (M0106-0010 step 3n)](0106-0010-step3n-pg-index-indkey-pg18-attnum-fixes.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: column is not in index` PG standby-boot blocker (every backend connection FATALed) that surfaced after Step 3m. | 0106-0010 step 3l | [Populate pg_opclass_oid_index btree leaf tuples (M0106-0010 step 3l)](0106-0010-step3l-pg-opclass-oid-index-tuples.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: could not find tuple for opclass 1986` PG standby-boot blocker that surfaced after Step 3k's empty metapage landed by overwriting `base/{1,5}/2687` + `global/2687` with a 2-block PG-conformant btree file: block 0 is a metapage with `btm_root = btm_fastroot = 1`, block 1 is a leaf-root (`BTP_LEAF|BTP_ROOT`) carrying one 16-byte `index_form_tuple`-shaped IndexTuple per `pg_opclass` row, keyed on the opclass OID and pointing at the heap-row TID (block 0, offset i+1) produced by Step 3b's `bootstrapPgOpclassTuples`. | 0106-0010 step 3k | [Nailed-index files start with PG-valid btree metapage (M0106-0010 step 3k)](0106-0010-step3k-btree-metapage-encoding.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: index "pg_opclass_oid_index" is not a btree` PG standby-boot blocker that surfaced after Step 3j. Root cause: `internal/initdb/initdb.go::makeBtreeRootPage` was emitting a `BTP_LEAF|BTP_ROOT` page with no `BTMetaPageData`, so block 0 of every nailed-index relfile failed PG's `_bt_getmeta` sanity check (`P_ISMETA(opaque) && metad->btm_magic == BTREE_MAGIC`). Function rewritten to mirror upstream `_bt_initmetapage`: writes `BTREE_MAGIC (0x053162)` + `BTREE_VERSION (4)` + `btm_root = P_NONE` + `btm_last_cleanup_num_heap_tuples = -1.0` + `btpo_flags = BTP_META`; `pd_lower` advanced past the metadata so xlog page-image compression preserves it. `btm_root = P_NONE` is the canonical "empty index" sentinel; an index scan returns zero rows, matching the behaviour of an index that has never been INSERTed into. Pinned by `TestMakeBtreeRootPageMatchesPGMetapage` (byte-level layout check incl. `BTREE_MAGIC`, `BTREE_VERSION`, `P_NONE` for both root/fastroot, `-1.0` sentinel, `BTP_META` flag, `pd_lower` offset). Next blocker surfaced (Step 3l): `FATAL: could not find tuple for opclass 1986` from `LookupOpclassInfo` — the empty btree returns zero rows, so PG can't find the `name_ops` opclass row; the fix requires populating each nailed index with real index tuples pointing at the heap. |
| 0106-0010 step 3j | [pg_class.relnatts must agree with pg_index.indnatts (M0106-0010 step 3j)](0106-0010-step3j-relnatts-indnatts-alignment.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: relnatts disagrees with indnatts for index 2662` PG standby-boot blocker that survived Step 3i. Root cause: `internal/initdb/relcache_init.go::flattenRels` hardcoded `RelNatts = 2` for every nailed index, but Step 3g's `pgIndexInitialEntries` faithfully sets `indnatts = len(IndKey)` per index — so every single-column nailed index (16 of 23, e.g. `pg_class_oid_index` keyed on `[oid]`) FATALed PG's `RelationInitIndexAccessInfo` consistency check (`postgres/src/backend/utils/cache/relcache.c:1490-1493`). Fix routes `flattenRels` through new `pgIndexNattsByOID()` (a single-source-of-truth map derived from `pgIndexInitialEntries`), so heap pg_class rows, init-file pg_class blobs, and per-index pg_attribute row counts are all driven by the same indnatts. Pinned by `TestNailedIndexRelnattsAgreesWithIndnatts` (walks every nailed index and asserts `RelNatts == indnatts` and `len(Attrs) == indnatts`) and `TestPgClassOidIndexHasSingleKeyColumn` (canary for OID 2662). Per-column type fidelity in `indexKeyAttrs` (all keys still typed as `oid`) deliberately deferred to the next E2E re-run. |
| 0106-0010 step 3i | [PG-conformant null bitmap encoding (M0106-0010 step 3i)](0106-0010-step3i-null-bitmap-encoding.md) | accepted | Closes the `FATAL: cache lookup failed for index 2662` standby-boot blocker that survived Step 3g by fixing two encoder bugs in `internal/executor/codec.go::encodeRowPG` + `internal/storage/heap.go`: (1) the null bitmap convention was inverted (goopg set bit=1 for NULL; PG's `heap_fill_tuple` sets bit=1 for NOT NULL), and (2) the bitmap was prepended to the column-data area while `t_hoff` stayed at the no-bitmap default (24) and `HEAP_HASNULL` was never stamped, so PG read the bitmap bytes themselves as the first columns of every NULL-bearing tuple. pg_index was the first bootstrapped catalog to seed NULL columns (`indexprs`/`indpred`), which is why the bug had been silent. Adds `storage.HeapTuple.Bitmap []byte`, `storage.NewHeapTupleWithNulls`, and `executor.NullBitmapPG`; `writeMultiPageHeapRows` routes NULL-bearing rows through the new constructor while keeping the no-NULL path byte-identical. Pinned by `TestNewHeapTupleWithNullsLayoutMatchesPG18` (header + bitmap location + alignment pad + roundtrip), `TestHeapTupleNullBitmapConventionMatchesPG18` (byte-level PG convention), `TestNullBitmapPGUsesPGConvention`, `TestNullBitmapPGNilWhenNoNulls`, `TestNullBitmapPGSpansTwoBytes`. E2E `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances to the next blocker (`relnatts disagrees with indnatts for index 2662`), a pg_class/pg_index consistency issue tracked as Step 3j. |

New `splitReplicationSlotOptionsBlock` peels the `(...)` block off before whitespace tokenisation (paren-aware, single-quote-aware); new `parseReplicationSlotOptions` accepts SNAPSHOT 'export'\|'use'\|'nothing', TWO_PHASE, RESERVE_WAL (PHYSICAL only), FAILOVER (LOGICAL only) as no-ops in v0 and rejects unknown options so future probe rungs surface loudly. | 0103-0014 | [Logical Walsender Keepalive + Slot RestartLSN Off-By-One (M0103-0008)](0103-0014-logical-walsender-keepalive-and-slot-restart-lsn-fix.md) | accepted | Closes rung 9 stream-stability gap. (a) `replyCreateReplicationSlot` now anchors `slot.RestartLSN` at `WrittenLSN()+1` (the next record's first byte LSN) instead of `WrittenLSN()` (last byte of previous record); without the +1, `NewRecordIterator`'s `pos = startLSN-1` lands inside the previous record and the first `readOneAt` decodes garbage as an XLogRecord header (`unknown rmid=240`). Same off-by-one M0094-0005 fixed for `startStandbyReplayer`. (b) `runLogicalWalsender` gains a 10 s keepalive goroutine via new `walsenderPgoutputAdapter.WriteKeepalive`, matching the physical-walsender path so PG's apply worker doesn't trip `wal_receiver_timeout` during quiet workloads. Pinned by `TestWalsenderPgoutputAdapterKeepalive`, `TestWalsenderPgoutputAdapterKeepaliveBeforeFirstWrite`, and `TestReplicationCreateLogicalSlotRestartLSNIsNextRecord`. With rungs 1–9 closed, the live interop test's failure mode shifts from "60 s timeout error" to "stable connection, no DML messages flow" — rung 10 (pgoutput emission for goopg-publisher DML) becomes the next target. |
| 0103-0015 | [Publication Table Canonicalization (M0103-0008)](0103-0015-publication-table-canonicalization.md) | accepted | Closes rung 10 of M0103-0008. `execCreatePublication` resolves each `parser.ObjectName` in `s.Tables` through `Catalog.LookupTable` (with a `Schema=""` → `Schema="public"` fallback matching PG's default search-path) and stores the canonical `Table.QualifiedName()` on the new publication. The previous path stored the literal user-supplied form (so `CREATE PUBLICATION p FOR TABLE t` against a `public.t` table stored `"t"`), then the walsender's `publicationFilter.byTable["public.t"]` lookup at decode time missed and every change was silently dropped — explaining "stable connection, no DML messages flow" after rung 9. Non-existent tables now raise `42P01` at DDL time instead of producing dead publication rows. Pinned by `TestCreatePublicationStoresCanonicalQualifiedName`, `TestCreatePublicationExplicitSchemaName`, and `TestCreatePublicationUnknownTableErrors` in `internal/executor/operators_ddl_pubsub_test.go`. |
| 0103-0016 | [`publication_names` Quoted-Identifier Unquoting (M0103-0008)](0103-0016-publication-names-splitidentifier.md) | accepted | Closes rung 11 of M0103-0008. libpq's logical-replication client emits `publication_names '"p"'` (each name wrapped in double-quotes to keep comma-containing names safe). goopg's `splitPublicationNames` used `strings.Split(raw, ',')` + `TrimSpace` so the lookup key became the literal `"p"` (with quotes), missing the stored publication entry — every decoded change was silently rejected by `publicationFilter.Allows` with `byTable=map[]` and `allTablesAllowed={false,false,false}`. Ported `SplitIdentifierString(rawstring, ',', ...)` semantics from `postgres/src/backend/utils/adt/varlena.c`: quoted identifiers (with `""` → `"` collapse), unquoted identifiers downcased to match `downcase_truncate_identifier`, whitespace tolerance. Pinned by `TestSplitPublicationNamesQuotedIdentifiers` in `internal/server/logicalwalsender_test.go`. With rung 11 closed, the live `TestPort_PgoutputInteropGoopgToPG` now lets Insert (kind=4) and Delete (kind=6) records flow through to the apply worker; UPDATE (`RecordKindHeapHotUpdate` kind=13) and first-INSERT-into-fresh-page (`RecordKindPageImage` kind=1) records are still dropped at the `SlotDecoder.Classify` stage — rung 12. |
| 0103-0017 | [Classify HeapUpdate / HeapHotUpdate records into ChangeUpdate (M0103-0008)](0103-0017-classify-heap-update-records.md) | accepted | Closes the UPDATE half of rung 12 of M0103-0008. `internal/wal/classifier.go::Classify` previously only switched on `RecordKindHeapInsert` / `RecordKindHeapDelete` / xact markers; UPDATE-shaped records (`RecordKindHeapHotUpdate` kind=13 from the in-place HOT path, `RecordKindHeapUpdate` kind=27 from the non-HOT path) fell into the default branch and were silently dropped, so PG subscribers saw zero `'U'` messages from a goopg publisher. The classifier now decodes both via the existing `DecodeHeapHotUpdate` / `DecodeHeapUpdate` helpers, extracts the updating xact's xid from the new tuple's xmin (same `xminFromTuple` helper HeapInsert uses), and dispatches a `ChangeUpdate{NewTuple: tupleBytes}`. `OldTuple` stays empty — neither record shape carries the pre-image; pgoutput's `writeUpdate` already handles the no-old-tuple case (rung 9 fix) by emitting `'U' relOid 'N' newTuple` directly, byte-identical to upstream's `logicalrep_write_update` under REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT. Pinned by `TestClassifyHeapHotUpdateRoutesByXmin` and `TestClassifyHeapUpdateRoutesByXmin` in `internal/wal/classifier_test.go`. PageImage synthesis for fresh-page inserts is the next rung's scope. |
| 0103-0018 | [Heap FPI and Logical-Record Coexistence (M0103-0008)](0103-0018-heap-fpi-and-logical-record-coexistence.md) | accepted | Closes the PageImage half of rung 12 of M0103-0008. `bufpool.MarkDirtyChangeRecord`'s "FPI alone replaces the change record" optimisation on first-dirty-in-epoch silently drops the per-row logical event — fine for redo, fatal for logical replication (PG subscribers receive zero rows from the most common shape, one INSERT into a fresh table). New method `Pool.MarkDirtyLogicalChange(s, emitter)` always runs the logical `emitter` and additionally emits the FPI on first-dirty-in-epoch, in WAL order: logical first (LSN_log) then PageImage (LSN_fpi > LSN_log). The order matches per-record replay idempotency: `replayHeapInsert` applies cleanly against the prior epoch's state at LSN_log, then `replayPageImage` overwrites with the post-mutation bytes at LSN_fpi (FPI bytes carry pd_lsn=LSN_log, so byte-identity holds). `markHeapInsertDirty`, `markHeapDeleteDirty`, and `markHeapHotUpdateDirty` re-routed from `MarkDirtyChangeRecord` to `MarkDirtyLogicalChange`. B-tree split / metapage / heap-prune-opt / heap-lock / vacuum keep `MarkDirtyChangeRecord` (their `emitter` IS the FPI; double-emit would just waste WAL). Pinned by `TestMarkDirtyLogicalChangeEmitsLogicalAndFPIOnFirstDirty`, `TestMarkDirtyLogicalChangeEmitsLogicalOnlyOnSecondDirty`, `TestMarkDirtyLogicalChangeWithoutFPIHookEmitsLogicalOnly`, `TestMarkDirtyLogicalChangeRequiresEmitter` in `internal/storage/storage_test.go`, plus `TestClassifyHeapInsertAfterPageImageStillEmitsChange` in `internal/wal/classifier_test.go`. |
| 0103-0019 | [LATERAL `pg_catalog`-qualified SRF parser dispatch (M0103-0008)](0103-0019-lateral-pg-catalog-qualified-srf.md) | accepted | Closes rung 13 of M0103-0008. PG's `CREATE SUBSCRIPTION` runs `fetch_table_list_from_publisher` whose probe shape contains `LATERAL pg_catalog.pg_get_publication_tables(t.pubname) AS gpt`. goopg's `parseRangeVar` only recognised TVF FROM items when the function name was unqualified (`obj.Schema == ""` gate), so the `pg_catalog.`-qualified spelling fell into the derived-subquery branch and choked with `expected ')' after subquery in FROM (got ()` at the LATERAL function's opening paren. CREATE SUBSCRIPTION therefore registered ZERO tables in `pg_subscription_rel`; the apply worker silently skipped every Insert/Update/Delete via `should_apply_changes_for_rel` → false. Net symptom: 'w' frames flow, CONTEXT lines appear in PG's apply-worker debug log, but `pg_replication_origin_status.remote_lsn` stays at 0/0 and subscriber `count(*)` stays at 0. Fix: the SRF dispatch gate now accepts `obj.Schema == ""` OR `strings.EqualFold(obj.Schema, "pg_catalog")` for `generate_series`/`pg_input_error_info`/`parse_ident`/`pg_get_publication_tables`. Pinned by `TestParseLateralPgCatalogQualifiedSRF` + `TestParseLateralPgCatalogQualifiedSRFCaseInsensitive` in `internal/parser/select_test.go`. Live diagnostic run with `t.Skip` removed (rolled back) confirms the failure mode shifted observably — the next rung is `pg_class.relnatts` column missing (42703) which CREATE SUBSCRIPTION's fetch query references inside the CASE expression. |
| 0103-0021 | [`pg_get_publication_tables.relid` matches `pg_class.oid` (M0103-0008)](0103-0021-pg-get-publication-tables-relid-matches-pg-class-oid.md) | accepted | Closes rung 15 of M0103-0008. After rung 14, CREATE SUBSCRIPTION's PG18 `fetch_table_list` query (`SELECT DISTINCT n.nspname, c.relname, gpt.attrs FROM pg_class c JOIN pg_namespace n ON n.oid = c.relnamespace JOIN ( SELECT (pg_get_publication_tables(VARIADIC array_agg(pubname::text))).* FROM pg_publication WHERE pubname IN ( 'p' )) AS gpt ON gpt.relid = c.oid`) parsed and executed but returned ZERO rows: goopg's virtual `pg_catalog.pg_class.oid` stores the relation NAME as text (the v0 "regclass cast is a no-op" pgbench convention, `catalog.go:707-712`), while `buildPgGetPublicationTablesRows` emitted `relid` as `NewIntDatum(int64(t.OID))`. `compareDatum(KindInt, KindString)` falls back to `strings.Compare(a.Format(), b.Format())`, so the join evaluated `"16384" = "t"` and never matched; `pg_subscription_rel` stayed empty, tablesync never launched, and the apply worker silently skipped every change despite acking all four `'w'` frames (`recv=0/146`). Fix: emit `relid` as `NewStringDatum(t.Name)` so the SRF aligns with the v0 catalog convention. Pinned by `TestPgGetPublicationTablesRelidMatchesPgClassOid` in `internal/executor/operators_pg_get_publication_tables_test.go`. Next rung is tablesync's `fetch_remote_table_info` first sub-query, which expects `pg_class.oid` to wire-decode as a numeric OID via `DatumGetObjectId`. |
| 0103-0020 | [`pg_class.relnatts` Column for CREATE SUBSCRIPTION Column-List Probe (M0103-0008)](0103-0020-pg-class-relnatts-column.md) | accepted | Closes rung 14 of M0103-0008. With rung 13 parsing fixed, `fetch_table_list_from_publisher` reached the executor and raised `42703: column "relnatts" does not exist` on the `(array_length(gpt.attrs,1) = c.relnatts)` test. The on-disk pg_class codec (`internal/catalog/codec.go::PgClassRow.RelNAtts`) already modelled the column, but the virtual `pg_catalog.pg_class` view in `internal/catalog/catalog.go::registerSystemTables` was a separate construct and only exposed eight columns (`oid`, `relname`, `relkind`, `relnamespace`, `relpersistence`, `reltoastrelid`, `relpages`, `relispopulated`). Added a 9th column `relnatts int4` at ordinal 8, populated with `strconv.Itoa(len(t.Columns))` (goopg has no system columns in its catalog, so user-column count is what PG would report). Pinned by `TestPgClassExposesRelNatts` in `internal/catalog/catalog_test.go`. |
| 0103-0023 | [M0103-0008 Scenario B closure — goopg primary + PG subscriber E2E](0103-0023-m0103-0008-scenario-b-closure.md) | accepted | Closes M0103-0008. After rung 16 landed the `pg_class.oid` numeric flip and `relreplident` column, the live `t.Skip` lift on `TestPort_PgoutputInteropGoopgToPG` produced a fully passing end-to-end Scenario-B run on first try (5/5 consecutive runs at 1.6–1.8 s each). The libpqrcv ladder completes cleanly: `fetch_table_list` returns `public.t`, `fetch_remote_table_info` returns `relreplident='d'` and numeric OID, the column-types LATERAL probe over `pg_attribute` / `pg_get_replica_identity_index` resolves (the latter returns 0=InvalidOid so the LEFT JOIN matches no `pg_index` row — functionally equivalent to upstream's REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT). Apply worker starts, `pg_subscription_rel` populates, INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE replicate within ~10 ms to the PG subscriber, and `pg_replication_origin_status.remote_lsn` advances. No new fix was needed in this loop — rung 16's catalog flip was the keystone; rung 17 (hypothesised pg_attribute probe) was already satisfied by goopg's existing pg_attribute view + the `pg_get_replica_identity_index → 0` stub. Only change: dropped the `t.Skip` and replaced the rung-17-OPEN comment with a rung-17-CLOSED note. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropGoopgToPG` running to its full final-state assertion (`id=2 v='updated'`, `id=1` deleted). |
| 0103-0022 | [`pg_class.oid` numeric + `relreplident` column (M0103-0008)](0103-0022-pg-class-oid-numeric-and-relreplident.md) | accepted | Closes rung 16 of M0103-0008. After rung 15 closed, `TestPort_PgoutputInteropGoopgToPG` advanced past `fetch_table_list` but `pg_subscription_rel` stayed empty: tablesync's `fetch_remote_table_info` first probe (`SELECT c.oid, c.relreplident, c.relkind FROM pg_class c …`) failed with `42703: column "relreplident" does not exist`, and even after adding the column would have failed the next way around — goopg emitted `pg_class.oid` as the relation-name text, so `lrel->remoteid = DatumGetObjectId("t")` parsed as uint32 → 0 and the column-list `WHERE gpt.relid = 0` matched nothing. Fix: flip `pg_class.oid` from text-name to numeric OID (decimal text under wire type 26), flip `pg_get_publication_tables.relid` to KindInt, add `relreplident char` column populated with `'d'` (REPLICA_IDENTITY_DEFAULT), and make `::regclass` cast catalog-aware so legacy text-name pgbench shapes resolve to an OID instead of breaking. The internal join `gpt.relid = c.oid` stays valid via `compareDatum`'s Format()-fallback — both sides format to the same decimal text. Pinned by `TestPgClassExposesRelReplident` + `TestPgClassOidIsNumericOID` (`internal/catalog/catalog_test.go`) and the updated `TestPgGetPublicationTablesRelidMatchesPgClassOid` (`internal/executor/operators_pg_get_publication_tables_test.go`). |
| 0103-0032 | [M0103-0007 rung 9 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber TRUNCATE replication](0103-0032-m0103-0007-rung-9-pg-to-goopg-truncate.md) | accepted | Closes rung 9 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–8 covered every INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE shape across pgoutput proto_version=1 but never the `'T'` (TRUNCATE) frame; the apply worker dispatched only on B/R/I/D/U/C and fell through to the `default` arm of `ApplyMessage` for `'T'`, returning `"applyworker: unsupported pgoutput kind \"T\""` and stalling the slot. Rung 9 closes the gap end-to-end: decoder gains a `case pgoTruncate` arm in `internal/wal/pgoutput_decoder.go::DecodeMessage` and two new `DecodedMessage` fields (`TruncateRels []uint32`, `TruncateOption byte`); `ApplyWorker.ApplyMessage` gains a `case 'T'` branch routing to `applyTruncate`, which walks the relid list, resolves each via the existing relation cache, and calls the existing `truncateRelation(ctx, rel)` primitive to stamp `xmax = currentTx.XID` on every visible tuple — transactional with the surrounding apply xact, symmetric with `applyDeleteByKey`. Soft-truncate semantics (no physical file shrink) are intentional: `Pool.Manager().TruncateRelation(rel)` is non-transactional and would diverge subscriber durability from publisher xact rollback. Both pgoutput option bits (`TRUNCATE_CASCADE`/`TRUNCATE_RESTART_SEQS`) are publisher-side decisions — CASCADE expansion already populates the relid list before the publisher emits `'T'`, RESTART IDENTITY is a no-op on goopg's apply path which has no replicated sequence state — but the byte is recorded for future rungs. Unknown-relid rejection mirrors `applyDelete`/`applyUpdate` (catalog drift surfaces with `event=apply_error`, `kind=T`). Pinned by: decoder unit `TestPgoutputDecoderTruncateMessage` (4 sub-tests), apply-worker units `TestApplyWorkerTruncate` + `TestApplyWorkerTruncateUnknownRelOid`, live E2E `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgTruncate` (publisher INSERT×3 → TRUNCATE → INSERT×2; subscriber must show only the two post-truncate rows). Verification: live E2E PASS (~2.0 s); all 9 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~15.4 s); race-tested regression on `executor/wal/catalog/pubsubcluster` → green. pgbench against PG publisher with `pgbench_history` polling, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, and `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect remain future rungs. |
| 0103-0031 | [M0103-0007 rung 8 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber multi-table interleaved DML](0103-0031-m0103-0007-rung-8-pg-to-goopg-multi-table.md) | accepted | Closes rung 8 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–7 all exercised a single published table `public.t (id int PRIMARY KEY, v text)`; the apply worker's relation cache (`internal/executor/applyworker.go`) only ever held one entry, the relOid was constant for the slot's lifetime, and the per-relation dispatch path was untested at that level. Rung 8 publishes two tables with deliberately different column shapes — `public.users` (2 cols: `id int PK, name text`) and `public.orders` (3 cols: `id int PK, user_id int, amount int`) — and interleaves INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE against both inside one top-level transaction plus a follow-up autocommit phase. The load-bearing property pinned is the **multi-relation dispatch contract**: the apply worker's relation cache must keep both `R` messages live across the `B…C` block, every change must route to the matching `*catalog.Table` on the subscriber, and per-table primary-key index maintenance (`maintainUniqueIndexesForInsert`) must run against the right table so subsequent fresh-session PK IndexScans find each row. Column-index drift between the wire tuple and the subscriber's `catalog.Table.Columns` would surface either as a parse error (text into int4 at the same ordinal across tables) or as the per-row identity assertions failing. Expected final state on the subscriber: `count(users)=2` with `id=1 name='alice-updated'` + `id=3 name='carol'`; `count(orders)=3` with `id=10 user_id=1 amount=99` + `id=12 user_id=1 amount=50` + `id=13 user_id=3 amount=75`. Per-table count assertion catches cross-relation dispatch leaks; per-id identity assertions catch UPDATE-routing bugs. No code change needed — rungs 1–7's machinery already covered both `applyRelation` map keying and per-table `*catalog.Table` resolution. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgMultiTable` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`). Verification: targeted test → PASS (~1.7 s); all 8 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~13.4 s); race-tested regression on `executor/wal/server/catalog/pubsubcluster` → green. pgbench against PG publisher with `pgbench_history` polling, DDL replication shapes, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, and `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect remain future rungs. |
| 0103-0030 | [M0103-0007 rung 7 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber SAVEPOINT subxacts](0103-0030-m0103-0007-rung-7-pg-to-goopg-savepoint-xact.md) | accepted | Closes rung 7 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–6 covered single-statement autocommit (1–5) and explicit multi-DML single-xact (6); this rung scales another dimension — **subtransactions**. At `proto_version=1` (goopg's default, set in `internal/server/logicalreceiver.go:149-151`) subxact boundaries are NOT streamed: the publisher's reorder buffer filters rolled-back subxact rows before emission, and the wire carries only the committed net effect of the top-level transaction as one `B…C` block. This rung pins the contract end-to-end with a SAVEPOINT-heavy workload (`SAVEPOINT s1 → INSERT + UPDATE → ROLLBACK TO s1`; `SAVEPOINT s2 → INSERT → RELEASE`; top-level INSERT; `SAVEPOINT s3 → DELETE → ROLLBACK TO s3`). Expected final state: `count(*)=3`, `id=1 v='one'` (s1 UPDATE rolled back), no `id=2` (s1 INSERT rolled back), `id=3 v='three'` (s2 RELEASE merged + s3 DELETE rolled back), `id=4 v='four'` (top-level INSERT). Each assertion fail-fasts on a distinct potential regression (publisher leaks rolled-back inserts → count=4/5; UPDATE leaks through → wrong `v`; ROLLBACK TO of DELETE fails → no `id=3` row). No code changes needed — the publisher does the work at buffer-flush time and the apply worker's existing one-TxnMgr-per-`B…C` machinery handles the resulting block exactly as rung 6 did. `proto_version=2+` streaming subxacts (with `Y`/`A` frames + parent-XID linkage) is out of scope here; promoting the default requires apply-worker subxact tracking and is a later rung. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgSavepointXact` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`). Verification: targeted test → PASS (~1.7 s); all 7 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~12.0 s); race-tested regression on `executor/wal/server/catalog/pubsubcluster` → green. pgbench against PG publisher with `pgbench_history` polling, DDL replication shapes, proto_version=2 streaming, and `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect remain future rungs. |
| 0103-0029 | [M0103-0007 rung 6 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber multi-DML single transaction](0103-0029-m0103-0007-rung-6-pg-to-goopg-multi-dml-xact.md) | accepted | Closes rung 6 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–5 ran every publisher DML in its own implicit autocommit xact (one pgoutput `B…C` per statement); this rung scales vertically — one explicit `BEGIN; INSERT×3; UPDATE; DELETE; COMMIT;` block on the publisher, one pgoutput xact on the wire, one `txnMgr.Begin/Commit` pair on the subscriber. The correctness property pinned is **own-xact write visibility**: subsequent UPDATE/DELETE handlers must locate rows that an earlier INSERT in the same pgoutput xact wrote with `xmin == currentTx.XID`. `mvcc.TupleVisibleSubxact` short-circuits on `isCurrentTxXID(h.Xmin, currentXID, r)` (`internal/mvcc/subxact_visibility.go:131-159`) before consulting the snapshot, so `applyDeleteByKey` and `applyScanFirstMatch`'s heap scans see same-xact tuples and the post-commit subscriber state reflects the net effect of all 5 DML statements atomically. No code change was required for this rung — the machinery landed across rungs 1–5 and the broader MVCC effort already supports the shape; the new `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgMultiDMLXact` asserts it end-to-end via fresh `database/sql` sessions (PK IndexScan path): `count(*) = 2`, `id=1 v='one'`, `id=2 v='two-prime'` (INSERT-then-UPDATE in same xact), `id=3` → 0 (INSERT-then-DELETE in same xact). Verification: `go test -count=1 -timeout 180s -run TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgMultiDMLXact ./internal/testport/` → PASS (~1.7 s); all 6 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~10.1 s); race-tested regression on executor/wal/server/catalog/pubsubcluster → green. SAVEPOINT/ROLLBACK TO subxacts, pgbench workload, DDL replication, and `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect remain future rungs. |
| 0103-0028 | [M0103-0007 rung 5 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber unchanged-TOAST ('u') decode](0103-0028-m0103-0007-rung-5-pg-to-goopg-toast-unchanged.md) | accepted | Closes rung 5 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–4 only exercised `'t'`/`'n'` per-column status codes; this rung adds the third upstream code `'u'` (unchanged TOAST). Real-world publishers with TOASTed columns emit `'u'` + zero bytes on every UPDATE that leaves the TOASTed column untouched, and the apply worker had been hard-rejecting it. Fix: `decodePgoutputTupleAsRow` now returns `(Row, []bool, error)` where the bool slice marks each `'u'` slot. The OldTuple/DELETE-key callers discard the mask — `NullDatum` + `rowMatchesKey`'s "skip NULL key cells" already yields the correct wildcard semantics. `applyInsert` defensively rejects `'u'`. `applyUpdate` threads the mask to `applyUpdateByKey`; when any cell is unchanged, a new read-only `applyScanFirstMatch` walks `rel`, returns the first row matching `oldKeyRow`, and the apply path copies its values into the corresponding `newRow` slots before the existing delete+insert+index-maintenance sequence (two-scan cost paid only when `'u'` is present). Pinned by `TestApplyWorkerDecodeReturnsUnchangedMask` + `TestApplyWorkerInsertRejectsUnchangedToast` (unit) and `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgUnchangedToast` (live, payload column with `SET STORAGE EXTERNAL` + 4 KiB value, UPDATE that doesn't touch payload, assertions on `length(payload)=4096` and `substr(payload,1,1)='X'` to fail-fast on NULL-fill and wrong-cell bugs). Verification: `go test -count=1 -timeout 120s -run TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgUnchangedToast ./internal/testport/` → PASS (~2.0 s); all 5 `TestPort_PgoutputInterop*` together → PASS (~10.2 s); race-tested regression on executor/wal/catalog/pubsubcluster → green. DDL replication, pgbench against PG publisher with `pgbench_history` polling, and `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect plumbing remain future rungs. |
| 0103-0027 | [M0103-0007 rung 4 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber REPLICA IDENTITY FULL](0103-0027-m0103-0007-rung-4-pg-to-goopg-replica-identity-full.md) | accepted | Closes rung 4 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–3 only exercised REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT (PK-only or no-old-tuple shapes). This rung pins the `'O'` full-pre-image branch in `wal.DecodeMessage` and `ApplyWorker.applyUpdate`/`applyDelete` via a no-PK table whose publisher carries `ALTER TABLE … REPLICA IDENTITY FULL`. Without a PK the rung-2 `primaryKeyOnlyRow` path is unreachable, so every UPDATE/DELETE must travel through `decodePgoutputTupleAsRow(m.OldTuple)` and `rowMatchesKey`'s full-row equality match against a sequentially-scanned heap. The new `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgReplicaIdentityFull` (INSERT×3, UPDATE×1 no-key-touched, DELETE×1) verifies fresh-session visibility through SeqScan-only predicates (`WHERE 1=1`, `WHERE a=2 AND v='bb'`, `WHERE a=1`, `WHERE a=3 AND v='c'`). No new fix was needed in this loop — the apply worker's existing `len(m.OldTuple) > 0` branch and `decodePgoutputTupleAsRow`'s `'t'`/`'n'` decoding cover the FULL shape correctly. Verification: `go test -count=1 -timeout 120s -run TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgReplicaIdentityFull ./internal/testport/` → PASS (~1.7 s). TOAST (`'u'` unchanged-TOAST status), DDL replication, `pgbench`, and `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect remain future rungs. |
| 0103-0026 | [M0103-0007 rung 3 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber batch DML](0103-0026-m0103-0007-rung-3-pg-to-goopg-batch-dml.md) | accepted | Scales rung-2's single-row INSERT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE round-trip into a sustained-workload variant via `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgBatchDML`: 50 single-row INSERTs (ids 1..50), then 25 UPDATEs over ids 1..25 (no-key-touched, hits the rung-2 `primaryKeyOnlyRow` path 25 times in a row), then 10 DELETEs over ids 41..50 (distinct range from the UPDATE range, exercises the apply-side DELETE path against rows that have only ever seen INSERT). Each phase crosses pgoutput xact boundaries and heap-page boundaries on the publisher (so the publisher emits `RecordKindPageImage` first-dirty-in-epoch records — the rung-14 M0103-0008 shape — but the apply worker only consumes pgoutput Insert frames, so all 50 must surface). Assertions read the subscriber from fresh `database/sql` connections via `psc.WaitForRow`, exercising the goopg PK IndexScan path: `count(*) = 40`, each updated id has `v = 'updated-N'`, each untouched-but-not-deleted id keeps `v = 'row-N'`, each deleted id returns 0 (heap re-fetch + MVCC dead-tuple filtering drops orphan PK entries). No new fix was needed in this loop — rung 1's index maintenance and rung 2's `primaryKeyOnlyRow` synthesis already scale to 50× the rung-2 workload. Verification: `go test -count=1 -timeout 160s -run TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgBatchDML ./internal/testport/` → PASS (~2.1 s). Next rungs (deferred within M0103-0007): pgbench against the PG publisher with `pgbench_history` polling, REPLICA IDENTITY FULL / TOAST / DDL replication shapes, kill -9 + libpq multi-host reconnect plumbing on the client side. |
| 0103-0025 | [M0103-0007 rung 2 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber full DML round-trip](0103-0025-m0103-0007-rung-2-pg-to-goopg-full-dml.md) | accepted | Closes rung 2 of M0103-0007 (Scenario A). Mirrors the M0103-0008 closure shape: the new `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgFullDML` drives a full INSERT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE round-trip through the pubsubcluster harness (PG publisher + goopg subscriber, pre-created slot since goopg's CREATE SUBSCRIPTION doesn't yet auto-create), then asserts fresh-session visibility on the goopg subscriber via PK IndexScan: `WHERE id = 2 AND v = 'updated'` returns 1, `WHERE id = 1` returns 0, `count(*)` returns 1. Lifting the test surfaced a concrete gap — `ApplyWorker.applyUpdate` returned nil whenever `m.OldTuple == []` and silently dropped every REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT UPDATE that didn't touch key columns (pgoutput's `logicalrep_write_update` omits OldTuple in that case: `'U' relOid 'N' newTuple` directly). Fix: when OldTuple is empty, synthesise the row-locator key from the new tuple's PK columns via a new `primaryKeyOnlyRow(catalog, tbl, full Row) Row` helper that returns a partial-key Row (PK positions match `full`, every other position is NullDatum so `rowMatchesKey`'s "skip NULL key cells" rule restricts matching to the PK). No-PK tables continue to skip silently.
