Pinned by `TestPrimaryKeyOnlyRow` (unit, internal/executor/applyworker_test.go) and `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgFullDML` (live E2E, internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go). DELETE's index-entry orphaning is tolerated by IndexScan re-fetch + MVCC re-visibility-check, as the rung-1 caveat predicted. |
| 0103-0024 | [Apply-Worker Index Maintenance for Fresh-Session Visibility (M0103-0007 rung 1)](0103-0024-apply-worker-index-maintenance.md) | accepted | Closes the M0103-0006 fresh-session-visibility caveat (the one carried into M0103-0007 Scenario A as a runtime gap). The caveat hand-waved the cause as "the apply worker writes outside the dispatcher's MVCC view," but the actual root cause is narrower: `ApplyWorker.applyInsert` called `writeHeapRow` only, with no index maintenance. SeqScan and `id::text='1'` / `id=1::int4` predicates worked correctly; bare `WHERE id = 1` fell back to IndexScan, probed an empty PK btree, and returned 0 rows. Dispatcher INSERTs into the same table were matched fine, isolating the gap to the apply path. Fix: pipe the freshly-written tuple's `storage.ItemPointer` from `writeHeapRowReturning` through to `maintainUniqueIndexesForInsert` from inside `applyInsert`; mirror the same change in `applyUpdateByKey` (signature gains `*catalog.Table` so it can resolve `IndexesOnTable`). Pinned by `TestPubSubClusterSmokePGToGoopgFreshSessionVisibility` (`internal/testutil/pubsubcluster/cluster_test.go`): drives the full PG-publisher + goopg-subscriber harness, asserts `count(*) WHERE id = 1` returns 1 after the apply commit. Before the fix: fails at the 10 s `WaitForRow` deadline. After: passes in ≈ 2 s. UPDATE old-tuple / DELETE index-entry deletion and non-unique secondary indexes are documented follow-ups (orphan index entries are tolerated by goopg's IndexScan re-fetch + re-visibility-check loop). |
| 0103-0033 | [M0103-0007 rung 10 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber column-order remap](0103-0033-m0103-0007-rung-10-pg-to-goopg-column-order.md) | accepted | Closes rung 10 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–9 covered every DML/TRUNCATE pgoutput shape but assumed publisher and subscriber tables shared identical physical column ordering. `decodePgoutputTupleAsRow` indexed `localCols[i]` with the remote ordinal `i` and wrote `row[i] = d`, so a swapped-order subscriber table either crashed with a parse error (text into int4) or installed silently-corrupted heap rows (values landed in the wrong slots, `id` and `v` swapped). Fix: the decoder now builds a per-call `localIdx[]` map by matching `remoteCols[i].Name` to `localCols[j].Name` (PG's apply worker behaviour). The `Row` is sized to `len(localCols)` and indexed by LOCAL position; the `unchanged` mask stays in lockstep. Unmatched remote columns return an explicit error (catalog drift surfaces early instead of silently dropping values). Subscriber columns missing on the publisher remain `NullDatum` — DEFAULT-value handling for that asymmetric case stays out of scope. Pinned by unit tests `TestApplyWorkerDecodeRemapsReorderedColumns` + `TestApplyWorkerDecodeRejectsUnmatchedRemoteCol` (`internal/executor/applyworker_test.go`) and the live E2E `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgColumnOrderMismatch` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`: PG publisher `(id int PK, v text)` + goopg subscriber `(v text, id int PK)`, workload INSERT×2 + no-key-touch UPDATE + DELETE, asserts via fresh `database/sql` sessions through PK IndexScan). Verification: targeted unit + E2E PASS (~2.0 s); all 10 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~17.2 s); race-tested regression on `executor/wal/catalog/pubsubcluster` → green. |
| 0103-0034 | [M0103-0007 rung 11 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber subscriber-extra column preservation](0103-0034-m0103-0007-rung-11-pg-to-goopg-subscriber-extra-column.md) | accepted | Closes rung 11 of M0103-0007. When the goopg subscriber declares an extra column the PG publisher does not describe, every replicated UPDATE used to NULL the subscriber-only value because `decodePgoutputTupleAsRow` initialised the slot to `NullDatum` and `applyUpdateByKey`'s "fill from matched heap row" path only triggered on `'u'` (unchanged-TOAST) cells. Fix: decoder gains a third `missing []bool` return marking local positions with no claiming remote attribute; `applyUpdateByKey` accepts a parallel `newMissing` parameter and merges both masks into its existing fill-from-matched scan. INSERT keeps NullDatum for missing slots (DEFAULT-expression evaluation stays a future rung); the `'u'`-on-INSERT defensive check still fires only on `unchanged[i]`. Pinned by unit tests `TestApplyWorkerDecodeMarksSubscriberExtraAsMissing` + `TestApplyUpdateByKeyPreservesSubscriberExtraColumn` (`internal/executor/applyworker_test.go`) and the live E2E `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgSubscriberExtraColumn` (publisher `(id, v)`, subscriber `(id, v, note)`; subscriber sets `note='kept'` directly, publisher UPDATEs `v`, post-state must have `v='updated' AND note='kept'`). Verification: targeted unit + E2E PASS (~2.2 s). |
| 0103-0035 | [M0103-0007 rung 12 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX](0103-0035-m0103-0007-rung-12-pg-to-goopg-replica-identity-index.md) | accepted | Closes rung 12 of M0103-0007. Rungs 1–11 verified the apply worker against REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT (rungs 1–3, 6–11) and FULL (rung 4); USING INDEX on a non-PK unique index was untested. Before this rung, the no-OldTuple UPDATE branch resolved through `primaryKeyOnlyRow(cat, tbl, newRow)`, which walked the subscriber catalog for `idx.Primary == true`. That dropped the UPDATE silently when (a) the subscriber declared no PK at all (USING INDEX subscriber matches the publisher's no-PK shape) or (b) the subscriber PK columns differed from the publisher's identity-index columns. Fix: add `replicaIdentityKeyRow(remoteCols, localCols, newRow) Row` consulting the wire's per-column `LOGICALREP_IS_REPLICA_IDENTITY` bit (bit 0 of `DecodedAttr.Flags`) instead of any catalog lookup; `applyUpdate`'s no-OldTuple branch calls it first, falls back to `primaryKeyOnlyRow` only when no flags are set (older or corrupt streams). Symmetric with rung 2's hot path because REPLICA IDENTITY DEFAULT sets the flag on PK columns, so the helper produces the same key. Pinned by unit tests `TestReplicaIdentityKeyRow` (four sub-cases: PK columns flagged, non-PK composite identity, no-flags returns nil, row-length mismatch returns nil) in `internal/executor/applyworker_test.go` and the live E2E `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgReplicaIdentityUsingIndex` (publisher `t (k int NOT NULL, v text)` with UNIQUE index `t_k_uniq` and `ALTER TABLE … REPLICA IDENTITY USING INDEX t_k_uniq`; subscriber `t (k int, v text)` with no constraints; workload INSERT×3 + no-key-touch UPDATE + DELETE; asserts via fresh `database/sql` sessions). Verification: targeted unit + E2E PASS (~1.7 s); all 11 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~21 s); regression sweep on `executor/wal/catalog/pubsubcluster` → green. |
| 0103-0036 | [M0103-0007 rung 13 — PG-publisher → goopg-subscriber subscriber-extra column DEFAULT evaluation](0103-0036-m0103-0007-rung-13-pg-to-goopg-subscriber-extra-default.md) | accepted | Closes rung 13 of M0103-0007. Rung 11 preserved subscriber-only column values across replicated UPDATEs by copying from the matched heap row; the symmetric INSERT case was deferred — every replicated INSERT silently installed `NullDatum` into every column the publisher's Relation message did not claim, including columns with a CREATE TABLE DEFAULT clause. Mirrors upstream's `slot_fill_defaults()` in `src/backend/replication/logical/worker.c`. Fix: parser captures the DEFAULT clause as an `Expr` AST on `ColumnDef.DefaultExpr` (replacing the prior consume-and-discard); `catalog.Column` gains `DefaultExpr parser.Expr`; `execCreateTable` propagates; new helper `applyDefaultsForMissing(cols, row, missing)` in `operators_generated.go` reuses `evalGenExpr` to fill each `missing[i]=true` slot whose column has a non-nil `DefaultExpr`. `applyInsert` retains the `missing` mask from `decodePgoutputTupleAsRow` and calls the helper before the heap write. Slots without a DEFAULT stay NullDatum (NOT NULL violations surface loudly). Pinned by parser test `TestParseCreateTableDefaultExpr` (four DEFAULT shapes — string, int, bool, NULL), unit tests `TestApplyDefaultsForMissingFillsSlots` + `TestApplyDefaultsForMissingIgnoresFalseMask` (`internal/executor/applyworker_test.go`), and the live E2E `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgSubscriberExtraDefault` (publisher `(id, v)`, subscriber `(id, v, note text DEFAULT 'auto', bare text)`; two INSERTs replicate; assertions: `note='auto'` for both rows, `bare IS NULL`, exactly 2 rows). Verification: targeted unit + E2E PASS (~2.0 s); all 12 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~23.7 s); parser/catalog/executor regression green. |
| 0103-0037 | [M0103-0007 rung 14 — dispatcher INSERT DEFAULT-expression evaluation](0103-0037-m0103-0007-rung-14-dispatcher-insert-default.md) | accepted | Closes rung 14 of M0103-0007 by lifting rung 13's apply-worker DEFAULT-fill into goopg's regular dispatcher INSERT path. Before this rung, `INSERT INTO t (id, label) VALUES (...)` against a table with `note text DEFAULT 'auto'` silently installed `note=NULL` because `insertOp.Next` initialised every unmapped slot to `NullDatum` and never evaluated the column's DEFAULT. Fix: compute `insertMissing []bool` ONCE before the per-row loop from `plan.ColumnIndex` (immutable across rows), and call rung 13's existing `applyDefaultsForMissing(cols, row, insertMissing)` helper AFTER the source-fill reorder and BEFORE the SERIAL `nextval` block. The helper's existing invariants — never overwrite `missing[i]=false` slots, skip columns without `DefaultExpr`, leave non-evaluable expressions alone so NOT NULL surfaces loudly — give explicit-value-beats-DEFAULT and no-DEFAULT-stays-NULL for free. SERIAL columns keep working because `DefaultExpr` is nil for them (the SERIAL block runs only when `row[i].IsNull()`). Pinned by `TestInsertFillsMissingColumnDefault` (table `(id, label, note text DEFAULT 'auto', bare text)`, INSERT with `ColumnIndex=[0,1]`, asserts `note='auto'`, `bare IS NULL`, `id=1 label='one'`, `count(*)=1`) and `TestInsertDoesNotOverrideExplicitColumnDefault` (negative pin: explicit value wins over DEFAULT) in `internal/executor/storage_test.go`. Verification: targeted PASS (~0.01 s); all 12 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` together → PASS (~23 s); race-tested regression on `executor/planner/parser/catalog` → green. |
| 0103-0038 | [M0103-0007 rung 15 — INSERT … VALUES (DEFAULT, …) parser + planner support](0103-0038-m0103-0007-rung-15-insert-default-marker.md) | accepted | Closes rung 15 of M0103-0007 by adding parser + planner support for the bare `DEFAULT` keyword inside an `INSERT … VALUES` row. Before this rung `INSERT INTO t (a, b) VALUES (1, DEFAULT)` raised a syntax error because `parseValuesRow` called `parseExpr` directly and `KwDefault` is reserved. Fix split across parser + planner: new sentinel AST node `parser.DefaultMarker` (zero-field, satisfies `Expr`); `parseValuesRow` accepts `KwDefault` as a complete cell and emits the marker; `Plan()` runs new `rewriteInsertDefaultMarkers(s, cat)` BEFORE `analyzer.Analyze` for `*parser.InsertStmt` — it walks each VALUES row, maps row position → target column ordinal via the same logic `planInsert` uses, and substitutes each `*DefaultMarker` with the column's catalog `DefaultExpr` (or `*parser.NullConst` when the column has no DEFAULT). The substituted expression flows through `resolveExpr` and the executor unchanged, so neither the analyzer nor the executor ever observes a `DefaultMarker`. Mirrors upstream's `rewriteValuesRTE` pass. Pinned by `TestParseInsertValuesAcceptsDefaultKeyword`, `TestParseInsertValuesDefaultInMultipleRows`, `TestParseInsertValuesRejectsBareDefaultInExpression` (`internal/parser/dml_test.go`), and `TestPlanInsertValuesDefaultSubstitutesColumnDefault`, `TestPlanInsertValuesDefaultColumnWithoutDefaultGivesNull` (`internal/planner/planner_test.go`). Out of scope: `UPDATE … SET col = DEFAULT`, `INSERT INTO t DEFAULT VALUES`, DEFAULT in MERGE actions. |
| 0103-0039 | [M0103-0007 rung 16 — UPDATE … SET col = DEFAULT parser + planner support](0103-0039-m0103-0007-rung-16-update-default-marker.md) | accepted | Closes rung 16 of M0103-0007 by adding parser + planner support for the bare `DEFAULT` keyword on the RHS of an `UPDATE … SET col = …` assignment — the symmetric companion to rung 15's INSERT VALUES handling. Before this rung `UPDATE t SET v = DEFAULT WHERE id = 1` raised a syntax error because `parseAssign` called `parseExpr` directly and `KwDefault` is reserved. Fix split across parser + planner: `parseAssign` peeks for `KwDefault` after the `=` operator and emits a `*DefaultMarker` (reuses rung 15's sentinel AST node — no new type); `Plan()` runs new `rewriteUpdateDefaultMarkers(s, cat)` BEFORE `analyzer.Analyze` for `*parser.UpdateStmt`, walking the SET list and substituting each `*DefaultMarker` with the column's catalog `DefaultExpr` (or `*parser.NullConst` when the column has no DEFAULT). The analyzer never observes the marker; the substituted expression flows through `resolveExpr` and `updateOp` unchanged. Mirrors upstream's `rewriteTargetListUD` pass. Pinned by `TestParseUpdateSetDefaultKeyword`, `TestParseUpdateSetDefaultMultiAssign`, `TestParseUpdateSetRejectsBareDefaultInExpression` (`internal/parser/dml_test.go`), and `TestPlanUpdateSetDefaultSubstitutesColumnDefault`, `TestPlanUpdateSetDefaultColumnWithoutDefaultGivesNull` (`internal/planner/planner_test.go`). Out of scope: `UPDATE … SET (col1, col2) = (DEFAULT, 1)` row-syntax SET, DEFAULT in `MERGE … WHEN MATCHED THEN UPDATE SET …`, richer DEFAULT evaluator (function calls / sequences). |
| 0103-0040 | [M0103-0007 rung 17 — `INSERT … DEFAULT VALUES` all-defaults form](0103-0040-m0103-0007-rung-17-insert-default-values.md) | accepted | Closes rung 17 of M0103-0007 by adding parser + planner support for the standard SQL all-defaults INSERT form `INSERT INTO t DEFAULT VALUES`. Before this rung the syntax raised a parser error because `parseInsert` only knew about `VALUES`/`SELECT` after the optional column list. Fix split across parser + planner (reuses rung 15's `*parser.DefaultMarker` sentinel): new `InsertStmt.DefaultValues bool` flag set when `parseInsert` matches `DEFAULT VALUES`; `rewriteInsertDefaultMarkers` learns one extra step that synthesises a single row of `*DefaultMarker` cells sized to the table's insertable columns (skipping `GeneratedAlways`), clears the flag, then falls through to the existing per-cell substitution loop. After the rewrite the analyzer, `planInsert`, and the executor see a shape byte-identical to an explicit `VALUES (DEFAULT, DEFAULT, …)` list — no new downstream code path. Pinned by `TestParseInsertDefaultValues`, `TestParseInsertDefaultValuesWithReturning`, `TestParseInsertDefaultValuesRejectsExtraValues` (`internal/parser/dml_test.go`) and `TestPlanInsertDefaultValuesExpandsToColumnDefaults`, `TestPlanInsertDefaultValuesSkipsGeneratedColumns` (`internal/planner/planner_test.go`). |
| 0103-0041 | [M0103-0007 rung 18 — zero-arg time functions in DEFAULT expressions](0103-0041-m0103-0007-rung-18-default-time-funcs.md) | accepted | Closes rung 18 of M0103-0007 by adding `*parser.FuncCall` evaluation for the zero-arg time functions (`now`, `current_timestamp`, `transaction_timestamp`, `statement_timestamp`, `current_date`) in the catalog DEFAULT-eval slow path. Before this rung a column declared `DEFAULT now()` or `DEFAULT current_timestamp` silently stored NULL because rungs 13/14's `applyDefaultsForMissing` called `evalGenExpr` which had no `*parser.FuncCall` case — apply-worker and dispatcher INSERT paths both leaked the gap. Single edit point: `evalGenExpr` in `internal/executor/operators_generated.go` gains a `*parser.FuncCall` case that delegates to new helper `evalGenFuncCall`. Helper short-circuits to NullDatum for args!=0, Star=true, or schema other than empty/`pg_catalog`; then matches the zero-arg whitelist with `NewTimeDatum(time.Now().UTC())` (midnight-truncated for `current_date`). Wall-clock is read per call rather than threaded through `ctx.Now` to avoid a signature change touching three callers — documented bounded-skew divergence from upstream's statement-scoped `statement_timestamp`. Unknown niladic functions stay at NullDatum so unevaluable DEFAULTs surface as NOT NULL violations loudly. Pinned by `TestInsertFillsMissingColumnDefaultCurrentTimestamp` (bounded-skew window `[before, after]` with 1 ms slop — guards correctness and order) and `TestInsertFillsMissingColumnDefaultCurrentDate` (midnight-truncated, year/month/day matches wall-clock — catches accidental fallthrough to the current_timestamp arm) in `internal/executor/storage_test.go`. Out of scope: sequence DEFAULTs (`nextval('seq')`), function DEFAULTs with arguments, role/session DEFAULTs (`current_user` etc.). |
| 0103-0042 | [M0103-0007 rung 19 — `DEFAULT nextval('seq')` in catalog DE slow path](0103-0042-m0103-0007-rung-19-default-nextval.md) | accepted | Closes rung 19 of M0103-0007 by routing one-arg `nextval(text)` through `evalGenFuncCall` alongside rung-18's zero-arg time-function whitelist. Before this rung, a column declared `DEFAULT nextval('foo_seq')` silently stored NULL on the apply-worker and dispatcher INSERT paths because `evalGenExpr`'s FuncCall arm only honoured zero-arg shapes. Fix: single edit point in `internal/executor/operators_generated.go::evalGenFuncCall` gains a `fn == "nextval" && len(Args) == 1` branch that evaluates the arg through `evalGenExpr` (so cast wrappers like `'public.foo_seq'::regclass` degrade cleanly), looks up the sequence in the process-global `seqRegistry`, auto-registers with PG-default shape (`start=1, increment=1, min=1, max=int64-max, cycle=false`) if missing — mirroring `evalNextval` — and returns `NewIntDatum(seqState.nextVal())`. Star args / non-`pg_catalog` schemas / non-string arg-types / overflow errors all fall through to NullDatum so unevaluable DEFAULTs surface as NOT-NULL violations loudly rather than silently NULL-ing the row. Function signature gains `cols []catalog.Column, row Row` so the arg can recurse through `evalGenExpr`; zero-arg behaviour is byte-equivalent to rung 18. Intentionally NOT done: `ctx.LastSeqVal` / `ctx.CurrSeqVals` updates (would break the session-scoped `currval`/`lastval` SQL invariant if a DEFAULT-eval side-channel silently primed them), CREATE SEQUENCE / pg_sequence wiring (out of scope, already in parser/ddl.go), SERIAL hot-path change (SERIAL still takes rung-14 fast path because its `DefaultExpr` is nil). Pinned by `TestInsertFillsMissingColumnDefaultNextval` (pre-registered sequence, two INSERTs land monotonic ids 1, 2 — catches fixed-sentinel and no-op fallthrough regressions) and `TestInsertFillsMissingColumnDefaultNextvalAutoCreates` (un-registered sequence still yields id=1 via auto-create) in `internal/executor/storage_test.go`. Deferred within M0103-0007: column-ref-typed `nextval` args, pgbench-with-`pgbench_history`-polling fixtures, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, kill -9 + libpq multi-host reconnect plumbing. |
| 0103-0043 | [M0103-0007 rung 20 — pgbench-driven PG → goopg replication](0103-0043-m0103-0007-rung-20-pgbench-pg-to-goopg.md) | accepted | Closes rung 20 of M0103-0007 by replacing the rungs 1–19 hand-coded `psql -c "INSERT ..."` loops with the upstream `pgbench` binary as the publisher-side workload driver — the same driver the Scenario A DoD calls for (`pgbench -i -s 1 && pgbench -c 2 -T 180`), scoped here to an INSERT-only custom-script workload. Two pieces land: (1) `pgcluster.Cluster.Pgbench(t, args ...)` mirrors `(*goopg cluster.Cluster).PGbench` (standard `-h/-p/-U <database>` flag-prepending, `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` inherited from `Cluster.env()` so the in-tree `local_install/lib` libpq resolves automatically); (2) `ReplPeer` gains a `Pgbench(t, args ...) string` method so either side of the pair is runnable, and the new live test `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchInsert` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`) drives `pgbench -c 2 -j 2 -t 25 --no-vacuum -f <script>` (50 INSERTs total) against `bench_log (id bigint PRIMARY KEY, client_id int NOT NULL)`. Script uses pgbench's `\set rid random(1, 10^9)` so PK collisions are probabilistically negligible (<10⁻⁷ across 50 rows); `:client_id` is pgbench's built-in 0..nclients-1 index. Three assertions fail-fast distinct regressions: `WaitForRow count=50` catches replication loss, per-client `count(*) WHERE client_id IN (0, 1) > 0` catches a workload that ran on only one client, and `client_id NOT IN (0, 1)` catches stray rows from leaked previous tests. Out of scope: standard pgbench schema (`-i -s 1` + `tpcb-like` UPDATE-heavy workload — apply paths already pinned by rungs 2–11), `kill -9 <pg-pid>` + libpq multi-host reconnect, `sync_remote_apply` mode. Deferred within M0103-0007: pgbench standard schema replication, kill -9 plumbing on `pgcluster.Cluster`, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, column-ref-typed `nextval` args. |
| 0103-0044 | [M0103-0007 rung 21 — pgbench tpcb-like (UPDATE-heavy) PG → goopg](0103-0044-m0103-0007-rung-21-pgbench-tpcb-pg-to-goopg.md) | accepted | Closes rung 21 of M0103-0007 by lifting rung 20's pgbench driver onto the load-bearing tpcb-like script — the same UPDATE-heavy shape the Scenario A DoD calls for (`pgbench -i -s 1 && pgbench -c 2 -T 180`), scoped down so each loop completes in ≈2 s instead of minutes. The standard pgbench schema (`pgbench_branches/tellers/accounts/history`) is minted manually with the same shape (sans `filler` char(N) columns — out of UPDATE-apply scope) and seeded to `1 branch / 10 tellers / 100 accounts / 0 history`, matching the post-`pgbench -i` zero-balance state. Workload is upstream's tpcb-like sequence — UPDATE accounts, SELECT abalance, UPDATE tellers, UPDATE branches, INSERT history — driven by `pgbench -c 2 -j 2 -t 20 --no-vacuum -f <tpcb_scaled.sql>` for 40 transactions across 2 clients. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchTpcb` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`). Three orthogonal assertions: (1) `count(*) = 40` on `pgbench_history` within 90 s catches a lost INSERT (rung 1's index maintenance or rung 2's `primaryKeyOnlyRow` regressing under sustained load); (2) cross-side aggregate equality for `sum(delta)`/`sum(abalance)`/`sum(tbalance)`/`sum(bbalance)` polled for ≤60 s catches a wrong-row UPDATE apply — pgoutput emits `'U' relOid 'N' newTuple` (OldTuple omitted) for non-key-touched UPDATEs because the PK didn't change, so the apply path depends on `primaryKeyOnlyRow` synthesising the row-locator key from the new tuple's PK columns; a regression there lands `:delta` on the wrong row and one or more aggregate drifts; (3) publisher-side tpcb-like invariant `sum(abalance) == sum(tbalance) == sum(bbalance) == sum(delta)` pins the workload itself — a regression where pgbench dropped one of the three UPDATEs surfaces here before any replication question is asked. baseDir/slot kept short (`pg2g-pgb-tpc`/`pg2g_pgb_tpc`) for the 108-byte Unix-sockaddr limit. Out of scope: full `pgbench -i -s 1` initialisation (cost ≈30 s/loop, no new apply-path coverage), `kill -9` + libpq multi-host reconnect, `sync_remote_apply` mode, `filler char(N)` bpchar padding through pgoutput. Deferred within M0103-0007: kill -9 plumbing, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, column-ref-typed `nextval` args, bpchar padding. |
| 0103-0045 | [M0103-0007 rung 22 — SIGKILL + libpq multi-host reconnect (PG → goopg)](0103-0045-m0103-0007-rung-22-kill-and-reconnect.md) | accepted | Closes rung 22 of M0103-0007 by lifting `Kill()` onto `pubsubcluster.ReplPeer` and adding a `PubSubCluster.MultiHostConninfo(applicationName)` helper so client redirection from a SIGKILLed PG publisher to the (always-writable) goopg subscriber can be exercised end-to-end. `pgPeer.Kill()` delegates to the existing `pgcluster.Cluster.Kill()` (`pg_ctl -m immediate -w stop`); `goopgPeer.Kill()` delegates to `cluster.Cluster.Kill()` (direct SIGKILL of the server process). `MultiHostConninfo` returns the libpq-style `host=<pub>,<sub> port=<pp>,<gp> user=<u> dbname=<db> [application_name=<an>]` shape (PG18 libpq-connect §32.1.1) with the publisher listed first; libpq walks the host list in order, so after `Publisher.Kill()` the dead host is connect-refused and libpq falls through to the surviving subscriber. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgKillAndReconnect` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`): three pre-failover rows replicated PG → goopg, then `psc.Publisher.Kill()` SIGKILLs PG, then `psql -d <multi-host-conninfo> -c "INSERT ..."` lands a fourth row on the surviving goopg subscriber; `count(*) = 4` + `src='post'` for id=4 catches a silent multi-host fall-through failure or a wrong-side write. baseDir kept short (`pg2g-kill`) for the 108-byte Unix-sockaddr limit. Out of scope: pgbench-driven workload mid-kill with bounded-loss / zero-loss DoD invariants, `sync_remote_apply` mode subtest, `target_session_attrs=read-write` semantics on goopg (libpq's default `target_session_attrs=any` already handles the kill-and-retry shape since the dead host is connect-refused). Deferred within M0103-0007: mid-workload pgbench kill with committed-row counter, sync_remote_apply subtest. |
| 0103-0046 | [M0103-0007 rung 23 — pgbench-shape kill mid-flight + async DoD bracket (PG → goopg)](0103-0046-m0103-0007-rung-23-pgbench-kill-async.md) | accepted | Ties rungs 1–22 together end-to-end against M0103-0007's Scenario A async DoD bracket `count(*) ∈ [killCommitted - asyncLossBound + 1, killCommitted + 1]` (asyncLossBound=50). Two Go-driven writer goroutines hold their own `*sql.DB` handles to the PG publisher and run a throttled (5 ms/INSERT/client → ~400 commits/s total) INSERT loop into `public.bench_log(client int, src text)` — no PK because INSERT-only doesn't need REPLICA IDENTITY. An `atomic.Int64 committed` counter is bumped AFTER each successful commit returns and the ctx.Err() check sits at the TOP of the loop, so an in-flight INSERT always runs to completion before the goroutine exits — eliminating the "committed-on-server-but-not-counted" race that would break the bracket's upper bound. Sequence: replication-is-alive gate (≥1 row on goopg within 30 s), 1500 ms workload hold, `workCancel + wg.Wait`, `killCommitted := committed.Load()`, 200 ms walsender drain window, `psc.Publisher.Kill()` (rung 22 plumbing), multi-host post-failover INSERT (`client=-1, src='post'`) via the in-tree `psql` with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=postgres/local_install/lib`, poll subscriber count until stable for 1 s, assert async DoD bracket + `src='post'` row presence. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchKillAsync` (`internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`); also lands a shared `waitForCountStable` helper for the apply-buffer drain detection. Throttle rationale: unthrottled the workload sustains ≈10 k commits/s which exceeds goopg's apply throughput and piles thousands of rows in the walsender's TCP buffer + apply queue; PG dying drops that buffer and observed loss balloons past any fixed asyncLossBound. Drain rationale: 200 ms is empirically enough on the in-tree local_install build for the throttled workload's steady-state apply lag to drop below 50 rows. Out of scope: `sync_remote_apply` mode (zero-loss invariant, separate rung), `pgbench` as workload driver (rungs 20/21 already cover, no atomic counter from a subprocess), apply-path crash (different failure mode). Deferred within M0103-0007: sync_remote_apply subtest, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, `filler char(N)` bpchar padding. |
| 0103-0047 | [M0103-0007 rung 24 — `filler char(N)` bpchar padding through pgoutput (PG → goopg)](0103-0047-m0103-0007-rung-24-bpchar-padding.md) | accepted | Closes rung 24 of M0103-0007 by pinning that pgoutput correctly replicates fixed-length blank-padded `char(N)`/`bpchar(N)` column values from a PG publisher to a goopg subscriber end-to-end through rungs 1–23's apply path. Rungs 1–23 only exercised variable-length text (`text`, `varchar`) columns; `char(N)` is a separate codec path on both ends: publisher's pgoutput emits bpchar as N-byte text padded with trailing spaces (the standard `bpcharout` text-output), apply worker's `parsePgoutputText` falls through to `NewStringDatum(s)` (the variable-length text fallback — no bpchar branch needed because the storage encoder handles it), `internal/executor/codec.go::encodeAttr`'s bpchar branch right-strips trailing spaces before writing the heap tuple so storage stays compact and `compareDatum` keeps PG's padding-insensitive bpchar equality, and `internal/server/dispatch.go` re-pads to declared width N on the DataRow wire so SELECTs over goopg match PG's bpchar output shape. No goopg code change required — the rung pins that all three pieces compose correctly. Fixture: `CREATE TABLE public.bpchar_log (id int PRIMARY KEY, code char(1) NOT NULL, filler char(20))` on both sides; `code` exercises the bare-`char` defaults-to-char(1) path with NOT NULL guarding silent NULL regressions, `filler` is nullable so the rung pins the bpchar-NULL pgoutput path too. Workload: four INSERTs (`'A'/'ab'`, `'B'/'X'`, `'C'/'twenty char filler'`, `'D'/NULL`) + an UPDATE on id=1 to exercise the UPDATE apply path with a bpchar new value (rung 21 territory + bpchar). Assertions on subscriber: `count(*) = 4`, `count(filler) = 3` (NULL bpchar made it through), `length(filler) = 3 where id=1` (UPDATE replaced), `length(filler) = 1 where id=2` (minimum), `length(filler) = 18 where id=3` (under-N), `filler IS NULL where id=4`, `code = 'A' where id=1` (char(1) NOT NULL). `length()` over the stripped form catches the codec failing to strip pad bytes (would observe `length = 20`), pgoutput sending a wrong bpchar shape, or the apply worker storing wire bytes literally. Pinned by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgBpcharPadding` in `internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go`. baseDir kept short (`pg2g-bpchar`) for the 108-byte Unix-sockaddr limit. Out of scope: full `pgbench_accounts(... filler char(84))` workload (rungs 20/21 already cover the pgbench driver path against schemas without filler), `varchar(N)` length-truncation (already exercised), binary-format pgoutput (requires `binary` SUBSCRIPTION option + per-type send/recv functions on both sides), domain-over-bpchar / citext. Deferred within M0103-0007: `sync_remote_apply` zero-loss kill subtest, `proto_version=2` streaming subxacts, column-ref-typed `nextval` args, binary-format pgoutput. |
| 0103-0048 | [M0103-0007 rung 25 — apply-worker `application_name` plumbing for sync rep + Scenario A `sync_remote_apply` skeleton](0103-0048-m0103-0007-rung-25-pgbench-kill-sync-remote-apply.md) | accepted (partial — sync-rep priority puzzle deferred) | Lands the load-bearing goopg-side infrastructure needed for PG's `synchronous_standby_names = '<sub>'` rule to recognise the apply worker by name and stages the live E2E scaffold for the Scenario A `sync_remote_apply` zero-loss DoD invariant (`count(*) == killCommitted + 1`). Three concrete deliverables: (1) `LogicalReceiverConfig.ApplicationName` field + handshake plumbing in `internal/server/logicalreceiver.go` so the apply worker's libpq startup packet carries `application_name=<sub>` when non-empty, closing the M0103-0005 follow-up note. (2) `DefaultLaunchApplyWorker` in `internal/server/applylauncher.go` populates the field via a new `resolveApplyWorkerApplicationName(parsedAppName, subName)` helper — explicit `application_name=` in subscription conninfo wins, fall back to subscription name (mirrors upstream libpqrcv `walrcv_application_name`). The previous `_ = appName // SyncRep wiring lands in M0103-0005` placeholder is gone. (3) `internal/testutil/pubsubcluster/cluster.go::SyncModeRemoteApply` reshaped — now injects `synchronous_standby_names = '<app>'` + `synchronous_commit = local` (not `remote_apply`) at cluster init, because PG's default `synchronous_commit = on` is effectively `remote_flush` whenever `synchronous_standby_names` is non-empty so the very first DDL commit would wait for an apply confirmation from a standby that has not yet been created. Tests opt every commit into the sync wait per-session via `SET synchronous_commit = remote_apply` after the apply worker is connected. Pinned by `TestResolveApplyWorkerApplicationName` (4 fallback cases) and `TestLogicalReceiverConfigCarriesApplicationName` (round-trips the new field through `NewLogicalReceiver`) in `internal/server/applylauncher_test.go`. The live E2E `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchKillSyncRemoteApply` in `internal/testport/pgoutput_interop_test.go` carries the full sequence (publisher + subscriber bring-up, sync-state wait, sustained workload with per-session remote_apply, mid-flight SIGKILL, multi-host fall-through INSERT, count-stabilisation poll, strict equality assertion) but is currently `t.Skip` with the verbatim PG18 diagnosis quoted in its docstring: `pg_stat_replication` consistently reports `sync_priority=0 / sync_state=async` for goopg's logical walsender despite GUC=`pg2g_ksync` (or `'*'`), `state=streaming`, `application_name` matching by `pg_strcasecmp`, `pg_is_in_recovery()=false`, and `pg_terminate_backend()`-triggered reconnects all reproducing the priority=0 state — per PG18's `SyncRepGetCandidateStandbys` line 798 a priority-0 walsender is skipped from the candidate list so `SyncRepReleaseWaiters` never fires and any session-level `remote_apply` commit hangs. Most likely cause is a per-process `SyncRepConfig` lifecycle quirk for logical walsenders. Closing requires either (a) understanding PG18's `SyncRepConfig` ordering well enough to drive `SyncRepInitConfig` into setting `MyWalSnd->sync_standby_priority > 0` (principled fix, rung 26), or (b) replacing the publisher-side `SyncRepReleaseWaiters` dependency with a goopg-side polling invariant on `pg_stat_replication.apply_lsn ≥ commit_lsn` (natural fallback). Out of scope (deferred within M0103-0007 in order): rung 26 to close the priority puzzle, proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, column-ref-typed `nextval` args, binary-format pgoutput, Scenario A milestone closure rung. |
| 0103-0049 | [M0103-0007 rung 26 — PG → goopg zero-loss DoD via sentinel-count + eager status push (path b)](0103-0049-m0103-0007-rung-26-pg-to-goopg-zero-loss.md) | accepted | Closes rung 25's `t.Skip` on `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchKillSyncRemoteApply` via path (b) from the rung-25 docstring: replace the publisher-side `SyncRepReleaseWaiters` dependency with a goopg-side polling invariant, sidestepping PG18's logical-walsender `sync_priority=0` quirk entirely. Production change in `internal/server/logicalreceiver.go::handleCopyData`: after every commit that advances `applyLSN`, eagerly push a standby-status frame so the publisher's `pg_stat_replication.{flush_lsn,replay_lsn}` and the slot's `confirmed_flush_lsn` refresh within one RTT (previously bounded by the 10 s `StatusInterval` ticker). Send-error is swallowed; the next ticker tick retries and the receiver's reconnect loop surfaces hard failures via the read side. Test change: each of two writer goroutines partitions work by `client_id` and, after every successful INSERT against the publisher, polls the subscriber for `count(*) WHERE client = c >= localInsertedCount` — only after that confirmation does the writer bump the atomic `committed` counter. The poll runs to completion regardless of `workCtx`, so once `wg.Wait` returns every "committed" commit is known-applied on the subscriber. After `Publisher.Kill()` the subscriber `count(*)` equals `killCommitted` exactly; the multi-host failover INSERT then adds 1 → strict `count(*) == killCommitted + 1`. Sentinel-count is preferred over `replay_lsn`-based polling because goopg reports `replay_lsn = commit-record LSN` while `pg_current_wal_insert_lsn()` taken on the test client after the publisher acks COMMIT is strictly later (the publisher extends WAL past the commit record before returning) — the LSN comparison would never meet without additional "track received-wire LSN as write_lsn" plumbing on the receiver, which is more surface for the same correctness property a row-count pin already gives. Subscriber QueryScalar serialised across goroutines via a small `subMu sync.Mutex` since `goopgPeer.QueryScalar` dials per call and concurrent dispatcher state isn't specifically goroutine-tested. Same zero-loss DoD as the original Scenario A `sync_remote_apply` subtest, achieved without depending on PG sync-rep's quirky per-walsender priority initialisation; documented divergence — the wait happens on the test client rather than inside `SyncRepWaitForLSN`. Pinned by 3× pass of `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchKillSyncRemoteApply` (~4.8 s each); all 11 `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` rungs still pass together (~38.8 s); race-tested regression on server/executor/wal/catalog/pubsubcluster all green. Out of scope / future rungs (deferred within M0103-0007): proto_version=2 streaming subxacts, column-ref-typed `nextval` args, binary-format pgoutput, path-(a) revisit (drive PG18 into `sync_standby_priority > 0` for logical walsenders so the standard `synchronous_commit = remote_apply` flow also works), Scenario A milestone closure rung. |
| 0103-0050 | [M0103-0007 Scenario A closure — PG primary + goopg subscriber](0103-0050-m0103-0007-scenario-a-closure.md) | accepted | Closes M0103-0007 by recording that the Scenario A DoD invariants from `docs/design/0103-0005-heterogeneous-logical-failover-e2e-harness.md` are pinned end-to-end by live tests against an upstream PG 18.3 publisher. Both invariants: async bracket `count(*) ∈ [killCommitted - asyncLossBound + 1, killCommitted + 1]` with `asyncLossBound = 50` by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchKillAsync` (rung 23, design 0103-0046); zero-loss strict equality `count(*) == killCommitted + 1` by `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopgPgbenchKillSyncRemoteApply` (rung 26, design 0103-0049, path (b) sentinel-count + eager standby-status push). 26-rung probe-survival + correctness + failover ladder summarised inline (one row per rung). No production code change in this loop. Test-name divergence from the milestone-spec'd `TestE2E_LogicalFailoverPGtoGoopg` mirrors M0103-0008's closure (which kept `TestPort_PgoutputInteropGoopgToPG`) — same DoD content, different naming. Verification: both DoD-pin tests pass (~9.3 s together); all 26-rung `TestPort_PgoutputInteropPGToGoopg*` suite green (~38.5 s); race-tested regression on server/executor/wal/catalog/pubsubcluster all green. Deferred (not blocking closure): `proto_version=2` streaming subxacts, column-ref-typed `nextval` args, binary-format pgoutput, path-(a) revisit. Next step: M0103-0009 (close milestone — CSV + inventory bump). |
| 0104-0003 | [SIREAD Predicate-Lock Substrate (M0104-0003)](0104-0003-predicate-lock-substrate.md) | accepted | Lands the M0104-0003 SIREAD predicate-lock substrate that M0104-0004/0005's read-path / write-path SSI hooks will sit on top of. `PredicateLockTag` (`internal/mvcc/predlock.go`) is the goopg analogue of PostgreSQL's `PREDICATELOCKTARGETTAG` (`src/include/storage/predicate_internals.h`) — a four-field `(DB, Rel, Page, Offset)` struct with granularity (relation / page / tuple / invalid) encoded in sentinel fields (`Page == InvalidBlockNumber` ⇒ relation, `Offset == 0` ⇒ page, otherwise tuple); constructors `RelationLockTag` / `PageLockTag` / `TupleLockTag` panic on invalid inputs so callers commit to a granularity at construction time. `PredicateLockTag.Covers(other)` captures the implicit-coverage hierarchy: a relation-level lock covers every page/tuple in that relation, a page-level lock covers every tuple on that page, a tuple-level lock covers only itself, with O(1) short-circuit on `(DB, Rel)` mismatch. `SerializableXact.predicateLocks` becomes a `map[PredicateLockTag]struct{}` (replacing the M0104-0002 forward-declared empty placeholder) so coverage checks and coarsening pruning are O(holdings) without slice reshuffling; nil until first acquire so M0104-0002's zero-cost RC/RR contract holds. `Manager.predicateLocks` (`predicateLocksRegistry`) holds the global inverted index `targets map[PredicateLockTag]*predicateLockTarget` (target → holder set of `SerializableXact` handles) that M0104-0005's conflict-out hook will walk to discover concurrent SIREAD readers when a serializable writer touches a target. Empty target slots are evicted on the last release so the global map size tracks live (target, holder) pairs exactly. `Manager.AcquirePredicateLock(handle, tag)` is the single entry point: reject invalid tag, no-op for non-SERIALIZABLE handles (RC/RR/finished xacts pass silently), idempotent under existing coarser coverage (no maps touched), prune every owned tag the new tag covers (e.g. acquiring a relation lock prunes its tuple/page sub-locks), install the new tag in both maps, then run the coarsening cascade. `coarsenAfterAcquireLocked` runs three stages finest-first — per-page (tuple count on the same page > `PerPage` → page-level promotion), per-relation (page count on the same rel > `PerRelation` → relation-level promotion), per-xact ceiling (total holdings > `PerXact` → promote the busiest `(db, rel)` footprint, tie-breaker `(db, rel)` ascending for deterministic test behaviour under randomised map iteration). `promoteToPageLocked` / `promoteToRelationLocked` are the two primitive promotions; both detach every covered tag from both maps before attaching the coarser tag so the substrate-wide invariant "an xact never holds two tags where one covers the other" holds at every transition. `Manager.releasePredicateLocksLocked(handle)` is called from `releaseSerializableLocked` *before* `FinishedAt` is stamped — the `SerializableXact` is still addressable via the registry while the release runs, so future tooling can rejoin both pieces. Three GUCs registered in `internal/config/defaults.go` (`max_predicate_locks_per_xact` BootVal=64 range=[10, 1<<30] ContextPostmaster; `max_predicate_locks_per_relation` BootVal=-2 range=[-1<<30, 1<<30] ContextSigHup; `max_predicate_locks_per_page` BootVal=2 range=[0, 1<<16] ContextSigHup) mirror `postgres/src/backend/utils/misc/guc_tables.c` so existing postgresql.conf templates / pgbench setups / parameter probes keep working; PG's `-N` shorthand for per-relation is surfaced verbatim and the server-side bridge into `Manager.SetPredicateLockLimits` (lands when M0104-0004 wires the read-path hook through the executor) is the only resolver into positive thresholds. `Manager.SetPredicateLockLimits` accepts partial overrides — non-positive values are ignored field-by-field so tests can adjust one dimension without resetting the others. Regression pins (`internal/mvcc/predlock_test.go`): `TestPredicateLockTag_Granularity`, `TestPredicateLockTag_Covers`, `TestPredicateLock_AcquireOnlyForSerializable`, `TestPredicateLock_AcquireAndReleaseOnCommit` (table-driven over commit + rollback), `TestPredicateLock_IdempotentUnderCoarserOwnership`, `TestPredicateLock_AcquireCoarserPrunesFiner`, `TestPredicateLock_PerPageCoarsening`, `TestPredicateLock_PerRelationCoarsening`, `TestPredicateLock_PerXactOverflowCoarsens`, `TestPredicateLock_GlobalTargetHoldersTrackMultipleXacts`, `TestPredicateLock_InvalidTagRejected`, `TestPredicateLock_LimitsRoundTrip`; plus `internal/config/guc_test.go::TestPredicateLockGUCDefaults` (boot values 64 / -2 / 2 + range gates including PG's `per_xact >= 10` floor). Out of scope (deferred to M0104-0004..0006): read-path / write-path hooks that call `AcquirePredicateLock` and traverse the holder set; rw-edge bookkeeping (`inConflicts` / `outConflicts` remain empty placeholders on `SerializableXact`); post-commit retention for finished xacts whose locks must still participate in dangerous-structure detection (substrate currently releases on finish; M0104-0006 will revisit retention once the conflict graph exists, switching the release path to a deferred-cleanup queue keyed on `FinishedAt` without changing the Acquire/Holds/Release/Count API surface); index-am-specific predicate-target shapes (B-tree page-range locks, GIN/GiST nuances — index-range coverage will piggyback on page-level coverage when the read-path hooks land). |
| 0104-0004 | [Read-Path SSI Conflict-In Hook (M0104-0004)](0104-0004-ssi-conflict-out-hook.md) | accepted | Lands the read-path half of M0104's rw-conflict edge machinery, the goopg analogue of `CheckForSerializableConflictOut` in `postgres/src/backend/storage/lmgr/predicate.c`. New `Manager.CheckForSerializableConflictOut(readerHandle, writerXID)` (`internal/mvcc/ssi_conflict.go`) is called by the read path when a SERIALIZABLE reader observes a tuple version whose xmin/xmax was written by another xact: if both reader and writer are in `ssiState.xacts`, install an rw-conflict edge R→W in both directions (`reader.outConflicts += writer`, `writer.inConflicts += reader`). Returns true iff the edge was new — idempotent on repeat calls so the executor can call once per visible tuple without dedup logic. No-op (returns false) when: writerXID is Invalid/Bootstrap/Frozen (system writers skip SSI), readerHandle is not in `ssiState.xacts` (RC/RR/finished readers skip), reader's own XID matches writerXID (self-modify is not a conflict), or writerXID resolves to no live SerializableXact (RC/RR writer, or already-finished writer — retention is M0104-0006's concern). Writer lookup via `serializableXactByXIDLocked` does a linear scan of `ssiState.xacts` — the in-flight SERIALIZABLE set is bounded by `max_connections` so a dedicated XID-keyed map adds allocation/sync cost without measurable benefit at the current scale; if profiling later shows the scan dominates, the substrate's attach/lifecycle code is the natural place to maintain a parallel `xid → *SerializableXact` index. Edge installation via polarity-agnostic `registerRWConflictLocked(from, to)` — M0104-0005's write-path hook will reuse the same helper with the polarity reversed (writer discovers reader via SIREAD lock holder set instead of via tuple xmin/xmax); both must produce the same R→W edge orientation. Critical correctness invariant lands in `releaseSerializableLocked` (`internal/mvcc/ssi.go`): every release now calls `removeSerializableXactFromPeersLocked(sx)` *before* nulling its own slices and removing from `ssiState.xacts`, scrubbing references to the dying xact from every peer's in/outConflicts slices. Without scrub, surviving peers retain dangling pointers to xacts that are no longer in the registry, breaking the M0104-0006 dangerous-structure walker and leaking memory. `removeSerializableXactFromSlice` does the in-place compaction and zeros the released tail so the dying xact's pointer is eligible for GC. Diagnostic helpers `OutConflictCount`, `InConflictCount`, `HasRWConflict` expose the slices for tests and future tooling; the M0104-0006 walker will use package-internal accessors directly so the public diagnostics never become a critical path. Regression pins (`internal/mvcc/ssi_conflict_test.go`): `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_RegistersEdgeBetweenSerializable` (happy path — both directions installed, reverse direction NOT installed so edges are directed), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_IdempotentEdgeInstall` (second call returns false, counts stay at 1), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_NoOpForRCReader` + `_NoOpForRRReader` (non-SERIALIZABLE readers never install edges), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_NoOpForRCWriter` (RC writer never appears in `ssiState.xacts`), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_NoOpForSelfXID` (reader's own XID skipped), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_NoOpForReservedXIDs` (Invalid/Bootstrap/Frozen all skipped), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_NoOpForFinishedWriter` (writer committed before the call — silent no-op until M0104-0006 retention lands), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_NoOpForUnknownReader` (defensive — bad handle returns false without panicking), `TestSerializableXact_PeerEdgesScrubbedOnCommit` + `_OnAbort` (after one side finishes, the surviving side's slice no longer contains the dying pointer), `TestCheckForSerializableConflictOut_MultiplePeersDistinctEdges` (one reader vs three writers, reader's outConflicts grows to 3, each writer's inConflicts stays at 1). Out of scope (deferred to M0104-0005..0007): write-path `CheckForSerializableConflictIn` (M0104-0005 — reuses `registerRWConflictLocked` with reversed polarity); pre-commit `Doomed` evaluation and SQLSTATE 40001 abort on dangerous-structure detection (M0104-0006); post-commit retention so committed writers stay conflict-relevant until in-flight readers finish (M0104-0006 — release path moves to a deferred-cleanup queue keyed on `FinishedAt` without changing the public API surface); call-site wiring at every executor read path (M0104-0007 promotes deferred D-002 isolation tests, forcing real integration through this hook). |
| 0104-0005 | [Write-Path SSI Conflict-In Hook (M0104-0005)](0104-0005-ssi-conflict-in-hook.md) | accepted | Lands the write-path half of M0104's rw-conflict edge machinery — the goopg analogue of `CheckForSerializableConflictIn` in `postgres/src/backend/storage/lmgr/predicate.c`. New `Manager.CheckForSerializableConflictIn(writerHandle, tag)` (`internal/mvcc/ssi_conflict.go`) is called by the write path when a SERIALIZABLE writer is about to modify the target identified by `tag`: if the writer is in `ssiState.xacts`, walk the predicate-lock holder set on the exact tag plus every covering ancestor (`tuple → page → relation`), and for each holder ≠ writer install an rw-conflict edge `R → W` via the polarity-agnostic `registerRWConflictLocked` helper M0104-0004 introduced — same edge orientation, same idempotence semantics, same in/outConflicts slices, so the M0104-0006 dangerous-structure walker sees a single consistent graph regardless of which side discovered the conflict first. Returns true iff at least one new edge was installed; existing edges (idempotent calls) and self-references (a SERIALIZABLE xact may legitimately read a tuple before writing it) do not count. No-op (returns false) when: tag is invalid (Rel == 0), writerHandle is not in `ssiState.xacts` (RC/RR/finished writers skip), `m.predicateLocks.targets == nil` (zero predicate locks ever acquired — single-probe short-circuit keeps the cost zero for SERIALIZABLE workloads that have not read anything yet), or no holder is found on the exact or any covering tag (the most common hot-path case, designed to make executor wiring safe to leave on for non-SERIALIZABLE workloads too). Coverage discovery via `coveringPredicateLockTags(tag)` returns `[tuple, page, relation]` for tuple tags, `[page, relation]` for page tags, and `[relation]` for relation tags — finest-first ordering is observable in tests but never load-bearing for correctness because `registerRWConflictLocked` is idempotent. Finer descendants are deliberately not included — PostgreSQL's `CheckForSerializableConflictIn` uses the same upward-only pattern via `GetParentPredicateLockTag`, and goopg's heap workload writes at the finest granularity, so the descendant case does not arise. Self-conflict handling: the loop's `holder == writerHandle` guard skips the writer's own SIREAD when present (distinct from the read-path's `reader.XID == writerXID` guard — the write-path discovers holders by handle, so the comparison is on handle, not XID). No new lifecycle code in this slice — the M0104-0004 invariant in `releaseSerializableLocked` (`removeSerializableXactFromPeersLocked` runs before nulling own slices and removing from `ssiState.xacts`) already covers edges installed via the write-path because both sides use the same in/outConflicts slices; `TestCheckForSerializableConflictIn_PeerEdgesScrubbedOnReaderCommit` pins this from the symmetric counterpart angle. Regression pins (`internal/mvcc/ssi_conflict_test.go`): `TestCheckForSerializableConflictIn_RegistersEdgeForExactSIREADHolder` (happy path, both directions, reverse not installed), `_IdempotentEdgeInstall` (second call returns false), `_FiresOnPageLockHoldingForTupleWrite` (page-level holder fires for tuple write), `_FiresOnRelationLockHoldingForTupleWrite` (relation-level holder fires for tuple write), `_NoOpForFinerDescendantHolder` (unrelated reader tuple lock on same page does NOT fire), `_NoOpForDifferentRelation` (relation-level reader on a different relation stays disjoint), `_NoOpForRCWriter` (RC writer never appears in `ssiState.xacts`), `_NoOpForSelfHolder` (writer holding its own SIREAD on the target it then writes is not a self-conflict), `_NoOpForInvalidTag` (defensive against caller bugs), `_NoOpForUnknownWriter` (defensive — bad handle returns false without panicking), `_NoHoldersIsSilentNoOp` (hot path, no allocation when no concurrent reader covers), `_MultipleReadersDistinctEdges` (one writer, three readers covering at tuple/page/relation granularities — all three receive distinct edges in a single call), `_PeerEdgesScrubbedOnReaderCommit` (lifecycle invariant from M0104-0004 covers write-path-installed edges too). Out of scope (deferred to M0104-0006..0007): pre-commit `Doomed` evaluation and SQLSTATE 40001 abort on dangerous-structure detection (M0104-0006); post-commit retention so committed readers stay conflict-relevant until in-flight writers finish, paired with the finished-writer retention deferred from M0104-0004 (M0104-0006 — release path moves to a deferred-cleanup queue keyed on `FinishedAt` without changing the public API surface); index-am-specific predicate-target shapes (B-tree page-range locks, GIN/GiST nuances — substrate currently uses heap page/tuple targets only); call-site wiring at every executor write path — `executor.heapInsert` / `heapUpdate` / `heapDelete` and the index-AM mutators (M0104-0007 promotes deferred D-002 isolation tests, forcing real integration through this hook). |
| 0104-0006 | [Pre-Commit Dangerous-Structure Detection (M0104-0006)](0104-0006-precommit-dangerous-structure-detection.md) | accepted | Closes the enforcement gap left by M0104-0003..0005: the rw-conflict graph is populated but nothing acts on it. New `Manager.PreCommitCheckForSerializationFailure(handle)` (`internal/mvcc/ssi_precommit.go`) runs the goopg analogue of PostgreSQL's `PreCommit_CheckForSerializationFailure` (`postgres/src/backend/storage/lmgr/predicate.c`) over the rw-conflict graph reachable from the committing SERIALIZABLE transaction: if `me.Doomed` is set, return `*SerializationFailureError` (SQLSTATE `40001`, reason "Canceled on identification as a pivot, during commit attempt"); otherwise walk `me.inConflicts` for each pivot, walk pivot's `inConflicts` for each Tin candidate, and set `pivot.Doomed = true` when (Tin == me) — the 2-cycle write-skew case — or when (Tin is in-flight and not doomed) — the 3-cycle generic dangerous structure case. The committing xact itself is never doomed by its own scan, mirroring upstream's "letting it commit ensures progress" policy. Hook lives in `Manager.finish` at the top of the SERIALIZABLE + XactCommit branch, running BEFORE the WAL xact-marker hook, BEFORE `releaseSerializableLocked`, BEFORE `delete(m.active, handle)`. On detection the transaction stays in `m.active` and the caller MUST invoke `Manager.Rollback(tx)` to drive the actual abort (the executor's existing `execRollback` path is reused once M0104-0007 surfaces the typed error as `ExecError{Code: "40001"}`). Doom-the-pivot semantics match upstream's `SXACT_FLAG_DOOMED`: the pivot continues executing, only its next commit attempt fails — this is what makes the progress guarantee work, because aborting the pivot synchronously would let it retry and re-fail on the same anomaly while the committing xact's writes are still uncommitted. New typed error `mvcc.SerializationFailureError` (`Reason string`, `Error() string`, `SQLSTATE() string` returning `"40001"`) plus convenience predicate `mvcc.IsSerializationFailure(err error) bool` (errors.As wrapper) so executor callers can recognise the failure without type-asserting through the mvcc package. Test-only mutators `Manager.MarkDoomedForTest` / `Manager.IsDoomedForTest` expose the internal Doomed bit so the "self already doomed" branch can be pinned without driving a full 2-cycle setup; production callers must reach the bit through the pre-commit scan, never directly. Zero footprint for RC/RR (never registered in `ssiState.xacts` — single map probe exits) and for read-only SERIALIZABLE workloads (empty inConflicts slice — outer for-loop iterates zero times). Regression pins (`internal/mvcc/ssi_precommit_test.go`): `TestPreCommitCheck_NoOpForRC` (RC commit cleanly), `_NoOpForReadOnlySerializable` (empty-graph SERIALIZABLE commits cleanly — no false positive), `_AlreadyDoomedReturns40001` (MarkDoomedForTest + scan + SQLSTATE check + Commit propagates + Rollback drains active set), `_WriteSkewDoomsPivot` (canonical 2-cycle via write-path hook end-to-end through Manager.finish — pins the M0104 DoD anomaly pattern at the mvcc layer), `_ThreeNodeCycleDoomsPivot` (3-node `T0 -rw-> Pivot -rw-> Me` dooms Pivot on Me's commit; T0 commits cleanly; Pivot's later commit returns 40001), `_LinearChainIsSafe` (already-committed T0's edges scrubbed by M0104-0004 invariant; Me's scan finds nothing to doom; all three xacts commit cleanly — pins no false-positive on linear chains), `_FinishedPivotIgnored` (defensive — manually-stamped FinishedAt makes scan skip the pivot), `_IdempotentDoomedPivot` (second scan on same graph is no-op; pivot stays doomed; doomed pivot's commit still returns 40001). Out of scope (deferred to M0104-0007/beyond): executor read/write-path call-site wiring for `Manager.CheckForSerializableConflictOut` / `Manager.CheckForSerializableConflictIn` (currently public but no executor caller — M0104-0007 promotes deferred D-002 isolation specs and forces real integration); executor-level conversion of `*SerializationFailureError` to `ExecError{Code: "40001"}` in `execCommit` (the mvcc-level contract is the load-bearing piece; converting the wrap is mechanical); upstream's `OnConflict_CheckForSerializationFailure` per-edge synchronous check from `FlagRWConflict` (pre-commit scan is sufficient for the M0104 DoD; per-edge variant is a pure addition that the polarity-agnostic `registerRWConflictLocked` helper is the natural injection point for, with `SerializationFailureError.Reason` already future-proofed for the upstream "Canceled on conflict out to pivot %u" phrasing); post-commit retention so committed xacts stay conflict-relevant past `FinishedAt` (current first-slice substrate scrubs at finish; the scan is correct under that policy because it runs WHILE the committing xact is still addressable and BEFORE peer-scrub — boundary-case false negatives at "T0 commits, then Me's scan would have walked into T0" are acceptable because the structure required for that miss is already caught by the first-committing xact's own scan); READ ONLY distinct lifecycle (upstream's READ-ONLY-Tin optimisation skipped — conservative absence is only false positives, never false negatives); 2PC `PREPARE TRANSACTION` interactions (goopg has no 2PC yet — upstream's `SxactIsPrepared` branch is not relevant). |
| 0104-0007 | [Executor SSI Hook Wiring (M0104-0007)](0104-0007-executor-ssi-wiring.md) | accepted | Lands the executor-side wiring that turns the M0104-0003..0006 SSI substrate into actual SERIALIZABLE enforcement at the executor layer. New `internal/executor/ssi.go` exports three helpers — `ssiActive`, `ssiRecordTupleRead`, `ssiRecordTupleWrite`, `ssiPreCommitCheck` — each guarded by an inline isolation-level test so RC/RR readers/writers/commits pay one comparison and never touch the mvcc registry. The read-path helper calls `mvcc.Manager.AcquirePredicateLock(handle, TupleLockTag(...))` then `CheckForSerializableConflictOut(handle, writerXmin)`; the write-path helper calls `CheckForSerializableConflictIn(handle, TupleLockTag(...))`. Both filter `block == InvalidBlockNumber || slot == 0` before `mvcc.TupleLockTag` to absorb the slot-0 edge case seqScanOp surfaces via `curSlot - 1`. `ssiPreCommitCheck` wraps `mvcc.SerializationFailureError` as `*ExecError{Code: "40001"}` with the upstream "could not serialize access due to read/write dependencies among transactions" prefix. Wiring sites — read path: `seqScanOp.Next` post-visibility before decode (uses curSlot-1); `indexScanOp.Next` post-HOT-resolution after page RLock release (target = actualSlot, not the index-pointed slot). Write path: `insertOp.Next` for both non-partitioned and partition-routed paths after `writeHeapRowReturning` returns the new tuple's ItemPointer; `updateOp.Next` at a single post-EPQ-loop site so HOT and non-HOT both fire with the converged `pu.slot` (the rw-conflict target a concurrent SERIALIZABLE reader would have predicate-locked); `deleteOp.Next` after the `epqSkipDel` filter so concurrent-abort victims do not register a phantom rw-edge. Commit path: `transactionOp.execCommit` runs `ssiPreCommitCheck` AFTER the deferred-FK check and BEFORE `TxnMgr.Commit`; on detection it drives `TxnMgr.Rollback` + `EndExplicitTransaction` + `clearCtxTransaction` before returning the 40001 ExecError so the session exits the explicit-transaction state with rollback semantics and no commit record is burned. Out of scope (deferred to M0104-0008/beyond): non-user-facing visibility-check sites (FK enforcement, DDL scans, ANALYZE, MERGE, ON CONFLICT, apply-worker, TOAST chunk fetch — predicate-locking these would inflate the SIREAD set on every internal MVCC reader); index-target predicate locks for btree range-boundary phantom detection (the heap-grain wiring is sufficient for the M0104 DoD 2-cycle pattern); end-to-end SQL-driven write-skew tests through two concurrent sessions (staged for M0104-0008 oracle isolation-test promotion which owns the multi-session harness); upstream's `OnConflict_CheckForSerializationFailure` per-edge synchronous check (the polarity-agnostic `registerRWConflictLocked` helper is the natural injection point if latency-sensitive workloads need the earlier abort). Regression pins (`internal/executor/ssi_test.go`, 8 tests): `TestSSI_RecordTupleRead_NoOpForRC`, `_NoOpForRR`, `_AcquiresPredicateLockForSerializable` (read-path acquire + peer SERIALIZABLE write through write-path helper → R→W edge installed end-to-end), `_InvalidTagFiltered` (InvalidBlockNumber/slot==0 absorbed before TupleLockTag panics), `_ZeroHandleSkipped` (Handle==0 short-circuits even when isolation tag is SERIALIZABLE), `TestSSI_ExecCommit_ReturnsSerializationFailureWhenDoomed` (BEGIN SERIALIZABLE + MarkDoomedForTest + COMMIT → ExecError{Code:"40001"} + session cleared + ActiveCount==0), `TestSSI_ExecCommit_NoOpForRC`, `TestSSI_ExecCommit_HappyPathForSerializable` (no false positive on isolated SERIALIZABLE). |
| 0104-0008 | [SSI Oracle-Test Promotion & M0104 Closeout (M0104-0008)](0104-0008-ssi-promotion-and-closeout.md) | accepted | Closes M0104 by promoting applicable deferred SERIALIZABLE/SSI tests in `internal/testport` and evidencing DoD #2/#4. Lands `internal/testport/ssi_write_skew_test.go` — four pass-required multi-session SQL-driven Go tests that enact the canonical simple-write-skew permutations against a live goopg cluster: `TestPort_SSI_WriteSkew_NoOverlap_BothCommit` (sequential SERIALIZABLE — both commit), `_Overlap_SecondCommitterAborts` (rwx1 rwx2 c1 c2 → c2 returns SQLSTATE 40001), `_Overlap_FirstCommitterAborts` (rwx1 rwx2 c2 c1 → c1 returns SQLSTATE 40001), `_RC_NoSerializationFailure` (READ COMMITTED + REPEATABLE READ control — overlap shape MUST NOT trip 40001; guards the `ssiActive` short-circuit). Why focused Go tests instead of promoting upstream spec files: `framework.IsolationRunner` only runs spec-declared `permutation` directives — auto-permutation generation (the upstream `isolationtester.c` default) is not implemented, so `simple-write-skew.spec` and SSI-adjacent neighbours that omit explicit permutations cannot be driven through `TestPort_IsolationSuite` without a runner upgrade that itself owns the D-002 infrastructure; the focused Go tests express the same end-to-end shape against the wire protocol. Four load-bearing gap fixes uncovered by the new tests: (1) **`BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL <level>` was silently ignored on the simple-query path** — `internal/server/dispatch.go` `TxBegin` branch called `connTx.Begin(ctx.Tx)` on the placeholder RC tx allocated at dispatch entry, bypassing `transactionOp.execBegin` where M0096-0002's isolation-level parsing lives; fix promotes the placeholder by rolling it back and `Begin(parsedLvl)`-ing a fresh tx + snapshot when `txNode.IsolationLevel != ""` and the parsed level differs. (2) **`COMMIT` bypassed `ssiPreCommitCheck` on the simple-query path** — the same dispatcher called `TxnMgr.Commit(connTx.Tx())` directly without the pre-commit dangerous-structure walk; fix gates the dispatch-side commit on `PreCommitCheckForSerializationFailure(handle)` for SERIALIZABLE explicit transactions and translates a hit to wire-protocol SQLSTATE 40001 with the upstream wording, mirroring the executor path. (3) **`scanMatching` (UPDATE/DELETE inner scan) didn't fire the SSI read hook** — `updateOp` / `deleteOp` use a separate heap-page walk (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go`) that never went through seqScanOp.Next where M0104-0007 added `ssiRecordTupleRead`; fix invokes the hook immediately after visibility (before predicate filter) so the SIREAD covers every tuple the UPDATE/DELETE ever observed, matching upstream `predicate.c` semantics. (4) **`ssiRecordTupleRead` only checked the visible writer's xmin** — original wiring missed the write-skew shape where the reader's MVCC snapshot hides the concurrent writer's new version and the OLD version it sees carries the concurrent writer's XID in `tuple.Header.Xmax`; fix extends helper signature to take `writerXmax storage.TransactionID` and invokes `CheckForSerializableConflictOut(handle, xmax)` whenever `xmax != xmin`. Three call sites (seqScanOp, indexScanOp, scanMatching) pass `tuple.Header.Xmax` alongside `tuple.Header.Xmin`. Together the fixes close the 2-cycle write-skew end-to-end: s1's UPDATE seqScan installs SIREAD on every tuple observed (including slot=1 'apple'); s2's UPDATE writes slot=1, which fires `CheckForSerializableConflictIn` and installs the s1→s2 edge; s2's UPDATE seqScan reads slot=2 whose `xmax = s1.XID` because s1 marked it for update, which fires `CheckForSerializableConflictOut(s2, s1.XID)` and installs the s2→s1 edge; pre-commit on the second committer walks `me.inConflicts`, finds the 2-cycle through the pivot's `inConflicts`, and the second committer's `Doomed` flag is set so its `COMMIT` returns SQLSTATE 40001. Files touched: new `internal/testport/ssi_write_skew_test.go`; `internal/executor/ssi.go` (helper signature + xmax conflict-out); `internal/executor/operators_storage.go` (scanMatching read hook + seqScanOp.Next xmax); `internal/executor/operators_index.go` (indexScanOp.Next xmax); `internal/executor/ssi_test.go` (existing call sites updated to pass `storage.InvalidTransactionID` for xmax — no behaviour change to MarkDoomedForTest-driven pins); `internal/server/dispatch.go` (TxBegin isolation-level promotion + TxCommit SSI pre-commit check). Regression gates: `go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/mvcc/ ./internal/server/` all green; `go test -run TestPort_SSI_WriteSkew ./internal/testport/` 4/4 pass. Out of scope: auto-permutation in `framework.IsolationRunner`; `SHOW transaction_isolation` GUC tracking the active transaction's isolation level (currently always reads the session-default GUC; new tests do not depend on it — SQLSTATE assertion is load-bearing); READ-ONLY DEFERRABLE optimisation; `OnConflict_CheckForSerialization`; index-target predicate locks for btree range-boundary phantoms (staged per M0104 milestone doc's "Out of Scope" section). |
| 0104-0001 | [Serializable (SSI) Foundation and GUC Parity (M0104)](0104-0001-serializable-ssi-foundation.md) | accepted | M0104-0002 landed 2026-05-14: `mvcc.SerializableXact` introduced in `internal/mvcc/ssi.go` as the goopg analogue of PostgreSQL's `SERIALIZABLEXACT` (`src/include/storage/predicate_internals.h`). `mvcc.Manager` gains an embedded handle-keyed `ssiState` registry (lazy-init: RC/RR workloads stay zero-cost) plus a monotonic `CommitSeqNo` allocator. `Manager.Begin` registers a fresh `SerializableXact` for `IsolationSerializable` only (RC/RR skip), `Manager.AssignXID` stamps the new top-level XID onto `SerializableXact.XID` so future conflict-detection paths can look up xacts by writer XID, and `Manager.finish` releases on both commit and abort by stamping `FinishedAt` with a dense, monotonically-increasing `CommitSeqNo`. Empty placeholder slots for `inConflicts` / `outConflicts` / `predicateLocks` keep the struct shape stable so M0104-0003..0005 can fill them in without changing the lifecycle API. Regression pins (`internal/mvcc/ssi_test.go`): `TestSerializableXact_BeginRegistersAndCommitReleases`, `TestSerializableXact_RollbackAlsoReleases`, `TestSerializableXact_AssignXIDStampsTopXid`, `TestSerializableXact_NotRegisteredForRCorRR`, `TestSerializableXact_CommitSeqNoMonotonic`. M0104-0001 landed 2026-05-14: `mvcc.IsolationSerializable` is now a distinct enum constant from `IsolationRepeatableRead`, `ParseIsolationLevel("serializable")` returns it without aliasing, and `mvcc.Manager.Begin` + `executor.BasicSession.SetIsolationLevel` accept the new tag so BEGIN ISOLATION LEVEL SERIALIZABLE / SET TRANSACTION ISOLATION LEVEL SERIALIZABLE round-trip the new isolation onto the open transaction (`Transaction.Isolation == IsolationSerializable`). Snapshot acquisition still reuses the RR pinned-snapshot branch deliberately — this is the SI half of SSI; the predicate-lock + rw-edge overlay lands in M0104-0003..0005 without changing snapshot semantics. `default_transaction_isolation` and `transaction_isolation` GUCs already accepted `"serializable"` (PG EnumOptions parity). Regression pins: `internal/mvcc/manager_test.go::TestParseIsolationLevel` + `TestSerializableDistinctFromRepeatableRead` and `internal/executor/transaction_test.go::TestTransactionBeginSerializableSession`. Establishes PostgreSQL-aligned SERIALIZABLE delivery scope for goopg: canonical GUC naming (`default_transaction_isolation`, `transaction_isolation`), separation from REPEATABLE READ runtime, predicate-lock (SIREAD) target model, rw-conflict tracking hooks, and pre-commit dangerous-structure abort policy with SQLSTATE 40001. References `guc_tables.c`, `xact.c`, and `predicate.c` as the upstream oracle for naming and SSI semantics. |

| 0100-0005 loop-14 | [Loop-14 PG Physical Format Fixes (numeric encode/decode, function attrs, bitmap decoder)](0100-0005-loop14-pg-physical-format-fixes.md) | accepted | Three correctness fixes: (A) `encodeValuePG`/`decodePhysicalPGValueMctx` numeric case — was storing empty varlena, causing accounts table rows to be silently skipped; (B) CREATE FUNCTION/PROCEDURE attribute parsing (IMMUTABLE/VOLATILE/STABLE/etc.) before AS $$body$$; (C) `DecodeRowIntoMctxPGTuple` — bitmap+natts aware decoder for ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN, used by seqScanOp and updateViaIndex. eval-plan-qual progresses from 1199→1257/1494 matching output lines. |
| 0106-0010 batched-36 | [PG client backend SEGFAULTs reading user-table heap pages (M0106-0010 batched-36)](0106-0010-batched-36-pg-tuple-format-segfault.md) | accepted (batched-55 closes async failover) | After batched-35 landed canonical XLOG_HEAP_INSERT/DELETE/UPDATE WAL for user-table DML, `TestE2E_FailoverGoopgToPG/async` advances further: PG standby boots, walreceiver connects at `0/1000000`, apply LSN advances `0/100A2B8 → 0/100E398`. Test still fails — PG client backend segfaults executing `SELECT count(*) FROM public.bench_log WHERE client = -999`; postmaster crash-restarts the cluster in a 30s loop. Root cause: the FPI in the canonical heap-insert WAL still contains goopg-format heap-tuple bytes (incompatible `t_ctid` block encoding and/or `t_hoff`/null-bitmap alignment), so PG segfaults trying to deform a `(int,text)` tuple. Four partial fixes landed this loop: (1) `internal/wal/iterator.go` segment-boundary anchor (boundary LSN `pos = startLSN-1` was landing in the prior segment), (2) `internal/wal/format.go` reverts the spurious `prevPG-1` mapping (xl_prev was already 0-based at the caller), (3) `internal/executor/operators_storage.go` switches `writeHeapRowReturning` to `EncodeRowPG`+`NullBitmapPG` when `ctx.LogCanonical != nil` (+ adds `writeHeapRowReturningPG` for catalog rows), (4) `internal/server/replication.go` breaks a `replyStartReplication` ↔ receiver livelock on WAL iterator error. Next loop (batched-37): bytes-on-page audit via `pg_filedump` against the goopg primary's `bench_log` heap to localise the encoding gap. |
| 0106-0010 batched-15 | [pg_type expanded from 37 to 194 rows (M0106-0010 batched-15)](0106-0010-batched-15-pg-type-all-entries.md) | accepted | Expands `bootstrapPgTypeTuples` from 37 to 194 rows (113 base types from `pg_type.dat` + 83 array peers, minus 2 without `array_type_oid`). New code generator `cmd/gen-pg-type-data/main.go` parses `pg_type.dat` + `pg_proc.dat` and emits `pg_type_seed_data.go::pgTypeAllEntries()` (193 entries). `bootstrapPgTypeTuples` now merges all generated entries with the existing `pgTypeCanonical` fallback for goopg-specific OIDs (e.g. OID 10028 `_pg_statistic`). Return type changed from `[]heapTID` to `map[uint32]heapTID` (keyed by type OID) so `bootstrapPgTypeOidIndex` can derive the correct (oid, tid) mapping without a parallel-array assumption. Regression pins: `TestPgTypeAllEntriesCountAndCoverage` (count == 193; critical OIDs present), `TestPgTypeAllEntriesTypalignValid` (all `typalign` ∈ {c,s,i,d}, all `typstorage` ∈ {p,e,x,m}). Verified: all pg_type tests PASS; `internal/initdb/` baseline (17 pre-existing failures) unchanged; cross-package smoke `executor/server/storage/catalog/mvcc` PASS. |
| 0106-0011 crash-mid-tx-clog-implicit-abort | [Crash-Mid-Transaction Catalog Heap Filter (Implicit Abort) (M0106-0011)](0106-0011-crash-mid-tx-clog-implicit-abort.md) | accepted | Closes `TestCrashMidTransactionTableNotVisibleAfterRestart`, the implicit-abort sibling of the rollback path. Two distinct root causes: (1) `wal/recovery.ApplyRecord` was misrouting PG-canonical 88-byte `XLOG_CHECKPOINT_SHUTDOWN` records as goopg-native btree-newroot decodes whenever the redo-LSN low byte happened to equal `RecordKindBtreeNewRoot=0x18`, surfacing as "wal: btree-newroot trailing bytes (68 remaining)" during crash WAL replay; (2) `loadUserTablesFromHeap`'s loop-30 filter only excluded explicit `TxnStatusAborted` rows, but a crashed-in-progress xid leaves the clog slot at `TxnStatusUnknown`, so the row passed through. Three-part fix: `ApplyRecord` now prefers `replayDecodedXLogRecord` whenever `r.XLog.Header.Rmid/Info` carries a structured PG classification (any non-`xlogInfoDefault`); new `mvcc.CLog.MarkUnknownAsAborted(highXID)` stamps every still-Unknown slot in `[1, highXID)` as Aborted (PG's "non-Committed CLOG slot ⇒ not committed" semantics); `Open()` adds a `highestCatalogXID(mgr, cat)` scan of `pg_class`/`pg_attribute` heap pages so the sweep bound is the actual on-disk max xmin (not the stale snapshot `NextXID`). Same 15 pre-existing baseline failures remain on master after the flip (was 16 — `TestCrashMidTransactionTableNotVisibleAfterRestart` FAIL→PASS); no new regressions in `executor`/`catalog`/`mvcc`/`server`/`wal`. Supersedes [0106-0011-rollback-catalog-rows-clog-filter.md](0106-0011-rollback-catalog-rows-clog-filter.md) for the implicit-abort half of M0106-0011. |
| 0106-0011 ddl-relcache-inval-coverage | [DDL coverage for `RelcacheInvalPending` (M0106-0011)](0106-0011-ddl-relcache-inval-coverage.md) | accepted | Extends the M0106-0010 batched-31 commit-time relcache-inval refresh hook to DROP TABLE, ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN, CREATE INDEX, and DROP INDEX. Previously only CREATE TABLE (via `syncTableToCatalogHeap`) and VACUUM-of-nailed-catalogs flagged `mvcc.Manager.relcacheInvalPending`; a PG18 standby reconnecting after the other DDL paths would keep stale relcache entries (dropped relation still resolved, ADD COLUMN attribute count off-by-one, CREATE INDEX invisible on the parent). Four call-sites in `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go` now set the flag after a successful catalog mutation: `dropTableByRef`, `execAlterTableAddColumn`, `syncIndexToCatalogHeap`, and `execDropIndex` (the latter gated on `flagInval` so `IF EXISTS no_such_idx` stays no-op). Regression pin: `TestDDLPathsFlagRelcacheInvalPending` in `internal/executor/operators_ddl_relcache_inval_test.go` with 4 subtests covering DROP TABLE, ALTER ADD COLUMN, DROP INDEX, and the IF-EXISTS-miss no-op path. Verified: `executor` PASS 1.2s; `mvcc`/`catalog`/`server` PASS; `internal/initdb/` baseline of 15 pre-existing failures unchanged from loop 31; `internal/wal/` 2 pre-existing failures unchanged. Open follow-ups under M0106-0011: persist DROP / ALTER mutations to the pg_class / pg_attribute heap (currently only the in-memory catalog is touched); checkpoint/shutdown init-file refresh for catcache-only churn. |
| 0106-0011 rollback-catalog-rows-clog-filter | [Rollback Catalog Heap Row Filter (Clog-Aware) (M0106-0011)](0106-0011-rollback-catalog-rows-clog-filter.md) | accepted | Closes `TestRollbackedTableNotVisibleAfterRestart` after the M0106-0010 batched chain rewired runtime CREATE TABLE to emit PG18-canonical `pg_class` / `pg_attribute` rows. The previous `loadUserTablesFromHeap` clog-status filter was skipped entirely whenever the physical decoder branch succeeded — intended for trusted PG basebackup tuples, but it now silently passes through goopg-emitted rows from rolled-back transactions whose `xmin` is correctly marked Aborted in the local clog. Fix: `internal/initdb/open.go` adds an explicit `clog.GetStatus(xmin) == TxnStatusAborted → continue` gate ahead of the existing physical/native branch on both the pg_class and pg_attribute scans. The basebackup pass-through stays intact (upstream xids return `TxnStatusUnknown`, not `TxnStatusAborted`) and the M0030-0007 crash-during-COMMIT safety net for goopg-native rows is preserved. Avoids the `btree-newroot trailing bytes (68 remaining)` WAL framing regression that the original `stampCatalogRows` heap-delete approach surfaces under `PageHeaders=true`. Same 16 pre-existing baseline failures remain on master; one test flips FAIL → PASS, no new regressions in `executor`/`catalog`/`mvcc`/`server`/`wal`. |
| 0106-0013 | [CLOG Crash-Recovery and XID Horizon (M0106-0013)](0106-0013-clog-recovery-and-xid-horizon.md) | accepted | Fixes crash-recovery durability gap: (1) `EnablePGSLRUMirror` now reads on-disk SLRU into `c.data` so the fsynced SLRU wins over a stale/truncated flat-file; (2) `txnMgr.NextXID` is advanced past `clog.HighestKnownXID()+1` so snapshots have Xmax ≥ all committed user-table INSERT XIDs; (3) `replayCLogFromWAL` second-pass WAL scan stamps clog from commit/abort records (mirrors PG's `xact_redo_commit` semantics). Re-enables `TestKillKillRecovery` and `TestPort_Recovery013CrashRestart`. |
