| 0107-0001-mctx | [mctx: PG-style Memory-Context Substrate Phase A (M0107-0001)](0107-0001-mctx-memory-context-substrate.md) | accepted | Creates `internal/mctx` hierarchical bump-allocator; deletes `executor.Arena` / `arenaRegistry`; ports `seqScanOp`, `operators_ddl` and `Datum.arena` to `*mctx.Context`; wires SessionCtx→StmtCtx lifecycle in serveConn / dispatchSimpleQueryViaExecutor. All 9 modified packages pass with -race. |
| 0107-0002 | [Datum 48 B: ArenaID + KindArena Merge (M0107-0002)](0107-0002-datum-48b-arena-id-merge.md) | accepted | Reduces `Datum` from 64 B (3 GC-traced fields) to 48 B (1 GC-traced field, nil for hot-path arena rows). Changes: `DatumKind` int→uint8; `mctx *Context`→`ArenaID uint16`; `Big *big.Int` removed (stored in mctx); `KindStringArena`/`KindBytesArena` merged into `KindString`/`KindBytes` (ArenaID≠0 signals mctx-backed). Hot-path arena rows: 0 GC-traced pointers per Datum. All core packages pass with -race. |
| 0107-0008 | [Phase D5 — `runtimeshim` Package + `Nanotime` Shim (M0107-0008, loop 1)](0107-0008-runtimeshim-nanotime.md) | accepted | Introduces `internal/runtimeshim` with the first `//go:linkname` shim. `Nanotime() int64` binds `runtime.nanotime` (Go `go1.24 && !go1.27`) with a `time.Now().UnixNano()` fallback under the inverse tag. Benchmark: 20.54 ns/op on Linux/amd64 vs ~50 ns for `time.Now()`. Race-clean unit tests cover monotonicity (`1 << 16` reads), wall-elapsed sanity (50 ms sleep ∈ [25 ms, 500 ms]), and a non-zero smoke. Call-site wiring (activity registry) deferred to a later loop. PG-compat — none (Go-runtime accessor; no on-disk effect). |
| 0107-0008b | [Phase D5 — `runtimeshim` PinP/UnpinP Shim (M0107-0008, loop 2)](0107-0008b-runtimeshim-pinp.md) | accepted | Adds the second `internal/runtimeshim` primitive: `PinP() int` / `UnpinP()` binding `runtime.procPin` / `runtime.procUnpin` under `go1.24 && !go1.27`, with a `sync.Mutex`-serialised fallback under the inverse tag. The linkname path is the same primitive `sync.Pool` uses to access per-P caches with zero atomics inside the pinned window. Benchmark: 2.067 ns/op for the pair on Linux/amd64 — below the parent design's ~3 ns target. Race-clean tests cover index range, nesting stability, balanced cycles across 32 goroutines, and a canonical per-P-counter pattern (16 goroutines × 16 K iterations, final sum == total). Caller wiring (per-P xid cache, per-P stats counters) deferred to subsequent loops. PG-compat — none (Go-runtime accessor; no on-disk effect). |
| 0107-0008d | [Phase D5 — Per-P XID Cache Caller Is Incompatible with Snapshot Semantics (M0107-0008, loop 4)](0107-0008d-perp-xidcache-snapshot-incompat.md) | accepted | Records the finding from loop 4: the per-P XID allocator cache called out in `perf-optimize/08-runtime-internals.md §4 "Use case 1"` cannot be wired into `internal/mvcc/XidGen` without a parallel rewrite of `captureSnapshot`. Per-P caching breaks (a) cross-backend xid monotonicity and (b) the `Snapshot.Xmax`-as-upper-bound-of-all-issued-xids invariant. Both candidate `Peek` definitions (min cached `next`, or `global.Load()`) trade one set of broken visibility invariants for another. The XID-cache caller is removed from M0107-0008 scope; the other two callers (Nanotime → activity registry, Sema → bufpool per-slot wait, per-P stats counters) remain in scope. The rolled-back implementation lived only in loop-4's working tree; the post-revert tree passes the full `mvcc`/`server`/`executor`/`wal`/`storage`/`runtimeshim` regression. |
| 0107-0008c | [Phase D5 — `runtimeshim` SemaAcquire/SemaRelease Shim (M0107-0008, loop 3)](0107-0008c-runtimeshim-sema.md) | accepted | Adds the third `internal/runtimeshim` primitive: `SemaAcquire(*uint32)` / `SemaRelease(*uint32)` binding `sync.runtime_Semacquire` / `sync.runtime_Semrelease` under `go1.24 && !go1.27`, with a global `sync.Mutex` + lazy `map[*uint32]*sync.Cond` fallback under the inverse tag. Non-handoff release (`handoff=false, skipframes=0`) matches `sync.Mutex.Unlock`'s call site — the right default for the [[06-bufpool-lockfree]] per-slot "I/O finished; any waiter may proceed" pattern. Benchmark: 5.6 ns/op for the uncontended pair (cell stays positive, no goroutine park). Race-clean tests cover the pre-released no-block path, block-then-wake correctness, 8-producer × 8-consumer balanced pairing with final `*s == 0` invariant, and per-cell wait-queue isolation (Release on cell B never wakes a waiter on cell A). Bufpool caller wiring deferred to a separate loop so the shim's contract is validated standalone. PG-compat — none (Go-runtime accessor; no on-disk effect). |
| 0107-0008e | [Phase D5 — Activity Registry `runtimeshim.Nanotime` Wiring (M0107-0008, loop 5)](0107-0008e-activity-registry-nanotime-wiring.md) | accepted | First Phase-D5 caller landing: `ActivityRegistry`'s five `time.Now().UnixNano()` call sites (WaitEventStart/End, UpdateState, BeginTransaction/EndTransaction, acquire) now go through `runtimeshim.Nanotime()` — ~20 ns vs ~50 ns/op, exercised on every protocol-frame boundary (~c × 30 k/s in pgbench c=100 SU). `stateChange`/`XactStart`/`QueryStart` are stored as monotonic-since-runtime-start nanos; `Snapshot()` converts back to wall-clock via a once-at-construction `(monoEpoch, wallEpoch)` pair (`wall = wallEpoch + (mono - monoEpoch)`) before `formatNanos` so `pg_stat_activity` continues to emit RFC3339Nano timestamps. The `mono == 0 → 0` guard preserves cold-field empty-string semantics. New regression `TestActivityRegistryStateChangeIsWallClock` asserts the converted timestamp parses as RFC3339Nano within ±2 s of `time.Now()`. PG-compat — none (display-only timing; no on-disk effect; pg_stat_activity wire timestamps unchanged). |
| 0107-0008f | [Phase D5 — `internal/stats` per-P `Counter` Primitive (M0107-0008, loop 6)](0107-0008f-perp-stats-counter.md) | accepted | New `internal/stats` package with a single public type `Counter` — an additive `int64` counter sharded across `maxShards = 256` cache-line-padded `atomic.Int64` slots via `runtimeshim.PinP`/`UnpinP`. `Add(delta)` is two function calls plus an atomic add inside the pinned window (no contention possible on the linkname path); `Sum()` does 256 atomic loads on the cold path; `Reset()` does 256 atomic stores. `atomic.Int64` inside the pinned window (not plain `int64`) so a concurrent `Sum` reader sees a well-defined value. Five race-clean tests cover single-goroutine round trip, `Reset`, concurrent-Add total-exact (32 g × 16 K = 524 288 Adds, final Sum exact), per-shard write distribution (GOMAXPROCS≥2), and Sum-vs-Add no-torn-read invariant. `BenchmarkCounterAdd-16` 0.8 ns/op on the parallel path (16 cores). Migration of specific global atomic counters to `stats.Counter` deferred per-consumer-family to subsequent loops. Finishes the parent chapter's two `PinP` consumers (the per-P xid cache was ruled out in [[0107-0008d]]). PG-compat — none (Go-process-internal counter; no on-disk format, wire protocol, or catalog touch). |
| 0107-0008j | [Phase D5 — AIO Per-Direction Counters via `stats.Counter` (M0107-0008, loop 10)](0107-0008j-aio-per-direction-stats-counter-wiring.md) | accepted | Fourth concrete consumer of [[0107-0008f]]: the AIO `Engine`'s per-direction submit/complete/error trio (`readSubmitted`/`readCompleted`/`readErrored`/`writeSubmitted`/`writeCompleted`/`writeErrored`) and the two per-direction latency-sum counters (`readLatencySumMicros`, `writeLatencySumMicros`) move from `atomic.Uint64` to `stats.Counter`. Closes the consistency-shape asymmetry [[0107-0008i]] introduced — both sides of `Submitted == ReadSubmitted + WriteSubmitted` are now eventual-consistent across 256 shards instead of one side being seq-consistent on a single line. `readLatencyMaxMicros`/`writeLatencyMaxMicros` stay `atomic.Uint64` (advanceMax needs CompareAndSwap; per-shard max is meaningless for monotonic-forward clamping). Per-target `*targetStats` defers — naturally sharded by target identity, and migration would inflate each record from ~48 B to ~80 KiB; cost/benefit deferred to a dedicated loop. `Stats()` reads use `uint64(.Sum())` casts at the boundary so all eight `Stats.{Read,Write}{Submitted,Completed,Errored,LatencySumMicros}` field types are preserved verbatim. Memory cost is 128 KiB per server (8 × 16 KiB Counter; on top of [[0107-0008i]]'s 48 KiB = 176 KiB total per server, still flat). Existing `internal/aio/aio_test.go` covers all eight migrated counters via the public `Stats()` API. PG-compat — none (goopg `pg_stat_io` column shape unchanged; no on-disk/WAL/wire change). |
| 0107-0008i | [Phase D5 — AIO `Engine` Aggregate Totals via `stats.Counter` (M0107-0008, loop 9)](0107-0008i-aio-engine-totals-stats-counter-wiring.md) | accepted | Third concrete consumer of [[0107-0008f]] (after [[0107-0008g]] btree and [[0107-0008h]] MemRing): the AIO `Engine`'s three aggregate totals — `submitted`, `completed`, `errored` — move from `atomic.Uint64` to `stats.Counter`. Hot path is `Engine.Submit` (one bump) and `Engine.finishHandle` (one bump + conditional error bump), called on every buffer-pool/WAL/walsender/checkpointer I/O — multi-P contention from the `method=worker` goroutine pool. Cold-path reader is `Engine.Stats()` only, feeding goopg's `pg_stat_io` view; uses `uint64(.Sum())` casts at the boundary so the `Stats.Submitted/Completed/Errored uint64` field types are preserved verbatim. Per-direction (read/write split), per-target (`*targetStats`), and latency `SumMicros`/`MaxMicros` fields are explicitly out of scope this loop — they couple to a wider `pg_stat_io` view-shape unification and migrate together in a later loop per [[0107-0008h]]'s "Why not a smaller change" decision. `inFlight`, `nextID`, and the latency-Max fields remain `atomic.*` (Max needs CAS; inFlight is a signed gauge against the inflight map; nextID is a monotonic id allocator, not a counter). Memory cost is 48 KiB per server (3 × 16 KiB Counter; exactly one Engine per server). Existing `internal/aio/aio_test.go` covers the migrated counters via the public `Stats()` API end-to-end. PG-compat — none (goopg `pg_stat_io` column shape unchanged; no on-disk/WAL/wire change). |
| 0107-0008h | [Phase D5 — WAL `MemRing` Hit/Miss Counters via `stats.Counter` (M0107-0008, loop 8)](0107-0008h-memring-stats-counter-wiring.md) | accepted | Second concrete consumer of [[0107-0008f]] (after the loop-7 btree counters): `MemRing.hits` / `.misses` move from a pair of `atomic.Uint64` fields touched via `.Add(1)` / `.Load()` to a pair of `stats.Counter` values. Hot path is `(*MemRing).ReadAt` — once per WAL record streamed by every walsender goroutine; multi-P contention when ≥2 subscribers stream concurrently (M0094-0005 hot-read E2E, M0102 heterogeneous-replication failover, any cascading-replica deployment). Public API (`Hits() uint64`, `Misses() uint64`) preserved verbatim via a single `uint64(.Sum())` cast at the boundary; `pg_stat_wal_io` / `pg_stat_replication.send_buffer_*` view callers (`internal/initdb/{wal_io_views,replication_views}.go`) unaffected. No `Reset()` is exposed by `MemRing` (counters are read-only-after-construction in production), so `stats.Counter.Reset()` is unused here. Memory cost is 32 KiB per server (2 × 16 KiB Counter; exactly one MemRing per server). Existing `internal/wal/mem_ring_test.go` already covers the counter contract end-to-end through the public API — hit-simple, miss-after-eviction, partial-overlap, nil-safe; the walsender-integration test at line 202 asserts the bump through the full `Writer → ReadAt → walsender` path. PG-compat — none (goopg-specific observability surface; no on-disk/WAL/wire change). |
| 0107-0008g | [Phase D5 — Btree Write-Path Counters via `stats.Counter` (M0107-0008, loop 7)](0107-0008g-btree-stats-counter-wiring.md) | accepted | First concrete consumer of [[0107-0008f]]: `(*BTree)` `Inserts` and `Splits` counters move from `atomic.AddUint64` / `LoadUint64` / `StoreUint64` on a shared `BTreeStats` field to a private `btreeStatsCounters{ inserts, splits stats.Counter }` field. Hot path (`Insert`, called once per index tuple — 22.7 K/s at the M0055 baseline bench) bumps the local P's shard with no cross-core cache-line invalidation; `Stats()` returns `BTreeStats{Inserts: uint64(.Sum()), Splits: uint64(.Sum())}` so the public snapshot type and field set are byte-identical to pre-migration callers. `ResetStats()` calls `.Reset()` on each. Memory cost is 32 KiB per BTree (2 × 16 KiB Counter); bounded by index count, not row count. M0055 baseline regression (100 K inserts → exact counter totals) passes under `-race` end-to-end. PG-compat — none (debug/regression instrumentation; no wire/catalog exposure). |
| 0107-0006 | [Phase D3 — bufmap & FPI Correctness Fixes (M0107-0006, loop 1)](0107-0006-bufpool-bufmap-correctness.md) | accepted | Three correctness fixes in the partially-landed Phase D3 lock-free buffer pool: (1) bufmap `packVal` shifted by +1 to avoid collision with `bufmapTombstone=1` (slot 0, gen 1 case); (2) drop Lookup's Robin-Hood early-exit (Insert is plain linear probing — early-exit was invalid); (3) `fpiSinceCheckpoint` → `atomic.Bool` to eliminate `contentMu` self-deadlock in `MarkDirty` callers that already hold the slot lock. Storage tests now pass with `-race`. pgbench TPS / mutex top-20 gates remain pending. |
| 0107-0006c | [Phase D3 — PinNew Stress Coverage (M0107-0006, loop 3)](0107-0006-pinnew-stress-coverage.md) | accepted | Adds `TestPoolPinNewVsPinStress` to close the coverage gap left by loops 1-2: the heap-extension path (`Pool.PinNew → Manager.Extend → bm.Insert`) is now exercised concurrently with cache-hit `Pin`/`Unpin` against an over-subscribed 32-slot pool. 4 writer goroutines drive PinNew while N readers (default 32; gate-tunable via `GOOPG_BUFPOOL_STRESS_GOROUTINES`) Pin random blocks from `[0, highestBlock)`. PG-compat — no production-code change; pure regression-pin work that gives M0107-0007 a signal for the contentMu-held Extend region. PASS clean under `-race` at 500 readers × 4 writers × 10 s and across `storage`/`mvcc`/`wal`/`btree`. |
| 0107-0006b | [Phase D3 — bufmap Atomic-Keys Refactor (M0107-0006, loop 2)](0107-0006-bufmap-keys-atomic.md) | accepted | New 1000-goroutine `TestPoolHighConcurrencyPinUnpinStress` surfaced two data races in the loop-1 bufmap: `compact()` rewriting `keys[i]` non-atomically against concurrent Lookups, and ABA on `Insert(tombstone→live)` racing a Lookup that read `keys[h]` on the previous live val. Fix: each bucket stores `key0`/`key1`/`val` as `atomic.Uint64`; `inner atomic.Pointer[bufmapInner]` swap on compact; seqlock-style Lookup (re-load val after key reads); `Insert` parks val at tombstone before rewriting keys. `-race` clean at 1000 goroutines × 5 s and across `storage`/`mvcc`/`wal`/`btree`. |
| 0107-0005 | [Phase D2 — Per-Backend ActivityRegistry with Atomic Hot Path (M0107-0005)](0107-0005-activity-registry-per-backend-slots.md) | accepted | Replaces `activity.Registry` single `RWMutex` (~91% of c=100 mutex delay) with per-backend 64 B cache-line-aligned `activitySlot` array. `WaitEventStart`/`WaitEventEnd` become O(1) atomic `uint32` stores (no mutex). `type Registry = ActivityRegistry` preserves backward compat. Background workers use fixed slots via `RegisterBackground`. Goroutine map updated to store `(reg, procNum)` instead of `(reg, pid)`. Expected: c=100 SO ≥ 10 000 TPS (vs 6 400). All activity/executor/server packages pass with -race. |
| 0107-0004 | [Phase D1 — ProcArray + Atomic XidGen + CLOG Bank Locks (M0107-0004)](0107-0004-procarray-xidgen-clog-bank-locks.md) | accepted | Replaces `mvcc.Manager.mu` hot path with lock-free `ProcArray` (64 B/slot) + `XidGen` (atomic). `SnapshotFor` now a lock-free slot walk; `Begin`/`Commit` do atomic slot ops only. CLOG sharded into 128 K-XID banks (per-bank `RWMutex`). `OldestXmin` skips idle slots to avoid zero-xmin vacuum anchor bug. Variadic `Begin(iso, procNums ...int32)` preserves backward compat for tests. All 11 key packages pass with -race. |
| 0107-0003 | [Phase C.1 — Concrete-Type Volcano Executor OpNode Framework (M0107-0003)](0107-0003-phase-c1-opnode-concrete-executor.md) | accepted | Phase C.1 of the concrete-executor refactor: lands `OpNode` sum-type, concrete `Slot` struct (implements `TupleSlot`/`SlotView`), `opNext` switch dispatcher, and `opAdapter` shim for non-migrated operators. `BuildFast`/`RunFast` drop-in replacements for `Build`/`Run`. SeqScan/Filter/Project/Limit migrated to concrete dispatch (no per-row itab call). All other 37 operators on adapter. Key design choice: `state any` (not raw bytes) for GC safety until Phase C.3 moves plan trees into mctx. `HasRow` flag distinguishes DML nil-rows from empty-column real rows. All executor/server packages pass with -race; 13 new regression tests. |
| 0107-0001 | [M0105/M0106 PG-Compatibility Invariants (DO-NOT-BREAK Catalog) (M0107)](0107-0001-m0106-pg-compat-invariants.md) | accepted | Catalog of every on-disk file format, byte-equivalent Go struct, catalog heap-tuple row layout, B-tree index, and WAL record format that M0105 + M0106 locked to PostgreSQL 18. Cited by every M0107 sub-milestone's DoD: no perf-optimize refactor (mctx, pointer-free Datum, concrete executor, ProcArray/CLOG-bank, per-backend activity, lock-free bufpool, WAL stripe locks + FSM, runtime `//go:linkname`) may silently modify any artefact in this catalog without a follow-up milestone that updates this doc in the same PR. Companion to the `perf-optimize/` bundle. |
| 0100-0005-lcku | [lockRowsOp: committed-update CTID chain follow + RR/SER 40001 (M0100-0005)](0100-0005-lockrows-committed-update-chain-follow.md) | accepted | `stampLock` now waits for in-progress xmax, follows CTID chain to live successor under RC, and raises 40001 under RR/SER. Fixes `TestPort_IsolationLockCommittedKeyupdate` (PASS count 10→11). |
| 0100-0005-loop17 | [Loop-17 MERGE NOT MATCHED INSERT parity + failed-transaction state (M0100-0005)](0100-0005-loop17-merge-not-matched-insert-and-failed-tx.md) | accepted | MERGE NOT MATCHED INSERT: partition routing, BEFORE INSERT triggers, unique-constraint wait (checkUniqueIndexesForInsert), index maintenance. MERGE MATCHED UPDATE EPQ "row gone" → errMergeSourceUnmatched → source row retries NOT MATCHED path. isLiveForUniqueCheck: HasAbortedXID arm added. Failed-transaction state (25P02) in connTxState. TestPort_IsolationMergeInsertUpdate: PASS. PASS count 11→12. |
| 0100-0005-dml-cte | [DML CTE Support (WITH MERGE RETURNING) (M0100-0005 Loop-20)](0100-0005-dml-cte-with-merge-returning.md) | accepted | Allow INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/MERGE as CTE bodies in parser. Plan DML CTEs via new `CTEDMLPrefix`/`MaterializedCTEScan` plan nodes. MERGE RETURNING: `Merge.Returning/ReturningSchema`; `mergeOp` collects RETURNING rows. `cteDMLPrefixOp` executes DML CTEs before outer query, materializing results in `ctx.MaterializedCTEs`. `mergeApplyUpdate` gains moved-partition sentinel check. MergeMatchRecheck: 489→501/503 lines. |
| 0100-0005-loop21 | [Loop-21 Inline NOTICE Delivery via NoticeFlush (M0100-0005)](0100-0005-loop21-notice-flush-inline-delivery.md) | accepted | Wire `ctx.NoticeFlush` in dispatch.go to immediately write+flush `NoticeResponse` inline during query execution. `AddNotice` short-circuits (no buffering) when `NoticeFlush` is set, so pre-blocking NOTICEs arrive at the pq client before blockDetectWait fires. Remove `queue.drain()` from `execStepFromQueue` to prevent clearing in-flight re-evaluation notices. Fix unused-step-name alphabetical ordering. eval-plan-qual first divergence L394→L411; eval-plan-qual-trigger L4→L38. No regression in 14 passing tests. |
| 0100-0005-loop22 | [Loop-22 MERGE EPQ Delete Chain Follow + RETURNING Propagation (M0100-0005)](0100-0005-loop22-merge-epq-delete-chain-and-returning.md) | accepted | (A) `mergeApplyDelete`: removed incorrect early `errMergeSourceUnmatched` for committed xmax (confused UPDATE with DELETE); now calls `mergeEPQRefreshSnap` then follows HOT/non-HOT chain — only returns unmatched when no successor. (B) `applyMod`: changed `mergePendingMod` arg to pointer so EPQ-recheck `newRow` propagates to `collectReturningRow`. MergeMatchRecheck first divergence L262→L416 (415/503 lines match). 14 existing PASS tests unchanged. |
| 0108-0001 | [`postgresql.conf.sample` template for goopg (M0108)](0108-0001-postgresql-conf-sample-template.md) | accepted | Hand-maintained `internal/config/postgresql.conf.sample` mirroring PG 18.3's section structure (FILE LOCATIONS / CONNECTIONS / RESOURCE USAGE / WAL / REPLICATION / QUERY TUNING / LOGGING / STATISTICS / AUTOVACUUM / CLIENT DEFAULTS / LOCK MGMT / ...). Every file-settable GUC in `config.BuildDefaultRegistry` (76 today) gets a commented-out entry with unit/range/restart-class/enum hints; default values equal the registry's `BootVal` so the file is a no-op until uncommented. `initdb` writes the embedded bytes to `<datadir>/postgresql.conf` via a new `config.SampleConfig() []byte` accessor (`//go:embed`), replacing the current 20-line `defaultPostgresqlConf`. Sync enforced by `TestSampleConfigCoversRegistry` (`internal/config/sample_test.go`) plus a written rule in `.ralph/AGENT.md` requiring every commit that adds/removes a GUC to update the sample in the same commit. Hand-maintained because per-GUC prose comments are not derivable from registry metadata (matches PG's own approach to `postgresql.conf.sample`). |
